# A06 — AUDIT: Signal intake and webhook lifecycle

**Status:** APPROVED — the AUDIT of topic A06 (Document 04, section 4, step 6), approved by Rama on 2026-11-20 with the A06 topic package (Decision Register, D-37); where it and the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD differ, the record governs
**Date:** 2026-11-18
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-18 (fieldwork of 18-Nov-2026, begun after ChatGPT's review passed the A06 STUDY, Revision 1, on 18-Nov-2026). Revision 1, 2026-11-18: ChatGPT's AUDIT review of 18-Nov-2026 (finding M-01): WD-01 reclassified from KNOWN DEFECT (provisional) to ISSUE, KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given (condition (a) not established); KNOWN DEFECT totals 2 to 1 (part WD 1 to 0); the dependent summaries in sections 1.8, 2.3, 7, 16, 18 and Appendix A updated; no other result, count or record changed. Revision 2, 2026-11-18: ChatGPT's re-review (finding M-02): the stale final clause of the Appendix A bullet on P03-S034 replaced; no result, count or record changed. Approved by Rama on 2026-11-20 with the A06 topic package (Decision Register, D-37): status marked APPROVED; content unchanged.
**Purpose and scope:** The comparison of what the legacy code actually does with what was intended, for the entire A06 scope of the Master System Investigation Index: the webhook receiver (its server, routes, request handling and lifecycle), authentication and request validation, deduplication, the per-IP limiter and the in-flight claims, the signal queue and back-pressure, the signal processor's intake, workers, expiry and normalisation up to the handoff to A07, signal-event creation and persistence, the entry throttle (Rama's decision B), the Chartink configuration and the scanner-URL checks with `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` (Rama's decision C), the end-of-day routing to the PB-01 capture worker (Rama's decision D), the `main.py` wiring at the A06 call sites, and, across them, the configuration A06 reads, the producers and consumers of every A06 artefact, the storage handoffs, the duplication record and the documents and records against the code, with every normal, error, malformed, duplicate, limit, timeout, restart, concurrency and shutdown flow, at the fixed baseline. It audits the STUDY's 101 AUDIT points, its 21 UNKNOWN items, its duplication rows, its findings on documents and records, the 52 items handed to A06, the 11 Open Issues Register entries that name it and the A06 scope beyond them, records each result (MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) with its evidence, and decides nothing.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Source Inventory, SRC-10), read from the local read-only clone on 18-Nov-2026; nothing in it was written or run, and no endpoint, scanner URL, broker or API was contacted.
**Related documents:** Documents 04, 05, 06, 07, 08 and 10; the Master System Investigation Index (entry A06, coverage item 5, Appendix A); the Decision Register, D-07, D-12, D-13, D-17, D-21, D-23 to D-25 and D-28; D-30 to D-33 and D-35 (the approved A01 to A05 topic packages whose records this AUDIT reads); and D-34 (recorded for A05 only; A06's working method is Rama's decision of 17-Nov-2026 in the Topic Completion Register, entry A06); the Topic Completion Register, entry A06 (Rama's decisions A to E of 17-Nov-2026); ChatGPT's A06 investigation direction of 17-Nov-2026 and its reviews of the A06 STUDY of 18-Nov-2026 (not kept as files, D-17); the A06 STUDY (`A06_STUDY_2026-11-17.md`, Revision 1) and its twelve evidence files; `evidence/A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md`; the A01 to A05 AUDITs and RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS, the Open Issues Register and the Audit Findings Register.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions, baseline and method

### 1.1 Purpose

This is the AUDIT of topic A06 (Document 04, section 4, step 6; Document 07, section 5). For every A06 area and flow it compares the actual behaviour, established by the STUDY and re-checked here against the code, with the intended behaviour found in the admitted sources, and records matches, issues and UNKNOWN items (Document 04, section 5.4). It classifies; it does not decide. Every issue is raised with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); no treatment, no repair of the legacy system and no V2 design is proposed here.

### 1.2 Scope

The scope is the STUDY's (its sections 1.2 and 3 to 17, and its source file inventory): the A06 paths of the Master System Investigation Index, `signals/webhook_receiver.py`, `signals/signal_processor.py`, `signals/entry_throttle.py` (with the empty `signals/__init__.py`), the Chartink integration and configuration references (`config/scan_webhook_map.yaml` and `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`, whose files A02 owns), the A06 call sites of `main.py` and `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py`; and the A06 side of the boundary files that other topics own (the configuration keys A06 reads, the `signals`, `webhook_audit` and `cron_heartbeat` tables, the readers and writers of A06's artefacts, the boot and pre-flight checks, the alerts and the schedule declarations), recorded at the interface. Rama's boundary decisions of 17-Nov-2026 (the Topic Completion Register, entry A06) apply unchanged: (A) `signals/signal_processor.py` is audited far enough to cover intake, queueing, expiry, the worker lifecycle, deduplication and in-flight controls, signal-event creation and persistence and the handoff to A07, and every later call-out is recorded at the interface only, with its receiving owner; (B) the entry throttle is audited in full with its three call sites, the admission decisions there staying with A07 and A09; (C) scanner-URL reachability and `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` are A06's, the checks of `utils/startup_checks.py` (A01) and `scripts/preflight/checks/signals.py` (A20) are recorded at the interface; (D) the receiver's routing of end-of-day alerts to the PB-01 capture worker and the contract it passes are A06's, the worker A18's; (E) `scripts/test_webhook.py` and `scripts/load_test_signals.py` are A19's and are cited as stated expectations only. Section 2 shows the coverage by part and file, by flow, by STUDY AUDIT point, by STUDY UNKNOWN item, by duplication row and for the documents and records.

Where A06's responsibility ends, the A06-to-A07 handoff line, is OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY section 17, point 1): reading (a) puts the line at the dequeue and the start of `_process_one`, reading (b) at the screening call (`signals/signal_processor.py` L998). This AUDIT adopts neither reading. The records are written so that either reading can be applied: the strategy lookup is recorded as a MATCH with its owner OPEN FOR RAMA (PQ-14), and the contract at each candidate line is set out in section 9.5.1 (EV-19).

### 1.3 Exclusions and ownership boundaries

- Callers' and callees' logic belongs to the owner topics: `main.py` as a file, orchestration, start-up checks and `MarketWindows` to A01; configuration sources, schemas, defaults and precedence to A02; the database schemas, persistence, connections and write discipline to A03; the broker adapter and the broker-wide rate limiter to A04; the instrument cache and the alias tool to A05 (the alias consumer is A06's); strategy selection, strategy control, screening, gates and the trade decision to A07; technical analysis, S&R and the retest path to A08; price, sizing, risk, capital, the governor and the reservation to A09; order placement to A10; the shadow tracker to A11; the end-of-day cleanup to A12; the kill switch to A13; alerts to A14; reports, the dashboard, the health server and the evidence recorder to A15; schedules, the cron registry and the heartbeat helper to A16; deployment and installed packages to A17; the V3 chain, the allocator and PB-01 to A18; tests and test scripts to A19; pre-flight checks to A20 (Master System Investigation Index). This AUDIT records the contract that A06 passes or receives and how the other side uses it at the interface, names the owner, and hands the rest over (each part's section .5, and section 14). A finding inside another topic's code is a handoff and is not counted as an A06 ISSUE; A06 records and counts its own part.
- The 52 inherited items and the 11 Open Issues Register entries that name A06 keep their original owners, IDs, classifications, statuses and treatments (D-13; D-30 to D-33 and D-35): A06 records its own side only (section 4). An AGREED new-system treatment or a Phase 2 input is never evidence of what the legacy system was meant to do. A03 G-19 is cited by its ID only.
- Credentials (D-07 and Rama's standing instruction): no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation is made, and no alert, issue or recommendation about it; `config/accounts.csv`, `.env` files, `*.local.yaml` overlays and token files were never opened; the webhook secret is named only as `WEBHOOK_SECRET`; credential variable names are recorded as configuration only (section 18, security restriction). The functional audit of what the A06 code does with the secret it is given (how the receiver checks a token or a signature) is in scope.
- Scheduling: cron, systemd and pre-flight declarations are boundary facts; no declaration is taken as proof that a job ran.
- Tests belong to A19 and are cited only as evidence of intent or behaviour (HISTORICAL), never run and never taken as proof of runtime behaviour.
- Paper-only paths are not audited (live-only project); they are marked where a Live and a paper branch split.
- Chartink's public help pages and the published library sources are read as current external context only, with their dates: a statement from them is a fact about the page or the published text, not about what Chartink sent in the legacy period or what was installed on the legacy machines. No scanner, screener or webhook address was opened.
- No repair of the legacy system, no treatment and no V2 design.

### 1.4 Baseline

Every line reference is to commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (D-21). Before and after the work of every part on 18-Nov-2026, `git rev-parse HEAD` in the clone returned it and `git status --short` was empty (each part's section .1 and section .7), and the same held before and after the work on the intended-behaviour sources and the cross-part check (section 18). The last recorded deployment is commits `686df1c` and `7d4970a` (both 2026-09-02); the baseline is 32 commits later, and two A06 files changed after `7d4970a`: the receiver (`3b15bbf`, WR4b, 2026-09-09) and the processor (six commits of 2026-09-10 and 11, the evidence capture) (STUDY section 1.8). Whether production ran any commit after `7d4970a` is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (WI-24; EV-09, EV-14).

### 1.5 Method

- Rama approved the use of read-only helpers for A06 on 17-Nov-2026, at most two at a time, a new pair starting only after both of the previous pair had finished (Topic Completion Register, entry A06), and repeated the approval on 18-Nov-2026 for the AUDIT, with his instructions that nothing be assumed and no work be lost. The audit was done by AI helpers under the same read-only rules as the STUDY: one drafted the intended-behaviour sources (the evidence file `evidence/A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md`) and eight drafted the audit parts, each with its own prefix: WI (receiver ingress, routes, server and lifecycle), WA (receiver authentication, validation and answers), WD (duplicate layers, limits, in-flight claims and queue admission), PQ (processor intake, workers, expiry and normalisation), EV (event creation, persistence and the handoff map), ET (the entry throttle), CK (Chartink configuration and scanner checks) and XA (cross-cutting: the configuration A06 reads, producers, consumers and storage at the A03 boundary, the duplication record, documents and records, and the inherited items and register entries assigned to it). Each STUDY AUDIT point, UNKNOWN item, inherited item and register entry was allocated to exactly one part before the fieldwork (sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4 name the owning part of each).
- Each part and the intended-behaviour sources file was checked by a second helper against the code and the sources, which re-read every record and corrected it where needed; no check changed a result except EV-12 (UNKNOWN to MATCH, on two sources found in the check) and the withdrawal of EV-16 (not a discrepancy in a project record or the STUDY); CK-19 was added in the check. A cross-part check then completed the sources file with the sources the parts had cited beyond it (IS-177 to IS-223), checked the eight parts against each other (coverage of all 185 allocated items with one owning disposition each; double counting; cross-references; dates and quotations; owners against the Master System Investigation Index; the A06-to-A07 line; the inherited items' quoted classifications, treatments and statuses), and mapped the parts' citations to IS numbers; no result, count or classification was changed by it. Claude assembled this document with a helper and made the assembly decisions recorded in section 1.8; at assembly 58 references to working files and helper labels and eight references to the parts' own check logs (which are not kept) were replaced in the parts' text by references to this AUDIT or by plain words, and one other topic's ID written without its topic was corrected (part EV, section 9.5.2), each logged in the part's check log (no result, count or classification changed). The assembled document was then checked independently (section 18).
- Work in progress was saved after each record batch and backed up to the temporary branch `wip/a06-fieldwork` of algocore_system (never merged into `main`). The session's server restarted several times on 18-Nov-2026; each interrupted helper resumed from its saved files and nothing was lost.
- Each part re-read every cited line at the baseline with `git show`, `git grep`, `git log` and `git blame` (hashes and dates only). Tests were read as documents only. Nothing in the clone was written, run or imported, and no broker, API, database, scanner, webhook or Chartink address was contacted. Every repository-wide search excluded the account file, environment files, local overlays and token files. No generic Python test was needed or run for this AUDIT; one part parsed the two scanner files' text with PyYAML's `safe_load` as data only (part CK, section 1), which executes nothing.
- Library and external-service behaviour was taken from authoritative sources only and is labelled current external context, with the date read; what the parts read on 18-Nov-2026 (the URLs are in each part's section 1): waitress 3.0.2's published source (`task.py`, `server.py`, `__init__.py` and `wasyncore.py`, part WI; `parser.py`, `rfc7230.py`, `channel.py`, `buffers.py`, `adjustments.py` and `utilities.py`, part WA); Python documentation pages of version 3.15.2 (`socket`, `queue`, `concurrent.futures`, `datetime`, `threading`, `time`, `collections`, `sqlite3`, built-in exceptions and functions, "The try statement", simple statements, the tutorial's "Errors and Exceptions"; parts WI, WD, PQ, EV, ET, XA) and of versions 3.11 and 3.13 (expressions, built-in functions, built-in exceptions, the `sys.path` initialisation; parts WA and CK); CPython's published source (3.11 `hmac`, `codecs`, `pystrhex`, `_hashopenssl`, `abstract`, `floatobject`, `longobject`, `urllib/parse` and `_sqlite/cursor`, with `hmac`, `_hashopenssl` and `_operator` on the 3.12, 3.13 and main branches, part WA; 3.12 `concurrent/futures/thread.py`, `queue.py` and `_sqlite/util.c`, parts WD, PQ and EV); Werkzeug's published `sansio/request.py` (part WA); SQLite's pages "Write-Ahead Logging", "Result and Error Codes", "PRAGMA Statements", "BEGIN TRANSACTION", "Date And Time Functions" and "Quirks" and its published source (parts WA, WD, EV, XA); cachetools' source at tag v7.1.1 (part WD); Requests' published source (`adapters.py` at tag v2.33.1 and on `main`, `exceptions.py`, `__version__.py`) and urllib3's `util/timeout.py` and `CHANGES.rst` (part CK); pydantic's "Models" page (part XA); and Chartink's own pages "Webhook support for Alerts", "Alerts FAQ" and "Alerts Guide" (the sources file) and "Stock Screener FAQ" and "Scanner User Guide" (part CK; the last four read in part). Not reachable: the Linux `socket(7)` manual page (part WI: the effect of `SO_REUSEADDR` on a port held by another listener stays not stated); in part WA's check, sqlite.org itself, so that part read SQLite's source from its official Git mirror. Pages read for the STUDY on 17-Nov-2026 are used through the STUDY statements that cite them. The installed versions on the legacy machines are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED unless `requirements.txt` pins them (section 18).
- Intended behaviour was sought in every admitted source: the module, class and function docstrings, comments, log, alert and response texts of the A06 paths; `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` (its A06 entries G2a, P6, P9a, P15, P16, P17, ID1 and ID2, S1, S10, S14, S15, SS14 and the `principles` block; it holds no WR, SP, SPW or PF block, whose labels live in the module instructions and the code's docstrings); the module instructions (M19, M20, M30, M33, M34, M35); the v2 design specification; the legacy design, decision, audit, investigation and operating documents (among them `DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8, the configuration guide, the entry-throttle admission decision of 19-Jul-2026, the security and integrity audits, the system map and `PATHS.md`); the configuration files and their comments; `config/expected_managers.yaml`; commit messages; the five existing technical records; the approved A01 to A05 records; and Rama's recorded answers in the Open Issues Register. Every legacy source is HISTORICAL. Where sources conflict, both are recorded and none is chosen; where no source states the intent, the record says "not established" and audits the code on its own (Document 04, section 5.3).

### 1.6 Record fields, results, classifications and the KNOWN DEFECT rule

- Record fields (Document 04, section 5.4; Document 10, section 7): area and flow; issue category (Document 04, section 5.3); intended behaviour and its source (path and lines or key, with its date), or "not established"; the actual code path; the actual behaviour and the effective value; the evidence (STUDY evidence statement IDs and code lines); the result and its classification; the severity or impact where established; the owner topic; the cross-reference to the STUDY point or question and to any inherited item; and whether Rama's intent or decision is needed. Issues and UNKNOWN items are blocks (each part's section .3); matches are table rows with the same information in short form (each part's section .4), except EV-12, a block kept in place after its result changed to MATCH in the check.
- Results, as in the A01 to A05 AUDITs (their section 1.6): MATCH (the code does what the sources intend); ISSUE (a difference from intended behaviour; a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way; or, where intent is not established, a defect or weakness that the code itself demonstrates, of a kind that Document 04, section 5.3 lists, the basis being named in the record); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the answer needs runtime evidence that does not survive, or intent is not established and the code demonstrates no such defect or weakness). A lack of intent evidence alone does not make an ISSUE. An item with a MATCH part and an ISSUE part is counted as ISSUE. A WITHDRAWN record keeps its ID with a note and is not counted (EV-16). Severity words ("minor", "latent") are used only where the record gives their basis.
- Records observations are a separate kind: a true discrepancy found in a project record or in the STUDY (WA-02, EV-15, XA-12). They are not results about the legacy system and are not counted as MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN; no register and no STUDY text is changed by them (section 13).
- Classifications (Document 04, section 6): FACT, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL, KNOWN DEFECT, OPEN QUESTION and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; anything Rama must decide is marked OPEN FOR RAMA.
- The KNOWN DEFECT rule is the A01 AUDIT's, section 1.6, applied unchanged: (a) the code implements a mechanism for the case; (b) by the language or library semantics that mechanism fails for certain whenever the case occurs; (c) the case is one that the code or an intent source says it handles, and it can arise under the documented set-up; (d) the outcome contradicts every intent source found. Each record that gives or considers KNOWN DEFECT states which conditions hold and which fails. Every KNOWN DEFECT is provisional until ChatGPT's review and the issue discussion with Rama. A prior topic's KNOWN DEFECT or classification keeps that topic's record (A05 IN-08 stays A05's; its A06 side is PQ-07).

### 1.7 Identifiers and conventions

- WI-nn, WA-nn, WD-nn, PQ-nn, EV-nn, ET-nn, CK-nn and XA-nn are this AUDIT's items in parts WI (section 5), WA (6), WD (7), PQ (8), EV (9), ET (10), CK (11) and XA (12). Within each part the issue and UNKNOWN items are numbered first and the match rows after them; an item added in the check continues the sequence (CK-19); no ID was renumbered. These prefixes collide with no ID of A01 to A05: no prior AUDIT record or Audit Findings Register row has any of them (checked by search of the project repository). A one-letter prefix of a prior topic followed by a letter can look alike (A01 W in "WI", "WA" and "WD"; A01 C in "CK"; A02 X in "XA"; A03 P in "PQ"; A03 E in "EV" and "ET"), but prior topics' IDs are written with their topic (for example A01 W-24, A01 C14, A02 X-01, A03 P-02, A05 XC-04) or, inside a passage, table row or list that already names the topic or the inherited item, by their bare ID (for example "K-12's throttle side", "RL-16 itself not counted again", "C14, B-28, IS-9.14, IN-08"), and an A06 ID is never written with a topic; no prior topic's ID has any of the eight A06 prefixes. They are not the legacy labels (for example WR4b, WR13, SP4, SPW3, PF3, FIX-011, FIX-069, M-S2, C-2, C6, AB-910, H-16, BL-18, G.1, P6, P15, P17, S10, DEC10) of the legacy documents.
- Inside sections 5 to 12, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means that part's own subsection (each part's closing check log, its section 8 or, in part WD, an unnumbered last section, is working material and is not kept: D-17) (for example, inside section 9, "section 5.1" is section 9.5.1). A part's own lower-level subsections are written with their full number in this AUDIT (for example "section 12.3.2"), and a reference to another part names the part or gives this AUDIT's section number. Every other section reference names its document ("STUDY section 17", "Document 04, section 5.3"), or is to this AUDIT where it says so ("this AUDIT, section 1.3") or where the number has a dot and is outside 5 to 12.
- IS-, IC-, CD- and RS-numbers are those of the evidence file `evidence/A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md` (written `A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md` in the parts: its sources, conflicts between sources, statements that only the code contradicts and statements attributed to Rama or the operator); another topic's IS or IC numbers are written with their topic (A01 IS-9.14, A01 IC-31, A02 IC-38, A02 IC-40) or in a phrase that names it (for example "A01 recorded the boot side as its IC-31", "the A02 AUDIT (IC-38 row, L161 ...)", "A02 X-01, IC-38"). The short names of legacy sources (RECV, PROC, THR, PFS, MAIN-A06, LOCKED-YAML, M19 to M35, SPEC, SYSCFG, CFGLOADER, SCHEMA, SCANMAP, CKS, DEPLOY, CONFIG-GUIDE, SMAP, PATHS, INTEGRITY-AUDIT, AUDIT-B, C2-IP, DEC10, EXPECTED, CK-WEBHOOK and the others) are those of that file, section 1.4. Pxx-Snnn are statement IDs of the STUDY's evidence files. "18.1 point n" is the STUDY's AUDIT point n, "18.2 item n" its UNKNOWN item n, and "STUDY question n" its question n (section 19 of the STUDY).
- Security: credential values, tokens, account identifiers, personal names other than the Project Owner's, e-mail addresses, IP addresses, fingerprints and absolute machine paths are never written; per-account names are written `<ACCOUNT_ID>`; the configured all-interfaces and loopback addresses are written in words; passages that hold such material (among them the WR4b docstring, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L112 to L128) are cited by path and line only.
- Line references are written "L123" or "L123 to L130"; dates as 2026-11-18 or 18-Nov-2026; times as IST.

- 160 IDs: 156 counted items (58 + 84 + 14), three records observations and one withdrawn ID. The totals were re-counted by script at assembly from the record blocks (each part's section .3) and match rows (section .4) of the eight parts, and agree with each part's section .6 and with the cross-part check. Where one behaviour appears in two parts, the part that owns the mechanism keeps the count and the other cites it; the cross-part check examined the pairs (for example the missing heartbeat, WI-01, against the release keyed by symbol, WD-08; the missing audit row, counted in WI-02 and cited by WA-08; the Chartink-retry UNKNOWN, WA-09's, against WD-05, which is an ISSUE on its own parts (the failed mark and the restart); the throttle side, the retry timing and the double release, ET-02, PQ-04 and EV-05; the reader side, layer 1, the writer side and WR10's list, XA-01, WD-07, EV-07 and WI-15) and found no behaviour counted twice.
- KNOWN DEFECT (provisional; one, within ISSUE):
  - PQ-07: A05 IN-08's A06 side: `_sector_for` asks the instrument cache for two methods it does not define and returns "UNKNOWN" for every symbol. All four conditions hold on A06's own sources: (a) `_sector_for` is the mechanism; (b) by `getattr` semantics with a default the mechanism fails whenever an `InstrumentCache` is wired and a builder runs; (c) the code and its comments say the cache is passed for the sector lookup, and both builders are reached at the committed `"shadow"` values; (d) every source found treats the result as a fault; the 16-Jul-2026 record's deliberate deferral is weighed under (d) for the review.
  - Considered and not given, each with the failing condition stated in its record: WI-01, WI-02, WI-03 part (ii), WI-07; WA-03, WA-07; WD-01 (condition (a) not established; refines the STUDY's provisional P03-S034), WD-02, WD-04, WD-05, WD-08, WD-09; PQ-01, PQ-02 part (ii), PQ-04, PQ-05 part (ii), PQ-06 part (i); EV-02, EV-03, EV-04, EV-05, EV-07 part (b), EV-08; ET-01, ET-02, ET-05; CK-04, CK-07, CK-08, CK-19.
- Assembly decisions recorded here (no result was changed at assembly):
  1. Records observations (WA-02, EV-15, XA-12) are listed separately and not counted, as STUDY section 16 lists its records observation; they are collected in section 13.
  2. WI-01 (the in-flight heartbeat never connected) is kept as the part classifies it: ISSUE, KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given because condition (b) fails: the heartbeat mechanism works whenever the optional callback is passed, and its silence follows from the `main.py` wiring, not from language or library semantics. This follows the A05 AUDIT's reading of LF-02 and the A01 AUDIT's of C30 ("unwired, not failing"). The record distinguishes A01 C3, which the A01 AUDIT gave KNOWN DEFECT because a check that `main.py` does wire can never be satisfied "in the wired code"; here the callback is not wired at all. The distinction is put to ChatGPT's review.
  3. WD-01 (claims kept after an exception inside the `IntegrityError` handler) is ISSUE, KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: condition (a) is not established, because the `except Exception` clause L1016 is a sibling handler that does not catch an exception raised inside the `IntegrityError` handler, and the sources do not settle whether it was meant for any store failure or for INSERT failures only (ChatGPT's AUDIT review of 18-Nov-2026, finding M-01). This refines the STUDY's provisional classification P03-S034 for that route (Appendix A); P03-S034 is not changed.
  4. PQ-07 is A06's classification of its own side of A05 IN-08, which A05 left to A06 (STUDY section 16, partly covered item (d)); A05's record of IN-08 ("ISSUE; FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL"; treatment A05 TG12, AGREED, 17-Nov-2026; status AGREED) is unchanged, and the KNOWN DEFECT is A06's, provisional.
  5. PQ-14 (the strategy lookup, MATCH) is counted in part PQ with its owner OPEN FOR RAMA; if Rama chooses reading (a) of the A06-to-A07 line, the row passes to A07's AUDIT and the MATCH total becomes 57. No other record's count depends on the line.
  6. EV-12, whose result changed from UNKNOWN to MATCH in the check, keeps its block in section 9.3 and is counted once, as MATCH.
- Items whose classification needs Rama's intent (section 16(b)): EV-13 (STUDY question 24), ET-06 (STUDY question 43) and XA-04 (STUDY question 36), each UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED and OPEN FOR RAMA. In addition, parts of records wait on Rama without changing any count: WI-09's UNKNOWN part (the production network path; STUDY section 17, point 4, and STUDY question 6), WA-08's part (i) (what the 1 MiB limit was meant to bound; STUDY question 12) and PQ-14's owner (STUDY section 17, point 1). The ISSUEs that rest on a recorded conflict between sources classify without him, and the choice between the sources is a treatment question (section 15). Every other ISSUE goes to Rama for treatment after ChatGPT's review.

## 2. Audit coverage matrix

### 2.1 By A06 part and file

One row per audit part, with the A06 components and files it audits (the STUDY sections in brackets; the part's section in this AUDIT in brackets after the prefix). Every counted item of a part is listed in its row (ISSUE and UNKNOWN items from the part's section .3, MATCH rows from its section .4, EV-12 from section 9.3); records observations and the withdrawn ID are named after the MATCH rows and are not counted. Components owned by other topics appear only through the A06 side that the part audits. Totals re-counted by script from the records of the eight parts: MATCH 58, ISSUE 84, UNKNOWN 14, KNOWN DEFECT 2 (within ISSUE); 156 counted records; one withdrawn; three records observations.

### 2.2 By flow

An item is listed under a flow only when its record's title or "Area and flow" field (for a match row, its area cell) names that route; the other fields are not used. Selected by script on those fields, with the keywords that each flow's description names, and read for loose matches (a keyword met in another sense is not counted as the route: for example "validated" for a configuration key, "(empty body)" in a network-failure text, an `order` token of the broker limiter, a bind failure under authentication, the "claims" of the existing records, the scanner script's own fetch retries, and a record file named `03_STARTUP_...`); an item can be listed under several flows, so the row counts do not add up to the totals, and the selection is a reading aid, not a classification. ### 2.3 The STUDY's AUDIT points (STUDY section 18.1)

Each of the 101 points of STUDY section 18.1 was allocated to one part, which disposes of it in its dispositions table (sections 5.2 to 12.2, table .2.1), written here "part: disposition; records". The disposition words are the part's own (CONFIRMED, SPLIT, COVERED, REJECTED as an issue, NO RECORD and so on); records of other parts named in the owning row are listed with it, and a side row in another part is named as such. ### 2.4 The STUDY's UNKNOWN items (STUDY section 18.2)

Each of the 21 items of STUDY section 18.2, the part to which it was allocated, and the records that carry it; each part keeps its own side as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED in its own records and in its "could not establish" list (sections 5.7 to 12.7), and no item is classified differently by two parts. None of them is resolved by the AUDIT for what ran: no runtime evidence of the legacy period survives (section 18). Where an item is narrowed, the record carries the code-establishable or published-source side, and the installed versions and the runtime stay UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

## 3. Intended-behaviour sources and conflicts

The inventory is the evidence file `evidence/A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md`, committed with this AUDIT (drafted by a helper, checked by a second, then completed in the cross-part check with the sources the parts had cited beyond it). It holds 223 sources by area (IS-01 to IS-223; IS-158 to IS-162 were added after the first pass, IS-163 to IS-176 in the check and IS-177 to IS-223 in the cross-part check, each at the end of its area table): (a) receiver ingress, routes, server and lifecycle, 43; (b) authentication and validation, answers and audit rows, 27; (c) duplicates, limits, in-flight claims, queue admission and back-pressure, 21; (d) processor intake, workers, expiry and normalisation, 32; (e) signal-event creation, persistence and the handoff, 20; (f) the entry throttle, 16; (g) Chartink configuration, the scanner map and the scanner checks, 26; (h) A06 configuration and its comments, 7; (i) storage, producers and consumers, 11; (j) the legacy records and documents, 12; (k) the approved A01 to A05 records and Rama's recorded statements, 8. It also holds 29 conflicts between sources (IC-01 to IC-30; IC-15 withdrawn in its check), 31 numbered statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-36; CD-11, CD-16, CD-18, CD-19 and CD-30 withdrawn in its check into IC-04, IC-27, IC-28, IC-29 and IC-30), 15 statements attributed to Rama or the operator (RS-01 to RS-15), its rows for the 101 STUDY 18.1 points (its section 6.1) and the 53 STUDY questions (its section 6.2), and what it could not establish (its section 7).

The source families searched: `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` at its A06 entries (G2a, P6, P9a, P15, P16, P17, P18 for the status vocabulary, ID1 and ID2, S1, S10, S14, S15, SS14 and the `principles` block; it holds no WR, SP, SPW or PF block, and every cited A06 decision line dates from 17-Apr-2026); the docstrings, comments, log and response texts of the A06 paths and the A06 call sites of `main.py`, dated line by line with `git blame`; the module instructions M19, M20, M30, M33, M34 and M35; the configuration and schema comments (committed values only; A02 and A03 own the files); the legacy documents at their A06 lines (`DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8, the configuration guide, the entry-throttle admission decision, the system map, `PATHS.md`, the integrity audit, the 16/17-Jul-2026 security audit for its functional findings only, the source-address investigation of 03-Jul-2026, the staleness sweep, the 19-Jul-2026 census and throttle reports, the 07-Aug-2026 rulings verification, and others); the early design and audit documents under `docs/web_claude/`; commit messages; the boundary files of other topics at their A06 lines (interface only); Chartink's three alert pages (read 18-Nov-2026); and the project's own records (the STUDY and its evidence, the Open Issues Register entries naming A06, the Topic Completion Register entry A06, the Decision Register). Passages of legacy documents that concern credential exposure, history or validity were not used (D-07).

No legacy source quotes Rama himself stating an intent for an A06 behaviour (sources file, section 5). RS-01 is this project's record of Rama's own answers on the eleven Open Issues Register entries that name A06 (history OPEN on every entry asked; treatments and Phase 2 inputs as recorded, which are not legacy intent). RS-02 to RS-15 are legacy documents' attributions to Rama or the operator, in their authors' words and at their dates: among them the 16/17-Jul-2026 security audit's "accepted, Rama-owned" for `require_hmac: false` with the all-interfaces bind (RS-05; it does not change Rama's recorded answer on A02-13, history OPEN), the entry-throttle admission decision "OPEN — Rama's call" (RS-06, RS-14), the operator's set-up of the Chartink side (RS-07, RS-08, RS-15) and the 07-Aug-2026 ruling that a flat symbol "immediately becomes eligible again" (RS-12, A09's rule; the throttle side is IC-24). They are cited in WA-01 (RS-05), ET-06 (RS-06, RS-14), ET-05 (RS-12), CK-06, CK-10, CK-11 and CK-17 (RS-07, RS-08) and WD-12 (RS-09), and bear on STUDY questions 2, 6, 28, 34, 42, 43, 46, 47, 50 and 51 (section 15).

The 29 conflicts are listed below with the items whose records rely on them (by citation; for part WI, which cites its sources by path and line, by the IS rows that the cross-part check mapped to its records); none is chosen here. Where a later source names and replaces an earlier one, the sources file says so; the code's side is stated in the records.

The withdrawn CD-11, CD-16, CD-18, CD-19 and CD-30 are cited only as withdrawn (in WD-06, WD-07 and the sources file's question rows).

A point that bears on every comparison with production: every file, value and declaration in this AUDIT is the one at the baseline. Which commit and which files were in force on either machine (whether production ran any commit after `7d4970a`, so whether WR4b and the evidence capture were ever in force), which configuration values, environment, working directory and network path were in effect, what Chartink sent and whether any declared job or operator check ran, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (Document 08, section 10; section 18).

## 5. Part WI — Receiver ingress, routes, server and lifecycle

**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (`970aabf`)
**Scope:** STUDY sections 3 and 11 (part P01; the `main.py` wiring of the receiver), with the receiver rows of section
12: bind, port, waitress threads, Flask routes and methods, the per-request flow order, `/health`, the construction and
start order, the sweeper, stop and shutdown, exceptions, restart behaviour, the network path. Direction questions 2, 3,
9, 12, 14, 21, 22 (and 26 to 28, which apply to every part). 43 allocated items (this AUDIT, sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4).

### 5.1 Scope and method

### 5.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### WI-01 — The in-flight heartbeat is never connected, so the sweeper evicts every claim older than 60 s and logs it as a stalled worker, against the FIX-011 design

- **Area and flow:** in-flight control at the receiver: the per-symbol claim (WR17), its heartbeat and the sweeper's
  eviction; the claim's release by the processor.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Failure handling
  (an alarm that fires wrongly); Flow and state (the same symbol processed concurrently).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L203 to L205 (`a826dfe`, 12-May-2026): FIX-011,
  "heartbeat-aware lock tracking ... Sweeper evicts if (now - heartbeat_at) > 60s, NOT (now - acquired_at)". (2) The
  sweeper docstring L1183 to L1187 (`a826dfe`): "A lock that has been held for 400s but continues heartbeating is NOT
  evicted (active processing). A lock with no heartbeat for 60s IS evicted (stalled worker)." (3) `update_heartbeat`
  docstring L1111 to L1115 (`a826dfe`): "Called by signal_processor at checkpoints during processing to prove the worker
  is still alive." (4) Commit message `a826dfe` (12-May-2026): "Wire heartbeat callback from WebhookReceiver to
  SignalProcessor". (5) The processor's helper docstring `signals/signal_processor.py` L665 to L667: "No-op if heartbeat
  callback is not wired." (6) WR17 (module docstring L29; `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L164 to L170, 17-Apr-2026):
  in-flight tracking "to prevent same-symbol concurrent processing". All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L206 to L208 (dict, lock, literal 60.0 s), L1110 to L1120
  (`update_heartbeat`), L1182 to L1207 (`_run_sweeper`); `signals/signal_processor.py` L172, L219, L664 to L673;
  `main.py` L3611 to L3612 (only `in_flight_release_fn` passed).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `update_heartbeat` has no non-test caller; `main.py` passes
  `in_flight_release_fn=webhook_receiver.release_in_flight` and no `in_flight_heartbeat_fn`, so the processor's
  `_heartbeat` does nothing at its five checkpoints (L669); no commit in the history of `main.py` ever added or removed
  `in_flight_heartbeat` or `update_heartbeat` (`git log -S`, no hit); `a826dfe` changed no `main.py` line (P01-S077,
  P01-S078). INTERPRETATION: `heartbeat_at` stays equal to `acquired_at`, so at each 60 s pass the sweeper deletes every
  claim more than 60 s old, whether or not a worker still holds it, and logs one CRITICAL "evicted STALLED symbol ...
  signal_processor worker likely crashed or deadlocked" line per symbol (L1199 to L1207); a second signal for the same
  symbol can then pass the claim while the first is still being processed (the dedup layers may still stop it); the
  processor's later release is by symbol, so it can remove the second signal's claim (P01-S079; P03-S032, P03-S033,
  part WD). The timeout is a literal 60.0 s (L208), not tied to `dedup_window_seconds` (P08-S034).
- **Evidence:** P01-S015, P01-S076 to P01-S079; P03-S032, P03-S033; P04-S057, P04-S058; P10-S053, P10-S168; P11-S010;
  code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The code does not do what sources (1) to (4)
  say: the eviction rule rests on the claim time alone, and the CRITICAL text names a crashed or deadlocked worker for
  every claim that simply lived longer than 60 s. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a heartbeat mechanism
  exists for the case); (b) fails: the mechanism works whenever the callback is passed; its silence follows from the
  `main.py` wiring, which never passes the optional callback (default `None`, helper a no-op), not from language or
  library semantics. This is the reading the A05 AUDIT applied to LF-02 (an optional kill switch and notifier never
  injected: "condition (b) is about the mechanism, which works when injected") and the A01 AUDIT to C30 ("unwired, not
  failing"). It differs from A01 C3, given KNOWN DEFECT because a check that `main.py` does wire can never be satisfied
  "in the wired code"; here the heartbeat callback is not wired at all. Read against the wired code instead, eviction
  would be certain only for a claim still held at the first sweep pass at which it is over 60 s old (between about 60 s
  and 120 s after the claim, P03-S032): for holds between 60 s and about 120 s the outcome depends on timing (the
  reading the A01 AUDIT applied to C14 and C19), and only for longer holds would it be certain; so the classification
  rests on the first reason. (c) holds: the code's own comments describe the
  long-running case (L1185 to L1187), and INTERPRETATION: it can arise under the documented set-up, because a claim spans
  the queue wait and the processing (claimed at receipt, released at the end of `_process_one`, P04-S057) and nothing
  bounds that span below 60 s (the file's `expiry_sec` is 600). (d) holds (the outcome contradicts every source found;
  none says that claims should be evicted by age alone). Provisional, as every KNOWN DEFECT judgement, until ChatGPT's
  review and Rama.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: how long claims were held, and whether any eviction or
  second admission occurred, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (no runtime evidence; STUDY 18.2 item 1).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver and processor); A01 (`main.py` wiring that omits the callback).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 1; STUDY question 1; part WD (the claims and the release by symbol), part PQ (the
  checkpoints and how long workers hold claims); WI-11 (the sweeper itself).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (the code's own comments and the commit message state the intent); STUDY question 1
  bears on treatment only.

##### WI-02 — WR13 says every POST writes one `webhook_audit` row regardless of outcome; some POSTs write none

- **Area and flow:** request intake and audit: which requests reach `_handle_webhook` and its `webhook_audit` writes.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Failure handling
  (forensic record missing for refused requests).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) WR13, module docstring L25 (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026): "webhook_audit row per
  POST regardless of outcome". (2) `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L128 to L134 (17-Apr-2026): "Every POST writes one
  row regardless of outcome. Used for forensics and rate analysis." (3) `core/schema.sql` L576 to L577 (A03's file): an
  "Append-only audit row per POST to /webhook/<scanner_name>", "Written regardless of outcome (WR13)" (P10-S136); it
  scopes the rule to POSTs to the webhook route. (4) `config/expected_managers.yaml` L154 (lines `a5c3704`, 01-Aug-2026; file last changed `0197923`, 04-Aug-2026; IS-134): the
  receiver's census unit means "a POST audited". All HISTORICAL. No source asks for an audit row for GET, HEAD or
  OPTIONS requests; `/health` was put behind the secret and the limiter on 17-Jul-2026 (L366 to L381, `84cee3e` and
  `6e2a826`; its authentication call rewritten by `e8ec604`, 25-Jul-2026, L382 to L393) with no audit write and no
  stated intent for one.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L251 (`MAX_CONTENT_LENGTH`), L364, L406 (route methods), L477 to
  L534 (`_handle_webhook`: body read at L480 outside the `try` of L510; audit writes at L487, L497, L531), L1126 to L1154
  (`_write_audit`, errors logged only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the three audit writes are all inside `_handle_webhook`, after the
  body read at L480. INTERPRETATION, current external context (Flask and Werkzeug 3.1.x, read 17-Nov-2026, as cited by
  P01-S027, P01-S030 and P02-S064): (a) a POST to `/webhook/<name>` whose declared length exceeds 1,048,576 bytes fails
  at L480 with 413 before any audit write and before the limiter; (b) a POST to `/health` (405), to a path with a
  further slash after `/webhook/`, or to any other path (404) is answered by Flask's routing and never enters the
  receiver; (c) waitress's own refusals (413, 431, 400 before the application runs; P02-S071, P02-S074) write nothing;
  (d) a failed audit INSERT is logged at ERROR and the answer is still returned (L1153 to L1154; P11-S031). FACT and
  INTERPRETATION: every POST that reaches `_handle_webhook` past L480 attempts exactly one row (the shutdown 503, the
  429, and the `finally` for everything else), which matches (1) and (2) for those requests; the row is missing only
  when that INSERT itself fails, (d).
- **Evidence:** P01-S027, P01-S028, P01-S030 to P01-S033, P01-S118; P02-S064, P02-S069 rows 1 to 4, P02-S071,
  P02-S074; P10-S051, P10-S167; P11-S031; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (with a MATCH part). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The oversize case (a) and
  waitress's refusals of POSTs to the webhook route (c) contradict every source, (3) included, because they are POSTs
  to `/webhook/<scanner_name>`; the misrouted POSTs (b) contradict only the wider wording of (1) and (2) ("every POST"),
  not (3)'s narrower one, so the sources differ on (b) and none is chosen. A failed audit INSERT (d) is recorded, not
  counted: no source speaks of a failed audit write. GET and OPTIONS requests (including every `/health` call) writing
  no row is outside WR13's words and is not counted as part of the issue. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: the
  audit write is reached by design only inside the handler; no mechanism exists for the refused cases (condition (a)
  fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** the forensic count ("Used for forensics and rate analysis") misses refused
  oversize and misrouted POSTs; whether any occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); A03 (the table, boundary only).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 2; STUDY question 2; part WA (the oversize path, 18.1 point 23); WI-18 (the counts of
  the rows that are written).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 2 asks whether non-POST requests and `/health` calls were also meant
  to be audited, which bears on treatment only.

##### WI-03 — Shutdown: the receiver's `stop()` neither flushes nor closes the server, nothing waits for requests in progress, and the documented orders conflict

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's lifecycle at stop: `stop()`, the `_shutting_down` gate, the waitress server thread,
  `main.py` `_shutdown` order, requests in progress.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across sources (conflicting stop orders); Flow and state
  (signals lost at stop); Failure handling (no safe stop).
- **Intended behaviour and source (sources conflict; all HISTORICAL):** (1) WR12, module docstring L24 (17-Apr-2026):
  "stop() for graceful shutdown"; `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L124 to L126 (17-Apr-2026): "receiver.stop() flushes
  pending writes, closes Flask app cleanly. Used by main.py shutdown handler." (2) MAIN15, `module_34_main.txt` L299 to
  L306 (17-Apr-2026): "Clean shutdown order (reverse of start)": `signal_processor.stop()` second, then the entry gate,
  then "webhook_receiver app.shutdown() if possible (Flask dev server doesn't support clean stop — acceptable for v2;
  daemon thread dies on process exit)"; `main.py` L1627 (`bcf03b5`): `_shutdown` docstring "Reverse-order shutdown
  (MAIN15)"; L1751 (`bcf03b5`): "Flask has no clean stop (daemon thread dies on process exit)". (3) H-16 (`0d80fe9`,
  19-Apr-2026): `main.py` L1681 to L1683 "stop webhook_receiver FIRST so new webhooks return 503. In-flight requests drain
  naturally; signal_processor stop below still works on queued items"; receiver L210 to L212 and L483 to L484 ("In-flight
  requests continue to completion; only newly arriving ones get 503"); `stop()` docstring L1167 to L1170. (4) `main.py`
  L1719 (`5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026): the PB-01 worker is stopped "After webhook_receiver.stop() above, so no new EOD alert
  can be captured". Source (2) accepts that the server is not stopped, against (1); source (3) reverses (2)'s order.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1166 to L1176 (`stop()`), L485 to L490 (gate); `main.py` L1629
  to L1692 (`_shutdown`: census, then `webhook_receiver.stop()` and `signal_proc.stop()`), L1718 to L1729, L1751, store
  checkpoint and close later (P01-S083); L3987 to L4000 (server thread, never referenced again).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `stop()` sets `_shutting_down` and `_sweeper_stop` and logs one line;
  it flushes nothing, closes nothing, does not stop or signal the server, joins nothing and releases no claim (P01-S080).
  `_shutdown` stops the receiver and then at once the processor, each in its own `try` (P01-S081); nothing holds or stops
  the waitress server (P01-S069). MATCH part: after `stop()`, new `/webhook` requests (EOD included) are answered 503 with
  an audit row (L485 to L490), as H-16 intends; the receiver is stopped before the processor and before the PB-01 worker,
  as (3) and (4) intend. INTERPRETATION: requests already past L485 continue on waitress worker threads while the
  processor stops and the store closes; nothing waits for them; such a request may insert a `signals` row and put an item
  on the queue after the processor's drain has finished, so the item is lost with the process and the row stays `QUEUED`
  (P01-S084; P04-S019, P04-S020); after the store closes, a late audit write may fail (logged at ERROR); `/health` keeps
  answering 200 (P01-S058, P01-S083). Current external context (waitress 3.0.2 tagged source `task.py`, read 18-Nov-2026
  and re-read in the check: `ThreadedTaskDispatcher.start_new_thread`, L57 to L62 of that file, creates each worker with
  `t.daemon = True`, and `create_server` uses that dispatcher by default, `server.py` L54 to L57; no `atexit` hook in the
  files read; Python `threading`, as cited by P01-S069: "Daemon threads are
  abruptly stopped at shutdown") and INTERPRETATION: at the pinned version a request still running at process exit is
  cut off, not completed; the installed waitress version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P01-S058, P01-S069, P01-S080 to P01-S084, P01-S097, P01-S099, P01-S103; P04-S017 to P04-S020; P10-S103;
  code lines above; waitress `task.py` as above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (with a MATCH part). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context.
  (i) WR12's long form (flush, close cleanly) is not implemented; MAIN15 and L1751 accept the missing server stop, so the
  sources conflict and the code follows MAIN15 on this point. (ii) "In-flight requests drain naturally" / "continue to
  completion" is not ensured: nothing waits, and at the pinned waitress a request running at exit is stopped abruptly.
  (iii) The stop order follows H-16 against MAIN15 and the `_shutdown` docstring (the receiver side of A01 C20, whose ID,
  classification "ISSUE; FACT" and treatment Group E stay A01's). (iv) Whether `/health` was meant to keep answering 200
  after `stop()` is not established (no source speaks of it); recorded, not counted as an issue part. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered for (ii) and not given: (a) fails: no draining mechanism exists, only a comment.
- **Severity or impact where established:** a signal accepted in the last moments before exit can be lost with a row
  left `QUEUED` (INTERPRETATION); whether any request or signal was in progress at a real shutdown is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED; the store's behaviour after `close()` is A03's and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (18.2 item 15, P01-S116).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver `stop()`; processor drain, part PQ); A01 (`_shutdown` order; C20, C18); A18 (PB-01 worker
  stop); A03 (store after close).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 3; STUDY question 3; A01 C20 and C18 (receiver side; C18's processor side is part
  PQ's); 18.2 item 15 (store after close); WI-23 (A01 W-24).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 3 (whether the server was meant to stop, whether requests in progress
  were meant to finish, whether `/health` was meant to keep answering) bears on treatment and on part (iv).

##### WI-04 — End-of-day alerts skip the kill switch and the excluded-symbol list as well as the entry window; only the window skip has a stated reason

- **Area and flow:** the EOD branch of `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>` (Rama's decision D: routing and payload only)
  against the intraday gates.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a route that skips a check or guard that another route applies).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L556 to L561 (`5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026): an "eod"
  scanner "SKIPS the intraday entry-window gate (a post-close alert is legitimate) and is NEVER enqueued to the intraday
  signal_queue — so it is structurally INCAPABLE of the intraday order path". (2) L191 to L194 (same commit): "An "eod"
  scanner routes here ONLY (never the intraday signal_queue)". (3) `main.py` L3750 to L3751 (same commit): the capture
  is "SHADOW / ANALYSIS-ONLY, NO order path". (4) WR3 and WR5 (module docstring L13 to L16; `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt`
  L29 to L30, L54; 17-Apr-2026): the kill switch is passed "to reject when active", 403 when it is active; written for the
  intraday route before the EOD branch existed. (5) `config/system_config.yaml` L130 (FIX-C): `excluded_symbols` are
  "rejected at webhook edge". No source says whether an EOD alert should be refused while the kill switch is active or
  should honour the exclusion list. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L547 to L564 (auth, scanner lookup, EOD branch), L567 to L583
  (kill switch, backpressure, window: intraday only), L708 to L717 (exclusion list: intraday loop only), L751 to L804
  (`_handle_eod`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: after authentication and the scanner lookup, an `eod` entry goes to
  `_handle_eod`, which applies none of the kill switch, backpressure, entry window, exclusion list, expiry, claims,
  deduplication, `signals` INSERT or queue (P01-S036, P01-S052). The shutdown gate and the per-IP limiter still apply
  (P01-S057). The repository map has one `eod` entry, `pb01_breakout_retest` (P01-S035). MATCH part: the window skip and
  the absence of any queue use agree with (1) to (3); backpressure, which measures the intraday queue, has no object on
  this route.
- **Evidence:** P01-S035 to P01-S037, P01-S052, P01-S057; P02-S060; P10-S166; P11-S046; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Basis: where no
  source states the intent, the code shows a route that skips two guards the other route applies (Document 04, section
  5.3, flow and state). "Minor" because, at the baseline, the EOD route reaches no order path (sources (1) and (3); the
  capture and the next-day stage are A18's): the effect is that an EOD symbol on the exclusion list, or an EOD alert
  during a kill, is still submitted for capture.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above); what the capture worker does with such a symbol is
  A18's.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver routing); A18 (what the capture does); A13 (the kill switch's intended scope).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 4 (first half; the order of checks is WI-26); STUDY question 4; WI-29 (the EOD
  routing contract, MATCH).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 4 asks whether this was intended; not needed to classify.

##### WI-05 — The server runs a fixed pool of eight waitress threads with a connection limit of 100, both literals in `main.py`; WR11 still describes one thread per request

- **Area and flow:** the HTTP server that serves the receiver: thread model and capacity settings.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Weakness
  (hardcoded values).
- **Intended behaviour and source (later sources replace earlier; all HISTORICAL):** (1) WR11, module docstring L23
  (17-Apr-2026): "Thread-safe; each Flask request in its own thread". (2) `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L115 to L122
  (17-Apr-2026): "Flask runs on its built-in werkzeug server (development) OR gunicorn (production) — receiver code is
  server-agnostic. Each request handled in own thread ... must return in <100ms typical"; MAIN11, `module_34_main.txt`
  L244 to L251: `app.run` with `"threaded": True`. (3) `main.py` L3983 to L3986 (`13b3d09`, 25-Apr-2026): waitress
  replaces Flask's development server because "Werkzeug's app.run drops connections under burst load (Chartink can fan
  out 50+ signals in a sub-second window)"; `requirements.txt` L12 (`95f2f47`, 26-Apr-2026): waitress "replaces app.run;
  supersedes gunicorn ask". (4) WR14 (`module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L142 to L145): the `webhook` section holds
  `bind_host`, `bind_port`, `require_hmac`; no source names a thread count or connection limit, or asks for them to be
  configurable.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3987 to L4000 (`serve(..., threads=8, connection_limit=100)` in the daemon thread
  `webhook-server`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `threads=8` and `connection_limit=100` are literals; no other waitress
  argument is passed (P01-S064). Current external context (waitress 3.0.2 documentation, read 17-Nov-2026, as cited by
  P01-S067 and P01-S068) and INTERPRETATION: receiver code runs on a fixed pool of eight worker threads; at most eight
  requests run receiver code at once; further complete requests wait in waitress's queue; 100 equals waitress's default
  connection limit. MATCH part: the server is waitress, as source (3) intends.
- **Evidence:** P01-S064, P01-S067, P01-S068, P01-S086; P08-S032; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external
  context. The module docstring's WR11 ("its own thread") no longer describes the server used (documentation against
  code), and the two capacity settings are hardcoded where the server's other settings are configuration (Document 04,
  section 5.3, weakness). Whether eight threads absorb the stated "50+ signals in a sub-second window" without delay
  depends on request durations (every answer waits for its audit write, P03-S051) and is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (documentation and settings); throughput effects not established.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver docstring); A01 (`main.py` server call); A02 (whether such settings belong in configuration).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 5; STUDY question 5; part WD (request concurrency and the audit write's wait).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 5 (fixed in code or configurable) bears on treatment only.

##### WI-06 — The 500 handler's CRITICAL line carries Flask's generic text, not the failing exception; the handler also depends on Flask debug mode staying off

- **Area and flow:** exception handling on the receiver's app: the registered 500 handler.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only logged, and logged without their cause).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) WR5, `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L57 (17-Apr-2026): "500 only for
  unhandled exceptions (logged at CRITICAL)". (2) The handler's own log text, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L412
  (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026): "Unhandled exception in webhook handler: {exc}". (3) L250 (`bcf03b5`): `TESTING = False`.
  All HISTORICAL. No source speaks of Flask debug mode.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L249 to L252, L410 to L413; the inner handler L525 to L528 (which
  logs the original exception text for `_process_request`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the handler logs CRITICAL with `{exc}` and answers JSON 500.
  Current external context (Flask 3.1.x "Handling Application Errors" and "Configuration Handling", read 17-Nov-2026, as
  cited by P01-S060 and P02-S073) and INTERPRETATION: a registered 500 handler is passed an `InternalServerError`, not the
  original exception, so the CRITICAL line carries the generic 500 text and no cause; on `/webhook` the handler covers
  only L478 to L504 (everything after L510 is caught by the inner `except`, which logs the original message), on
  `/health` it covers the whole view (for example the comparison `TypeError` of P02-S018). In debug mode the 500 handler
  is not used; `DEBUG` is set from the `FLASK_DEBUG` environment variable; no non-test, non-document file in the
  repository sets `FLASK_DEBUG` or `app.debug` (search above); the environment is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P01-S021, P01-S060; P02-S018, P02-S069 row 8, P02-S073; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. The 500 is
  answered and logged at CRITICAL as (1) intends, but the logged line cannot say which exception occurred, which (2)
  evidently meant it to (Document 04, section 5.3, failure handling). "Minor": the request path (`_process_request`)
  logs its own exception text; the gap concerns `/health` and the first lines of `_handle_webhook`.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 6; part WA (the `/health` `TypeError`, 18.1 point 15); WI-22 (the environment).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-07 — After a partial PB-01 wiring failure the receiver keeps the capture worker, against the code's own "continuing without it" and "fail-safe miss" texts

- **Area and flow:** late binding of the EOD capture worker (`set_eod_capture`) and `main.py`'s recovery from a failure in
  the PB-01 wiring block (Rama's decision D: the receiver's routing; the worker is A18's).
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (state not reset after a failure); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) `main.py` L3752 to L3753 (`5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026): "never let the watchlist
  break startup"; "pb01 watchlist wiring failed (continuing without it)", then the locals are set to `None`. (2) Receiver
  L191 to L194 and `set_eod_capture` docstring L1157 to L1159 (`5a71fb6`): with no worker, "an EOD alert is a fail-SAFE
  miss (logged, never silent)". (3) Receiver L782 to L785 (`cf8d723`, 25-Jul-2026), C3: "A boot-wiring failure leaves
  _eod_capture None, the 17:00 alert still answers 200 ... FAILED + func=DISABLED makes the two distinguishable". (4)
  `main.py` L3747 (`5a71fb6`): "EOD alerts now captured". All HISTORICAL. Source (3) assumes that a wiring failure leaves
  the receiver without a worker.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3734 to L3755 (worker built, `set_eod_capture` at L3747, `start()` at L3748 and
  L3749, `except` at L3752 to L3755); `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1156 to L1160, L776 to L804.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `set_eod_capture` only assigns the reference, without lock or check;
  the `except` sets `main.py`'s locals `pb01_capture_worker` and `pb01_entry_stage` to `None` but does not call
  `set_eod_capture(None)` (P01-S024). INTERPRETATION: if L3748 or L3749 raised after L3747, the receiver keeps a worker
  that `main.py` no longer tracks and that `_shutdown` is not asked to stop; every later EOD alert is then submitted to
  it and answered 200 with a `SUCCESS` heartbeat (L801 to L804; functional status `EMPTY_NO_DATA` when no symbol was
  queued), not the `FAILED` / `DISABLED` heartbeat that source (3) relies on to show a wiring failure. A failure before L3747 leaves `_eod_capture` `None`, which matches (2) and (3).
  What `submit` does on a worker whose `start()` failed is A18's.
- **Evidence:** P01-S023, P01-S024, P01-S053 to P01-S055, P01-S104; P09-S022; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The A06 side of A01 C37 ("A failing optional
  feature can leave half its wiring in place"; A01's classification "ISSUE; FACT, INTERPRETATION", treatment "Group B
  (AGREED, 06-Nov-2026)", status "AGREED", unchanged). The receiver's state contradicts sources (1) and (3) in the case
  where the worker's or the entry stage's `start()` raises. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) fails: no
  mechanism resets the receiver's reference.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established; whether the wiring ever failed is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A01 (`main.py` wiring; C37); A06 (the receiver's reference and heartbeat); A18 (the worker).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 7; A01 C37; WI-29 (the EOD routing contract).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-08 — Detecting a failed webhook server: the boot self-check works as stated, but a bind failure can be masked and nothing watches the server after boot; the runbook's first check calls `/health` anonymously

- **Area and flow:** the server's start and its supervision: the waitress thread, the post-start self-check, the
  liveness map of the 8080 health server, the operations runbook.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a failure that nothing detects after boot); Intended against actual
  (documentation that the code no longer supports).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) P17, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L372 to L373 (17-Apr-2026):
  "(c) own webhook endpoint reachable via local /health curl ... CRITICAL on any failure"; it catches "Flask not bound".
  (2) MAIN11, `module_34_main.txt` L254 to L259: after `sleep(2)`, `check_webhook_endpoint`; "if not result.reachable:
  log CRITICAL, exit 1". (3) `main.py` L4007 to L4033 (`14305e1`, 27-Jul-2026): "DEGRADE, do not block the boot", and
  "DEGRADING IS ONLY SAFE IF SOMETHING ALARMS, and liveness_probe is NOT that something ... So the alarm has to be raised
  here"; the sentinel text (L4047 to L4048): "Investigate the webhook server; a restart is the fix once the cause is
  known". (4) `utils/startup_checks.py` L795 to L806 (`e393b3f`, 17-Jul-2026): a 401 "is an EXPECTED answer here, and it
  proves the one thing this check exists to prove: Flask is listening". (5) `main.py` L3980 to L3981 (`39110d8`,
  16-May-2026): FIX-081 removed the port-conflict check because the instance lock "already binds a persistent socket
  lock" (A01 B-32 found that the lock binds another port). (6) `docs/03_daily_operations_runbook.md` L128 to L129
  (`37e6698`, 03-Jun-2026): "No Signals Coming In ... 1. Check webhook health: `curl http://localhost:5000/health`";
  L126 (same commit): "Port 5000 in use", checked by hand with `lsof` (an operator step for a port conflict, added in
  the check). Source (3) replaces (2)'s exit; (4) post-dates (6).
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3987 to L4000 (server thread), L4002 to L4054 (self-check and sentinel), L4056 to
  L4071 (E-4 liveness map, `dd4f3f4`, 08-Jul-2026); `utils/startup_checks.py` L797 to L809 (A01's); receiver L364 to
  L404 (`/health`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH parts: FACT: two seconds after the thread starts, the boot probes the
  loopback `/health` with no secret and counts a 2xx or a 401 as reachable; on failure it logs CRITICAL, writes the
  critical sentinel and keeps running (P01-S059, P01-S071; P02-S026), as (3) and (4) state (A01 C28's MATCH part).
  FACT (`main.py` L4050; `utils/startup_checks.py` L138 to L142, L815 to L826): the sentinel's context reads
  `getattr(wh_result, "detail", None)`, and the result type has only `reachable`, `status_code` and `response_body`, so
  `detail` is always `None` and the status code is not passed on (A01 C28's ISSUE part, A01's; the sentinel's contents
  are A14's; recorded at the interface, not an A06 issue part).
  Bind failure: FACT (`main.py` L3988 to L3999): the thread's target is waitress's `serve` itself, with no wrapper.
  Current external context (waitress 3.0.2 tagged source, read 18-Nov-2026 and re-read in the check: `serve()`,
  `__init__.py` L6 to L19, calls `create_server` and then `run()` with no `try`/`except`; `create_server`, `server.py`
  L31 to L136, builds a `TcpWSGIServer` per listen address with no `try`/`except`; `BaseWSGIServer.__init__` sets
  `SO_REUSEADDR` (L240) and calls `bind_server_socket()` (L243), which calls `self.bind(sockaddr)` (L362 to L364), and
  `wasyncore`'s `bind` returns `self.socket.bind(addr)` (`wasyncore.py` L372 to L374); Python `socket` documentation,
  page for 3.15.2, read 18-Nov-2026: "Errors related to socket or address semantics raise OSError or one of its
  subclasses") with Python `threading` (as cited by P01-S070) and INTERPRETATION: a failed bind raises `OSError` (or a
  subclass) out of `serve()`, ends the `webhook-server` thread with a traceback on stderr, and the self-check two
  seconds later reports unreachable, unless another listener on the same port answers 2xx or 401 (A01 B-32's
  INTERPRETATION, as A01 recorded it). Whether another process listening on the port makes waitress's bind fail,
  given the `SO_REUSEADDR` option it sets first, is operating-system behaviour that this part did not read in an
  authoritative source (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED here). The repository holds one stand-in that would answer that way
  if it were run on the same host, A19's `scripts/test_webhook.py` (P01-S092: a separate Flask app answering
  `GET /health` with 200 on port 5000 on all interfaces). Under Rama's decision E it is evidence of stated expectation
  only: A06 does not analyse it, records no finding on it and hands the point to A19 (section 5); whether it, or any
  other listener, ever held the port on a legacy host is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. After boot: FACT: nothing refers to the server thread after
  L4000; the E-4 liveness map (L4066 to L4071) lists the order monitor, reconciler, EOD scheduler and live feed, not the
  webhook server; no `excepthook` is set (P01-S069, P01-S070). INTERPRETATION: a server thread that ended after boot
  would not be detected by the trading process. The runbook's anonymous `curl` of `/health` is answered 401 since
  17-Jul-2026 (L387 to L393; P10-S145).
- **Evidence:** P01-S059, P01-S062, P01-S069 to P01-S071, P01-S088, P01-S092, P01-S100, P01-S101; P02-S026; P07-S045;

##### WI-09 — The documented network path (loopback bind behind a TLS proxy) conflicts with the configured all-interfaces bind and with the receiver's address handling; which path production had is unknown

- **Area and flow:** exposure of the webhook port: bind address, firewall, proxy or tunnel; the source address that the
  per-IP limiter and the audit row use.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across sources (competing descriptions of the same path); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source (sources conflict; all HISTORICAL):** (1) `DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8, L300 to L310,
  L331 to L341, L379 to L387, L399 to L403 (`c3ce214`, 16-May-2026): "Do not expose this port directly to the internet";
  the process "binds to" the loopback address on port 5000 "(localhost only)"; a reverse proxy terminates TLS on 443,
  forwards `X-Real-IP` and `X-Forwarded-For` and can rate-limit; the firewall denies 5000; "Current state (as of F.1)":
  port 5000 open "for direct testing", "must be locked down before production use". The section is headed "Nginx
  Reverse Proxy Recommendation (DOCUMENT-001)" (L298), and its own step 5 (L386 to L387) and current-state note record
  that Chartink then posted to port 5000 directly (added in the check). (2) WR14,
  `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L143 (17-Apr-2026), and the schema comment `core/config_loader.py` L619 (`bcf03b5`,
  17-Apr-2026): `bind_host` "default" the loopback address. (3) `config/system_config.yaml` L327 to L334 (`2d1e580`, 27-Apr-2026): all interfaces
  "so the VM can accept Chartink webhooks routed via ngrok/external"; loopback "is the safer default; the
  all-interfaces bind here relies on require_hmac=true plus firewall/security-group restrictions". (4)
  `deploy/install_vm_services.sh` L33 to L41 (`0a2c163`, 27-Apr-2026): an iptables rule accepting TCP 5000 so the
  listener "is reachable on the external interface". (5) The C-2 limiter (`d922007`, 02-Jul-2026): receiver L70 to L76,
  `config/system_config.yaml` L338 to L339, `core/config_loader.py` L623 to L625: "Keyed on request.remote_addr",
  "Chartink's legitimate open-bell burst (~40 signals from one IP)", "a spoofed-IP flood". (6) Receiver L366 to L370
  (`84cee3e`, 17-Jul-2026): the endpoint "sits on" the all-interfaces address and port 5000 "(open for Chartink)" and was
  reachable "from the internet". (7) `docs/03_daily_operations_runbook.md` L131 (`37e6698`, 03-Jun-2026): "Check
  ngrok/tunnel is active". Added in the check (each re-read at the baseline; first found in the intended-behaviour
  sources file, which is under its own check): (8) receiver L196 to L199 (`95f2f47`, 26-Apr-2026), G.1: a "token in
  URL is logged by nginx", which presumes a proxy in front. (9) Commit message `d922007` (02-Jul-2026), C-2: the webhook
  binds all interfaces "with require_hmac:false; the sole live auth is ?token="; "Network-layer control (IP allowlist /
  reverse proxy) is the Phase-3 decision, proposed separately". (10) `PATHS.md` L470 (`3d34357`, 02-Jul-2026): the C-2
  network item (all-interfaces bind, `require_hmac: false`) listed as "PARTIAL HIGH (after P1, Rama's call)".
  (11) `docs/audit/c2_chartink_ip_investigation_03jul2026.md` L3 to L6 and L13 (dated 03-Jul-2026 in its text; lines
  `87c6afa`, 06-Jul-2026): facts for "Rama's later C-2 (Option A: IP allowlist vs Option B: reverse proxy + HMAC)
  decision session", "No verdict"; it states that no reverse proxy sits in front of the receiver and that
  `request.remote_addr` is the raw TCP peer (a statement about the host: HISTORICAL; what the host had is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED). (12) `docs/audit/audit_b_phase9_ops_phase10_security_16jul2026.md` section 1.7, L166 to L180
  (`57f28a1`, 17-Jul-2026): `/health` bound to all interfaces on port 5000, "An unauthenticated internet endpoint"
  (cited for this functional finding only). (13) `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L392 to L394 (`05595a7`,
  01-Aug-2026), OQ-P1-2: whether anything fronts port 5000 was left "UNCERTAIN", and it "decides whether the per-IP
  limiter buckets real IPs or one proxy IP". The passages that attribute a pending decision to Rama ((9) to (11)) are
  their authors' words, not recorded statements of Rama; his own answer on A02-13 is OPEN. Sources (1), (2) and (8)
  describe or presume a loopback bind behind a proxy; (3) to (6), (9), (11) and (12) describe a direct, all-interfaces
  listener, (9) to (11) with the network control still undecided; (3) and (7) also name a tunnel; (13) leaves the
  question open. (14) `docs/gui_project/G0_BACKEND_INVESTIGATION_REPORT.md` L21 and L39 (`2f0d4b5`, 03-Jul-2026)
  names (1): "an intended-but-unimplemented reverse-proxy setup; the config is authoritative", and section 8 "a
  recommendation" (its author's judgement, HISTORICAL); `docs/chatgpt_project_clarification.md` L73 to L74 (`5a71fb6`,
  13-Jul-2026) repeats that the network edge is "recommended" to be handled by a proxy (`DEPLOYMENT.md` section 8). (1)
  is later than (3) and (4) but names neither; (14) is later than (1) and names it.
- **Actual code path:** `config/system_config.yaml` L328, L335 (A02's file); `main.py` L3979 to L4000 (no
  `trusted_proxy`, no TLS argument); `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L376, L479 (`request.remote_addr`), L379, L495
  (limiter key), L487 to L534 (`source_ip` in the audit row).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the repository file binds all interfaces, port 5000 (effective values
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, P01-S065); the receiver reads no forwarded header and adds no proxy middleware; waitress is
  started with no trusted proxy (P01-S075; P02-S029, P02-S030; P10-S188). Current external context (waitress 3.0.2 tagged
  source and Werkzeug source, read 17-Nov-2026, as cited by P03-S025) and INTERPRETATION: `remote_addr` is the address of
  the connection's peer as accepted by waitress. INTERPRETATION: under the layout of source (1), every request would
  reach waitress from the proxy, so all senders would share one limiter bucket and every audit row would carry the
  proxy's address; under a direct listener, as (3) to (6) describe, the bucket and `source_ip` are the sender's.

  X-01 (batch T5, group G4, 09-Nov-2026)"), both unchanged here.
- **Evidence:** P01-S065, P01-S066, P01-S074, P01-S075, P01-S114; P02-S029, P02-S030, P02-S040 to P02-S042; P03-S025;
  P08-S010, P08-S031; P09-S048, P09-S049, P09-S075; P10-S101, P10-S172, P10-S188 to P10-S190, P10-S192, P10-S193; code
  lines and the documents above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. HISTORICAL; FACT; INTERPRETATION; current

  network path production actually had (firewall, security group, tunnel, proxy, TLS) and so which address the limiter
  and the audit saw (STUDY 18.2 item 6). No source is chosen. Nothing here touches the A02-13 history or the
  security of the token (D-07).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established.
- **Owner:** A06 (the receiver's address handling); A02 (the bind keys and comment; X-01); A17 (the install script and
