##### WI-10 — The alias file is read once from a path relative to the working directory, outside `--config`; an alias miss keeps the received letter case

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's alias consumer (A06 owns the consumer; A05 the alias tool; A02 the file): loading
  at construction and lookup per symbol.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a file read with different results depending on where the process
  starts); Consistency (one symbol normalised in some checks and not in others).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) Receiver L222 to L226 (`5dc8563`, 14-May-2026), FIX-032: "Load
  symbol aliases once at startup ... resolve at webhook edge before any DB write or in-flight check uses the wrong symbol
  name"; L697 to L700: "to prevent ghost locks and instrument cache misses". (2) The loader docstring L260 to L263
  (same commit): "Empty dict if file doesn't exist or is empty (fail-open: no aliases = passthrough)". (3)
  `core/config_loader.py` `load_all(config_dir)` takes a configuration directory; A02 F-11 records that some readers
  ignore `--config` (P09-S039). No source says whether Chartink sends symbols in lower case.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L227, L258 to L292 (load), L701 to L706 and L792 (lookup), L709 to
  L710 (exclusion check, case-insensitive).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the map is read once, at construction, from the relative path
  `config/symbol_aliases.yaml`; a missing file logs WARNING and an error logs ERROR, each giving an empty map; keys and
  values are upper-cased; the map is never reloaded (P01-S018); no alias value is checked against the instrument list
  (L277 to L281 convert and keep every pair with true values; 18.1 point 10). The lookup upper-cases the received symbol for the lookup
  only; on a miss the symbol is kept exactly as received (P01-S039), while the exclusion check compares case-insensitively
  (P01-S040). INTERPRETATION: the path resolves against the process working directory, not the `--config` directory; the
  declared unit sets the working directory to the project directory (P08-S005; a declaration, not proof); the working
  directory of production runs is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (STUDY 18.2 item 7). A lower-case symbol absent from the map
  continues in lower case to the claim, the dedup key, the `signals` row and the queue item. MATCH parts: loaded once at
  start-up, and an absent file means passthrough, as (1) and (2) state.
- **Evidence:** P01-S018, P01-S039, P01-S040; P04-S045 to P04-S049; P08-S005, P08-S047, P08-S048; P09-S039, P09-S067 to
  P09-S070; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. (i) The A06 side of
  A02 F-11 (A02's "ISSUE; FACT; INTERPRETATION", treatment "Batch T4 group G2 (AGREED, 09-Nov-2026)", unchanged): the
  receiver opens the one file it reads itself outside the configuration directory. (ii) The receiver normalises letter
  case for the alias lookup and the exclusion check but not for the symbol it then stores and queues (Document 04,
  section 5.3, consistency); its effect depends on whether lower-case symbols ever arrived, which is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED (STUDY 18.2 item 9). A05 IR-10, IR-13 and IR-30 keep their records; the processor side of normalisation is
  part PQ's.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06 (the consumer); A02 (F-11, the file); A05 (the alias tool); A17 (the unit's working directory).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 10; 18.2 item 7; STUDY question 27; A02 F-11; A05 IR-10, IR-13, IR-30, XC-04; part PQ.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 27 bears on treatment.

##### WI-11 — The in-flight sweeper has no exception handling and nothing supervises or restarts it

- **Area and flow:** the `in_flight_sweeper` daemon thread started by the receiver's constructor.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no safe recovery; a failure only visible on stderr).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) Receiver L215 (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026): "HIGH #9: background
  sweeper evicts stuck in_flight entries". (2) Sweeper docstring L1183 to L1187 (`a826dfe`, 12-May-2026): "Background
  daemon that evicts in_flight entries with no heartbeat for >60s. Runs every 60s." (3) WR17
  (`module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L164 to L170): the claim prevents same-symbol concurrent processing, so a claim that
  is never released blocks its symbol. No source speaks of the sweeper's own failure.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L216 to L220 (thread created and started once), L1182 to L1207
  (loop body with no `try`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the loop body has no exception handling; the thread is started once in
  the constructor and never restarted; no liveness entry or `excepthook` covers it (P01-S017, P01-S069, P01-S070,
  P01-S076). INTERPRETATION (Python `threading`, as cited by P01-S070 and P01-S076): an exception inside a pass would end
  the thread with a traceback on stderr, after which claims would be released only by the receiver's reject paths and
  the processor's release calls; a claim left behind by the paths of P03-S034 (the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT; ISSUE in WD-01) or P03-S070
  would then refuse its symbol with `IN_PROCESS` until restart. FACT (L1189 to L1207): the pass reads entries that every
  claim creates with both keys and calls the logger; no raising path in it is shown at the baseline.
- **Evidence:** P01-S017, P01-S069, P01-S070, P01-S076; P03-S034, P03-S070; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Basis: the stated purpose (1)
  and (2) depends on one unsupervised thread, which is also the only backstop for claims that are never released (WR17);
  if it ended, nothing would notice (Document 04, section 5.3, failure handling). "Latent": no raising path in the pass is
  shown at the baseline.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent (basis above); whether the thread ever ended is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 11; WI-01; part WD (claims kept after an exception, P03-S034, P03-S070).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-12 — The scan-name check: a non-text `scan_name` is answered 500, not 400; the space-to-underscore rule is as the comment states; WR4b depends on AR12's cached first result

- **Area and flow:** the optional body field `scan_name` checked against the path segment (WR4), with the account-tag
  prefix strip (WR4b, commit `3b15bbf`).
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a payload fault answered as an unhandled exception); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) WR4, `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L45 to L47
  (17-Apr-2026): "scan_name in body must match scanner_name path param (defense in depth)". (2) WR5, L52 and L57: 400 for
  "malformed JSON / missing fields / bad date"; "500 only for unhandled exceptions". (3) Receiver L617 to L619 (commit
  `114d466`, 28-Apr-2026, P02-S046): "Chartink sends "GAP FADE LONG" but URL uses "gap_fade_long", so we normalize both
  to lowercase with underscores before comparing"; L608: "scan_name optional". (4) WR4b docstring L112 to L128 and
  L130 to L131 (`3b15bbf`, 09-Sep-2026; identifier-bearing, not reproduced): strip one leading account-tag prefix, the tag
  read through AR12, "PREFIX ONLY", a no-op on production; "unknown" is AR12's fallback and stripping it "would be a
  silent widening of the check".
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L620 to L633 (check), L111 to L138 (`_strip_account_prefix`),
  L525 to L528 (500 path).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: when `scan_name` is present and not `null` it is lower-cased, spaces
  become underscores, one leading `<tag>-` or `<tag>_` is removed when AR12 gives a tag other than empty or "unknown",
  and the result must equal the path segment exactly (P01-S047, P01-S049). INTERPRETATION: a value that is not a string
  has no `.lower()`, raises `AttributeError` and is answered 500 by L525 to L528 (P01-S048; P02-S046); a trailing or
  doubled space yields an extra underscore and a 400. FACT (A01 M-37 and M-43, as A01 recorded them, P01-S051): AR12
  caches its first result, including its fallback, for the process's life. INTERPRETATION: if the first AR12 call in a
  process returned the fallback, WR4b is a no-op for that process. MATCH parts: optional field and exact comparison after
  lower-casing and the space rule, as (1) and (3) state; "unknown" treated as no tag, as (4) states.
- **Evidence:** P01-S047 to P01-S051, P01-S105; P02-S046; P09-S031, P09-S032; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The non-text case
  contradicts (2): a payload fault is answered as an unhandled exception (and logged at CRITICAL). The A01 items keep
  their records: M-37 ("ISSUE (with a MATCH part); FACT, INTERPRETATION", "Group H (AGREED, 06-Nov-2026, with its
  exceptions)") and M-43 ("MATCH (no cycles)"); their A06 side is confirmed and adds no further A06 finding. Whether WR4b
  was in force in production is WI-24.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor; which `scan_name` texts Chartink sent is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED
  (STUDY 18.2 item 9).
- **Owner:** A06; A01 (AR12, M-37, M-43).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 18; STUDY question 7; A01 M-37, M-43; part WA (validation answers); WI-24.
- **Rama:** STUDY question 7 (which `scan_name` forms were meant to be accepted) bears on treatment only.

##### WI-13 — The FIX-074 numeric cast converts six body keys that nothing downstream ever receives; its only effects are a 400 for two of them, WARNING lines for the others and a 500 on overflow

- **Area and flow:** the parse of an intraday body: `_cast_numeric_fields` before the required-field checks.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (stated purpose not served); Weakness (code that runs although its result is
  unused); Failure handling (an overflow answered 500).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** the method docstring `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L421 to L431
  (commit `fc11ad8`, 16-May-2026, P02-S051): "Chartink sends numeric values as strings. Cast them before queueing to
  prevent downstream TypeError in PositionSizer or other components"; "Critical fields: price, entry_price (must be
  castable or reject)"; L458: `qty` "currently none in Chartink format, but prepare for future".
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L419 to L471, L598 to L606.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the cast acts on `price`, `entry_price` (failure: 400),
  `trigger_price`, `sl_pct`, `target_pct` (failure: `None` with a WARNING) and `qty` (`int()`; failure: `None`); it
  catches only `ValueError` and `TypeError`; none of the six keys is read again, and the queue item and the `signals` row
  take the price from `trigger_prices` (P01-S038, P01-S042; P02-S051, P02-S052). Current external context (Python 3.13
  built-in functions and the Chartink page, read 17-Nov-2026, as cited by P02-S052) and INTERPRETATION: the Chartink
  sample payload has none of these keys; an `OverflowError` in the cast is not caught and ends the request in 500.
- **Evidence:** P01-S038, P01-S042; P02-S049, P02-S051, P02-S052; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. The stated
  purpose ("before queueing to prevent downstream TypeError") is not served, because the cast values never reach the
  queue or any downstream component; the code runs on every intraday body for no effect on a Chartink-format payload.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 19; part WA (validation answers).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-14 — The body is parsed as JSON whatever its declared type; repeated keys and non-finite literals pass without notice; extra keys are tolerated as the code's own comments expect

- **Area and flow:** the intraday body parse (`json.loads` of the raw bytes) and what is stored from it.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) WR4, `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L37 to L44
  (17-Apr-2026): "Request body: JSON" with `stocks`, `trigger_prices`, `triggered_at`, `scan_name`. (2) Receiver L681 to
  L687 (S-1B.1, 05-Jul-2026, P02-S049): "Chartink echoes the configured URL ... inside the body's `webhook_url` field, and
  the raw body is stored verbatim below"; the stored copy is sanitised; the parsed body used for processing is untouched.
  No source states a Content-Type rule or a rule for repeated keys or non-finite numbers.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L480, L586 to L596, L609 to L674, L688 to L690, L722.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the receiver never reads the Content-Type and passes the raw bytes to
  `json.loads` with no `parse_constant` or `object_pairs_hook` (P01-S119; P02-S048). Current external context (Python
  `json` documentation, read 17-Nov-2026, as cited by P02-S048) and INTERPRETATION: repeated keys keep the last value;
  `NaN` and `Infinity` literals are parsed as numbers. FACT: keys other than the four (and the six cast keys) are
  accepted and ignored; the whole sanitised body is stored with every inserted row (P02-S049). INTERPRETATION: because
  the used fields must be strings (L667 to L668), a non-finite literal is kept only in unused keys and in the stored copy;
  a repeated `stocks` or `trigger_prices` key is processed with its last value, without notice. (Corrected in the check:
  a non-finite literal in a used field is refused rather than ignored: in `stocks` or `trigger_prices` with 400 (L667
  to L668), in `triggered_at` with 400 (its text matches no format, L640 to L662), and in `scan_name` with 500 (WI-12).)
  MATCH parts: extra keys
  tolerated and the sanitised body stored, as (2) expects (Chartink's page also lists `scan_url`, `alert_name` and
  `webhook_url`, P02-S049).
- **Evidence:** P01-S119; P02-S048, P02-S049; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external
  context. Basis: missing validation of repeated keys and non-finite literals (Document 04, section 5.3, weakness); the
  effect is limited to which duplicate value is used and to what is stored. Ignoring the declared Content-Type is
  recorded, not counted: no source states a rule, and the type Chartink declares is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 20; STUDY question 8; part WA (validation).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 8 (fixed key set or not) bears on treatment only.

##### WI-15 — The receiver's own docstrings list response codes, statuses and a start-up call that differ from the code

- **Area and flow:** module and class documentation of the receiver against its behaviour.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement; implemented
  behaviour that nothing describes).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** (1) WR5, module docstring L16 (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026): response codes
  "200/400/401/403/404/503/500". (2) WR10, L21 to L22: per-signal statuses
  "ACCEPTED/DUPLICATE/EXPIRED/INVALID_SYMBOL/INVALID_PRICE/QUEUE_FULL/STORE_ERROR/OUTSIDE_HOURS/IN_PROCESS". (3) The class
  docstring L145 to L149 (`bcf03b5`): `receiver.app.run(host=config.webhook.bind_host, port=config.webhook.bind_port)`.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L380, L504 (429), L715 (`REJECTED_EXCLUDED_SYMBOL`), L581 to L583
  (the window refusal is a request-level 403); `main.py` L3979 to L4000 (waitress with `app_config.system.webhook`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: both routes return 429 from the per-IP limiter (on `/webhook` since
  `d922007`, 02-Jul-2026; on `/health` since `84cee3e`, 17-Jul-2026), which (1) omits (P01-S007); the code returns `REJECTED_EXCLUDED_SYMBOL`, which (2) omits, and never
  produces `OUTSIDE_HOURS` per symbol (search: the name occurs only at L22) (P02-S071); the server is waitress, started by
  `main.py` from `app_config.system.webhook`, not `app.run` with `config.webhook` (P01-S006). Added in the check: WR3
  (L13 to L14) lists the constructor's arguments without `eod_capture` (L161, `5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026). INTERPRETATION, current
  external context (as cited by P02-S071): Flask (404, 405, 413) and waitress (413, 431, 400) also answer outside the
  views.
- **Evidence:** P01-S006, P01-S007; P02-S071; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). HISTORICAL against FACT (documentation against code).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 24; part WA (the full answer table, P02-S069).
- **Rama:** not needed (STUDY section 19 lists this as answerable by the AUDIT).

##### WI-16 — The intraday and end-of-day routes parse the same Chartink body with separate code and different rules; the EOD docstring says the formats are the same

- **Area and flow:** the two body parsers of `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>`: the intraday parse in `_process_request` and
  `_handle_eod` with `_parse_eod_triggered_at`.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (duplicated logic that disagrees); Intended against actual
  (documentation).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** (1) `_handle_eod` docstring L752 to L756 (`5a71fb6`, 13-Jul-2026):
  "Self-contained (does NOT touch the intraday parse, so that path stays byte-identical)". (2) `_parse_eod_triggered_at`
  docstring L837 to L839 (same commit): "Accepts the same formats the intraday path does; for an EOD daily scan the DATE
  is what matters". No source states how empty list elements should be treated.
- **Actual code path:** intraday `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L637 to L674 (formats at L640; split at L670 to L671;
  equal-length check L673); EOD L763 to L774 (formats at L841; split at L774), L836 to L851.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the intraday split keeps empty elements, so a trailing or doubled comma
  gives an `INVALID_SYMBOL` result; the EOD split drops them (P01-S045). The intraday path requires both `stocks` and
  `trigger_prices` to be strings of equal length; the EOD path requires `trigger_prices` to be present but never reads
  or checks it (P01-S052). The intraday path accepts three `triggered_at` formats (L640); the EOD path accepts those and
  also `%Y-%m-%d` (L841). MATCH part: the separate, self-contained EOD parser is what (1) states.
- **Evidence:** P01-S045, P01-S052; P11-S045; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL. The separation is intended, but
  the two parsers apply different rules to the same payload shape (Document 04, section 5.3, consistency), and (2)'s
  "the same formats" is inaccurate (the EOD parser accepts one more).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 26; part XA (the duplication record, STUDY section 14).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-17 — The receiver writes the scanner name into the `signals.strategy` column; nothing later writes the strategy the scanner map assigns

- **Area and flow:** signal-event creation at the receiver: the `signals` INSERT (the processor's lookup and the readers
  at the interface).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (a column whose value and readers disagree); latent.
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** (1) WR16, module docstring L28 and `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt`
  L155 to L158 (17-Apr-2026): "NOT in scope: ... Strategy lookup / scan_webhook_map resolution beyond "is this
  scanner_name known?"". (2) WR9, `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L87 to L92: the row holds "signal_id, scanner_name,
  symbol, trigger_price, triggered_at, received_at, fingerprint, status" (no strategy). No source says what the
  `strategy` column should hold when the receiver writes it.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L963 to L980 (columns `scanner`, `strategy`; both bound to
  `scanner_name`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: both `scanner` and `strategy` receive the path segment; the receiver
  does no strategy lookup (P01-S043). FACT, interface (parts PQ and EV): the processor resolves the strategy from the map
  but never writes it to the row (P04-S031, P04-S034; P05-S003); readers group by `strategy` (P11-S015). FACT
  (`config/scan_webhook_map.yaml`, P01-S043): every repository key equals its `strategy` value. INTERPRETATION: the two
  coincide while that holds; a map entry whose key differed from its strategy would store the scanner name as the
  strategy. MATCH part: no strategy lookup at the receiver, as (1) states.
- **Evidence:** P01-S043; P02-S045; P04-S031, P04-S034; P05-S003; P10-S175; P11-S015; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Basis: the
  stored value contradicts the column's name and its readers' use, and no later step corrects it (Document 04, section
  5.3, consistency); latent because the repository map makes the values equal.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver INSERT; processor write path, part EV); A15 (readers); A02 (the map; A02 T-02 for the unrun
  check).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 52; STUDY question 28; parts EV, PQ, XA; A02 T-02.
- **Rama:** STUDY question 28 bears on treatment only.

##### WI-18 — The audit row's counts do not match the rows stored on several routes, while a report states an identity between them

- **Area and flow:** the counts written to `webhook_audit` (`signals_accepted`, `signals_rejected`) against the
  `signals` rows created by the same request.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two records of the same event disagreeing); Flow and state.
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** (1) WR13, `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L128 to L134
  (17-Apr-2026): the row carries `signals_accepted`, `signals_rejected`; "Used for forensics and rate analysis". (2)
  `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L709 to L718 (A15's file, `01e07b7`, 01-Jul-2026, P11-S050): "Reached storage
  (accepted) = COUNT(signals) == SUM(webhook_audit.signals_accepted)" (L716) and "Dropped pre-storage =
  SUM(signals_rejected) [dupes + invalid/out-of-window/backpressure" (L717). (3) The EOD answer L804 (`5a71fb6`): `accepted` is the number of symbols; the
  heartbeat docstring L812 to L817: "queued=N proves the alert ARRIVED ... it does NOT prove any row reached
  `pb01_watchlist`".
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L506 to L534 (counts read back only for a 200, L514 to L517), L733
  to L745 (batch 503), L788, L804 (EOD answers).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the counts are taken from the answer only when the status is 200
  (P01-S033, P11-S003). INTERPRETATION: a batch 503 (`QUEUE_FULL` or `STORE_ERROR` for any symbol) records 0 and 0
  although rows exist for its accepted and `QUEUE_FULL` symbols; an EOD 200 records its symbol count as accepted with no
  `signals` row; a 500 records 0 and 0 although rows may exist for symbols handled before the exception; a failed audit
  INSERT leaves rows with no audit row; no column links a row to its request (P01-S044, P01-S055; P02-S067; P11-S030,
  P11-S031, P11-S050). Added in the check (18.1 point 74 and P11-S050 name them; L575 to L583, L1012 to L1013 re-read):
  a re-accepted `QUEUE_FULL` retry is counted `ACCEPTED` in its 200 although it creates no new row (it reuses the row
  that its earlier batch 503 recorded as 0), so differences can offset each other; the request-level refusals (the
  kill-switch and window 403s, the backpressure 503, and the 400, 401 and 404 answers) record 0 rejected, so
  out-of-window and backpressure refusals are not in `SUM(signals_rejected)`, which (2) says they are. The report's two
  sides are selected by different date derivations (P11-S050; part XA).
- **Evidence:** P01-S033, P01-S044, P01-S055; P02-S067; P11-S003, P11-S030, P11-S031, P11-S050; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The identity that (2) states does not hold on
  the routes above, its "Dropped pre-storage" figure omits the request-level refusals it names, and the counts
  misdescribe what the request did, against (1)'s forensic purpose.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established (how A15's reports present the difference is A15's; no
  runtime rows survive).
- **Owner:** A06 (the writer); A15 (the report's identity, interface only); A03 (the table).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 74; STUDY question 34; WI-02; part XA (consumers, counts and the date derivations).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 34 bears on treatment only.

##### WI-21 — Planned signal modules and exception classes never came into use; the `SignalError` docstring still names the receiver and a module that never existed

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's error types and the planned `signals/` layout against the code (the A06 side of

  check") and `signals/signal_queue.py` ("300-cap queue with backpressure threshold"), with a boot order that inits the
  queue, the validator, the processor and the receiver. (2) `core/exceptions.py` L350 to L356 (`bcf03b5`): `SignalError`

  `ValueError` at L178; duplicates are handled through `sqlite3.IntegrityError` (P01-S003, P01-S107; P09-S030, P09-S094);
  no commit reachable from any ref ever contained a path with `signal_validator` or `signal_queue` in it (re-run in the check: `git log --all` on both name patterns, no commit); the queue is built in `main.py`;
  identifier, fingerprint and expiry are done in the receiver; `SignalError`, `SignalExpiredError`,
  `DuplicateSignalError` and `DedupViolation` are used by no non-test code (P10-S124 to P10-S126). Added in the check
  (18.1 point 96 names it): `DedupViolation`'s docstring (`core/exceptions.py` L141 to L142) names a unique index on

##### WI-23 — Library behaviour at the server boundary: two points now read in waitress's published source, the rest not established

- **Area and flow:** the waitress and Flask behaviour that the receiver's lifecycle depends on.
- **Issue category:** UNKNOWN (library behaviour without an authoritative statement; installed versions unknown).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not applicable.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3987 to L4000; `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L249 to L252, L364, L406, L480.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** Current external context (waitress 3.0.2 tagged source, read 18-Nov-2026;
  section 1): (a) the worker threads are created with `t.daemon = True` and named `waitress-<n>` (`task.py`,
  `ThreadedTaskDispatcher.start_new_thread`); (b) `serve()` has no `try`/`except`, and an exception raised while binding
  propagates out of `create_server` (`__init__.py`, `server.py`). Added in the check (same source, re-read 18-Nov-2026,
  line numbers in WI-08): `create_server` starts the eight worker threads (`set_thread_count`, `server.py` L57;
  `task.py` L87 to L104) before it builds the listening server, which sets `SO_REUSEADDR` and then binds; Python
  `socket` documentation (page for 3.15.2, read 18-Nov-2026): errors "related to socket or address semantics raise
  OSError or one of its subclasses". INTERPRETATION: at the pinned version, requests running at process exit are
  stopped abruptly (WI-03); a bind failure raises `OSError` (or a subclass) and ends the `webhook-server` thread
  (WI-08), leaving the eight idle daemon worker threads behind. Still
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the installed waitress, Flask and Werkzeug versions (WI-22); whether waitress 3.0.2 sets
  `wsgi.input_terminated` (so how a body without Content-Length is read, P01-S030); the status and headers of Flask's
  automatic OPTIONS answer (P02-S069 row 3); what `get_data()` returns for a form-encoded or multipart body (P01-S119);
  the exact subclass and message of a bind failure; whether another process listening on the port makes the bind
  fail once waitress has set `SO_REUSEADDR` (operating-system behaviour; the Linux manual page could not be reached in
  the check).
- **Evidence:** P01-S030, P01-S070, P01-S088, P01-S102, P01-S112, P01-S118, P01-S119; P02-S069, P02-S074; waitress source
  as above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, with points (a) and (b) settled as current external context
  for the pinned version only. A01 W-24 ("The behaviour of third-party threads (the webhook server's workers, the ticker
  thread)"; "UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED"; "None decided (information only; no decision needed)") keeps its classification:
  its A06 side now has published-source context for waitress 3.0.2 (daemon worker threads), but the installed version
  remains unknown.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not applicable.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver side); A01 (W-24); A17 (installed packages).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.2 item 4 (server and Flask points; the `hexdigest()` case and `int()` of an infinite float
  are part WA's, the `requests` timeout part CK's); A01 W-24; WI-03; WI-08.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WI-25 — The receiver has exactly the two routes WR2 names; the operator procedures of 18-Apr-2026 send kill requests to routes on port 5000 that never existed

- **Area and flow:** the routes served on the webhook port, at the baseline and through the history.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across sources (an operator procedure against the module's design and code);
  Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement).

  17-Apr-2026): "POST /webhook/<scanner_name> + GET /health"; `module_19_webhook_receiver.txt` L20 to L22: "Single
  endpoint: POST /webhook/<scanner_name>. Health endpoint: GET /health ... No other endpoints in v2." (2) The operator
  procedures recorded by A01 as intent source IS-9.14 (RUNBOOK, PLAYBOOK and PLAN sections, commit `37f6ba5`,
  18-Apr-2026, as quoted in P09-S033): "the kill requests go to `/kill-switch` and `/hard-kill` on port 5000", each kill
  followed by a `/health` call to read the kill state.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L360 to L413 (`_register_routes`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the app registers `GET /health`, `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>` and a
  500 handler, nothing else (P01-S025, P01-S026). FACT (this part, `git log -G "route\("` on the receiver): no commit
  after the first, `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026), added or removed a route declaration in the receiver. FACT (this part,
  `git log -G "[\"']/(kill-switch|hard-kill)[\"']" -- '*.py'` over the whole history reachable from `970aabf`): no Python
  file ever contained either quoted route path. Re-run in the check (18-Nov-2026), with the same pattern and two wider
  ones (unquoted path forms with a hyphen or an underscore; any `route`, `add_url_rule`, `.get` or `.post` call whose
  line names "kill") over all 1,537 commits of the clone (the history of every ref equals the baseline's): no route
  declaration for a kill path in any Python file at any commit; the wider matches were dictionary reads, test
  assertions and docstrings; at the baseline the non-Python mentions of the two words are prose, not routes.
  INTERPRETATION: the kill routes of source (2) never existed on the
  receiver, at any date reachable from the baseline; the `/health` answer does carry `kill_switch_active` (L395 to L404),
  so the procedure's read-back step has a matching route, now behind the secret (since 17-Jul-2026). MATCH part: the
  routes are those of (1).
- **Evidence:** P01-S025, P01-S026, P01-S106; P09-S033; code lines above; the history searches above (this part and
  the check).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The code follows
  (1); procedure (2) describes routes that never existed (documentation against code). This completes the A06 side of
  A01 IS-9.14 at every date reachable from the baseline (the gap noted in STUDY section 16, partly covered item (c));
  IS-9.14 stays A01's intent-source entry, with no Audit Findings Register row (P09-S012). How the operator was meant to
  trigger a kill is A13's.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor for A06 (the receiver's routes match their design); the operational
  consequence for kill handling is A13's.
- **Owner:** A06 (the routes); A13 (the kill controls and procedures); A15 (the dashboard's kill controls).
- **Cross-reference:** A01 IS-9.14; STUDY section 16, partly covered (c); 18.1 point 100 (part XA lists the partly
  covered items).
- **Rama:** not needed.

## 6. Part WA — Receiver authentication, validation and answers

**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (`970aabf`)
**Scope:** STUDY section 4 (part P02): the token and HMAC paths, `require_hmac`, `compare_digest`, the secret's
presence check, the A06 side of A02 X-01 and A02-13 (history OPEN, kept exactly), body parsing, required fields,
types, timestamps, prices, empty, malformed and oversized bodies, unknown fields, every response code and body, the
side effects of rejected requests, the `webhook_audit` rows per path. Direction questions 6 to 9 (and 26 to 28, which
apply to every part). 13 allocated items (this AUDIT, sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4), the A02-13 records point raised in the check of the intended-behaviour sources (IS-152), and the
points handed to WA by part WI (`hexdigest()` letter case and `int()` of an infinite float from 18.2 item 4; the NaN
price from 18.2 item 15).

### 6.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### WA-01 — Authentication at the repository values rests on one shared secret, as a URL token or as the HMAC key over the raw body, with nothing that binds a request to a time; the mechanism matches the later sources, but the C6 docstring's "not replayable" holds only for the signature value

- **Area and flow:** the single authentication decision `_authenticate` (receiver L298 to L354) on `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>`
  and `GET /health`; the `webhook.require_hmac` setting; the A06 side of A02 X-01.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a docstring that the code does not bear out); Weakness (no freshness
  check in the authentication).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL; the sources conflict on the posture, IC-02):** (1) M19 WR8, L78 to
  L85, and RECV docstring WR8, L19 (IS-32; 17-Apr-2026): HMAC "optional but locked", a `sha256=` signature over the raw
  body, "Reject 401 on mismatch (timing-safe compare)", "Production deployment MUST set secret_token via env var".
  (2) M19 WR14, L145 (IS-19; 17-Apr-2026): `require_hmac` "default true in production" (the schema comment, CFGLOADER
  L621, `bcf03b5`: "if True, reject requests missing valid HMAC"); the first file value (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026) was
  `true`, "production must validate HMAC". (3) SYSCFG
  L328 to L334 (IS-18; `2d1e580`, 27-Apr-2026): the all-interfaces bind "relies on require_hmac=true plus
  firewall/security-group restrictions". (4) Commit `a1704df` (IS-168; 28-Apr-2026): `false`, "TEMP: disabled for
  Chartink (no HMAC support)". (5) Commit `67f21c0` (01-Jun-2026; IS-185, added in the cross-part check): body line "webhook:
  clarify require_hmac=false is permanent (Chartink uses ?token= auth)"; the comment became "Chartink cannot sign
  payloads; use ?token= auth". (6) `docs/first_day_live_runbook.md` L12 to L16 (`65ef57d3`, 03-Jun-2026; IS-186, added in the
  cross-part check): among the "7 TEMP config values" to check before live, "`webhook.require_hmac`: false -> keep
  false (Chartink `?token=` auth)". (7) RECV G.1 comment L196 to L199 and docstring L303 to L310, L346 to L347 (IS-21,
  IS-33; `95f2f47`, 26-Apr-2026; `e8ec604`, 25-Jul-2026): two methods, the token "ONLY accepted when
  require_hmac=False"; tokens in the URL are "weaker than HMAC over the body"; "A bad signature is NOT rescued by a
  valid token". (8) Commit `d922007` and `main.py` L222 to L225 (IS-22; 02-Jul-2026): "the sole live auth is
  ?token="; "the webhook must never be unauthenticated"; network-layer control is "the Phase-3 decision, proposed
  separately". (9) AUDIT-B section 1.8, L184 to L193 and L350 (IS-37, RS-05; lines 17-Jul-2026): the query token is
  "replayable"; authentication is "timing-safe ... verified correct, so this is a design acceptance, not a defect.
  Known and accepted pending Phase-3"; "Tag: Rama-owned (accepted risk; unchanged)" (its author's attribution, not a
  statement of Rama recorded by this project). (10) The C6 docstring, RECV L318 to L321 (`e8ec604`, 25-Jul-2026): "A
  valid /health signature is therefore a CONSTANT for a given secret and indefinitely replayable; /webhook's is
  body-bound and is not." (11) RECV L366 to L375 and L382 to L386 (IS-16; L382 to L386 are IS-187; `84cee3e` and `6e2a826`, 17-Jul-2026; `e8ec604`):
  `/health` has the "same rate limiter, same secret, and same require_hmac posture as /webhook". Sources (2) and (3)
  rest on HMAC; (4) to (9) accept the token, (5) calls it permanent, and (8) and (9) leave the network-layer control
  pending; none of the later sources names the bind comment (that comment is A02 X-01's).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L190, L200, L298 to L354, L387 to L393, L547 to L549;
  `main.py` L3414; `config/system_config.yaml` L336 (A02's file).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: at the file value `require_hmac: false` a request authenticates with
  a correct `sha256=` signature over the raw body or with a `token` query parameter equal to `WEBHOOK_SECRET`; a
  wrong signature is not rescued by a correct token; both comparisons use `hmac.compare_digest`; `/health` runs the
  same decision over an empty body; a failure answers 401 (P02-S015 to P02-S017, P02-S023, P02-S025). FACT: nothing
  signed or checked carries a time, a nonce or a request identifier, and the token is a fixed value in the URL
  (P02-S019). INTERPRETATION (P02-S019; WA-06): an identical request authenticates again whenever it is re-sent. On
  `/webhook` the signature is bound to the body, so a re-sent request is bounded only by the later checks on what the
  body says: for a full-format `triggered_at`, the 600 s age check; for a time-only `triggered_at`, which the receiver
  dates with the day of receipt, the same body re-sent on a later day, at any moment inside the entry window up to
  600 s after the same clock time (a moment before that clock time gives a negative age, which also passes, WA-06),
  passes the age check and the duplicate layers (the in-flight claim, the 300 s cache key, and the fingerprint, whose
  bucket comes from the re-dated `triggered_at` and whose uniqueness is per `fingerprint_date`, the day of receipt:
  receiver L940 to L954, L977; P03-S005, P03-S013) (WA-06);
  the EOD route has no age check (P01-S052 to P01-S055). FACT and INTERPRETATION (added in the check; recorded, not
  counted): the signature covers the body only, not the path (L337 to L339, L547), as WR8 defines it; since `scan_name`
  became optional (L608, L617, L620 to L633; IS-167, 28-Apr-2026), a signed body without `scan_name` is accepted on any
  scanner path in the map, while a body carrying it (as Chartink's sample does, IS-38) is refused 400 on another path;
  the URL token is the same for every path. The effective `require_hmac` and whether `WEBHOOK_SECRET` was
  set are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (WI-22).
- **Evidence:** P02-S009 to P02-S025, P02-S040 to P02-S042; P01-S052 to P01-S055; P03-S005, P03-S013; the code lines
  above; the `require_hmac` history (section 1; WA-02).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. MATCH parts: the
  mechanism is as the later sources (4) to (8) and (11) describe: the token only while `require_hmac` is false, the
  signature tried first, a timing-safe comparison, 401 on mismatch, one decision for both routes, and the secret
  required before the receiver is built (WA-12). ISSUE part: source (10)'s contrast holds for the signature value (on
  `/webhook` it changes with the body), but read as a statement that `/webhook`'s signature is not indefinitely
  replayable it is not borne out: the authentication itself never expires, and the later checks bound a re-sent
  request only for a full-format `triggered_at` (documentation against code; Document 04, section 5.3). The replay
  property of the URL token is recorded by (9) as an accepted design pending Phase-3 (its author's words) and is not
  counted separately. The conflict between (2) and (3) and the later sources (IC-02) is resolved by the file value of
  an A02 key; its comment side is A02 X-01 ("ISSUE; FACT"; treatment batch T5, group G4, AGREED, 09-Nov-2026;
  unchanged) and is not counted again here.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor for the documentation part; the effect of the replay property is
  not established (runtime evidence does not survive).
- **Owner:** A06 (the authentication decision and its docstring); A02 (the key, its comments, X-01).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 12; A02 X-01 (A06 side); IC-02; RS-05; Open Issues Register A02-13; WA-02; WA-06;
  WA-12; WI-09 (the network path).
- **Rama:** A02-13 already holds the question of what the bind was meant to rest on (history OPEN; Rama's answer of
  09-Nov-2026, "Keep all five OPEN"); it is not re-asked and nothing here answers it. No new question.

##### WA-02 — Records discrepancy: Open Issues Register entry A02-13 dates the setting of `require_hmac` to false on 02-Jul-2026; the repository history shows 28-Apr-2026 (commit `a1704df`), with the comment rewritten on 01-Jun-2026 and again on 02-Jul-2026

- **Area and flow:** the project records about the `webhook.require_hmac` setting (A02's key; A06's token check).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across records (a project record against the legacy repository history).
- **What the records say (project records, quoted exactly):** Open Issues Register, entry A02-13, "Question or
  discrepancy": "After `webhook.require_hmac` was set false on 02-Jul-2026, was the webhook receiver's all-interfaces
  bind meant to rest on the URL token check alone (with the firewall and security-group restrictions)?"; its Evidence
  field dates the value's comment: "the value's comment (`d922007`, 2026-07-02, C-2: ...)". The A02 AUDIT (IC-38 row,
  L161: "the `require_hmac` value and its comment (02-Jul-2026)"; X-01, L2415: "L336 (`d922007`, 2026-07-02, C-2):
  `require_hmac: false`, ...") and the A02 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (IC-38 row, L284: "the `require_hmac` value and its
  comment (02-Jul-2026)") give the date of the line as it now stands (`git blame` of L336 at the baseline gives
  `d922007`); the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD's short form of A02-13 (L452) gives no date. The A06 STUDY quotes the entry
  exactly (P02-S036, P09-S075) and dates the value's comment by the same line date (P02-S041: "commit `d922007`,
  02-Jul-2026: "Chartink cannot sign payloads; use ?token= auth ...""); the STUDY (passed, Revision 1) is not edited.
- **What the history shows:** FACT (`git log -G require_hmac 970aabf -- config/system_config.yaml`, with `git show`
  limited to the `require_hmac` lines; re-verified for this part): `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026) adds `require_hmac: true`,
  "production must validate HMAC"; `2d1e580` (27-Apr-2026) adds the bind comment that names `require_hmac=true`;
  `a1704df` (28-Apr-2026) changes the value from `true` to `false`, "TEMP: disabled for Chartink (no HMAC support)";
  `67f21c0` (01-Jun-2026) keeps `false` and replaces the comment with "Chartink cannot sign payloads; use ?token= auth
  (WEBHOOK_SECRET env var required in live mode)" (commit body: "clarify require_hmac=false is permanent");
  `d922007` (02-Jul-2026) keeps `false` and changes only the bracketed text, to "(WEBHOOK_SECRET required in BOTH modes
  as of C-2 02-Jul)". No commit after `a1704df` changes the value. Claude's verification at the check of the intended-behaviour sources (`a1704df` on
  28-Apr-2026; `d922007` changed only the comment; IS-152) is confirmed, and the intermediate comment change of 01-Jun-2026 is
  added. FACT (re-run in the check: `git log -G require_hmac 970aabf -- signals/webhook_receiver.py core/config_loader.py

  flag is true), 17-Jul-2026 (`6e2a826`, `/health` honours the flag) and 25-Jul-2026 (`e8ec604`, C6: one decision); the
  schema field is from `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026); `main.py` has no line naming the flag. No commit of 02-Jul-2026 changes

- **Correction for the intended-behaviour sources file (reported for the cross-part check; that file was not edited by this part):** IS-168
