##### WA-03 — A token or signature text holding a non-ASCII character makes `hmac.compare_digest` raise `TypeError`, so the request is answered 500, with a CRITICAL line, instead of 401

- **Area and flow:** the two comparisons in `_authenticate` (L340, L351) on `/webhook` and `/health`.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an authentication failure answered as an unhandled exception).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** (1) M19 WR8, L83 (IS-32; 17-Apr-2026): "Reject 401 on mismatch
  (timing-safe compare)"; WR5, L53 (IS-44): "401 Unauthorized - HMAC mismatch"; L57: "500 only for unhandled exceptions
  (logged at CRITICAL)". (2) RECV L350 to L353 (`e8ec604`, 25-Jul-2026): a token that does not match gives "Invalid
  token" (401). No source mentions non-ASCII input (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 15).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L333 to L334, L340, L351; `/webhook`: `try` L510, handler L525
  to L528, audit L529 to L534; `/health`: L387, the 500 handler L410 to L413.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: both comparisons receive two `str` values, and the receiver does
  not check that the incoming text is ASCII (P02-S018). Current external context (CPython 3.11 source as read by the
  STUDY on 17-Nov-2026, P02-S018; CPython 3.12, 3.13 and main, read 18-Nov-2026, section 1): `Lib/hmac.py` binds
  `compare_digest` to `_hashlib.compare_digest` whenever the `_hashlib` (OpenSSL) module imports, and to
  `_operator._compare_digest` only when it does not (3.11 to 3.13 L7 to L15; main L6 to L14); both implementations
  raise `TypeError` ("comparing strings with non-ASCII characters is not supported") when either `str` argument holds
  a non-ASCII character (`Modules/_hashopenssl.c`, `_hashlib_compare_digest_impl`: 3.11 L1994 to L1998, 3.12 L2108 to
  L2112, 3.13 L2126 to L2130, main L2632 to L2636; `Modules/_operator.c`: 3.11 L832, 3.12 L834, 3.13 L831, main L861).
  (Corrected in the check: the draft extended the point to 3.12, 3.13 and main through `_operator.c` only, which is the
  fallback; the OpenSSL-backed function is the one normally bound, and it behaves the same.) Current external context
  (read 18-Nov-2026) on how such text reaches the comparison: Werkzeug 3.1.3 and 3.1.5, `sansio/request.py`, `args`:
  the query string is parsed by `urllib.parse.parse_qsl` with `errors="werkzeug.url_quote"` ("Invalid bytes remain
  percent encoded"), and CPython 3.11 `Lib/urllib/parse.py` L745 to L746 decodes percent-escapes as UTF-8 by default;
  waitress 3.0.2 admits bytes 0x80 to 0xFF ("obs-text") in header values (`rfc7230.py`, `FIELD_VCHAR`) and decodes
  header values as Latin-1 (`parser.py` L238 to L246). INTERPRETATION: a percent-encoded valid UTF-8 sequence for a
  non-ASCII character in the `token` parameter, or any byte 0x80 to 0xFF after `sha256=` in the signature header,
  reaches L351 or L340 as a non-ASCII `str` (installed Werkzeug and waitress versions UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED,
  P02-S008). INTERPRETATION: on any of those interpreters, such a token value or
  signature text raises at L340 or L351; on `/webhook` the request ends in the generic 500 of L525 to L528 with a
  CRITICAL log line and an audit row (500, 0, 0); on `/health` it reaches the 500 handler (CRITICAL with Flask's
  generic text, WI-06) while Flask debug mode is off (P02-S073). The request is refused either way. The installed
  Python version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the deployment documents name Python 3.11 (`docs/06_deployment_guide.md`
  L26 and L33; `docs/disaster_recovery.md` L192 to L195; HISTORICAL, not proof of what was installed).
- **Evidence:** P02-S008, P02-S018, P02-S069 rows 8 and 29, P02-S073; the code lines above; the CPython, Werkzeug
  and waitress sources (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. A
  mismatching credential is answered 500, against WR8's "Reject 401 on mismatch" (failure handling, Document 04,
  section 5.3). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the comparison and its 401 answer are the mechanism);
  (b) holds for every CPython source read (3.11, 3.12, 3.13 and main), but the installed interpreter is UNKNOWN;
  (c) holds (the code handles an invalid token, L352, and any caller can send one); (d) fails: WR5's rule "500 only for
  unhandled exceptions (logged at CRITICAL)" is what happens, so not every intent source is contradicted.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the request is still refused; the effects are the status code, a
  CRITICAL log line and, on `/webhook`, an audit row with 500.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 15; WI-06 (the 500 handler's log text); WA-11 (library points).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WA-04 — Raw request body text is written to the log on every 400 path without the storage sanitiser; for a UTF-16 or UTF-32 body the sanitiser's matches also miss in the stored copy; two sanitiser comments point to stale lines

- **Area and flow:** what the receiver does with the body text after authentication: the WARNING log lines (L519 to
  L523, L589 to L592) and the stored copy `signals.webhook_payload` (L681 to L690, L857 to L886).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated purpose not served on another path of the same request);
  Weakness (a sanitiser that depends on the body's encoding); documentation against code (stale line references).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) RECV L518 and commit `d7c2a66` (IS-48; 28-Apr-2026): "Log
  raw body on 400 errors for debugging Chartink format" (commit subject: "debug: log raw body on 400 errors for
  Chartink diagnosis"). (2) RECV L681 to L687 and the sanitiser docstring L858 to L872 (IS-49; `58ff1e7`,
  05-Jul-2026): sanitise "the copy PERSISTED to signals.webhook_payload so the webhook secret never lands at rest",
  because "Chartink echoes the configured URL (incl. ?token=...) inside the body's `webhook_url` field"; "STORAGE-ONLY".
  (3) RECV L687 and L865 (`58ff1e7`): authentication "(:410-432) ran before this" (on IS-49's lines; noted in IS-49 in the cross-part check). No
  source states a rule for log text or for the body's encoding (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 16). Source
  (1) is a debugging aid that predates (2) by about ten weeks; (2) does not name it.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L480, L518 to L523, L586 to L593, L688 to L690, L857 to L886;
  `core/logger.py` L240 to L243, L391 to L402 (the logging set-up; not A06's, interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: every 400 answer of `/webhook`, the EOD route's 400s included, logs
  `raw_body[:1000]` at WARNING, and a malformed-JSON body is also logged as `raw_body[:500]`; neither passes through
  the sanitiser (P02-S034). FACT (`core/logger.py` L240 to L243, L391 to L402; interface only): the logging set-up
  writes daily log files, and the system file's filter passes every record at INFO or above ("INFO+, JSON, all
  loggers"); whether the receiver's logger reached it in a given run is not examined.
  INTERPRETATION: when a body refused with 400 carries a `webhook_url` with its query string, as source (2) says
  Chartink's bodies do (the Chartink page's sample URL has no query string, P02-S033), that text is written to the log
  unsanitised (as the `repr` of the body's first 1,000 bytes, or 500 on the malformed-JSON line), so the sanitiser's
  stated purpose (the secret "never lands at rest") is served for the stored copy only. FACT and INTERPRETATION (P02-S047, P02-S050): `json.loads`
  also accepts UTF-16 and UTF-32 bodies, while the stored copy is decoded as UTF-8 with replacement (L689), so for such
  a body the sanitiser's literal and `token=` matches do not find their targets. FACT: at the baseline the
  authentication is at L298 to L354, called at L547, not at L410 to L432 as L687 and L865 say; when `58ff1e7` wrote
  those comments (05-Jul-2026) the authentication block stood at L411 to L433 of that version (`git show
  58ff1e7:signals/webhook_receiver.py`), and the C6 refactor of 25-Jul-2026 (`e8ec604`) moved it into `_authenticate`.
  No log, row or environment value was read; what any log held is not examined (D-07).
- **Evidence:** P02-S033, P02-S034, P02-S047, P02-S050; the code lines above; blame dates (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The debugging log path of (1) still writes
  unsanitised body text after (2) introduced a sanitiser whose stated purpose that path does not share (a stated
  purpose not served on another path; Document 04, section 5.3). The UTF-16 or UTF-32 case is latent (whether any
  sender used such a body is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, STUDY 18.2 item 9). The stale line references are
  documentation against code (minor). No exposure finding is made and no recommendation is given (D-07): the record
  states only what the code does with the body text it receives.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established (whether any 400 occurred and what any log held are
  runtime facts that do not survive); minor for the line references.
- **Owner:** A06 (the log lines and the sanitiser); A01 (the logging set-up, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 16; IS-48, IS-49; STUDY 18.2 item 9.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### WA-05 — `/health` withholds whether `require_hmac` is on, as its comment intends, while `/webhook` tells any unauthenticated caller the same thing in its 401 reason

- **Area and flow:** the 401 answers of the two routes (L348 to L354, L388 to L393, L548 to L549).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (the stated purpose of one route undone by the other).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL; both from commit `e8ec604`, 25-Jul-2026):** (1) RECV L389 to L392 and
  the docstring L322 to L324 (IS-17): `/health`'s failure path "Deliberately says nothing about system state"; the
  reason is "DISCARDED on purpose: naming it here would tell an anonymous caller whether require_hmac is on". (2) RECV
  L539 to L543 (IS-15): `/webhook` keeps "the granular failure message /webhook has always returned".
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L343 to L354, L388 to L393, L547 to L549, L551 to L554.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `/webhook` answers 401 with the reason (P02-S035): with `require_hmac`
  true and no signature header, "HMAC signature required (require_hmac=True); token param is not accepted" (L348 to
  L349); with it false, "Invalid token" or "Missing auth: provide X-Webhook-Signature header or ?token= param" (L352,
  L354). Authentication runs before the unknown-scanner check (L547 before L553), so any path segment reaches it.
  INTERPRETATION: an unauthenticated POST without a signature header learns from the 401 text whether `require_hmac`
  is on, the fact that (1) withholds on `/health`. MATCH parts: each route does what its own comment says.
- **Evidence:** P02-S015, P02-S023, P02-S025, P02-S035; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The purpose stated
  in (1) is not achieved at the receiver as a whole, because (2) keeps the granular text on the other route of the same
  server (consistency; Document 04, section 5.3).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the text names one configuration flag; it gives no system state and
  no secret.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 17; IS-15, IS-17; WA-17 (the state fields that `/health` withholds).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WA-06 — `triggered_at` is read as IST, as the sources say; a time-only text is dated with the day of receipt even when it is later than the moment of receipt, and no check refuses a time ahead of the receiver's clock; the `%p` match depends on the process locale

- **Area and flow:** the intraday parse of `triggered_at` (receiver L635 to L662) and the receiver's age check (L916 to
  L926); the processor's age check at the interface (part PQ).
- **Issue category:** Weakness (a timestamp check with no lower bound; a date assigned without a check);
  documentation in an answer text. (The IST rule is a MATCH part.)
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL, except the external page):** (1) M19 WR4, L42 (IS-39;
  17-Apr-2026): `triggered_at` as "IST naive" text of the form "2026-04-15 10:15:00". (2) RECV L635 and L644
  (`31a03c72`, 28-Apr-2026; IS-41): "Chartink sends "HH:MM am/pm", we also accept "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS""; "Time-only
  format: use today's date". (3) RECV L650 to L651 (`0a734e4`, 13-May-2026; IS-41): "Chartink sends naive strings; we
  assume IST". (4) SCHEMA L47 (`bcf03b5`, 17-Apr-2026): `triggered_at` "ISO IST from Chartink". (5) M19 WR6, L62 to L65
  (IS-87): "Compute age = now_ist() - triggered_at. If age > signal_expiry_sec ..., reject signal individually". (6)
  CK-WEBHOOK (IS-38; read 18-Nov-2026; current external context): sample `triggered_at` "2:34 pm"; no time zone stated.
  (7) SYSCFG L127 and commit `2e8448c` (IS-84; 28-Apr-2026): `triggered_at` "is scan time, not delivery time".
  The sources differ on the form Chartink sends (IC-05) and agree on IST; no source speaks of a time later than the
  moment of receipt, or of the locale (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 21).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L581, L635 to L662, L677, L916 to L926.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the text is tried in three formats (`%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S`, `%I:%M
  %p`, `%H:%M`); a time-only value takes the IST date of `now`, read at L581 before the parse, with seconds 0; a naive
  value is given the IST zone with no conversion; no zone, offset, `T` separator or fractional seconds is accepted
  (P02-S055, P02-S056). FACT: the age test is `age_sec > expiry_sec` only; a time later than the receiver's clock gives
  a negative age and passes, and a full-format date need not be today (P02-S058); the processor has no lower bound
  either (P04-S040). INTERPRETATION (P02-S058; P04-S040): a time-only text later in the day than the moment of receipt
  (for example "2:45 pm" received at 11:00, inside the file's 10:00 to 15:00 entry window, `config/system_config.yaml`
  L28 and L47) is dated today and accepted as fresh, although, read as a scan time (SYSCFG L127, IS-84: "Chartink
  triggered_at is scan time, not delivery time"), it cannot be a scan of the same day unless the two clocks differ
  (corrected in the check: the draft attributed the scan-time reading to source (2), which says only "use today's
  date"); with the duplicate layers keyed on the day (P03-S005, P03-S013), a body from an earlier day re-sent at any
  moment of the entry window up to 600 s after its clock time is likewise taken as today's (WA-01); a full-format time
  on a later date passes both age checks whatever its distance.
  FACT: the 400 text (L662) names "HH:MM am/pm or YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS" and not the third, 24-hour `HH:MM` format that
  L640 also accepts. Current external context (Python 3.12 `datetime` documentation and CPython 3.12 `_strptime.py`, as
  cited by P02-S057, read 17-Nov-2026): letter case does not prevent a `%p` match, but the AM and PM texts come from
  the process locale; the locale and the Python version are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P02-S055 to P02-S058; P04-S039, P04-S040; P03-S005, P03-S013; the code lines above; IS-84.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with MATCH parts and an UNKNOWN part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION;
  current external context. MATCH parts: every naive value is read as IST, as (1), (3) and (4) state; both forms that
  the sources name are accepted (IC-05). ISSUE parts: where no source states the intent for future times, the code
  itself shows a weak check (Document 04, section 5.3): the date given to a time-only text is not checked against the
  receipt time, and the only freshness check has no lower bound, so a text that cannot be today's scan is accepted as
  today's; the 400 text omits one accepted format (minor, documentation). UNKNOWN part: the process locale, on which a
  `%p` match of "pm" depends (STUDY 18.2 item 5); the time zone Chartink used (STUDY 18.2 item 9).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor for the answer text; the effect of the date rule is not established
  (whether any such text arrived is a runtime fact that does not survive).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); part PQ for the processor's age check and the expiry order.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 21; 18.1 point 50 (the lower bound; WA-10); STUDY 18.2 items 5 and 9; IC-05; STUDY
  question 10; WA-01; WI-16 (the EOD parser's fourth format, CD-36).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 10: its first half (IST) is answered by the sources at their dates; its second half (were
  times later than receipt meant to be refused?) stays a draft question for Rama and bears on treatment only.

##### WA-07 — Trigger prices "nan" and "inf" pass the positive-price check, which has no finiteness, upper-bound or tick test; a symbol is refused only when empty, although WR10 names "empty/malformed"; no instrument check

- **Area and flow:** the per-symbol checks at the start of `_process_signal` (receiver L904 to L914), after the alias
  lookup and the excluded-symbol check (L696 to L717).
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Intended against actual (WR10's "malformed" and "non-numeric").
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** M19 WR10, L103 to L104 (IS-46; 17-Apr-2026): `INVALID_SYMBOL`
  "empty/malformed"; `INVALID_PRICE` "<= 0 or non-numeric". RECV L904 and L908 (`bcf03b5`): "WR10: validate symbol",
  "WR10: validate price". No source states a rule for non-finite values, bounds, ticks or an instrument check
  (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 22); A05-02 (history OPEN) concerns a symbol missing from the instrument
  cache at A05's side.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L701, L905 to L914, L975 to L978, L1047 to L1049.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `INVALID_SYMBOL` only for an empty symbol (L905 to L906); no other
  symbol check and no instrument lookup exist at the receiver; an alias miss keeps the received case (P02-S059,
  P02-S061). FACT: `INVALID_PRICE` when `float(price_str)` raises `ValueError` or `TypeError`, or when `price <= 0`
  (L909 to L914); no upper bound, tick-size or market-price comparison (P02-S061). Current external context (Python
  3.13 `float()` and the Python 3.11 language reference, as cited by P02-S062, read 17-Nov-2026): `float()` accepts
  "nan", "inf" and "infinity" in any case, surrounding whitespace, underscores between digit groups and any Unicode
  decimal digit; any ordered comparison with a not-a-number value is false (both pages re-opened in the check,
  18-Nov-2026). INTERPRETATION (P02-S062): an item "nan" or "inf" passes `price <= 0`, is passed to the insert as
  `trigger_price` (L977) and is put on the queue item with the signal (L1047); a non-empty symbol of any form (inner
  spaces, punctuation, lower case after an alias miss) is accepted and flows into the claim, the duplicate keys, the
  row and the queue item. Current external context (read 18-Nov-2026, in the check; storage is A03's, interface fact
  only): CPython 3.11 `Modules/_sqlite/cursor.c` L570 to L577 binds a Python float with `sqlite3_bind_double`, and
  SQLite's published source (official Git mirror, master) `src/vdbeapi.c` L1860 to L1870 passes it to
  `sqlite3VdbeMemSetDouble`, which sets the value to NULL when it is NaN and stores any other double as REAL
  (`src/vdbemem.c` L1118 to L1124). INTERPRETATION: if the store's cursor is the standard `sqlite3` cursor (A03's
  `transaction()` is annotated `sqlite3.Cursor`, `core/state_store.py` L506) and the installed SQLite behaves as that
  source, a "nan" price is stored as NULL in `signals.trigger_price` (REAL, nullable; SCHEMA L73) while the queue item
  carries the NaN float, and an "inf" price is stored as a REAL infinity; the installed Python and SQLite versions are
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (STUDY 18.2 item 15, narrowed; corrected in the check: the draft left the storage wholly
  open). How later stages treat such a price is A07's to A10's.
- **Evidence:** P02-S059, P02-S061, P02-S062; P01-S039; the code lines above; the CPython and SQLite sources
  (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context.
  MATCH parts: an empty symbol, an unparsable price and a price at or below zero are refused as WR10 says. ISSUE parts:
  the price check has no finiteness test, so "nan" (literally "not a number") and "inf" pass a check that WR10 describes
  as refusing non-numeric prices (whether WR10's "non-numeric" was meant to cover them is STUDY question 9); WR10 names
  "malformed" symbols, and the code tests only emptiness (no source defines "malformed") (Document 04, section 5.3,
  missing validation). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the price check is the mechanism); (b) holds
  (an ordered comparison with NaN is false by the language reference, whenever such an item arrives); (c) is not
  established: whether "nan" is a "non-numeric" price in WR10's sense is not stated, and whether the documented sender
  can send such an item is UNKNOWN; (d) is therefore not established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established (whether such a value ever arrived, how the installed
  versions stored it and how later stages used it are not established).
- **Owner:** A06 (the receiver's checks); A03 (storage of a NaN or infinite value); A07 to A10 (later use of the
  price); A05 (the instrument data; A05-02).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 22; STUDY 18.2 item 15 (NaN price storage, with part EV); STUDY question 9; WI-10
  (the case kept on an alias miss); Open Issues Register A05-02 (history OPEN; unchanged).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 9 (were trigger prices meant to be finite positive numbers?) stays a draft question; it is
  not needed to classify (the ISSUE rests on the missing finiteness test) and bears on treatment.

##### WA-08 — The oversize path: the 1 MiB Flask limit bounds what the application reads, not what the server receives and buffers, and the body is read before the shutting-down and per-IP checks, so an oversized request is never counted by the limiter

- **Area and flow:** the body read of `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>` (receiver L480) against the shutting-down check
  (L485), the per-IP limiter (L495) and waitress's own limits (`main.py` L3991 to L3996).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated purpose not served for one class of requests); Weakness (the
  receipt bounded only by the server's 1 GiB default).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) RECV L251 (FIX-077; `39110d8`, 16-May-2026; IS-43, CD-05):
  "1MB hard limit prevents OOM". (2) RECV L492 to L494 and the limiter docstring L70 to L77 (C-2; `d922007`,
  02-Jul-2026; IS-45): the per-IP limit "Bounds a single IP flooding /webhook"; "On exceed -> 429 (before
  auth/parse/queue work, so a flood is cheap to reject)". No source states whether the 1 MiB limit was meant to bound
  what the server receives (STUDY question 12; intent-sources file, section 6.2, question 12: OPEN QUESTION narrowed).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L249 to L251, L479 to L504; `main.py` L3988 to L3999 (A01's
  file; no waitress size argument).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and current external context (Flask 3.1.x configuration, Werkzeug
  3.1.x, waitress 3.0.2 arguments, design page and tagged source, as cited by P02-S063 to P02-S065 and P02-S074, read
  17-Nov-2026): `get_data()` (L480) runs before the shutting-down check, before the limiter and outside the `try` of
  L510; a body over 1 MiB raises 413 there, answered by Flask with no audit row and no limiter token (P02-S064); at the
  pinned waitress the whole body, up to waitress's default 1 GiB, is received and buffered (in memory up to 512 KiB,
  in a temporary file above) before Flask runs, and waitress itself answers 413, 431 or 400 above its own limits
  (P02-S065, P02-S074). Re-checked in the waitress 3.0.2 tagged source (read 18-Nov-2026): `HTTPRequestParser.received`
  sets `completed` for a request with a body only when the body receiver has completed, the body being held in an
  `OverflowableBuffer(inbuf_overflow)` (`parser.py`; `buffers.py`: a temporary file once the buffered size reaches the
  overflow; `adjustments.py`: `inbuf_overflow` 524,288, `max_request_body_size` 1,073,741,824,
  `max_request_header_size` 262,144), and `HTTPChannel.received` queues the request and calls `server.add_task` only
  once it is completed (`channel.py`); a declared `Content-Length` at or above the body limit is refused 413 when the
  headers end, without receiving the body; and (added in the check) a `Transfer-Encoding` other than `chunked` raises
  `TransferEncodingNotImplemented`, answered by waitress with 501 (`ServerNotImplemented`, `utilities.py`), a waitress
  answer not in the STUDY's table (P02-S069 rows 30 to 32). INTERPRETATION: (i) the application never reads more than 1 MiB of a body into memory, which is
  what (1)'s purpose names; the server still receives and buffers up to 1 GiB per request. (ii) Every body, oversized
  or not, is received before the limiter runs: a request up to 1 MiB is read into memory before the limiter can refuse
  it, and a request over 1 MiB is refused 413 before the limiter and is never counted, so the bound of (2) does not
  apply to oversized requests at all. The installed waitress version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P02-S008).
- **Evidence:** P02-S063 to P02-S065, P02-S069 rows 4 and 30 to 32, P02-S074; the code lines above; the waitress
  3.0.2 source (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; current external context. MATCH part:
  (i) for the application's own memory, as (1) states (INTERPRETATION). ISSUE part: (ii) the limiter's stated purpose,
  to bound one address flooding `/webhook`, is not served for oversized requests, which bypass it, and the body is
  received before any refusal (a stated purpose not served; Document 04, section 5.3). Whether (i) is also a difference
  depends on what (1) was meant to bound (STUDY question 12, OPEN). The missing audit row for the 413 is WI-02's and is
  not counted again; the limiter's own algorithm is part WD's.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established (whether any oversized or flooding request arrived is a
  runtime fact that does not survive).
- **Owner:** A06 (the body-read order and the Flask limit); A01 (`main.py`'s waitress arguments, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 23; CD-05; IS-43, IS-45; STUDY question 12; WI-02 (no audit row); part WD (18.1 point
  33, the limiter).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 12 (what the 1 MiB limit was meant to bound) stays a draft question; not needed to classify
  part (ii).

##### WA-09 — The 503 answers rely on a sender retry that Chartink's own pages do not describe; whether Chartink retried, and its other behaviour at the legacy dates, are not established

- **Area and flow:** the retryable answers of `POST /webhook/<scanner_name>` (batch 503 for `QUEUE_FULL` or
  `STORE_ERROR`, L730 to L734; request-level 503 for back-pressure, L575 to L578, and for shutting down, L485 to L490)
  and what the receiver assumes of the sender.
- **Issue category:** UNKNOWN (external behaviour without surviving evidence).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL, except the external pages):** (1) RECV L59 to L66 and L730 to L732
  (P3-s14; `a9a686e`, 17-Jul-2026; IS-47): the batch answers 503 "so the sender (Chartink) retries"; commit
  `a9a686e`: with a 200 answer "Chartink would never retry". (2) The HIGH #6 fix direction (IS-170; 17-Apr-2026):
  "Return 503 Service Unavailable when queue.Full. Client can retry." (3) M-S2 (IS-64; 14-Jul-2026): a request that
  meets a `QUEUE_FULL` row "is a legitimate 503 retry". (4) LOCKED-YAML P6, L289, and the duplicate exception docstring,
  `core/exceptions.py` L144 (IS-53, IS-56; 17-Apr-2026): "Chartink's 'send duplicate notifications' setting causes
  retries"; "Chartink retries are expected". (5) CK-WEBHOOK,
  CK-FAQ and CK-GUIDE (IS-38, IS-130; read 18-Nov-2026; current external context): no retry, response-code or timeout
  behaviour is stated; "Duplicate" allows repeated notifications for the same stock and "Unique" one per stock per day.
  IC-06 (a reliance on a retry that the external pages do not describe) and IC-07 (what the "duplicate" setting does).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L59 to L66, L485 to L490, L575 to L578, L730 to L745, L981 to
  L1013.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the batch answers 503 when any symbol is `QUEUE_FULL` or
  `STORE_ERROR`, with the per-symbol results in the body; a `QUEUE_FULL` row is recovered only when a later request with
  the same fingerprint reaches the re-accept path (P02-S072; P03-S039, P03-S040). INTERPRETATION (P02-S072): a sender
  that retries a mixed batch re-sends symbols already accepted, which the duplicate layers then refuse (part WD).
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether Chartink retried on any status, after what delay and how often; its timeouts; and
  the rest of STUDY 18.2 item 9 (its source addresses, the time zone of `triggered_at`, which body the legacy alerts
  sent, which alerts existed and how they were set, the `scan_name` texts, whether it read the `X-Queue-*` headers, the
  size of its open-bell burst, and whether any sender used a UTF-16 or UTF-32 body).
- **Evidence:** P02-S031, P02-S072; P03-S039, P03-S040; P07-S048 to P07-S053; IS-38, IS-130; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. HISTORICAL; FACT; current external context. Chartink's
  pages are silent on retries rather than contrary, so no ISSUE is raised on IC-06; the effect of the 503 contract
  cannot be established without Chartink's behaviour at the legacy dates. IC-07 is recorded: P6's reading ("causes
  retries") and the external page's ("repeated notifications for the same stock") both lead the code to the same
  duplicate refusal, so no code difference follows from it. Recovery of `QUEUE_FULL` rows by the system itself is part
  WD's (18.1 point 32).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not applicable.
- **Owner:** A06 (the answers); part WD (recovery and duplicates); part CK (the alert configuration).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 25; STUDY 18.2 item 9; IC-06, IC-07; WA-04 (UTF-16 or UTF-32 bodies); WA-06 (time
  zone); WI-09 (source addresses); WI-12 (`scan_name` texts); part WD (18.1 point 32; burst size); part CK (STUDY
  question 51).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify. How the alerts were set in Chartink (unique or duplicate, frequency) is STUDY
  question 51 (part CK).

##### WA-10 — Two expiry notions with different start times stand side by side: both age checks measure from `triggered_at`, while the stored `expires_at` counts from receipt, so the stored deadline is not the one the code applies

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's age check (L916 to L926) and the `expires_at` it stores (L957 to L961, L976);
  the processor's age check at the interface (part PQ); the reader of `expires_at` at the interface (A15).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (a stored value that misdescribes the rule applied); Consistency
  across sources (IC-12, IC-10).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL; sources conflict):** (1) SYSCFG L127 and commit `2e8448c9`
  (IS-84; 28-Apr-2026): `expiry_sec: 600`, "10 min - Chartink triggered_at is scan time, not delivery time". (2) SCHEMA
  L49 (IS-85; 17-Apr-2026): `expires_at` is "received_at + signal_expiry_sec". (3) Commit `e55cb59`, Item 51 (IS-174;
  31-May-2026): "expires_at now correctly computed as received_at + expiry_sec (was incorrectly set to received_at)".
  (4) M19 WR6, L62 to L65 (IS-87; 17-Apr-2026): age from `triggered_at`, "default 60". (5) LOCKED-YAML P9a and the
  `SignalExpiredError` docstring (IS-86; 17-Apr-2026): 90 s. (6) Commit `af98746c` (IS-167; 28-Apr-2026): "60 -> 300 (5
  min for paper mode latency)". IC-12 (the base time: scan time against receipt; the 31-May source is the later and
  names neither) and IC-10 (the age limit: 60, 90, 300 and 600 s; the 600 s decision of 28-Apr names the scan-time
  reason).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L677 to L679, L916 to L926, L957 to L961, L976; the processor
  L914 to L921 (P04-S037; corrected in the check from L917 to L921: the age is computed at L915 to L916);
  `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/db_reader.py` L826 (A15's, as P04-S041 records).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the receiver refuses `EXPIRED` when `now - triggered_at >
  expiry_sec`, with `expiry_sec` read per request (600 in the file); the processor applies the same limit from
  `triggered_at` when a worker starts the signal; the stored `expires_at` is `received_at + expiry_sec`; no processor
  code reads `expires_at`, and the only reader found is A15's dashboard reader (P02-S058, P02-S075; P04-S036, P04-S037,
  P04-S041; P11-S039). INTERPRETATION: the stored deadline is later than the one the checks apply by the delivery
  delay: a signal whose `triggered_at` is already 500 s old at receipt is refused by the worker's check if a worker
  starts it more than about 100 s after receipt (the worker checks once, when it starts the signal), while its row says
  that it expires 600 s after receipt. Both checks use the 600 s value, the later
  decision of IC-10; the 60 s and 90 s texts remain elsewhere (part PQ, 18.1 point 57; WI-20 for the schema comment).
- **Evidence:** P02-S058, P02-S075; P04-S036 to P04-S041; P11-S039; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Each side of IC-12 is implemented in
  a different place, so the stored `expires_at` misdescribes when the code treats the signal as expired (consistency;
  Document 04, section 5.3). The threshold (IC-10) follows the later 600 s decision at both checks (MATCH for the
  threshold; the stale texts are counted by part PQ and WI-20, not here).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: no decision in the code reads `expires_at`; its only reader shown is
  A15's dashboard (what that display shows the operator is A15's).
- **Owner:** A06 (the receiver's check and the stored value); A03 (the column); A15 (the reader); part PQ (the
  processor's check, its order and the resume paths of 18.1 point 50).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 50 (receiver side; the worker-side order, the resume paths without an age check and
  the 60 s and 90 s texts are part PQ's); IC-10, IC-12; STUDY question 22 (part PQ); WA-06 (the missing lower bound,
  also named in point 50).
- **Rama:** STUDY question 22 (from which moment the expiry was meant to be measured) stays a draft question (part PQ);
  not needed to classify this record, since the two notions differ whichever was intended.

##### WA-11 — Library behaviour at the authentication and parsing boundary: four points now read in published source (CPython; for storage, also SQLite); the installed versions and the rest are not established

- **Area and flow:** the Python behaviour that the receiver's authentication and numeric casts depend on.
- **Issue category:** UNKNOWN (library behaviour without an authoritative statement for the installed versions).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not applicable. (M19 WR8, L80 to L83, IS-32, shows the header as `sha256=<hex>`
  and the expected value as `hexdigest()`; no source states a letter case.)
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L336 to L341, L351, L459 to L469.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** Current external context (CPython published source, read 18-Nov-2026;
  section 1): (a) letter case of `hexdigest()` (STUDY 18.2 item 4, handed over by WI-23): in branch 3.11 the
  OpenSSL-backed `hexdigest` of a hash and of an HMAC object return `_Py_strhex(...)` (`Modules/_hashopenssl.c` L566,
  L1748), which writes digits from `Py_hexdigits`, defined as "0123456789abcdef" (`Python/codecs.c` L18;
  `Python/pystrhex.c`); `Lib/hmac.py` uses the OpenSSL HMAC when the digest constructor is OpenSSL's (L15, L58, L66 to
  L67), and its `hexdigest` (L161 to L165) returns the `hexdigest()` of `_current()` (L139 to L149): the OpenSSL HMAC
  object on that path, otherwise a copy of the outer hash object; the non-OpenSSL hash modules were not read.
  INTERPRETATION: on such an interpreter the expected signature is lower-case hexadecimal, and a signature sent in
  upper case is refused with "HMAC signature mismatch" (401) by the exact comparison (P02-S020). (b) `int()` of an
  infinite float (STUDY 18.2 item 4, handed over by WI-23): in branch 3.11 `int(x)` without a base forwards to
  `PyNumber_Long` (`Objects/longobject.c`, `long_new_impl`, L5377 to L5378; added in the check), which converts a float
  through the `nb_int` slot (`Objects/abstract.c`, `PyNumber_Long`, L1533 to L1537), which for a float is `float___trunc___impl`
  (`Objects/floatobject.c` L923 to L927, L1876), returning `PyLong_FromDouble`, which raises `OverflowError` ("cannot
  convert float infinity to integer") for an infinite value and `ValueError` ("cannot convert float NaN to integer") for
  NaN (`Objects/longobject.c` L397 to L407). INTERPRETATION: with JSON's `Infinity` or `-Infinity` literal in `qty`
  (accepted by `json.loads`, P02-S048) the cast at L463 raises `OverflowError`, which L464 does not catch, so the
  request ends in the generic 500 (one more case of WI-13's 500; that ISSUE is WI-13's and is not counted again); with
  `NaN` in `qty` it raises `ValueError`, which is caught, and `qty` is set to `None` with a WARNING. (c) the ASCII-only
  rule of `compare_digest` holds in the 3.12, 3.13 and main sources as in 3.11, in both the OpenSSL-backed and the
  fallback implementation (WA-03). (d) (added in the check) the storage of a NaN or infinite `trigger_price`: on the
  published CPython 3.11 and SQLite sources read, a NaN is bound as a double and stored as NULL, an infinity as REAL
  (WA-07; A03's storage, interface fact only). Still UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED: the installed Python version (not pinned; the deployment documents name 3.11, HISTORICAL; WA-03), and
  the installed Flask, Werkzeug and waitress versions (WI-22); the non-OpenSSL hash path; the installed SQLite version
  and so how it stored a NaN or an infinite `trigger_price` (A03's; WA-07); the library points of WI-23 (Flask's
  automatic OPTIONS answer; what `get_data()` returns for form bodies; `wsgi.input_terminated`); which malformed HTTP
  inputs waitress refuses with 400 (P02-S074).
- **Evidence:** P02-S008, P02-S018, P02-S020, P02-S048, P02-S052, P02-S074; the CPython and SQLite sources
  (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, with points (a) to (d) settled as current external context
  for the published sources read only. No ISSUE follows from (a): no source states a letter case. (b) adds a case to
  WI-13 without a new record.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not applicable.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver side); A17 (installed packages and interpreter); A03 (SQLite storage).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.2 item 4 (the `hexdigest()` and `int()` points, from WI-23); STUDY 18.2 item 15 (with
  WA-07); WA-03; WI-13; WI-22; WI-23.
- **Rama:** not needed.

## 7. Part WD — Duplicate layers, limits, in-flight claims and queue admission

**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (`970aabf`)
**Scope:** STUDY section 5 (part P03): the three duplicate layers, the per-IP token bucket, the in-flight claims (the
claim mechanism itself; the unwired heartbeat and the sweeper are part WI's record WI-01, cited and not counted
again), the TTL cache, the fingerprint INSERT and its conflict handler (the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT P03-S034
weighed by the four-condition rule), back-pressure at 240 and 300 and `QUEUE_FULL`, missing or corrupt state, races,
restart, fail-open or fail-closed; the receiver's storage writes allocated here (`webhook_audit.ts`, the census read,
the EOD heartbeat). Direction questions 10 to 14 (and 26 to 28, which apply to every part). 19 allocated items
(this AUDIT, sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4).

- **External pages read for this part** (current external context, read 18-Nov-2026): Python Tutorial "Errors and
  Exceptions", page for 3.15.2 (https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/errors.html): "Handlers only handle exceptions that
  occur in the corresponding try clause, not in other handlers of the same `try` statement"; "If an unhandled exception
  occurs inside an except section, it will have the exception being handled attached to it". Python `queue`, page for
  3.15.2 (https://docs.python.org/3/library/queue.html): maxsize "less than or equal to zero, the queue size is
  infinite"; `qsize()` "Return the approximate size of the queue"; `put_nowait` "Equivalent to `put(item,
  block=False)`", which raises `Full` if no free slot "is immediately available"; `put` "Raises `ShutDown` if the queue
  has been shut down", `ShutDown` "Added in version 3.13". SQLite "Write-Ahead Logging", page last updated 2026-08-25
  (https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html): "readers do not block writers and a writer does not block readers", "This is
  mostly true. But there are some obscure cases where a query against a WAL-mode database can return SQLITE_BUSY"; the
  cases listed "include" another connection in exclusive locking mode, the last connection closing while it cleans up,
  and recovery after a crash. SQLite "Result and Error Codes", page last updated 2026-02-18
  (https://www.sqlite.org/rescode.html): SQLITE_IOERR "the operation could not finish because the operating system
  reported an I/O error"; SQLITE_BUSY "could not be written (or in some cases read) because of concurrent activity";
  SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE "a UNIQUE constraint failed". cachetools 7.1.1 tagged source
  (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tkem/cachetools/v7.1.1/src/cachetools/__init__.py): `TTLCache` "LRU Cache
  implementation with per-item time-to-live (TTL) value", default `timer=time.monotonic`, `__contains__` returns
  `self.timer() < link.expires` with "no reordering", `__setitem__` calls `expire()` and sets `link.expires = time +
  ttl`, `popitem` removes the least recently used unexpired item. CPython 3.12 published source
  `Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py` (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/3.12/Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py):
  `self._work_queue = queue.SimpleQueue()`, `submit` calls `self._work_queue.put(w)`. Other library statements (the
  CPython `_sqlite` result-code mapping, Python `json`, `codecs`, `datetime`, `time`, `sqlite3`, waitress and
  Werkzeug) are used through the STUDY statements that cite them, with their date of 17-Nov-2026.
