### 7.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### WD-01 — Claims kept after an exception inside the `IntegrityError` handler: the store-failure release does not cover the handler's own read and put (the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT P03-S034 weighed: condition (a) not established; ISSUE)

- **Area and flow:** in-flight control and duplicate layers at the receiver: the stored-fingerprint conflict handler
  of `_process_signal`, after both claims (the in-flight claim and the TTL-cache key) have been taken.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an exception that no handler on the path catches); Flow and state (signals
  lost: the rest of the batch is abandoned; while the claims last, the symbol is refused, which can defeat the
  recovery of a `QUEUE_FULL` signal and drops other scanners' signals for the symbol).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) M-1 comment, receiver L932 to L933 (`0d80fe9`, 2026-04-19): "From here every
  reject path MUST release; every accepted path lets signal_processor release at completion" (IS-62). (2)
  `_claim_in_flight` docstring L1078 to L1080 (`0d80fe9`/`a826dfe`, 2026-04-19 and 2026-05-12): "Caller MUST call
  _release_in_flight(symbol) on any reject path after a successful claim (DUPLICATE / QUEUE_FULL / IntegrityError)"
  (IS-62). (3) P3-s14 comment L1017 to L1043 (`a9a686e`, 2026-07-17): a store failure "is not a duplicate, so roll
  BOTH claims back"; claims left held "bounce this (symbol, scanner) as DUPLICATE for the whole dedup window, so the
  sender's retry is silently dropped"; returning instead of raising "keeps the rest of the batch alive: the caller's
  per-symbol loop has no try/except, so a raise here abandoned every symbol after this one" (IS-65). (4) P3-s14
  comment L59 to L66 (`a9a686e`): every retryable status "MUST have released both the in-flight claim and the
  fast-path dedup claim before returning" (IS-47). (5) P3S14-INV L60 to L63 (2026-07-17; IS-189, added in the
  cross-part check): "Every reject path that exists does release correctly — ... `:891` (IntegrityError) ... The gap is the path
  nobody enumerated: any exception that is not `IntegrityError` escapes `_process_signal` with both claims still held";
  L100: "a real defect on an unprotected path"; L147 to L162: the escaping exception "abandons the rest of the batch".
  CD-08 records (1) and (3) as statements that only the code contradicts. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L934 (claim), L940 to L947 (cache key stored), L963 to L980
  (INSERT in `transaction()`), L981 to L1015 (`except sqlite3.IntegrityError`: `fetch_one` at L987 to L991; re-queue
  `put_nowait` at L995 inside a `try` that catches only `queue.Full`, L994 to L1001), L1016 to L1044 (`except
  Exception`: both claims released, `STORE_ERROR`), L1049 (first-route `put_nowait`, catching only `queue.Full`,
  L1048 to L1050), L696 to L728 (per-symbol loop, no `try`), L510 to L534 (`_handle_webhook`: `except Exception` → 500;
  `finally` audit row); `core/state_store.py` L564 to L574 (`fetch_one`: no handler of its own; `execute` on the
  thread's connection), L524 to L541 (`transaction()` rolls back and re-raises), L107 to L113 (WAL, `busy_timeout`
  30000), L232 to L237 (`timeout=30`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `fetch_one` (L987) and the re-queue `put_nowait` (L995) run inside
  the `except sqlite3.IntegrityError` clause of the `try` at L963. The sibling `except Exception` clause (L1016), which
  releases both claims, belongs to the same `try` statement. `_process_signal` has no outer `try` or `finally` after the
  claim at L934, and `fetch_one` catches nothing (state_store L570 to L574). Current external context (Python Tutorial,
  quoted in section 1) and INTERPRETATION: an exception raised by L987, or an exception other than `queue.Full` raised
  by L995, is not handled by L1016; it leaves `_process_signal` with the in-flight claim and the cache key both still
  held. FACT (L696 to L728; L525 to L534): the loop has no `try`, so the later symbols of the batch are not processed,
  and `_handle_webhook` answers 500 and writes the audit row (500, 0, 0). INTERPRETATION (P03-S034; WI-01): the request
  that reaches this handler is itself a layer-3 conflict, either a duplicate of a stored signal or a request meeting a
  `QUEUE_FULL` row (a retry, or a new alert in the same slot, WD-04). After the exception the symbol gets `IN_PROCESS`
  from layer 1 until the sweeper evicts the claim (60 to about 120 s, logged CRITICAL as a stalled worker, which names
  the wrong cause, as P3S14-INV L141 to L145 itself noted for this kind of leak) and `DUPLICATE` from layer 2 until the
  window ends (300 s at the file value). What is lost is therefore: the later symbols of the batch, until a
  whole-request retry processes them; for a `QUEUE_FULL` row, its recovery while the claims last, since its retry is
  refused, and if the delay carries the retry past its age limit (600 s in the file) the row is never recovered
  (WD-05); and any other scanner's signal for the symbol, refused `IN_PROCESS` with a 200 while the in-flight claim
  lasts (WD-07). For a plain duplicate the symbol itself would have been refused anyway. The same holds for an exception other than
  `queue.Full` from the first route's `put_nowait` (L1049), which in addition leaves a `QUEUED` row with no queue item;
  `put` raises `ShutDown` after a queue shutdown (Python 3.13 and later), but no non-test code shuts the signal queue
  down (P03-S034's search), so no trigger for either `put_nowait` is shown at the baseline. The trigger is a failure
  of the read at L987. What can make it fail (current external context; INTERPRETATION): every store connection
  requests WAL mode (FACT, state_store L108, L241 to L242; the pragma's answer is not read). By SQLite's pages, WAL "is
  persistent" once set on a file (WAL page, section 3.3), and `PRAGMA journal_mode` returns "the original journal mode"
  when the mode "could not be changed" (pragma page); whether WAL was in effect on the production file is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED (section 7; A20's pre-flight mode check, `scripts/preflight/checks/database.py` L97 to L110, is A20's and
  is no proof that it ran). If WAL was in effect, readers and writers do not block one another except in "some obscure
  cases"; of the cases listed, exclusive locking mode is set by no legacy code (search), and the other two concern a
  connection closing or recovering; a lock wait is then a less likely trigger than the STUDY's example (an
  `OperationalError` after the 30 s busy wait) suggests. If WAL was not in effect, the STUDY's example stands. An
  operating-system I/O error remains in either mode (SQLITE_IOERR: "the operation could not finish because the
  operating system reported an I/O error"; SQLITE_FULL is described for "a write", so for this read the I/O half
  applies), and the CPython mapping (P03-S034; re-read 18-Nov-2026) turns BUSY, LOCKED, IOERR and FULL into
  `OperationalError` (SQLITE_CORRUPT into `DatabaseError`). P3S14-INV L79 to L80 rates a lock wait "Low" and "disk
  full / disk I/O error" "Low, but `busy_timeout` does not help — this is the most plausible real trigger", for the
  INSERT on the same connection (HISTORICAL; its L79 rests the WAL point on another module's pragma, `core/db_connect.py`
  L85, not on the store's).
- **Evidence:** P03-S034, P03-S006, P03-S010, P03-S030, P03-S032; P01-S033; P02-S070; WI-01 (eviction timing); code
  lines above; P3S14-INV L60 to L63, L79 to L80, L100, L141 to L162, L174 to L176; commit `a9a686e` (subject and body);
  the external pages of section 1 (the pragma page and the CPython mapping added in the check).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (code path); INTERPRETATION (language and library semantics; the
  effect). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given (ChatGPT's AUDIT review of 18-Nov-2026, finding M-01): condition (a)
  is not established. The `except Exception` clause L1016 to L1044 is a sibling handler of the `try` at L963; by the
  language semantics quoted in section 1 it does not catch an exception raised inside the `IntegrityError` handler, so
  it is not a release mechanism for the handler's own read (L987) or put (L995). Whether L1016 was meant as the
  mechanism for any store failure after the claims (the commit subject, "release both claims on a store failure", and
  the comment L1024 to L1025) or for failures of the INSERT only (the comment's first line, L1017, and the commit body)
  is not settled by the sources; on the narrower reading no mechanism exists for this route, as in WD-02. The other
  conditions, as tested: (b) an exception raised at L987 (or a non-`Full` exception at L995) leaves both claims held
  every time it occurs, since no enclosing handler in `_process_signal` releases them; (c) the code says every reject
  path and every store failure must release (sources (1) to (3)), and a database read failure can arise under the
  documented set-up (the reading the A03 AUDIT applied to a write failure through the same store, A03 P-01,
  condition (c)); (d) the outcome contradicts every intent source found (sources (1) to (5); CD-08). The ISSUE rests on
  these code facts and intent sources: on this route both claims stay held, the rest of the batch is abandoned and a
  retry is refused for the window, against the stated release rule. INTERPRETATION: the trigger is a database fault
  that the legacy's own investigation rated rare (source (5), L79 to L80); rarity bears on impact, not on the result.
  Refinement of the STUDY (Appendix A): for this handler-internal route the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT P03-S034
  is not confirmed; the AUDIT classifies the route as ISSUE, condition (a) not established; P03-S034 itself is not
  changed. Also against the STUDY: its lock-wait example is joined by an I/O error, the more likely trigger if WAL was
  in effect (see above); P3S14-INV, not cited by the STUDY, lists the `IntegrityError` path among the reject paths that
  "release correctly" (L60 to L61) and directs an `except Exception` "beside the existing `except
  sqlite3.IntegrityError`" (L174 to L176), which commit `a9a686e` added (L1016; 2026-07-17); INTERPRETATION: the
  direction and the fix address failures of the INSERT and do not consider the handler's own read.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established. Bounded per occurrence, by the code, to the claims of
  one symbol for at most the window (300 s at the file value), the later symbols of that request until a retry, and
  the recovery of at most one `QUEUE_FULL` signal.
  Whether the trigger ever occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; P3S14-INV's count of no 500 answers in five weeks of
  audited POSTs (L17 to L18, L90 to L98) is a HISTORICAL runtime claim, not evidence admitted here.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); A03 for the store's behaviour on failure (boundary only).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 31 (first part); STUDY question none (intent is stated); CD-08; IS-47, IS-62, IS-65;
  WI-01 (the sweeper's eviction bounds the kept claim; counted there); WD-02 (the second part of point 31); WD-19 (the
  release on `QUEUE_FULL` and `STORE_ERROR`, MATCH).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (the code's own comments state the intent).

##### WD-02 — Claims kept after an exception between the claim and the INSERT's `try`; one source shown (a symbol carrying an unpaired surrogate)

- **Area and flow:** in-flight control and duplicate layers: `_process_signal` L934 to L962, after the claim and before
  the `try` of L963.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an exception path with no release); Weakness (missing validation of the
  symbol's characters).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** as WD-01, sources (1) to (3): every reject path after the claim releases both
  claims; a raise abandons the rest of the batch (IS-62, IS-65; CD-08). No source addresses an exception raised while
  computing the cache key, the fingerprint or the identifiers.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L934 to L962: the cache test and store (L940 to L947), the slot
  and fingerprint (L952 to L954, `fp_raw.encode()` with the default codec and error handling), `new_signal_id()`
  (L957; `core/ids.py` L50 to L52: `"sig_" + uuid.uuid4().hex`), the time stamps (L958 to L961); L587 (`json.loads`
  of the body); L670 (split and strip only); L905 (`if not symbol`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: these lines run outside any handler of `_process_signal`; an
  exception raised there leaves the function with the claim, and after L947 also the cache key, still held, with the
  effects of WD-01 (500, the later symbols not processed, a retry refused while the claims last). FACT: no symbol
  check tests the characters (L670, L701, L710, L905). Current external context (Python `json` and `codecs`, read
  17-Nov-2026, through P03-S070) and INTERPRETATION: `json.loads` (L587) accepts a JSON escape of an unpaired surrogate,
  and the UTF-8 encode at L954 then raises `UnicodeEncodeError`; this is the one source shown. `new_signal_id` and the
  time stamps show no raising path (code read). The claims kept are those of that unusual symbol only; that signal is
  new (no row yet), and a retry of the same body raises again at L954, so it is never accepted.
- **Evidence:** P03-S070; P02-S061; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given:
  (a) **fails**: the code implements no mechanism for an exception in these lines (the comment of L932 to L933 states a
  requirement; no handler covers L934 to L962), and a requirement the code does not implement is an ISSUE under the A01
  AUDIT's rule; (b) not reached; (c) **fails**: the one trigger shown needs an input for which no validation step
  exists (no check of the symbol's characters), which the rule excludes; (d) holds (no source says the claims are kept).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that the trigger shown needs a body with an escaped
  surrogate in `stocks`, which no source shows Chartink sending (P03-S070), and it holds only that symbol's claims;
  latent: no occurrence is shown (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED).
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 31 (second part); CD-08; WD-01; part WA (symbol validation).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-03 — Every `IntegrityError` from the INSERT is read as a fingerprint conflict; when no row carries the fingerprint the answer is still `DUPLICATE`

- **Area and flow:** layer 3, the stored fingerprint: the conflict handler's reading of the exception.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a silent fallback); Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** M-S2 comment L982 (`735a6c3`, 2026-07-14): "A row with this (fingerprint,
  fingerprint_date) already exists." (IS-64); P3-s14 comment L1017 to L1018 (`a9a686e`, 2026-07-17): the `except
  Exception` route is for an INSERT that failed "for a reason that is NOT a constraint violation" (IS-65). No source
  addresses an `IntegrityError` from another constraint, or a conflict with no matching row.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L981, L987 to L992, L1014 to L1015; `core/schema.sql` L42 to L83.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: every `sqlite3.IntegrityError` from the INSERT enters the handler;
  if the read finds no row with the fingerprint and date (`existing is None`), the claim is released and the answer is
  `DUPLICATE` (L1014 to L1015), the cache key kept; no line is logged. FACT (schema re-read): the INSERT supplies every
  NOT NULL column, the status `'QUEUED'` (in the CHECK list, L60 to L65), a non-empty symbol (L905), a fresh UUID4
  primary key (`core/ids.py` L50 to L52) and no `trade_id` (the only foreign key, L72, L78), and no trigger is defined
  on `signals` (search `CREATE TRIGGER|RAISE\(` in non-test files: the one trigger is on `trades`, schema L291 to
  L298; added in the check), so at the baseline the unique index (L82 to L83) is the only constraint the INSERT can
  violate. INTERPRETATION: under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (state_store L531) the conflicting row is committed, and the read at
  L987 starts after the failed transaction; the only non-test deletion of `signals` rows prunes earlier dates
  (`fingerprint_date < ?`, `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` L234 to L236, L264 to L266; added in the check), so `existing is
  None` is not shown to arise at the baseline either. Current external context: SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_UNIQUE
  is one extended code of SQLITE_CONSTRAINT, which the CPython mapping turns into `IntegrityError` with every other
  constraint failure (P03-S010; rescode page, section 1).
- **Evidence:** P03-S010, P03-S011; code and schema lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION. The comment's assumption holds at the
  baseline (MATCH part); the handler would answer a non-duplicate `DUPLICATE`, silently and with a 200, if another
  constraint ever failed or no row were found (ISSUE part, a weakness the code demonstrates). KNOWN DEFECT not
  considered: no source says such a case is handled, and the case cannot arise at the baseline.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor and latent: the basis is the schema re-read above (no other
  constraint can fail at the baseline).
- **Owner:** A06 (handler); A03 (schema).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 30; IS-64, IS-65.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-04 — The re-queue route treats every request that meets a `QUEUE_FULL` row as a retry: a new alert in the same slot revives the earlier signal with the new price and trigger time (A03 R-08's A06 side extended)

- **Area and flow:** layer 3 and queue admission: the M-S2 re-queue route inside the conflict handler.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a source's premise the code cannot check); Flow and state (the queued
  item and the stored row differ; a status write ordered after the push).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** M-S2 comment L983 to L986 (`735a6c3`, 2026-07-14): if the existing row is
  `QUEUE_FULL`, "THIS request is a legitimate 503 retry — flip that row back to QUEUED and re-queue it, reusing its
  signal_id (audit row preserved)"; commit `735a6c3` (IS-64). The test `tests/integration/test_hardening_scenarios.py`
  L236 to L262 states the same expectation for a retry "reusing its signal_id" (P03-S018; TEST, never run). CD-09
  records the "legitimate 503 retry" premise as a statement only the code contradicts. Whether Chartink retries at all
  is not stated by any source on Chartink's side (IC-06). No source considers a new alert in the same slot. All
  HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L987 to L1013 (read; `requeue = (existing["signal_id"],
  scanner_name, symbol, price, triggered_at)` with the current request's `price` and `triggered_at`; `put_nowait` at
  L995; unconditional `UPDATE signals SET status = 'QUEUED' WHERE signal_id = ?` at L1002 to L1007 in its own
  transaction, a failure logged at ERROR; `ACCEPTED` with the existing id); L952 (slot from `triggered_at`); L947 (the
  current request's cache key stays stored).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the route runs for any request whose fingerprint (scanner, symbol,
  slot of `triggered_at`) and receipt date match a `QUEUE_FULL` row; nothing compares the request with the stored row
  (`trigger_price`, `triggered_at`, `webhook_payload`). INTERPRETATION: a genuine retry (same body) carries the same
  price and trigger time, so for it the route does what M-S2 states; a different alert of the same scanner and symbol
  whose trigger falls in the same five-minute slot on the same day takes the same route, and the queue item then
  carries the new alert's price and trigger time under the earlier `signal_id`, while the row keeps the first
  request's `trigger_price`, `triggered_at`, `received_at`, `expires_at` and `webhook_payload` (only `status` is
  updated). The age check (L919 to L926) uses the current request's `triggered_at`, so such an alert can revive a row
  whose own trigger is older. FACT (A03 R-08, unchanged): the item is pushed before the flip, the reverse of the
  comment's order, and the flip has no status condition, so a worker's earlier status write can be overwritten
  (P04-S064). The current request's cache key stays, so the retry starts a new layer-2 window (P03-S006).
- **Evidence:** P03-S006, P03-S012, P03-S015 (R1), P03-S018; P04-S064; P09-S056 (A03 R-08 as recorded); code lines
  above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH part: for a genuine retry the route
  re-queues the existing id as M-S2 states. ISSUE part (A06's own, beyond R-08): the route cannot tell a retry from a
  new alert in the same slot, and for the latter it queues values that differ from the row it revives. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered and not given: (a) holds for retries; (b) fails: nothing fails by language semantics, the route does what
  it is coded to do for any matching request. A03 R-08 ("ISSUE; FACT; INTERPRETATION"; "A03 treatment group TG2
  (AGREED, 11-Nov-2026)"; "AGREED"; owners "A06; A03 (the unconditional flip)") keeps its record for the push-before-flip
  order and is not counted again here.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: the route needs a `QUEUE_FULL` row, so a depth at 300
  (WD-10), and then a second request in the same slot below the threshold; whether either occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); A03 (the flip, R-08).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 29; STUDY question 15 (whether a new alert was meant to revive the earlier signal:
  OPEN QUESTION narrowed, no source considers it); CD-09, IC-06, IS-64; A03 R-08; WD-05; part PQ (the processor's use
  of the item's fields).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 15 bears on the intended handling of a new alert (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### WD-05 — `QUEUE_FULL` recovery rests on a sender's retry, as stated; a failed `QUEUE_FULL` mark and a restart leave `QUEUED` rows with no queue item, whose retries are refused and which nothing re-reads

- **Area and flow:** queue admission and recovery: the per-symbol put, the `QUEUE_FULL` mark, the retry route, restart.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no safe recovery on restart; errors only logged); Flow and state (signals
  lost; a status not updated).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) M19 WR9, L95 to L96 (2026-04-17): "If queue.put_nowait fails (Full): mark
  signal status "QUEUE_FULL" in DB, return in rejected list" (IS-67). (2) Receiver L1051 (`735a6c3`, 2026-07-14):
  "Mark QUEUE_FULL in DB so the signal is not silently lost (audit row kept)" (IS-64). (3) HIGH #6 fix direction
  (`mega_memory_and_fix_plan.txt` L297 to L301, 2026-04-17): "Return 503 ... when queue.Full. Client can retry."
  (IS-170); P3-s14 comments L59 to L66 and L730 to L732 (`a9a686e`, 2026-07-17): the 503 makes "the sender
  (Chartink)" retry (IS-47); M-S2 (IS-64): the retry is re-queued. (4) P16, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L362
  (2026-04-17): "Every signal_id assigned at webhook gets a final status before the system 'forgets' it", the list
  including `DROPPED_QUEUE_FULL` (IS-98). STUDY question 16 is ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date: recovery was meant to
  depend on the sender's retry; no source describes recovery by the system; whether Chartink retries is not
  established (IC-06). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1046 to L1068 (put; on `queue.Full` the UPDATE to
  `QUEUE_FULL` in its own transaction, a failure logged at ERROR at L1058 to L1059; cache key removed; claim
  released); L987 to L1015 (a retry meets the row: `QUEUE_FULL` → re-queue; any other status → `DUPLICATE`); L919 to
  L926 (age check before any claim); L730 to L734 (batch 503); `main.py` L3402 to L3404 (the queue in memory).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the code marks the row, answers the batch 503, and recovers the
  signal only through a request that meets the `QUEUE_FULL` row below the threshold, within `expiry_sec` (600 in the
  file) of its trigger time and on the same receipt date (P03-S015 R1, R6; P03-S040). FACT (search, P03-S040,
  P04-S020): no non-test code reads `QUEUED` or `QUEUE_FULL` rows back into a queue, at start-up or later. FACT
  (L1058 to L1059): if the `QUEUE_FULL` UPDATE fails, the row stays `QUEUED` with no queue item. INTERPRETATION: a
  retry then meets a `QUEUED` row and is answered `DUPLICATE` with a 200, so the signal is neither queued nor
  recoverable (P03-S039; P05-S005). INTERPRETATION (P03-S015 R7; P04-S020): at a restart the queue is lost with the
  process while the rows of its items stay `QUEUED`; a retry of such a signal is refused `DUPLICATE`; nothing closes
  these rows (the end-of-day cleanup touches `PROCESSING` rows only, P04-S020). A `QUEUE_FULL` row never retried, or
  retried after its age limit, stays `QUEUE_FULL`, a visible status in the stored row.
- **Evidence:** P03-S015, P03-S039, P03-S040; P04-S020; P05-S005; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH part: marking the row, answering
  503 and relying on the sender's retry are as sources (1) and (3) state; a `QUEUE_FULL` row keeps a visible status
  (source (4)). ISSUE part: after a failed mark, and for items lost at a restart, the row says `QUEUED` for a signal
  that is in no queue; the retry the design relies on is then refused as a duplicate, and nothing re-reads or closes
  the row, against source (2) ("not silently lost": the failed mark is logged, but the signal can no longer be
  recovered) and source (4) (no final status is ever written for these ids). UNKNOWN part: whether the premise of the
  design held, that is whether Chartink retries after a 503 (IC-06; WD-16). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given:
  (a) holds for the mark (a handler logs its failure); (b) fails: the handler works as coded; the gap is the absence of
  any recovery step, a requirement the code does not implement.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: each route needs a full queue, a failed UPDATE or a
  restart with items queued; whether any occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); part PQ (no boot recovery in the processor); part EV and XA (rows never closed, STUDY
  question 37).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 32; STUDY questions 16 (answered by evidence at its date) and 37 (part EV's); IC-06;
  IS-47, IS-64, IS-67, IS-98, IS-170; WD-04; WD-16; WA-09 (whether Chartink retries: counted there, not here); EV-08 (the
  rows of other statuses that nothing closes).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 37 (part EV) bears on the intended closing of such rows.

##### WD-06 — The duplicate window: FIX-131's five-minute setting replaced the minute rule, but the two layers start their windows differently, the minute texts remain in five places, and FIX-131's "59s apart ... share same epoch" holds only while the cache holds the key

- **Area and flow:** duplicate layers 2 (TTL cache) and 3 (stored fingerprint): window width, start and restart.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency
  across components (two layers with one width and different starts).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** IC-04. Minute precision (2026-04-17 unless stated): P6, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml`
  L288 ("same scanner_name AND same trigger_minute (1-minute resolution)"); M19 WR7 L67 to L76 and the receiver
  docstring L18 ("MINUTE precision"; "SHA-256 fingerprint dedup at minute precision"); the schema comments L74 and L81
  ("hash(scanner+symbol+trigger_minute)"; "same minute on same day = duplicate"); the `DedupViolation` docstring; and,
  later, `docs/01_system_architecture.md` L62 to L68 (lines 2026-06-03: "SHA-256 fingerprint at minute precision")
  (IS-53 to IS-56, IS-60). Five minutes: FIX-036 comment L229 to L231 and L937 to L939 (`5dc8563`, 2026-05-14: a TTL
  cache "(replaces minute-string fingerprint)", "seen within last 300 seconds"); FIX-131 comment L949 to L951 and commit
  `8e884db` (2026-05-30: one setting "configures both TTLCache TTL and fingerprint bucket", "duplicates 59s apart across
  minute boundary now share same epoch → DB-level dedup"); SYSCFG L337 ("5-min fingerprint bucket (was 60s/1-min)"); CONFIG-GUIDE L359
  (2026-06-19: "identical signals within 5 min are de-duplicated") (IS-57 to IS-59). P3-s14 comment L1025 to L1028
  (`a9a686e`, 2026-07-17): "Double-entry stays impossible without the cache ... UNIQUE(fingerprint, fingerprint_date)
  is the authoritative within-window dedup (a retry inside the same bucket recomputes the same fingerprint)" (IS-65);
  P3S14-INV L185 to L196: "The cache is a fast-path optimisation, not the control". No source states how the two
  windows should start (intent-sources file, section 6.1 point 27). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L232 to L238 (window `max(60, int(dedup_window_seconds))`, 300
  if absent or unconvertible; `TTLCache(maxsize=10000, ttl=<window>)`), L940 to L947 (layer 2 key `(symbol,
  scanner_name)` stored before the INSERT), L952 to L954 (layer 3 slot `int(triggered_at.timestamp() // window)`),
  L977 (`fingerprint_date = today_iso`, the IST receipt date), L1065 (key removed on `QUEUE_FULL`), L1029 to L1030
  (on `STORE_ERROR`); `core/schema.sql` L74 to L83; `config/system_config.yaml` L337 (300).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: one setting (300 in the file) is the cache's TTL and the slot width,
  as FIX-131 states (MATCH part); the floor of 60 is A02 K-12's A06 side (P03-S003; not counted here). Current
  external context (cachetools 7.1.1 source, section 1) and INTERPRETATION (P03-S005, P03-S009, P03-S013): layer 2's
  window runs for 300 s from the moment the key is stored, on the monotonic receipt clock, and does not slide with
  repeated hits; layer 3's window is a fixed slot of the trigger time on a grid whose edges fall at IST times ending in
  :00 and :05; the two share a width but not a start. Two alerts of one scanner and symbol whose triggers sit on either
  side of a slot edge (for example 09:19 and 09:20) get different fingerprints: layer 3 accepts the second, and only
  layer 2 refuses it (layer 1 also refuses it, with `IN_PROCESS`, while the first is still claimed; added in the
  check), so FIX-131's "59s apart ... share same epoch → DB-level dedup" holds for layer 3 only when the minute
  boundary is not a slot edge (one minute boundary in five is one), and P3-s14's "authoritative within-window dedup" holds for a retry (same body, same slot) but not
  for such a pair. Layer 2 is empty after a restart (P03-S016, P03-S035) and loses the key after `QUEUE_FULL` (L1065),
  so in those cases the second alert of such a pair is accepted as a new signal (after `STORE_ERROR` the first alert
  has no row, so no second row arises). The cache also evicts its least recently stored live key early above 10,000
  live keys (cachetools `popitem`), letting a duplicate of that pair pass layer 2 (P03-S005 (f)). A key stored by an
  alert that layer 3 then refuses starts a new 300 s window (P03-S006). FACT: the minute texts (P6, WR7 in M19 and the
  docstring, the schema comments, the `DedupViolation` docstring, the architecture document) and FIX-036's "replaces"
  remain; FIX-131 kept the fingerprint (IC-04; CD-11 withdrawn into IC-04).
- **Evidence:** P03-S001 to P03-S009, P03-S013, P03-S016, P03-S017, P03-S035; code lines above; the cachetools source
  (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH part: the five-minute width from one
  setting for both layers is what FIX-131, the configuration comment and the guide state; FIX-131 is later than the
  minute sources and names the rule it widens ("(was 60s/1-min)"), so the width conflict of IC-04 is resolved by a
  later source that replaces the earlier one. ISSUE part: (i) the minute texts, among them the architecture document
  dated after FIX-131, and FIX-036's "replaces" no longer describe the code (documented behaviour the code does not
  implement; minor); (ii) FIX-131's stated effect and P3-s14's "authoritative" claim hold only while layer 2 holds the
  key: a slot-edge pair is accepted after a restart or a `QUEUE_FULL` removal (intended against actual, INTERPRETATION).
  KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no mechanism fails by language semantics; whether a slot-edge pair is a duplicate
  depends on the window meant, which no source settles (STUDY question 14).
- **Severity or impact where established:** part (i) minor (texts only; basis: no code reads them); part (ii) not
  established: whether a restart or a `QUEUE_FULL` met such a pair is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver); A03 (schema comments); A02 (the window key, K-12); the document owners for the
  architecture document and the guide.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 27; STUDY question 14 (OPEN QUESTION (conflict) narrowed); IC-04; IS-53 to IS-60,
  IS-65; A02 K-12; WD-20 (the fingerprint layer, MATCH); part XA (documents against code); WI-10 (symbol case, which
  also splits the keys, P03-S002).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 14 bears on which window start was meant (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### WD-07 — Layer 1, keyed on the symbol alone, refuses another scanner's signal with `IN_PROCESS`, no stored row and a 200 answer; the sources of 17-Apr-2026 conflict on this case

- **Area and flow:** duplicate layer 1 (the in-flight claim) for a second scanner's signal on a symbol already
  claimed.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a signal lost without a record); Consistency across components (conflicting
  sources resolved one way).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** IC-08 and IC-29 (all 2026-04-17). For acceptance: P6 L288 "Different scanner OR
  different minute = new signal, allowed through screening" (IS-53); the M19 test list L219 "Different scanner, same
  symbol same minute -> ACCEPTED" (IS-54). For refusal: M19 WR17 L164 to L170 "If symbol already in _in_flight: reject
  with status "IN_PROCESS"", "to prevent same-symbol concurrent processing", and the test line L220 (IS-61). On the
  record: the schema comment L36 to L37 "Immutable log of EVERY incoming webhook signal. One row per (symbol, scanner)
  pair extracted from a webhook" (IS-102) against WR9's row "For each accepted" signal (IS-97) (IC-29); P16 L362 lists
  `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT` among the final statuses, "Every status writes a row to signals table with timestamp and
  reason", rationale "many signals vanish silently" (IS-98). Neither side of IC-08 is later. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L934 to L935 (`IN_PROCESS` before the cache, the fingerprint
  and the INSERT); L66 (`IN_PROCESS` not retryable); L725 to L734 (counted rejected; batch 200 unless another symbol
  is `QUEUE_FULL` or `STORE_ERROR`); L1084 (the claim key is the symbol).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: while a symbol is claimed, every signal for it from any scanner is
  answered `IN_PROCESS`; no `signals` row and no status are written; the request answers 200 if no other symbol is
  retryable, and the audit row counts the symbol as rejected (for a 200 answer). INTERPRETATION (P03-S014, P03-S032;
  WI-01): the claim lasts from receipt until the processor's release or the sweeper's eviction, at most about 60 to
  120 s with the heartbeat unwired; a second scanner's signal in that time is dropped with no stored row, and the 200
  invites no retry; after the claim ends, a second scanner's signal is accepted (layers 2 and 3 include the scanner).
  INTERPRETATION (added in the check; L934 to L1015; P03-S030, P03-S051): the claim is also held while the receiver
  itself checks the cache, runs the INSERT (whose `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` can wait on another writer, up to the 30 s busy
  timeout) and reads a conflicting row, so a signal that ends as `DUPLICATE` can, during those steps, cause another
  scanner's signal for the same symbol to be refused `IN_PROCESS`.
  The code thus follows WR17 and leaves P6, the WR7 test line and P16's `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT` row unimplemented for this
  case. INTERPRETATION (added in the check): P16 speaks of "Every signal_id assigned at webhook", and the
  `IN_PROCESS` refusal comes before any id is assigned (`new_signal_id()` at L957, after the claim at L934), yet P16
  lists `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT` among the final statuses; so P16 expected an in-flight drop to carry an id and a row,
  which the code's order of steps does not provide.
- **Evidence:** P03-S001, P03-S014, P03-S032, P03-S060; P02-S069; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A conflict between sources of the same date
  that the code resolves one way (WR17's refusal), with the record sources (schema comment, P16) not implemented for the
  refused signal. INTERPRETATION (as the intent-sources file notes for IC-08): P6 and the WR7 test line do not say
  whether the first signal is still in flight, so the conflict exists only while it is. KNOWN DEFECT not considered:
  sources conflict, so condition (d) cannot hold.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: how often two scanners named the same symbol within a
  claim's life is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 28; STUDY question 13 (OPEN QUESTION (conflict) narrowed); IC-08, IC-29 (CD-19
  withdrawn); IS-53, IS-54, IS-61, IS-97, IS-98, IS-102; WD-08; WI-01; part XA (row coverage).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 13 asks which handling was meant (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### WD-08 — The in-flight claim records no signal: any release removes whatever claim the symbol holds, so after an eviction a late release can free a newer signal's claim (WR17 tracks "(signal_id, symbol)")

- **Area and flow:** in-flight control: the claim's identity and its release by the receiver, the processor and the
  sweeper.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated pair not kept); Flow and state (the same symbol processed
  concurrently).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) M19 WR17 L165 to L167 (2026-04-17): "track (signal_id, symbol) in in-memory
  _in_flight set (thread-safe). signal_processor removes the entry when done" (IS-61). (2) FIX-011 sweeper docstring
  L1183 to L1187 (`a826dfe`, 2026-05-12): "A lock with no heartbeat for 60s IS evicted (stalled worker)" (IS-11): the
  design evicts claims whose worker may still be alive. (3) FIX-069 comment `signals/signal_processor.py` L1260 to
  L1262 (lines 2026-05-16, `397105d`): for a re-queued signal the "lock must travel with signal to prevent duplicate
  admission while signal is pending retry"; V3 03.05 comment L1263 to L1265 (lines 2026-07-12, `536172c`): "the
  allocator worker owns the symbol claim until it admits/rejects (A8)". No source addresses a late release removing
  another signal's claim (intent-sources file, section 6.1 point 1). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L206 (dict from symbol to `acquired_at` and `heartbeat_at`; no
  signal id), L1083 to L1091 (claim), L1093 to L1096 and L1102 to L1108 (`_release_in_flight` and `release_in_flight`:
  `pop(symbol, None)`), L1192 to L1198 (sweeper deletes); `signals/signal_processor.py` L1267 to L1271, L1699 to L1703,
  L1733 to L1737 (releases by symbol; skipped while re-queued or handed off).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the claim holds one entry per symbol and no signal identity; every
  release removes the symbol's entry whoever took it. INTERPRETATION (P03-S033): if the sweeper evicts signal A's claim
  and signal B for the same symbol then claims it, A's later release (for example in the processor's `finally`, or at
  the end of a re-queued or handed-off signal that source (3) means to keep the claim) removes B's claim while B is
  still processed, and a third signal C can pass layer 1 (layers 2 and 3 stop C only for the same scanner and window).
  With a `(signal_id, symbol)` entry as in source (1), a release could name its own claim; the code's cannot. This
  holds whether or not the heartbeat is wired: source (2) evicts a stalled worker that may later resume and release.
  With the heartbeat unwired, every claim older than about 60 to 120 s is evicted (WI-01), which makes the
  interleaving reachable by any long-held claim.
- **Evidence:** P03-S029, P03-S031 to P03-S033, P03-S061; P04-S057; WI-01; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The stated `(signal_id, symbol)`
  pair is not kept, and the code demonstrates a race in which one signal's release frees another's claim. WI-01 counts
  the unwired heartbeat and the age-only eviction; this record counts only the claim's lack of identity. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered and not given: (a) fails (no mechanism guards a release against removing another signal's claim).
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent: the basis is that the race needs an eviction followed by a late
  release while a newer claim is held; whether it occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S062).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver and processor).
- **Cross-reference:** related to 18.1 point 1 (allocated to part WI and disposed there by WI-01; this record covers
  only the claim's identity named in that point's last clause); P03 section 9, sixth point; IS-11, IS-61; WI-01,
  WI-11; WD-07; part PQ (which processor routes keep the claim).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-09 — The per-IP limiter works as C-2 and AB-910 state, but its map has no fixed cap, against "so the map can't grow unbounded"

- **Area and flow:** throttling at the receiver: `_PerIpRateLimiter` on `/webhook` and `/health`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a docstring claim the code does not meet); Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** C-2 class docstring L70 to L77 and comment L103 to L105 (`d922007`, 2026-07-02):
  "Bounds request flooding from a single IP while tolerating Chartink's legitimate open-bell burst (~40 signals from
  one IP)"; "one token per request; empty -> deny"; "Idle, fully-refilled buckets are evicted lazily so the map can't
  grow unbounded under a spoofed-IP flood"; evict "on IDLE TIME ... A bucket untouched for >60s is idle" (IS-71;
  CD-10). SYSCFG L338 to L342 and CFGLOADER L623 to L628 (2026-07-02): on, 60, 5.0, "Keyed on request.remote_addr; 429
  on exceed" (IS-71, IS-133). AB-910 comments L366 to L378 (`6e2a826`, `84cee3e`, 2026-07-17): `/health` behind "the
  same rate limiter", "Limit BEFORE auth, exactly as /webhook does" (IS-16). STUDY question 17 is ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE
  at its date (one token per request; `/health` in the same limiter). No source on a refill of 0. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L79 to L108 (bucket, `allow`, `_evict_idle`), L241 to L247
  (built when enabled; `or 60`, `or 5.0`; `max_ips` default 8192), L376 to L380 (`/health`), L479, L495 to L504
  (`/webhook`: audit row 429, WARNING, 429).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (MATCH part): a token bucket per source address under one lock; one
  token per HTTP request whatever its symbols; `/health` and `/webhook` share the bucket and test it before
  authentication; file values on, 60 and 5.0; at the limit `/webhook` writes an audit row (429, 0, 0), logs a WARNING
  and answers 429, while `/health` answers 429 with no audit row or log (P03-S020 to P03-S023, P03-S026).
  INTERPRETATION (P03-S022): a burst of 40 requests fits within 60 tokens. FACT (ISSUE part): eviction runs only when
  an unknown address arrives with at least 8,192 entries held, removes only buckets idle for more than 60 s, and the
  new address is added whether or not anything was removed (L91 to L93, L106). INTERPRETATION: the map is bounded only
  by the number of addresses seen in the last 60 s (with 8,192 as the point where eviction starts), not by a fixed
  cap, and once it is full every new address runs a full scan of the map under the limiter's lock (L106); "can't grow
  unbounded" holds only for a flood whose rate of new addresses is bounded. INTERPRETATION: eviction after 60 s idle
  equals "fully-refilled" only while burst divided by refill is at most 60 s (12 s at the file values); a burst-only
  limit cannot be configured because a refill of 0.0 becomes 5.0 (A02 K-12's A06 side, P03-S021; not counted here).
  Missing state counts as a full bucket, and a restart gives every address a full bucket (P03-S027); the code states
  no fail-open or fail-closed rule. The key is the connection's peer address with no forwarded header read, so which
  address production saw depends on the network path (WI-09; P03-S025).
- **Evidence:** P03-S019 to P03-S028; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH parts: the algorithm, the
  shared bucket and the one-token-per-request rule are as C-2 and AB-910 state. ISSUE part: the docstring's bound is
  not a fixed cap (CD-10). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (an eviction mechanism exists); (b) fails:
  eviction does what it is coded to do and removes idle buckets whenever the map is full; the absence of a cap is a
  requirement not implemented.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that growth past 8,192 entries needs more than 8,192
  distinct source addresses within 60 s, which nothing shows occurred.
- **Owner:** A06; A02 (the refill fallback, K-12).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 33; STUDY question 17 (answered by evidence at its date); CD-10; IS-16, IS-71,
  IS-133; A02 K-12; WI-09 (network path); 18.1 point 97 (part WI: the A19 load test's single-address flood meets this
  limiter first).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-10 — The 503 threshold fires at 240 as P15 states, but it measures only the dispatcher's hand-off buffer: work waiting for the five workers sits in an unbounded executor queue that back-pressure never sees

- **Area and flow:** queue admission: the request-level threshold, the per-symbol puts, and what the depth measures.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Flow and state (signals admitted while a backlog grows unseen);
  Consistency across components (one boundary stated two ways).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** P15, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L352 to L355 (2026-04-17): "Default queue
  capacity raised to 300. Backpressure (HTTP 503) still fires at 80% capacity = 240", rationale "Bigger buffer before
  backpressure"; SYSCFG L125 to L126 (2026-04-17): "Max signals in queue before HTTP 503 (P15)", "503 threshold =
  capacity * backpressure_pct (P15)" (IS-66). M19 WR6 L59 to L61 (2026-04-17): "When queue size > capacity *
  backpressure_pct (e.g., 80%), respond 503 immediately"; M19 test line L215: "Queue at backpressure threshold -> 503"
  (IS-67). M20 SP2 (2026-04-17): "queue draining never blocks on entry_gate sleeps or screening I/O"; the dispatcher
  "NEVER processes signals itself. Pure handoff." (IS-73). No source separates the dispatcher's buffer from the
  executor's queue (intent-sources file, section 6.1 point 34). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L570 to L578 (`qsize() >= int(capacity * backpressure_pct)` →
  503 with `X-Queue-Depth`, before the window and the parse), L1049 (per-symbol `put_nowait`); `main.py` L3402 to
  L3404 (`queue.Queue(maxsize=capacity)`); `signals/signal_processor.py` L304 to L320 (executor of `worker_count`
  threads), L377 to L390 (dispatcher: `get`, `_warm_zones`, `submit`; part PQ's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (MATCH part): at the file values (300, 0.80) the receiver answers 503
  when the depth is 240 or more, before parsing, as P15 and the M19 test line state. FACT: WR6 says "> capacity *
  backpressure_pct"; the code uses `>=`, so the two differ by one signal at exactly 240 (P15's "fires at ... 240" and
  the test line fit the code). Current external context (Python `queue`, CPython 3.12 executor source; section 1) and
  INTERPRETATION (P03-S038, P03-S044, P04-S014, P04-S050): the check and the later puts are separate steps under no
  common lock, with up to eight request threads, and `qsize()` is approximate, so the depth can pass 240 and reach the
  hard limit of 300, where per-symbol `QUEUE_FULL` takes over (WD-05). The dispatcher moves each item at once into the
  executor's `SimpleQueue`, which is unbounded, whether or not a worker is free; so `signal_queue` stays near empty
  while a backlog for the five workers builds where no threshold looks. The 240 and 300 limits engage only when the
  dispatcher itself lags or has stopped (for example a slow `_warm_zones` call; 18.2 item 11, WD-16), and the backlog's
  only limit is the age check when a worker takes the signal (part PQ, P04-S062). The 503 text "Signal queue at
  capacity" and the comment "Max signals in queue before HTTP 503" therefore describe the hand-off buffer, not the
  signals waiting to be processed.
- **Evidence:** P03-S036 to P03-S038, P03-S044, P03-S047; P04-S014, P04-S050, P04-S062; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH part: the refusal point (240) and its
  place before the parse. ISSUE part: the limit that P15 calls a "buffer before backpressure" does not bound the work
  waiting for workers, because SP2's executor (same date) holds that work in a queue the check does not measure; the
  code demonstrates that admission continues whatever the executor backlog (a weakness of a listed kind, Flow and
  state). Minor part: WR6's ">" against the code's `>=` (one signal at the boundary; P15 and the test line agree with
  the code). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no mechanism fails by language semantics.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether a backlog formed, and for how long, is UNKNOWN /
  NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S050, P04-S062).
- **Owner:** A06 (receiver threshold; processor dispatcher and executor, part PQ).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 34; STUDY question 18 (OPEN QUESTION narrowed); IS-66, IS-67, IS-73; A02 K-11
  (owners as recorded: A02 keys, A20 pre-flight, A06 receiver; the pre-flight's own threshold); WD-05, WD-11, WD-13, WD-16; part PQ.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 18 asks what the capacity was meant to protect (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### WD-11 — A capacity of 0 or less, or a `backpressure_pct` outside 0 to 1, reaches the receiver unguarded: every intraday request refused, or the request-level 503 never firing (A02 L-12's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** queue admission: the receiver's use of `signal_queue.capacity` and `backpressure_pct`.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** M19 WR14 L139 to L140 (2026-04-17): `backpressure_pct` "(default 0.80, validated
  0-1)"; CFGLOADER L194 to L195 (2026-04-17): `capacity` "(P15 default: 300)", `backpressure_pct` "(P15 default:
  0.80)" (IS-131). A02 L-12 (detail row 6) records that `SignalQueueConfig` has no validator for "range of the two
  fractions; positivity of capacity and expiry". No source on a capacity of 0 or less (intent-sources file, section
  6.1 point 35). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_loader.py` L192 to L197 (types only); `main.py` L3402 to L3404; `signals/webhook_receiver.py`
  L571 to L578.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the schema accepts any integer capacity and any float fraction;
  the receiver uses them without a guard. Current external context (Python `queue`: maxsize "less than or equal to
  zero, the queue size is infinite") and INTERPRETATION (P03-S036): with a capacity of 0 or less and a
  `backpressure_pct` of 0 or more, the queue is unbounded and the threshold is 0 or less, so `qsize() >= threshold`
  holds for every intraday request, each answered 503 with no log line (WD-12); a negative `backpressure_pct` with a
  positive capacity does the same; a `backpressure_pct` above 1 puts the threshold above the capacity, so the
  request-level 503 never fires and only per-symbol `QUEUE_FULL` remains. At the file values (300, 0.80) none of this
  arises.
- **Evidence:** P03-S036; A02 AUDIT L-12 (detail row 6); code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; INTERPRETATION. A06's own part is the consumer: the
  receiver applies the values unguarded with the effects above; the missing loader validation is A02 L-12's and is
  not counted here. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no source says such values are handled, and no mechanism for them
  exists.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor and latent: the basis is that the file values are valid; effective
  values are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (WI-22).
- **Owner:** A06 (consumer); A02 (schema, L-12).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 35; IS-131; A02 L-12; WD-10, WD-12.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-12 — Back-pressure refusals leave no log line; the request-level 503 is recorded only as an audit row (503, 0, 0) that other 503 answers also write; no alert, as intended

- **Area and flow:** queue admission: what the receiver records when it refuses for back-pressure.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a refusal recorded only ambiguously); Flow and state (signals refused without
  a record of what was refused).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** receiver docstring L35 and M19 WR16 L155 to L162 (2026-04-17): "Does not send
  Telegram alerts on rejection"; "Receiver is a thin validating gateway" (IS-51). FIX-134 Item 35 comments L570 and
  L740 and SYSCFG L128 (`03ac3b0`, 2026-05-31): "graduated backpressure", "X-Queue-Warning header above 60% fill"
  (IS-68). P16 L362 to L363 (2026-04-17): every signal id gets a final status; "Currently many signals vanish silently.
  Operator visibility is essential" (IS-98). `docs/audit/signal_mortality_census_19jul2026.md` L413 to L416
  (2026-07-19; RS-09, IS-149), on the POSTs refused with 403 by the entry window: they "were refused before their
  payload was parsed, so `signals_accepted` and `signals_rejected` are both 0 and the signals inside were never counted
  anywhere. Nothing tells Rama how many signals those POSTs carried" (a legacy record's concern, in its author's words;
  INTERPRETATION: the request-level 503 is refused at the same stage, before the parse). STUDY question 19 is split:
  the alert ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE (none intended); a log line: no source. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L575 to L578 (request-level 503: no log); L996 to L1001
  (`QUEUE_FULL` on the re-queue route: no UPDATE and no log) and L1050 to L1068 (`QUEUE_FULL` on the first route: a
  log line only if the UPDATE fails; corrected in the check); L740 to L744 (headers); L514 to
  L517 and L529 to L534 (counts are read only from a 200 answer, so every 503 is audited as (503, 0, 0)); L487 to L490
  (the shutting-down 503, also (503, 0, 0)); L1036 to L1039 (`STORE_ERROR` logs CRITICAL).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the request-level 503 and a per-symbol `QUEUE_FULL` write no log
  line and send no alert; the warning at 180 is a response header only; the receiver keeps no counter of these
  refusals. FACT: the audit row of each is (503, 0, 0), the same row that the shutting-down 503 and a batch 503 caused
  by `STORE_ERROR` write. INTERPRETATION: the stored record does not tell a back-pressure refusal from a shutdown or a
  store failure, nor how many signals the refused request carried; only the response sent to the sender carried the
  depth (`X-Queue-Depth`); a `QUEUE_FULL` row in `signals` remains for a per-symbol refusal.
- **Evidence:** P03-S026, P03-S037, P03-S039, P03-S042; P02-S067; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH parts: no alert (WR16, the
  docstring); the warning header at 60 % (FIX-134). ISSUE part: a request-level back-pressure refusal is recorded only in a
  form that cannot be told from other 503 answers, and a per-symbol `QUEUE_FULL` leaves its row but no log line, a
  weakness the code demonstrates, against P16's stated purpose of operator
  visibility (INTERPRETATION: P16 speaks of signal ids, which a request-level refusal never assigns) and the concern
  the 19-Jul census states for refusals made before the parse. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no source requires a log
  line.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that the refusal is answered to the sender with a
  retryable 503 and, per symbol, leaves a `QUEUE_FULL` row; only the request-level refusal's content is unrecorded.
- **Owner:** A06; A14 (alerts) and A15 (reports) at the interface.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 36; STUDY question 19 (split); IS-51, IS-68, IS-98, IS-149; RS-09; WD-05, WD-10;
  WI-18 (audit counts against stored rows).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 19's log-line half bears on treatment.

##### WD-13 — The queue settings carry different meanings at different consumers, and no A06 code supplies the live depth to the port-8080 `/metrics`, so its queue fields stay 0 (A20's check passes whenever it reads `/metrics`; A15's row shows UNAVAILABLE)

- **Area and flow:** queue admission and observability: `signal_queue.capacity`, `backpressure_pct`, `warning_pct`
  and the live depth at the receiver, the processor's metrics, the health server, the pre-flight and the dashboard.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two modules disagreeing on a value); Failure handling (a check
  that cannot fire); Intended against actual (a docstring use that does not exist).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** P15 and SYSCFG L125 to L126 (IS-66): 503 at 80 %; FIX-134 (IS-68): warning at
  60 %; M20 SP13 L178 to L187 (2026-04-17): the processor's `stats()` is "Used by /health endpoint and daily report",
  with `queue_depth` among its keys (intent-sources file, section 6.1 point 37; IC-03 notes that `/health` reads the
  queue directly). No A06 source for the 0.9 factor (the pre-flight's own; A02 K-11, owners as recorded A02, A20, A06) or for the `/metrics` fields (A15's server).
  All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L573 to L574 (`int()` thresholds), L395 to L403 (`/health`
  reads `qsize()` and `capacity` directly), L741 to L744 (headers); `signals/signal_processor.py` L683 to L738
  (`get_runtime_metrics`: no queue key, search), L2663 to L2703 (`stats()` with `queue_depth`; no non-test caller,
  search); `main.py` L4074 to L4076 (`metrics_provider=signal_processor.get_runtime_metrics`); interface only:
  `scripts/healthcheck_server.py` L153 to L154, L251 to L257 (A15); `scripts/preflight/checks/signals.py` L72 to L82
  (A20); `ops_dashboard/backend/services/capacity.py` L170 to L181 (A15).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the receiver's thresholds use `int()` (240 and 180 at the file
  values); the live depth leaves A06 code only through the receiver's authenticated `/health` and the response
  headers; the processor's metrics provider, which `main.py` gives to the port-8080 server, returns no queue key, and
  `stats()["queue_depth"]` has no non-test reader. FACT (interface): the port-8080 `/metrics` starts `queue_depth` and
  `queue_capacity` at 0 and merges only the provider's keys; A20's check warns only `if cap and depth >= cap * 0.9`;
  A15's row computes `round(bp * capacity)` and looks for `queue_size` in the port-8080 `/health` answer, which has
  none. INTERPRETATION (P03-S045 to P03-S047; P04-S065; P11-S025): `capacity` is a hard put limit, the base of two
  receiver thresholds, a display denominator, and at the pre-flight a value read as 0; `backpressure_pct` is a refusal
  point truncated with `int()` at the receiver and an annotation rounded with `round()` at the dashboard (the two agree
  at 300, 240, and differ for some other capacities, for example 301: 240 against 241); the pre-flight's 0.9 is its
  own. At the baseline the pre-flight's backpressure check passes ("webhook queue 0/?") whenever `/metrics` answers 200
  and is skipped otherwise (A20's L74 to L75; P11-S025; qualifier added in the check), and the dashboard's queue row
  always shows UNAVAILABLE, whatever the receiver's queue holds; and even a real value would be the
  hand-off buffer of WD-10.
- **Evidence:** P03-S045 to P03-S047; P04-S050, P04-S065; P11-S025; code lines above; searches in section 1.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. A06's own parts: (i) no A06 code
  supplies a queue value to the metrics surface that the pre-flight and the dashboard read, so their queue readings
  are blind at the baseline (consistency across components; a check that cannot fire); (ii) SP13's "Used by /health
  endpoint and daily report" does not match the code (`stats()` has no non-test caller; `/health` reads the queue
  itself). The readers' own logic (the 0.9 factor, `round()`, the health URL, the server's initial zeros) is A20's
  and A15's and is handed off, not counted. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: whether the `/metrics` fields were meant to
  carry the receiver's queue is not established (STUDY question 18).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that the receiver's own admission does not depend on
  these readings (FACT); only monitoring surfaces are affected.
- **Owner:** A06 (processor metrics, receiver thresholds); A15 (health server, dashboard); A20 (pre-flight check;
  A02 K-11).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 37; STUDY question 18 (OPEN QUESTION narrowed); IS-66, IS-68; IC-03; A02 K-11;
  WD-10.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 18 bears on what the `/metrics` fields and the fill levels were
  meant to report (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### WD-14 — `webhook_audit.ts` is the time the row is written, after processing, while the schema says "when request arrived"; every `/webhook` answer waits for the audit write

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's `webhook_audit` write at the A03 boundary: its time stamp and its place in the
  request.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Weakness
  (request latency tied to a storage write).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** SCHEMA comments L576 to L583 [A03] (`bcf03b5`, 2026-04-17): "Append-only audit row
  per POST", `ts` "ISO-8601 IST when request arrived" (IS-137; CD-28). M19 WR11 L121 to L122 (2026-04-17): "No long
  blocking work in request handler — must return in <100ms typical" (IS-03). STUDY question 34's first half is
  ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date (`ts` meant as the arrival time; part XA holds the question). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1136 (`ts = now_ist().isoformat()` inside `_write_audit`),
  L478 and L530 (`duration_ms` from the monotonic start to just before the write), L486 to L489, L496 to L499, L529 to
  L534 (the three write sites, each before the answer leaves); L1137 to L1154 (own `transaction()`, failure logged at
  ERROR); `core/schema.sql` L583, L592 (`date` = `substr(ts, 1, 10)`); `core/state_store.py` L112, L232 to L237.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `ts` is taken when the row is written, after the request was
  handled; `duration_ms` is stored beside it; `date` derives from `ts`. INTERPRETATION (P03-S050): the arrival time is
  about `ts` minus `duration_ms`, so `ts` and `date` record the end of handling. INTERPRETATION (P03-S051; current
  external context through P03-S051, Python `sqlite3` timeout and `try`/`finally`): every `/webhook` answer, the 429
  and 503 answers included, leaves only after the audit write has finished or failed, and that write can wait up to
  about 30 s for another connection's write lock; a write failure never changes the answer (R-39, A03's MATCH).
- **Evidence:** P03-S049 to P03-S052; P11-S035; code and schema lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The time stamp does not record what
  the schema comment states (CD-28). The audit write before every answer is the design of WR13 ("Every POST writes one row regardless of outcome", M19
  L133), which the code implements in `finally` (L529 to L534; the registry calls it "WR13 finally", IS-134;
  corrected in the check); how long
  such waits were in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S069), so the latency part is recorded without a
  finding against WR11. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: no handling mechanism is involved.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that `duration_ms` is stored with every row, so the
  arrival time can be derived; a row written just after midnight IST would carry the next date (INTERPRETATION).
- **Owner:** A06 (writer); A03 (schema comment).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 71; STUDY question 34 (part XA's; first half answered by evidence); CD-28; IS-03,
  IS-137; A03 R-39 (MATCH, unchanged); WI-02 (which POSTs write a row); WI-18 (the counts).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### WD-15 — The EOD heartbeat, meant to make the 17:00 capture visible, can itself fail with no record of the failure: the receiver ignores the helper's False (A03 D-08's A06 side; registry and retention handed over)

- **Area and flow:** the EOD route's `pb01_capture` heartbeat (`_record_eod_heartbeat`) at the A03 and A16
  boundaries.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (a failure signal discarded; an observation that can fail silently).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** C3 docstring L809 to L822 and comment L830 (`cf8d723`, 2026-07-25): "one
  cron_heartbeat row per EOD alert, so the 17:00 capture stops being invisible"; "Every failure mode is swallowed ...
  Observability must never be able to break the thing it observes" (IS-138); comment L782 to L785 (`cf8d723`): the
  `FAILED`/`DISABLED` row makes the two states "distinguishable in one query" (P11-S019). The helper's docstring
  `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L77 to L78: returns "True if heartbeat was recorded, False on error (never raises)" (A16's
  helper). No source on registry membership or retention (STUDY question 38, part XA's). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L776 to L788 and L799 to L803 (the alert's log lines and the
  calls), L824 to L834 (`try`; the
  return value of `record_heartbeat` is not read; WARNING only for exceptions raised in the receiver's own `try`, for
  example the import at L825); `utils/cron_heartbeat.py` L61 (relative default `data_store/trading_system.db`), L80 to
  L95 (missing file → False; new `StateStore`; any exception → False with `close()` skipped).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: when the helper returns False (the relative path missing from the
  working directory, or any store error), the receiver writes no log line and the request answers as usual. FACT
  (P11-S018, P11-S019, P11-S037): `pb01_capture` is not a job of `config/cron_registry.yaml`; the Control Tower's cron
  check iterates registry jobs only; no code deletes `cron_heartbeat` rows. FACT: the receiver does log each EOD alert
  (INFO at L799 to L800; WARNING at L779 to L781 when no worker is set), but nothing it logs says that the heartbeat row
  was not written. INTERPRETATION: a reader of `cron_heartbeat` alone cannot tell a missing heartbeat from a missing
  alert, and only the log's alert line, not the receiver, reveals the gap; whether the relative path names the trading
  database in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P03-S067; A03 D-09).
- **Evidence:** P03-S056, P03-S067; P11-S018, P11-S019, P11-S036, P11-S037; P09-S051 (A03 D-08 as recorded); code
  lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH part: no heartbeat failure
  can change the answer, as C3 states. ISSUE part (A06's own): the receiver discards the helper's failure signal, so
  the heartbeat meant to end the capture's invisibility can be absent with no record of why. A03 D-08 ("ISSUE; FACT;
  INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL"; "A03 treatment group TG1 (AGREED, 11-Nov-2026)"; "AGREED"; owners "A16 (the helper);
  A06 (the EOD webhook call site); A15 (`capture_metrics_baseline.py`); A03 (the open cost)") keeps its record for the
  full store opened per heartbeat on the request thread; A03 D-09 (relative default) and D-13 (unclosed store) are
  A03's; registry membership, the Control Tower's check and retention are handed to A16, A15 and A03 and are not
  counted here. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: the helper's never-raise guard works as coded (A03 D-29, MATCH).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the basis is that the capture itself (A18's worker) does not
  depend on the heartbeat; only its trace can be missing.
- **Owner:** A06 (call site); A16 (helper; cron tools); A15 (Control Tower, readers); A03 (storage; D-08, D-09, D-13).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 73; STUDY question 38 (part XA's); IS-138; A03 D-08 (one of the 52), D-09, D-13,
  D-29; WI-29 (the EOD route's contract, MATCH).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 38 bears on monitoring and retention (part XA).

  `QUEUE_FULL`, `STORE_ERROR`, 429 or request-level 503 occurred; the depths reached; how long claims were held;
  whether the routes of WD-01, WD-02, WD-04 (a new alert reviving a row), WD-05 (a failed mark; items lost at a
  restart), WD-06 (a slot-edge pair after a restart) or WD-08 (a late release) ever occurred (STUDY 18.2 item 1, this

  cachetools 7.1.1's. (6) The database lock waits met by the receiver's writes (P03-S069). (7) Whether the heartbeat's
  relative path named the trading database (P03-S067).

## 8. Part PQ — Processor intake, workers, expiry, normalisation

**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (`970aabf`)
**Scope:** STUDY sections 6 and 7 (part P04): the processor's construction and settings, `start()`, the dispatcher,
the executor and its workers, ordering, retries (the FIX-069 re-put and the unwired FIX-007 pre-check), worker and
dispatcher failure, `stop()` and the drain, the boot order of the processor, expiry on the worker side (which
timestamp, time zone, thresholds, order, the resume paths), the `EXPIRED` alert, symbol and alias normalisation at the
A05 boundary (A05 owns the instrument cache and the alias tool; the receiver's loading of the alias file is part WI's
WI-10), the processor's instrument-cache call (A05 IN-08's A06 side), field survival from the queue item to the
screening call, and the processor's in-flight releases. The steps called before the screening call keep their owners
(A07 strategy control and screening, A01 the per-strategy window, A09 the governor, A11 the shadow guard, A13 the kill
switch); the ownership of the strategy lookup and the A06-to-A07 handoff line stay OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY section 17,
point 1): neither reading is adopted. Direction questions 15 to 19 (and 26 to 28). 16 allocated items
(this AUDIT, sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4).

- **External pages read for this part** (current external context, all read 18-Nov-2026; page version 3.15.2 where
  shown): Python `concurrent.futures` (https://docs.python.org/3/library/concurrent.futures.html): `Executor.shutdown`
  "If wait is True then this method will not return until all the pending futures are done executing"; "Changed in
  version 3.9: Added the cancel_futures parameter"; "Calls to Executor.submit() and Executor.map() made after shutdown
  will raise RuntimeError"; "All threads enqueued to ThreadPoolExecutor will be joined before the interpreter can exit";
  `Future.cancel()`: "If the call is currently being executed or finished running and cannot be cancelled then the
  method will return False"; `Future.result`: "If the call raised an exception, this method will raise the same
  exception". Python `queue` (https://docs.python.org/3/library/queue.html): `put` with a positive timeout "raises the
  Full exception if no free slot was available within that time"; `put_nowait` "Equivalent to put(item, block=False)",
  which raises Full unless a slot "is immediately available"; `get` with a positive timeout "raises the Empty exception
  if no item was available within that time"; `qsize()` "Return the approximate size of the queue"; `SimpleQueue`
  "Constructor for an unbounded FIFO (first-in, first-out) queue". Python `datetime`
  (https://docs.python.org/3/library/datetime.html): `now(tz)`: "If tz is not None, it must be an instance of a tzinfo
  subclass, and the current date and time are converted to tz's time zone"; "If you merely want to attach a timezone
  object tz to a datetime dt without adjustment of date and time data, use dt.replace(tzinfo=tz)"; aware datetimes with
  the same `tzinfo`: "No time zone adjustments are done in this case"; with different `tzinfo`, both are treated as if
  converted to UTC; aware minus naive raises `TypeError`. Python `threading`
  (https://docs.python.org/3/library/threading.html): "you must call is_alive() after join() to decide whether a
  timeout happened"; "Daemon threads are abruptly stopped at shutdown"; the daemon flag, when not given, "is inherited
  from the current thread"; `excepthook`: an uncaught exception from `Thread.run()` "is printed out on sys.stderr"
  (`SystemExit` is ignored). Python "Built-in Exceptions" (https://docs.python.org/3/library/exceptions.html):
  `BaseException`: "If str() is called on an instance of this class, the representation of the argument(s) to the
  instance are returned, or the empty string when there were no arguments"; `SystemExit`, `KeyboardInterrupt` and
  `GeneratorExit` inherit from `BaseException`, not `Exception`; `KeyError` "Raised when a mapping (dictionary) key is
  not found in the set of existing keys". Python Tutorial "Errors and Exceptions"
  (https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/errors.html): "Handlers only handle exceptions that occur in the corresponding try
  clause, not in other handlers of the same try statement"; "The finally clause runs whether or not the try statement
  produces an exception". CPython 3.12 published source `Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py`
  (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/3.12/Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py): `self._work_queue =
  queue.SimpleQueue()`; `submit` raises `RuntimeError('cannot schedule new futures after shutdown')` (or "after
  interpreter shutdown"), then `self._work_queue.put(w)` and `self._adjust_thread_count()`; `_adjust_thread_count`
  returns if an idle worker exists, else creates `threading.Thread(name=..., target=_worker, args=...)` with no daemon
  argument while fewer than `max_workers` exist; `_WorkItem.run` catches `BaseException` and calls
  `self.future.set_exception(exc)`; `_worker` keeps looping and returns only when the interpreter or the executor is
  shutting down or the executor is gone; `_python_exit`, registered by `threading._register_atexit`, wakes and joins
  every worker; `shutdown(wait=True)` joins every worker thread. CPython 3.12 published source `Lib/queue.py`
  (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/python/cpython/3.12/Lib/queue.py): `put` raises `Full` with no argument (`raise
  Full`) in both its non-blocking and its timeout branch. Other library statements are used through the STUDY
  statements that cite them (read 17-Nov-2026), as named in each record.
- **Added in the check (18-Nov-2026; every page above re-opened and each quotation found, page version 3.15.2):**
  Python Language Reference, "Simple statements", "The raise statement"
  (https://docs.python.org/3/reference/simple_stmts.html): "If it is a class, the exception instance will be obtained
  when needed by instantiating the class with no arguments"; "If no expressions are present, raise re-raises the
  exception that is currently being handled". CPython 3.12 `Lib/queue.py` (as above): `class Full(Exception)` holds a
  docstring and `pass` only (no `__init__`, no `__str__`). CPython 3.12 `Lib/concurrent/futures/thread.py` (as above):
  `_WorkItem.run` has `except BaseException as exc:`; `shutdown` sets `self._shutdown = True` under its lock before it
  joins the threads. Python "Built-in Functions" (https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html): `getattr`: "If the
  named attribute does not exist, default is returned if provided, otherwise AttributeError is raised"; `callable`:

| 18.1 point 98 (IN-08 under the four-condition rule) | allocated to part XA | covered by PQ-07, which weighs all four conditions on A06's own sources; part XA may cite PQ-07 rather than count it again | PQ-07 |
| A02 K-13, the A06 side (no per-signal deadline) | allocated to part XA | the A06 side is PQ-01; K-13 itself is not counted again | PQ-01 |
