### 8.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### PQ-01 — No per-signal deadline: `pipeline_timeout_sec: 30`, stated as a "hard per-signal processing deadline", is validated but read by nothing; a worker blocked in a call holds one of the five slots without limit

- **Area and flow:** the worker pool: how long one signal may hold a worker (`_process_one_safe`, `_process_one`, the
  executor of `worker_count` threads).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a configured limit not enforced); Inconsistent settings (a key that
  changes nothing); Failure handling (no bound on a stuck call).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) M20 SP15, L198 to L203 (2026-04-17): `pipeline_timeout_sec:
  int (default 30, hard kill on executor.submit not finishing)` (IS-76). (2) SYSCFG L347 (2026-04-17): "hard
  per-signal processing deadline"; `core/config_loader.py` L568 (schema comment); `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L355 (lines
  2026-06-19): "hard deadline per signal" (IS-76). (3) LOCKED-YAML `config_honor_principle`, L424 to L427
  (2026-04-17): "Every limit in config must be enforced AND alerted AND reported", rationale "No 'namesake' config
  values" (IS-103). (4) The crash test `tests/crash_test/ct_day2_isolated.py` L187 prints "pipeline_timeout_sec
  enforced at signal_processor, not screener" (TEST; P04-S004). (5) The legacy integrity audit
  (`docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L3746 to L3749, 2026-08-01) already lists the key as consumed by nothing
  (RECORD; it states no other intent). CD-15. No source calls the key a placeholder (intent-sources file, section 6.2,
  question 20).
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_loader.py` L568, L602 to L607 (field and validator); `signals/signal_processor.py`
  L155 to L198 (no such parameter), L304 to L313 (executor), L389 to L390 (`submit`, future discarded), L463 to L480,
  L867 to L1276; `main.py` L3575 to L3623 (not passed).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the key is read only by its schema field and validator (`>= 1`); the
  processor has no deadline parameter, keeps no future and measures no elapsed time against a limit; the only time
  limit before the screening call is the age check against `signal_queue.expiry_sec`, applied once when a worker starts
  the signal (P04-S004; P08-S059, P08-S060). Current external context (`concurrent.futures`, section 1):
  `Future.cancel()` returns False for a call "currently being executed", and the executor offers no way to stop a
  running call; INTERPRETATION: SP15's "hard kill" could not be had from the executor's own API for a running call,
  and the code attempts no other form (a timed wait on the future, a deadline check between steps). INTERPRETATION
  (P04-S062): a worker blocked in any collaborator call (before or after the screening call) holds one of the five
  slots until the call returns; while all five are held, later signals wait in the executor's unbounded work queue,
  unseen by the receiver's back-pressure (WD-10), and each is then rejected `EXPIRED` if its age from `triggered_at`
  has passed 600 s by the time a worker starts it (PQ-12). File value 30; effective value UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P04-S004, P04-S013, P04-S014, P04-S050, P04-S062; P08-S059, P08-S060; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. The processor does
  not implement the per-signal deadline that SP15, the configuration comment, the configuration guide and the crash
  test state, against the configuration-honour principle. This is the A06 side of A02 K-13 ("`signal_processor.
  pipeline_timeout_sec` has no consumer"; "ISSUE; FACT"; treatment "Batch T5 group G1 (AGREED, 09-Nov-2026)"; status
  "AGREED"; owners "A06"; P09-S045): K-13 keeps its record and is not counted again here; this record counts the
  processor's missing deadline and its effect on the pool. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) fails (no
  deadline mechanism exists).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: how long any collaborator call took in production is
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (18.2 item 11; PQ-09).
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor); A02 (the key and its schema; K-13).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 48; STUDY question 20 (ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date: IS-76, IS-103); A02 K-13
  (allocated to part XA, whose A06 side this record holds); WD-10 (the backlog that back-pressure does not see);
  PQ-12; CD-15.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 20 is answered by the sources at their date.

##### PQ-02 — `stop()`: the executor wait has no limit, against SP4's "5s timeout" and the docstring's "up to 5s"; every drained or backlogged signal runs intake, screening, sizing, risk and reservation before the pre-placement `SHUTDOWN` reject; a timed-out join could leave items behind or drop one (A01 C18's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** the processor's lifecycle at stop: `stop()`, the dispatcher's drain, the executor shutdown, the
  FIX-070 shutdown check before placement; `main.py` `_shutdown`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated bound not implemented); Flow and state (work done on signals
  that will not be placed; items that can be left or dropped); Failure handling (no bound on shutdown).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) M20 SP4, L45 to L48 (2026-04-17): "stop() signals shutdown,
  drains in-flight tasks (5s timeout), shuts down executor cleanly" (IS-73). (2) The `stop` docstring, processor L327
  to L329 (root commit, 2026-04-17): "drain remaining queue items, wait for in-flight tasks to complete (up to 5s per
  SP4)" (IS-77). (3) The dispatcher docstring L381 and comment L392 (2026-04-17): after stop it "drains any remaining
  queue items" so "queued work is not silently lost" (IS-78). (4) H-16, `main.py` L1681 to L1683 (`0d80fe9`,
  2026-04-19): "signal_processor stop below still works on queued items" (IS-05). (5) FIX-070, processor L1384 to
  L1388 (check introduced by `31c6a62`, 2026-05-16; lines from `536172c`): "Also check shutdown event - system shutdown
  could have been initiated", then abort the placement. (6) A01 C18 as recorded: "The longest shutdown (84 s, or 99 s
  with a close-out) exceeds the service's 30 s stop timeout"; owners "A17; A06 and every owner of a `stop()`"; its
  owner field names "A06 for SP4's docstring against its code" (P09-S017). CD-13. No source states what a drained
  signal should do before placement (intent-sources file, section 6.2, question 21).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L326 to L347 (`stop()`), L383 to L399 (loop and drain), L1398 to
  L1405 (`SHUTDOWN` reject, reservation released); `main.py` L1629 (`_shutdown_event`), L1681 to L1692 (receiver, then
  processor).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): `stop()` sets `_stop_event`, joins the dispatcher with
  `timeout=10.0` without checking `is_alive()`, then calls `self._executor.shutdown(wait=True,
  cancel_futures=False)` and sets `_executor = None`; it changes no signal status and releases no claim (P04-S017).
  The drain is `get_nowait` and `submit` until any exception (L393 to L399). Current external context
  (`concurrent.futures`, `threading`; section 1) and INTERPRETATION (P04-S018): `shutdown(wait=True)` returns only when
  every submitted signal, running or still waiting in the executor's work queue, has finished its run; the only fixed
  wait is the 10 s join. A signal whose run reaches L1398 after the flag is set is rejected `SHUTDOWN` (status
  `REJECTED_SHUTDOWN`) after the intake checks, the screening call, price derivation, sizing, risk approval and
  reservation (released at L1402 to L1404); a placement already under way continues. MATCH part: no drained signal is
  silently lost: each reaches a final status (normally `REJECTED_SHUTDOWN`, or an earlier reject), as (3) and (4)
  state, and no new placement starts after the flag, as (5) states. ISSUE parts: (i) the wait is unbounded, against
  (1) and (2), and grows with the number of signals submitted, each running its full pre-placement pipeline (A07, A09
  and A04 calls whose durations are UNKNOWN); this is the processor's share of A01 C18; (ii) if the 10 s join timed
  out while the dispatcher was still draining, the executor would be shut down underneath it: the next `submit` raises
  `RuntimeError` ("Calls to Executor.submit() ... made after shutdown will raise RuntimeError"; the CPython 3.12
  source sets the executor's shutdown flag before it joins the workers, section 1), the drain's `except Exception:
  break` ends it, the item that `get_nowait` had just taken for that `submit` is dropped, and the remaining items stay
  in `signal_queue`; if instead the dispatcher reached the drain only after `_executor` had become `None` (L340), the
  test at L396 would skip every `submit` while the loop went on, so every item then in the queue would be taken and
  dropped (and one item at L389, in the main loop); all with no status change, against (3) (P04-S018 (b); its "an item
  already got ... is dropped" made exact in the check). Either route needs the join to time out, which needs producers
  to keep the queue non-empty for 10 s, which the drain's tight loop makes unlikely (latent). INTERPRETATION:
  a FIX-069 re-put made after the drain's last read (for example by a placement already under way when `stop()` began)
  would stay in the in-memory queue like the late receiver put of WI-03, with the claim moot and the row at its last
  status; whether the limiter can still raise `BrokerRateLimitError` once `_shutdown_event` is set (`main.py` L1628:
  "so rate limiter aborts immediately") is A04's. If the service manager ends the process at its stop timeout (A01 C18;
  A17's unit), runs still in progress stop where they are (A01 W-24 and the executor's exit join, P04-S012).
- **Evidence:** P04-S012, P04-S016 to P04-S020; P01-S081; P09-S017; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external
  context. KNOWN DEFECT considered for part (ii) and not given: (a) holds (the drain is the mechanism for queued work);
  (b) holds for the case (once the join has timed out, the item whose `submit` raises the documented `RuntimeError` is
  dropped by the drain's handler, and every item got after `_executor` became `None` is dropped by the code's own test);
  (c) not established (a drain lasting over 10 s needs a producer that the documented stop order
  does not provide: the receiver is stopped first, WI-03); (d) holds (the outcome contradicts (3)).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: the actual stop durations are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED
  (A01 C18's own UNKNOWN part; 18.2 items 11 and 21).
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor's `stop()` and drain); A01 (`_shutdown` and C18's total); A17 (the unit's stop
  timeout); A07, A09, A04 (the durations of the calls made by drained signals).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 49; STUDY question 21 (ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date for the drain and the 5 s;
  the handling of drained signals before placement has no source); A01 C18 (allocated here: A06 side); WI-03 (the
  receiver's stop, the late put, A01 C20); EV-11 part (b) (the four other entry points, which run outside the executor
  and which `stop()` does not await; counted there, not here); PQ-08 (the other stale texts); CD-13 (its "or drop one"
  covers the first route of part (ii); the second route can drop every queued item).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 21 bears on treatment (what drained signals should do).

##### PQ-03 — The `EXPIRED` alert: one warning per 60 s for the whole processor, read and written without a lock; its text repeats "age"; the receiver's own `EXPIRED` refusals raise none

- **Area and flow:** the worker's `EXPIRED` reject and its alert (processor L1221 to L1231); the receiver's `EXPIRED`
  at receipt (interface).
- **Issue category:** Concurrency (an unguarded check-then-set); Failure handling and observability (which expiries
  reach the operator); Documentation in an alert text.
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) FIX-136 Item 51, processor L295 (`e55cb59`, 2026-05-31):
  "rate-limit expired alerts" (IS-88). (2) The commit message of `e55cb59` (2026-05-31), Item 51: "Telegram WARNING on
  expired signals in signal_processor (rate-limited to 1/min)" (IS-174, read at its Item 51 for this part). No source
  says whether the limit is per symbol or for the whole processor, or names the receiver's refusals (intent-sources
  file, section 6.1 point 51 and section 6.2 question 26).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L295 (`_last_expired_alert_ts = 0.0`, no lock), L917 to L921
  (the reason text), L1221 to L1231 (the alert).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): after an `EXPIRED` reject, if a notifier is set and more
  than 60.0 s (monotonic) have passed since `_last_expired_alert_ts`, the worker stores the new time and calls
  `send_warning(f"[{self._mode}] Signal EXPIRED: {symbol} age={rej.reason}")`, swallowing any error; `rej.reason` is
  "Signal age <x>s > expiry <n>s", so the text reads "... age=Signal age <x>s > expiry <n>s". The test (L1224) and the
  store (L1225) are separate steps with no lock, while up to five workers run this handler. FACT (P04-S043): the
  receiver has no notifier and sends nothing for its own `EXPIRED`. INTERPRETATION: MATCH part: worker-side expiries
  raise a Telegram warning limited to about one a minute, as (1) and (2) state. ISSUE parts: (i) two workers rejecting
  `EXPIRED` within the same instant can both pass the test and both send (a lock-free check-then-set; current external
  context `threading`, section 1); (ii) the alert text repeats "age" (minor); (iii) all other expiries in the 60 s
  window, of any symbol, leave only an INFO log line and the `REJECTED_EXPIRED` status, and every `EXPIRED` refusal at
  receipt leaves only its 200 body entry and no stored row (P02-S069), so a delivery delay that makes most signals
  arrive stale shows at most one warning a minute and only for those that reached a worker; recorded as not
  established, not as an issue part, since no source states the scope.
- **Evidence:** P04-S043, P04-S060, P04-S061; P02-S058, P02-S069; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part and a not-established part. FACT; HISTORICAL;
  INTERPRETATION. Basis for "minor": the race can at most add one warning per concurrent worker inside one instant,
  and the text fault repeats one word. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism fails by language semantics; the
  limit is approximate, not absent).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above). Whether any such alert was sent is UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED (18.2 item 16; PQ-09).
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor's alert call); A14 (the notifier).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 51; STUDY question 26 (OPEN QUESTION narrowed); IS-88, IS-174; PQ-12.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 26 (was the limit meant to cover all symbols together?) bears on
  part (iii) and on treatment.

##### PQ-04 — FIX-069 retries re-queue at once, although the comment says "15s each" and "reconciler will retry"; each retry re-runs the whole intake and A07 path against the original age; the timeout retry was removed as the later A-2 decision states (A04 RL-16's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** the processor's own put back into the intake queue after a `BrokerRateLimitError` from
  `place()` (processor L1486 to L1532, inside `_admit_and_place`, after the screening call; placement is A10's and the
  exception A04's; the A06 side is the re-queue and what the retry meets at intake).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated delay not implemented); Documentation against code; Flow and
  state (a retry repeats every intake step and the screening call).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) FIX-069 comment, processor L1487 to L1489 (lines 2026-07-12,
  `536172c`, the extraction commit): a client-side rate limit is raised before submission, so "re-queuing for retry is
  idempotent"; "Keep lock held to prevent duplicate admission. Max 3 retries (45s total, 15s each)"; L1521: "Release
  reservation but keep lock - reconciler will retry" (IS-82). The original FIX-069 commit `397105d` (2026-05-16) read
  "Max 3 retries (45s total with 15s delays)" and had no delay in its code either (`git show 397105d`, section 1). (2)
  The `397105d` message: "Re-queue signal up to 3 times before abandoning"; the lock "travels with signal until
  terminal state" (IS-173). (3) A-2, commit `fd09a38` (2026-07-02) and processor L1465 to L1477: a `place()` timeout
  is ambiguous, "Do NOT retry"; the rate-limit error "KEEPS its retry" (IS-105, IS-173). IC-25 (FIX-069 re-queued
  timeouts; A-2, later, removed that retry). CD-17. (4) A04 RL-16 as recorded: "`acquire`'s 30-second bound"; owner
  "A04"; what crosses: the comment "describes A06's retry policy wrongly": "no 15 s delay exists, each attempt waits up
  to the limiter's 30 s" (P09-S063).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L1464 to L1484 (timeout: `TIMEOUT`, not re-queued), L1486 to
  L1532 (rate limit), L876 to L887 (the dict read back), L895 to L921 (intake again), L1248 to L1271 (claim kept when
  `requeued`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): on `BrokerRateLimitError` the worker records an API failure;
  with `retry_count >= 3` it re-raises (`PLACEMENT_FAILED`); otherwise it increments `retry_count`, sets
  `ac.requeued = True`, builds the dict `{signal_id, scanner_name, symbol, trigger_price, triggered_at, retry_count}`
  and calls `self._queue.put(signal_dict, timeout=1.0)`; on success it releases the reservation and returns, the
  receiver's claim kept; on `queue.Full` it sets `ac.requeued = False` and re-raises (PQ-05 for the status written).
  No sleep, timer or scheduled time exists anywhere on this path (search of the processor for `sleep(`, `Timer(` and
  `.wait(`: no hit), and no reconciler takes the item: only the receiver and the processor hold the queue (P04-S008),
  no code reads `signals` rows back into a queue (P04-S020), and the dispatcher gets the dict as soon as it reaches the
  front of `signal_queue`. INTERPRETATION (P04-S044, P04-S052): each
  retry runs the whole intake again (status `PROCESSING`, kill switch, window, the age from the original
  `triggered_at`, shadow guard, strategy lookup and controls) and the screening call, price derivation, sizing, risk and
  reservation again, before a new `place()`; at most four attempts; the retry count lives only in the dict. MATCH parts:
  at most three retries, as (1) and (2) state; a timeout is not re-queued, as the later A-2 states (IC-25 resolved by
  the code in A-2's favour); the claim travels with the retry, as (2) states, subject to the sweeper's 60 s eviction
  (WI-01) and to release by symbol (WD-08). ISSUE parts: the "15s each" delay and "45s total" do not exist (CD-17), and
  "reconciler will retry" names a component that plays no part; under A04's recorded 30 s limiter wait (RL-16), the
  time per attempt is that wait plus a full pipeline, not 15 s. What the retry meets at the entry throttle (refused by
  the 20 s gap or the 300 s same-symbol cooldown measured from its own first admit, at the file values) is part ET's
  18.1 point 39 (P06-S036) and is not counted here.
- **Evidence:** P04-S044, P04-S052; P05-S049; P06-S036, P06-S050; P09-S063; code lines above; `git show 397105d`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The stated retry timing
  (a 15 s delay per attempt) is not implemented and never was; the comment misstates who retries. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered and not given: (a) fails (no delay mechanism exists).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether `BrokerRateLimitError` reached the processor in
  production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P04-S052); with the throttle's effect (part ET), whether a retry could ever
  place an order at the file values is part ET's question.
- **Owner:** A06 (the re-queue and its comment); A04 (RL-16, the limiter wait); A10 (placement); ET part (the
  throttle's effect on the retry).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 53; STUDY question 23 (ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE at its date: IS-82); A04 RL-16 (allocated
  here: A06 side; RL-16 itself keeps "ISSUE; FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL", treatment "A04 treatment group TG28
  (AGREED, 13-Nov-2026)", status "AGREED", and is not counted again); IC-25, CD-17; 18.1 point 39 (part ET); WI-01,
  WD-08; EV-06 (the row left `RESERVED` with no reservation while the retry waits, and the reason-less
  `PLACEMENT_FAILED` of a failed re-queue; counted there); EV-12 (a broker 429 takes another branch); PQ-05.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 23 is answered by the source at its date.

##### PQ-05 — The FIX-007 rate-limiter pre-check never runs under `main.py`; inside it, a FIX-069 retry dict would raise `KeyError` before its clean-up, and its full-queue reject writes bare `REJECTED` while its own log line, FIX-048 and Record 02 name `REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL`; a full queue is written three ways

- **Area and flow:** `_process_one_safe`'s optional pre-check (processor L415 to L461) and the full-queue outcomes of
  the three puts (receiver, FIX-007, FIX-069); the A04 `try_acquire` boundary at the interface.
- **Issue category:** Dead or unreachable code; Weak failure handling inside it (latent); Documentation against code;
  Consistency across components (one condition, three status forms).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) FIX-007 comment L415 to L417 and constructor comments L189
  ("FIX-007: optional RateLimiter for order pre-check") and L233 ("FIX-007: optional pre-check") (`31075d0`,
  2026-05-12): with the order bucket exhausted, "re-enqueue the signal and return immediately" (IS-81). (2) FIX-048,
  commit `d604c95` (2026-05-15): with the queue full the re-put waits 1 s, then "the signal is abandoned and a
  REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL warning is logged" (IS-81). (3) FIX-165f comment L428 (`1bf65ff`, 2026-06-13): "Queue at capacity
  — abandon signal but clean up." (4) FIX-069 comment L407 (`397105d`, 2026-05-16): "Support both tuple and dict
  signal formats". (5) Record 02 (R02-18, 2026-09-18): status `REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL` (IS-150). (6) The `853a3a1`
  message (2026-09-10): the evidence capture added because the bare `REJECTED` status escaped an earlier scan; "Batch
  1: NOT deployed to either VM" (P04-S053). (7) A04 RL-18 as recorded: MATCH for `try_acquire`; "its only non-test
  caller, the signal processor's pre-check, is never reached because `main.py` passes no limiter"; owners "A04; A06
  (the caller)" (P09-S064). No source says the pre-check was meant to be wired in production (intent-sources file,
  section 6.2, question 24).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L189, L233, L405 to L461, L1519 to L1532; `main.py` L3575 to
  L3623 (no `rate_limiter`); `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1052 to L1057 (interface, P04-S059).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): the block runs only when `self._rate_limiter` is not
  `None`; `main.py` passes none, so under `main.py` the pre-check, its re-put, its `QUEUE_FULL` reject and its
  evidence capture never run (P04-S053). Inside the block: on a full re-put it logs WARNING "REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL: ..."
  but writes `update_signal_status(signal_id, "REJECTED", "QUEUE_FULL")` (bare `REJECTED`, reason `QUEUE_FULL`);
  L429, inside the `except queue.Full` handler of the re-put's `try` (L425 to L427) and covered by no other `try` of
  the method, takes `signal_tuple[1] if len(signal_tuple) > 1 else "unknown"`. Current external context
  (Built-in Exceptions, `KeyError`; Tutorial, handlers; CPython 3.12 executor source; section 1) and INTERPRETATION
  (P04-S054, P04-S055): for a FIX-069 dict (length 6, string keys) `signal_tuple[1]` raises `KeyError`, which no
  handler of `_process_one_safe` covers (it is raised inside the `except queue.Full` handler, before the method's own
  `try` at L465), so it is stored in the discarded future: no log line, no status, no evidence record, no claim
  release. FACT: the same full-queue condition is written as `QUEUE_FULL` by the receiver, as bare `REJECTED` with
  reason `QUEUE_FULL` by this branch, and as `PLACEMENT_FAILED` by the FIX-069 branch (P04-S059). Current external
  context (CPython 3.12 `Lib/queue.py`: `class Full(Exception)` defines no `__init__` or `__str__`, and `put` raises
  `raise Full`, the class, in both its non-blocking and its timeout branch; Language Reference, "The raise statement":
  "If it is a class, the exception instance will be obtained when needed by instantiating the class with no
  arguments", and a bare `raise` "re-raises the exception that is currently being handled"; Built-in Exceptions:
  `str()` of an instance with no arguments gives "the empty string"; Tutorial: handlers do not cover exceptions raised
  in other handlers of the same `try`; section 1) and INTERPRETATION: in the FIX-069 branch the bare `raise` at L1532
  re-raises the `queue.Full` instance being handled; it leaves `_admit_and_place` (the later handlers at L1533 and
  L1537 do not cover it) and reaches the generic handler, which writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with `str(exc)`, an empty
  text (L1238); the store writes `NULL` for an empty reason (`core/state_store.py` L1425, A03's file, interface only),
  so no reason is stored and the cause shows only in the ERROR line "FIX-069: queue full, cannot re-queue" (L1528 to
  L1530) and the traceback (L1235 to L1237). That reason-less status is counted in EV-06 and not here (corrected in the
  check: the draft also counted it here, as part of (iv)).
- **Evidence:** P04-S052 to P04-S055, P04-S059; P05-S047 to P05-S050; P09-S064; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor, latent). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context.
  (i) Context, not counted here (corrected in the check: the draft counted it as issue part (i)): the pre-check,
  described as optional, is unreachable under `main.py` (dead code at the documented set-up); EV-13 records that wiring
  at the A04 boundary (never passed at three commits) as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, because whether it was meant to be
  wired is STUDY question 24 (OPEN QUESTION narrowed, for Rama), and leaves the dead branch's own defects to this part.
  Issue parts, on the basis named: (ii) within the branch, a weakness the code itself shows: a dict item breaks the
  clean-up that FIX-165f states, against FIX-069's "both ... formats"; (iii) its status `REJECTED` differs from its
  own log text, FIX-048's and Record 02's `REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL`; (iv) one condition, a full queue, has three status
  forms across the three puts (`QUEUE_FULL`, bare `REJECTED` with reason `QUEUE_FULL`, `PLACEMENT_FAILED`) (Document
  04, section 5.3: consistency across components; the reason-less third form is EV-06's). "Latent": (ii) and (iii)
  cannot occur under `main.py`; of (iv), the receiver's form is reachable and the FIX-069 form needs a
  `BrokerRateLimitError` and a full queue together. KNOWN DEFECT considered for (ii) and not
  given: (a) holds (the FIX-165f clean-up is the mechanism); (b) holds (indexing a dict by 1 raises `KeyError` whenever a
  dict item meets an exhausted bucket and a full queue); (c) fails: the case cannot arise under the documented set-up
  (no limiter is passed); (d) holds (no log, status or release, against (3) and SP12).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor and latent (basis above): (ii) and (iii) have no effect at the
  documented set-up; (iv) concerns status texts only.
- **Owner:** A06 (the pre-check, its status and the FIX-069 status); A04 (`try_acquire`; RL-18); A01 (`main.py`'s
  wiring); A03 (the status column's vocabulary).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 54; STUDY question 24 (OPEN QUESTION narrowed); EV-13 (the wiring and its intent,

##### PQ-06 — Exceptions raised outside the worker's `try` blocks end in discarded futures, unlogged, with no status and no claim release; a dispatcher that ended would not be noticed or restarted, and nothing reads `is_running()`; several intake handlers swallow errors silently

- **Area and flow:** worker and dispatcher failure: `_process_one_safe`, the unpacking in `_process_one`, the
  dispatcher loop; the silent handlers on the intake side.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors lost; no supervision); Consistency across sources (the
  `fail_fast_no_silent` principle against later "never affects" guards).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) M20 SP12, L167 to L172 (2026-04-17): "Unexpected exception
  in _process_one: logged ERROR with full traceback, signal status PLACEMENT_FAILED, in_flight released, NOT raised
  (worker thread continues)" (IS-75). (2) `_process_one_safe` docstring L406 and banner L402 (2026-04-17): "catches all
  exceptions so workers never die" (IS-79); module docstring L24: "SP12 -- ... all exceptions caught per worker". (3)
  LOCKED-YAML `fail_fast_no_silent`, L434 to L437 (2026-04-17): "Every exception must either be logged with full
  traceback AND raised, OR explicitly handled with documented recovery. NO bare except, NO try/except/log/continue
  patterns that swallow errors" (IS-103). (4) M20 SP4 L49: `is_running() -> bool` as part of the lifecycle. (5) Later
  guards that state silence as intended: `_warm_zones` docstring L362 to L364 (`9f56b07`, 2026-06-27): "Fully guarded
  — never affects dispatch"; `_bump_metric` L681: "metrics must never affect the pipeline". CD-14.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L349 to L350 (`is_running`), L361 to L375, L383 to L399, L405 to
  L480, L675 to L681, L876 to L895, L1226 to L1231.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): `_process_one_safe` catches `Exception` only around
  `_process_one` (L465 to L477); the code before it (L408 to L464, the FIX-007 block included) is outside any handler
  of the method; `_process_one` unpacks the item at L878 to L887, before its own `try` at L895. In the dispatcher's main
  loop only `get` is inside a `try`; `submit` is not (L383 to L390). FACT (search, section 1): no non-test code calls
  the processor's `is_running()`; `_running` changes only in `start()` and `stop()`. Current external context (CPython
  3.12 executor source; `threading`; Built-in Exceptions; section 1) and INTERPRETATION (P04-S055, P04-S056): (a) an
  exception from the unpacking is logged by `_process_one_safe` and `PLACEMENT_FAILED` is attempted, but the claim is
  not released, because `_process_one`'s `finally` was never entered; (b) an exception raised in `_process_one_safe`
  before its `try`, or a `BaseException` that is not an `Exception` (such as `SystemExit`), is caught by the executor's
  `_WorkItem.run` and stored in the future, which the dispatcher discarded: no log line, no status, no release; (c) the
  worker thread survives in every case, as (2) states; (d) if the dispatcher thread ended, nothing would restart it,
  `is_running()` would still return true (and nothing reads it), the uncaught exception would go to standard error by
  `threading.excepthook`, not to the logger (no non-test file sets `threading.excepthook`: search for
  `excepthook`), and the only symptom would be the queue filling until the receiver
  answers 503 and `QUEUE_FULL` (WD-10, WD-12). At the baseline no producer creates a malformed item (P04-S026), the
  only pre-`try` code that can raise is the unreachable FIX-007 branch (PQ-05), and `submit` raises only once the
  executor or the interpreter is shutting down; so (a), (b) and (d) are latent. FACT (re-read): on the intake side
  these handlers swallow an error with no log line: `_warm_zones` (L374 to L375, `except Exception: pass`), the
  `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write in `_process_one_safe` (L476 to L477), the `EXPIRED` alert (L1230 to L1231) and
  `_bump_metric` (L680 to L681); FIX-007's two (L438 to L439, L459 to L460) are in the dead branch.
- **Evidence:** P04-S025, P04-S026, P04-S055, P04-S056; code lines above; search for `is_running`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external
  context. MATCH part: workers never die, as (2) and SP12 state. ISSUE parts: (i) exceptions outside the two `try`
  blocks are neither logged nor turned into a status, and route (a) leaves the claim held until the sweeper evicts it
  (WI-01), against (1) and (3) (CD-14); (ii) the dispatcher has no handler, supervisor or liveness reader (the lifecycle
  getter of (4) has no non-test caller); (iii) the silent handlers at L374 to L375, L476 to L477 and L1230 to L1231
  conflict with (3); for `_warm_zones` and `_bump_metric` the later guards (5) state the silence as intended (a
  conflict between sources, the code following (5)); for the failed `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write and the failed alert no
  source states the silence. "Latent": no route of (i) or (ii) is reachable at the baseline with the producers
  `main.py` wires. KNOWN DEFECT considered for (i) and not given: (a) holds (SP12's catch-all); (b) holds for the case
  (by the scope of `try`, a pre-`try` exception escapes the handler); (c) fails (the case cannot arise under the
  documented set-up: no malformed item, no reachable pre-`try` raise); (d) holds.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent for (i) and (ii); (iii): a failed `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write leaves
  only the earlier ERROR line, not the failed write itself (EV-02, 18.1 point 60). Whether the dispatcher
  ever ended early is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (18.2 item 11).
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor); A14 (no alert path for a dead dispatcher).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 55; CD-14; IS-75, IS-79, IS-103; WI-11 (the receiver's sweeper, likewise
  unsupervised); WD-10, WD-12 (the only symptom); WI-01 (eviction of a kept claim); PQ-05; EV-02 (18.1 point 60, the
  failing status writes) and EV-03 (the wrapper retries only the status, so a held reservation stays held; counted
  there; this record counts only that the wrapper's failed write is swallowed without a log line).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### PQ-07 — A05 IN-08's A06 side: `_sector_for` asks the instrument cache for `sector_for` and `get_sector`, which do not exist, so it returns "UNKNOWN" for every symbol; all four KNOWN DEFECT conditions hold on A06's own sources (provisional)

- **Area and flow:** the processor's only use of the instrument cache (A05 owns the cache): `_sector_for`, called by
  the V3 signal builder and the allocator candidate builder after sizing (A18 owns the use of the value).
- **Issue category:** Logic error (a lookup that can never succeed); Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) Processor constructor comment L176 (root commit `bcf03b5`,
  2026-04-17): `instrument_cache=None, # IC: optional InstrumentCache for lot_size/sector`. (2) `main.py` L3592 (root
  commit, 2026-04-17, A01's file): `instrument_cache=instrument_cache, # IC: lot_size/sector lookup`. (3) The code
  itself: `_sector_for` (L1605 to L1617, `536172c`, 2026-07-12) is written to return the cache's sector for the symbol.
  (4) `docs/audit/f1_trades_sector_16jul2026.md` L29 to L31 and L116 to L134 (2026-07-16): "BUG B ...
  `_sector_for` ... looks for `sector_for`/`get_sector` (neither exists) → always "UNKNOWN""; "DEFERRED with evidence"
  because the two fields it fills are "set-but-never-read (inert)"; "A trivial one-line future fix ... deliberately
  deferred to avoid perturbing the forward-shadow M-S4 baseline" (L133 to L134, added in the check); the canonical
  resolver is `InstrumentCache.sector` (L18 to L22). (5) INTEGRITY-AUDIT L798 (2026-08-01): "Second dead consumer found: `_sector_for` ... no such method
  exists anywhere"; SMAP L941 (2026-07-16): "BUG B (`signal_processor._sector_for` always UNKNOWN)". IS-90. (6) A05's
  IN-08 record: the conditions as read at the interface, "(a) holds ... (b) holds ... (c) holds on code reading ... (d)
  holds ... A06's own sources not having been searched"; "the classification is A06's alone" (P09-S083, P09-S084). No
  source states that "UNKNOWN" was the intended result. Note (added in the check): the intent-sources file records
  that no A06 source states what `_sector_for` was meant to return (IS-90; narrowed in the cross-part check to "no
  docstring or comment of `_sector_for`"); source (1), found by this part outside that file (section 1; IS-191 since
  the cross-part check), states the purpose of the cache parameter (a lot-size and sector lookup), not the method's
  return value; its lot-size half describes no processor code (PQ-08 (8)).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L176, L223, L1601, L1605 to L1617, L1644, L1136 to L1168;
  `main.py` L2439 to L2441 (P09-S104), L3473, L3592, L3633 to L3651, L3673 to L3694; `config/system_config.yaml` L565,
  L574 (committed `allocator_mode: "shadow"`, `v3_chain_mode: "shadow"`); `core/instrument_cache.py` L75, L257
  (P09-S096, P09-S104).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (re-read): `_sector_for` returns "UNKNOWN" when the cache is `None`;
  otherwise it tries `getattr(ic, name, None)` for "sector_for" and "get_sector" and calls the first callable result;
  after the loop it returns "UNKNOWN". `InstrumentCache` is declared with no base class (L75), defines `sector` (L257)
  and neither name nor `__getattr__`, and no non-test file defines or assigns either name (P09-S096, P09-S104;
  re-checked: search for `sector_for|get_sector`, the hits outside the processor being documents and one test).
  Current external context (Python "Built-in Functions", `getattr`: "If the named attribute does not exist, default is
  returned if provided"; `callable`; through P09-S104, read 17-Nov-2026, re-opened 18-Nov-2026) and INTERPRETATION: with an
  `InstrumentCache` both lookups give `None`, neither branch runs, and the method returns "UNKNOWN" for every symbol.
  FACT: `main.py` passes the loaded cache unconditionally (L3592); it is `None` only if the load at L2441 raised, a
  case its comment says the start-up check blocks (L2437 to L2438; A01's); with the committed `"shadow"` values `main.py` wires
  the V3 chain and the allocator (L3473, L3633), and `_process_one` then calls `_build_v3_signal` (L1138) and
  `_build_candidate` (L1164) for every signal that passes sizing, each calling `_sector_for` (L1601, L1644).
  INTERPRETATION: under the documented (repository) set-up the lookup runs and always fails; the V3 signal's and the
  allocator candidate's `sector` are always "UNKNOWN". Whether either field is read by any decision is A18's; the
  16-Jul record says neither is read (HISTORICAL; not checked here).
- **Evidence:** P04-S045; P09-S066, P09-S083 to P09-S085, P09-S096, P09-S104; P05-S037; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT (provisional). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The four
  conditions, on A06's own sources: (a) holds: `_sector_for` is the mechanism for taking a symbol's sector from the
  cache; (b) holds: by `getattr` semantics with a default, a name the object does not define gives the default, so the
  mechanism fails for certain whenever an `InstrumentCache` is wired and a builder runs; (c) holds: the code (3) and
  the comments (1) and (2) say the cache is passed for the sector lookup, and the case arises under the documented
  set-up (cache passed unconditionally; both builders reached at the committed `"shadow"` values); (d) holds: every
  source found, (4) and (5) included, treats the always-"UNKNOWN" result as a fault ("BUG B", "dead consumer"), and none
  states it as intended; the 16-Jul record's deliberate deferral (L133 to L134) postpones the repair for a stated
  reason and calls it a "fix", so it accepts the fault for a time but does not state "UNKNOWN" as the intended result
  (INTERPRETATION; recorded in the check so that the review can weigh it under (d)). A05's IN-08 keeps its own record ("ISSUE; FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL"; treatment "A05
  treatment group TG12 (AGREED, 17-Nov-2026)"; status "AGREED"; owner field "A06 (the call sites and the
  classification); A18 (the use of the value); A05 (the interface ...)"); this record is A06's classification of its own
  code, which A05 left to A06, and is provisional until ChatGPT's review and Rama.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established by A06: whether the two fields reach any decision is A18's
  (the legacy record's "inert" is HISTORICAL); the effective flags and whether the effective `instrument_cache` was an
  `InstrumentCache` or `None` are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (18.2 items 2 and 20).
- **Owner:** A06 (the call site and the classification); A18 (the use of the value); A05 (the cache's interface).
- **Cross-reference:** A05 IN-08 (allocated here); 18.1 point 98 (allocated to part XA: "A05 IN-08's A06 side under
  the four-condition rule"): covered by this record, which part XA may cite rather than count again; 18.1 point 100
  (part XA, IN-08's partly covered side, completed here); 18.2 item 20 (part XA); IS-90.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; the KNOWN DEFECT is provisional and goes to ChatGPT's review and the issue
  discussion with Rama.

##### PQ-08 — Texts in the processor and its configuration that the code contradicts, not counted elsewhere: the "naive datetime" docstring, the plain-dict map comments, the 60-second default, the resume paths' ":607 60s expiry", the "60s signal expiry" allocator comments, and the 60 s and 90 s figures of P9a, the exception docstring and Record 02; the cache parameter's "lot_size" purpose (added in the check)

  zone of the queued time, IC-27).

  (1) `_process_one` docstring L874 (root commit, 2026-04-17): "triggered_at: naive datetime (IST)" (IS-80) against
  the later FIX-022 (IS-41, 2026-05-13: naive times made IST-aware at parsing): the queued time is IST-aware
  (P04-S038; P02-S055). IC-27; the later source governs; the docstring was not updated.

  passes `dict[str, ScannerEntry]` pydantic models (P04-S006); the lookup accepts both forms (L939 to L942), so the
  text misdescribes the object without effect.
  (3) The constructor's own default `signal_expiry_sec: int = 60` (L175, 2026-04-17), in line with WR6's "default 60"
  (IS-87): the 600 s decision of 2026-04-28 (IS-84, IC-10) is applied because `main.py` passes the configured value
  (L3614); the code default is dormant under `main.py`. (The schema comment "(default 60)" is WI-20's and is not
  counted here.)
  (4) `continue_from_retest` docstring L2378 and comment L2396 (`9f56b07`, 2026-06-27): "NOT the :607 60s expiry" and
  "NO 60s expiry — park-and-resume" (IS-83): the check is at L917 and uses `expiry_sec` (600 in the file); the
  statement that the resume path has no age check is correct (PQ-13).

  vs the 60s signal expiry", "Both far below the 60s signal expiry"; `docs/v3/V3_STEP6_PORTFOLIO_ALLOCATOR_PLAN.md`

  IC-10 records the thresholds (60, 90, 300 and 600 s): the 300 s commit of 2026-04-28 names the 60 s it replaces and

  Python version (unpinned; the executor internals relied on are as published for 3.12, and `cancel_futures` needs
  3.9 or later, P04-S063). (4) Whether production ran any processor commit after `7d4970a` (the evidence lines of
  `8d27fdb`, `853a3a1`, `056905b`; P04-S035, P04-S053; part EV holds 18.2 item 8's processor side). (5) Narrowed for
  this part (FACT at the interface, A08's file `sr_detector/zone_warmer.py` L84 to L99, L103 to L119, `9f56b07`,
  2026-06-27; current external context, `queue`): `ZoneWarmer.enqueue` holds a lock only to test and add a set member
  and puts with `put_nowait`, dropping the symbol with a WARNING when its queue is full; the slow warm-up runs on the
  warmer's own thread outside that lock. INTERPRETATION: at the baseline code the dispatcher's `_warm_zones` call does
  not wait on the warmer's queue or its fetches, only on that short lock and a log call; whether the warmer was enabled
  (built only with the V2 S&R or structure-exit flags, P04-S015) stays UNKNOWN. This bears on WD-10 and WD-16 (3),
  whose classification it does not change.

## 9. Part EV — Event creation, persistence and the handoff map

**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (`970aabf`)
**Scope:** STUDY sections 8 and 13 (part P05): when the `signals` row is written, its fields and statuses, the
transaction and commit order, failure between steps, restart (no re-read of rows), the handoff contract at both
candidate lines (the line itself stays OPEN FOR RAMA; neither reading is adopted), the later call-outs at the interface
only (A07, A08, A09, A10, A14, A18: interface facts, logic not audited), and A06's own side of the A04 limiter wiring
(A04 RL-14, RL-16 and RL-18 keep A04's classification). Storage implementation is A03's; the receiver's `QUEUED`
rows with no queue item are part WD's record WD-05 and the audit counts against stored rows part WI's record WI-18
(cited, not counted again). Direction questions 20, 24 and 25 (and 26 to 28, which apply to every part). 28
allocated items (this AUDIT, sections 2.3, 2.4 and 4).

(P09-S061) | confirmed: on `TIMEOUT` the processor drops its handle without releasing it (MATCH with A-2), unless the kill-switch call or the `TIMEOUT` write raises first | EV-21 (MATCH); EV-04 (ISSUE); CS-08 not counted again |
