### 9.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### EV-01 — Status writes are unconditional: a later write replaces the stored status and reason, so a final status can be overwritten; no time and no trail of changes is kept, against P16 and SP9

- **Area and flow:** the processor's `signals` status writes on every path (FCFS, gate, retest, allocator); the A06
  side of A03 P-02 ("the pipeline's status sequence and whether a final status can be overwritten").
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a status updated inconsistently; a final status overwritten); Intended against
  actual (documented behaviour not implemented); Consistency across components (conflicting sources resolved one way).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) P16, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L362 (2026-04-17): "Every signal_id
  assigned at webhook gets a final status before the system 'forgets' it"; "Every status writes a row to signals table
  with timestamp and reason" (IS-98). (2) M20 SP9, `module_20_signal_processor.txt` L136 to L146 (2026-04-17): write on
  "EVERY state change" through `update_signal_status(signal_id, status, reason, ts)`; "Audit P16: every signal_id has
  final status + ts + reason"; processor docstring L22 (IS-99). (3) The store method, `core/state_store.py` L1413 to
  L1420 (2026-04-17, A03's file): `ts` "not stored (no updated_at col)"; called "so every signal_id has a terminal
  status + reason" (IS-100). (1) and (2) against (3) are IC-13 (same date; neither later). (4) M30 SPW3 step 9,
  `module_30_signal_processor_wiring.txt` L110 to L111 (2026-04-17): "DO NOT overwrite PASSED with PROCESSING
  mid-pipeline" (IS-101). (5) SCHEMA L36 to L38 (2026-04-17): "Immutable log of EVERY incoming webhook signal ... P16
  visibility: every signal has a final status" (IS-102). (6) `core/evidence_contract.py` L7 to L11 (`d2aa35d`,
  2026-09-10; IS-197, added in the cross-part check): "`signals.status` keeps a terminal verdict". No source says whether a
  write should test the current status (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 59). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `core/state_store.py` L1408 to L1426 (one `UPDATE signals SET status = ?, rejection_reason = ?
  WHERE signal_id = ?`, no predicate on the current status, no `rowcount` read); the processor's 26 call sites
  (section 1), none passing `ts`; L897, L2034, L2393 (`PROCESSING` first on each path); L1486 to L1525 (FIX-069
  re-queue); L1193 to L1246 (the FCFS handlers); L589 to L612 (`_evidence_capture` catches every `Exception`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S003, P05-S007, P05-S008): each write replaces the status and
  sets `rejection_reason` to the given reason or NULL; the table has no column for the time of a change and keeps no
  earlier status. FACT and INTERPRETATION, the routes on which a later write replaces a status: (i) the FIX-069 retry
  writes `PROCESSING` over `RESERVED` (L897) and runs intake and screening again (P05-S009; P04-S052); (ii) the gate
  and retest resumptions write `PROCESSING` over the parked `GATE_*` or `RETEST_*` status, and over whatever status
  the row holds, with no check (L2034, L2393; A03 Q-11's A06 side; EV-11); (iii) after a successful FIX-069 re-queue
  whose reservation release raises, the generic handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` while the item is in the queue, and
  the retry then writes `PROCESSING` over that final status (EV-05 part (c)). INTERPRETATION (L1193 to L1246, L589 to
  L612): on the FCFS path, once a handler's final write has succeeded, every later statement is guarded (the releases
  L1214 and L1240, the notifier L1226), takes a lock or cannot raise (the evidence call), so no second processor write
  follows a successful final write there; and the designed sequence never writes `PROCESSING` after `PASSED`.
  INTERPRETATION (P05-S008, P05-S012): a reason-less write (`PROCESSING`, `RESERVED`, `PROCESSED`,
  `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`) clears the reason the screener stored with `PASSED` and any earlier reason; only the last
  status and reason survive.
- **Evidence:** P05-S003, P05-S007 to P05-S012, P05-S043, P05-S044, P05-S053; P04-S052; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. ISSUE parts: (a) P16 and SP9 ask for the
  time and reason of each change; the store keeps one row updated in place without time, as source (3) says: a
  conflict of same-date sources (IC-13) that the code resolves on the store's side; the missing time stamp is A03
  P-02's and is not counted again; A06's own side is that the pipeline keeps no trail of its statuses and clears
  earlier reasons; (b) a final status can be overwritten on routes (ii) and (iii), so the row can stop showing the
  terminal verdict that sources (1), (3), (5) and (6) rely on. MATCH part: on the FCFS path a successful final write is
  not followed by another processor write, and `PROCESSING` is never written after `PASSED`, as SPW3 step 9 says
  (EV-18). KNOWN DEFECT not considered: for (a) the sources conflict, so condition (d) cannot hold; for (b) no
  mechanism protects a final status (condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: routes (ii) and (iii) need a resumption of a finished
  signal or a failing release; whether either occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (EV-14).
- **Owner:** A06 (the status sequence and the overwrites); A03 (the method and the missing time column, P-02); A07 and
  A08 (the gate, screener and retest values).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 59; STUDY question 29; A03 P-02, Q-11; IC-13; IS-98 to IS-102; EV-05, EV-11,
  EV-15, EV-18.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 29 asks which was meant (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### EV-02 — A failing status write leaves a status that misdescribes the signal: `PLACEMENT_FAILED` for a signal never checked or for an order handed to the placer; `PROCESSING` after a screener reject; `PASSED` after an allocator drop (case (d), added in the check)

- **Area and flow:** the processor's handlers when `update_signal_status` itself raises; the processor never re-reads
  the row.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (errors only logged; no safe recovery); Flow and state (a status updated wrongly
  or not at all).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) M20 SP9 L138 to L143 (2026-04-17): `PROCESSING` "set when _process_one
  starts", `PROCESSED` "set after step 9 (full pipeline success)", `PLACEMENT_FAILED` "set on step 8 exception" (IS-99).
  (2) M20 SP12 L167 to L172 (2026-04-17): "Unexpected exception in _process_one: logged ERROR with full traceback,
  signal status PLACEMENT_FAILED, in_flight released, NOT raised" (IS-75). (3) P16 (IS-98): a final status for every
  id; the `fail_fast_no_silent` principle, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L434 to L437: "NO try/except/log/continue
  patterns that swallow errors" (IS-103). INTERPRETATION (not an IC of the intent-sources file): (1) and (2), of the
  same date, give `PLACEMENT_FAILED` different scopes, a placement exception (SP9) or any unexpected exception (SP12).
  No source addresses a failed status write (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 60; STUDY question 31). All
  HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L895 to L897, L1233 to L1246, L465 to L477 (the `PROCESSING`
  write and the handlers); L1446 to L1463, L1540 to L1547 (`PROCESSED` after `place()`); L1008 to L1022 (the processor
  acts on the screener's result); `screening/secondary_screener.py` L604 to L637 (A07's file; interface only: its own
  write inside a `try` that logs and continues); L1706 to L1737 (`reject_prepared`; added in the check).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (P05-S016, P05-S020, P05-S021, P05-S053): (a) if the
  `PROCESSING` write raises, no check runs; the handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; if the store accepts that write, the
  row reads `PLACEMENT_FAILED` for a signal that reached no check, no screening and no broker; if the store refuses
  every write, the row stays `QUEUED`; (b) if the `PROCESSED` write raises after `place()` returned, the handler
  writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with the store error as reason (no reservation is then held, L1461), counts
  `PLACEMENT_FAILED` in `_stats` and skips the S&R observer, while `entries_placed` was already counted (L1463), the
  placer holds the order (A10) and, where the recorder is wired, `P1_ACCEPT` was already written (EV-09); (c) if the
  screener's write fails, the row keeps `PROCESSING`; after a screener reject the run ends with the row `PROCESSING`.
  (d) (added in the check; FACT, L1711 to L1737) `reject_prepared`, the allocator's drop of a candidate before
  admission, writes `REJECTED_<reason>` inside a `try` that logs ERROR ("reject_prepared status write failed") and
  continues, then counts the reject and releases the receiver's claim; INTERPRETATION: if that write fails, the row
  keeps the screener's `PASSED` for a signal the processor has dropped (latent: reached only under
  `allocator_mode: "enforce"`, committed `"shadow"`, P05-S041; EV-11). The `QUEUED` row with no queue item after a
  failed `QUEUE_FULL` mark is WD-05's. Current external context (Python
  `sqlite3`; SQLite "BEGIN TRANSACTION", section 1): a connection waits up to `timeout` (30 s here, P05-S004) and then
  raises `OperationalError`, and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` "might fail with SQLITE_BUSY if another write transaction is
  already active"; INTERPRETATION: case (a) can arise from lock contention alone. FACT (P05-S006): the processor never
  reads `signals`, so nothing corrects these rows.
- **Evidence:** P05-S004, P05-S006, P05-S016, P05-S020, P05-S021, P05-S053; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Case (a) follows SP12 literally (a MATCH
  part) but misdescribes the signal by SP9's meaning of `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; case (b) records a failure for an order the
  placer accepted, against SP9 and P16's visibility; case (c) leaves a non-final status for a rejected signal, against
  P16; the swallowed write is A07's (IS-103's principle bears on A07's code), and A06's side is that the processor goes
  on without the status being stored; case (d) is the same misdescription from a write the processor itself swallows,
  against P16 and IS-103's "NO try/except/log/continue patterns that swallow errors" (latent). KNOWN DEFECT considered
  and not given: (a) holds (handlers exist); (b) holds by Python semantics whenever the write raises (P05-S053); (c)
  fails: neither the code nor any source says it handles a failed status write; (d) fails for case (a) (SP12 is met).
  The same analysis holds for case (d).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: each case needs a store failure; whether any occurred is
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (EV-14); case (d) is also latent at the committed values.
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor's handlers and `reject_prepared`); A07 (the screener's swallowed write); A18 (the
  allocator that calls `reject_prepared`); A03 (store failure modes); A10 (the order the placer holds in case (b)).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 60; STUDY question 31; IS-75, IS-98, IS-99, IS-103; WD-05; EV-03, EV-09, EV-11.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 31 bears on treatment.

##### EV-03 — The reservation release depends on a status write that precedes it: a failing reject or `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write leaves the reservation held, and the safe wrapper retries only the status

- **Area and flow:** the processor's reject and generic handlers when a reservation is held (FCFS, `admit_prepared`,
  gate, retest); the A06 side of A03 P-11 point (3) and of A04 CS-05.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no safe recovery); Flow and state (capital held for a signal not so marked).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The processor's comments L1212 ("Release reservation if we had one", lines
  2026-04-17) and L1407 to L1409 (lines 2026-07-12, `536172c`, the extraction commit; the text from FIX-190 Bug G,
  `575a1dc`, 2026-06-19: the throttle reject "Released reservation on reject so the slot frees for a later signal"). (2) M20 SP12 (IS-75): on an unexpected exception the status is set and the claim released;
  it says nothing of reservations. (3) A04 CS-05 (P09-S060): "which reservation, if any, is then released is A06's and
  A09's to establish". No source states the order of the status write and the release, or what follows a failed write
  (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 61; STUDY question 31). All HISTORICAL except (3), a project record.
- **Actual code path:** L1193 to L1217 (the reject handler: the write L1201 first, the release L1213 to L1217 after);
  L1233 to L1243 (the generic handler: write L1238, release L1239 to L1243); L1661 to L1688 (`admit_prepared`); L2309
  to L2352 (gate); L2594 to L2625 (retest); L465 to L477 (the FCFS wrapper writes a status only); L1340, L1368 (the
  target rejects raised while the reservation is held); L1328 to L1344 (the `RESERVED` write inside the lock).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: in every processor handler that can hold a reservation, the status
  write is the first effective statement, unguarded, and the release follows it. INTERPRETATION (P05-S017, P05-S018,
  P05-S053): if the write raises, the exception leaves the handler, the release is skipped, and on the FCFS path the
  wrapper retries only the status (L472 to L477); on the gate path `main.py`'s worker only logs the exception (L872 to
  L877); the retest and allocator callers are A08's and A18's. So (a) a target reject (`UNKNOWN_TGT_METHOD`,
  `TGT_DISTANCE_TOO_SMALL`, `REJECTED_NO_ATR_DATA`) whose write fails leaves its reservation unreleased by the
  processor; (b) any exception reaching a generic handler with a reservation set (a failed `RESERVED` write, which is
  A03 P-11's case; a failing late-abort release; an exception from `place()`) leaves it unreleased when the
  `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write fails too. INTERPRETATION: the two failures are correlated, because a store that refuses one
  write (a lock wait ending in `OperationalError`, section 1) can refuse the next. What then happens to the held
  reservation is A09's.
- **Evidence:** P05-S010, P05-S017, P05-S018, P05-S053; P09-S053, P09-S060; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Basis: the code's own comments state that a
  reject releases the reservation "so the slot frees" (source (1)); the release is made conditional on an unrelated
  store write succeeding, so on a store failure capital stays reserved for a signal the processor has abandoned
  (Document 04, section 5.3: failure handling, no safe recovery). The A06 sides of A03 P-11 and A04 CS-05 are
  established here and those items are not counted again; this record counts the ordering finding. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered and not given: (a) holds; (b) holds by Python semantics whenever the write raises; (c) fails: neither the
  code nor a source says it handles a failed status write; (d) not established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: it needs a store failure while a reservation is held;
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED whether one occurred.
- **Owner:** A06 (the order of statements in the handlers); A09 (the reservation's fate); A03 (P-11, two transactions).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 61 (first and second parts); STUDY question 31; A03 P-11; A04 CS-05; EV-02, EV-04,
  EV-05.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### EV-04 — Placement timeout: a failing kill-switch call or `TIMEOUT` write sends the reservation to the `PLACEMENT_FAILED` handler, which releases it, against A-2's "do NOT release"

- **Area and flow:** the processor's `BrokerTimeoutError` branch on the FCFS, gate and retest paths; the A06 side of
  A04 CS-08.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (the guarded outcome reached through an error path); Flow and state (a second
  capital owner; a status that misdescribes the signal).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) A-2 comment L1465 to L1477 (lines 2026-07-12, `536172c`; A-2 dated
  02-Jul-2026): mark the signal `TIMEOUT` and "relinquish the reservation handle WITHOUT releasing it (recovery owns it
  — SINGULAR ownership)" (IS-105). (2) The gate-path comment L2270 to L2275 and the retest-path comment L2566 to L2570
  (`fd09a38`, 2026-07-02; IS-198, added in the cross-part check): an ambiguous timeout "must NOT fall through to the
  PLACEMENT_FAILED handler (which would RELEASE the reservation that order_placer KEPT for the UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT trade
  — a second capital owner)". (3) The A-2 commit message `fd09a38` (IS-173; the later side of IC-25). No source
  differs. All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** L1464 to L1484 (`record_api_failure` L1478 to L1479, the `TIMEOUT` write L1480 to L1482, the
  handle cleared L1483); L1188 to L1191 (the `finally` copies `_ac.reservation_id`); L1233 to L1243; `admit_prepared`
  L1681 to L1688 (the same branch of the shared `_admit_and_place`; added in the check); gate L2269 to L2282 with L2340
  to L2352; retest L2565 to L2577 with L2616 to L2625.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the handle is cleared only after the kill-switch call and the
  `TIMEOUT` write. INTERPRETATION (P05-S019, P05-S053): if either raises, the exception, raised inside the `except
  BrokerTimeoutError` clause, is not handled by the sibling clauses and leaves with the reservation still set; the
  generic handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` and, if that write succeeds, releases the reservation as
  `"placement_failed"`; `admit_prepared`, the gate and the retest paths do the same: the fall-through that source (2)
  says must not happen. Current external context (Python tutorial, "Defining Clean-up Actions", 3.15.2, read
  18-Nov-2026): "An exception could occur during execution of an except or else clause. Again, the exception is
  re-raised after the finally clause has been executed" (with P05-S053).
  The row then reads `PLACEMENT_FAILED` while the placer's trade is `UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT` (A10) and the order may be live
  at the broker. On the normal path (no failure) the code does what the sources say (EV-21).
- **Evidence:** P05-S019, P05-S023, P05-S053; P09-S061; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Against IS-105, the two A-2 comments and
  IS-173. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the branch exists to keep the reservation); (b) fails: the
  fall-through itself follows by Python semantics whenever the case occurs, but the release that the comments name as
  the harm follows only if the next `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write succeeds, so the outcome is not certain; (c) not established: the comments name the fall-through as what the branch prevents, not a failing write or
  kill-switch call as a case it handles; (d) holds.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: it needs a timeout and then a failure of the kill-switch
  call or of the store; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED whether this occurred.
- **Owner:** A06 (the statement order of the branch); A13 (`record_api_failure`); A10 (the trade state); A09 (the
  reservation); A12 and A04 (the recovery, which A04 CS-08 records never runs).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 61 (third part); A04 CS-03, CS-08; IC-25; IS-105, IS-173; EV-03, EV-21.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### EV-05 — Two release calls for one reservation: after a `BrokerError` from `place()` (including a client-side limit that FIX-069 re-queues), after a failing late-abort release, and after a failing release that follows a successful FIX-069 re-queue (which also leaves the signal queued while its row reads `PLACEMENT_FAILED`)

- **Area and flow:** reservation release around placement on the processor's paths; the A06 side of A04 CS-05.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a release made twice; a status updated inconsistently; the same signal processed
  again after being recorded as failed); Failure handling.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) A-2 (IS-105): "SINGULAR ownership" of the reservation (stated for a timeout).
  (2) The placer's `BrokerError` handler, `orders/order_placer.py` L1466 to L1475 (comment L1467 to L1471, lines
  2026-04-19: "non-429 BrokerError = single attempt ... just FAILED + release"), its `_handle_placement_failure`
  docstring L4402 to L4404, "release capital reservation", and the `place()` docstring L893 to L894 (2026-04-17):
  "Raises BrokerError on hard broker failure (after cleanup). signal_processor catches this and marks signal
  PLACEMENT_FAILED" (A10's file; interface only, P05-S052; L893 to L894 added in the check), against the processor's
  L1212 "Release reservation if we had one" and L1407 to L1409 "Released reservation on reject". (3) FIX-069 comment
  L1487 to L1489 and L1521 ("Release reservation but keep lock") and commit `397105d` (IS-82, IS-173): a client-side
  limit is re-queued, the reservation released and the claim kept ("Keep lock held to prevent duplicate admission").
  INTERPRETATION: source (2) gives the processor the status mark after the placer's own clean-up and names no release
  by the processor, but no source says in so many words which module releases after a `BrokerError`, and none speaks
  of a failing release (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 61). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** (a) L1446 to L1461, L1533 to L1536, L1233 to L1243 (and `admit_prepared` L1681 to L1688, which
  shares `_admit_and_place`); gate L2283 to L2288 with L2346 to L2352; retest L2578 to L2581 with L2621 to L2625;
  `orders/order_placer.py` L1403 to L1419 (a broker 429 after the placer's own retries) and L1466 to L1475, L4402 to
  L4404 (interface). (b) L1393 to L1396, L1402 to L1405, L1412 to L1418 (the late-abort releases, in no `try`), then
  L1233 to L1243 (or L1681 to L1688); the same unguarded late-abort releases on the gate path, L2210 to L2212, L2219 to
  L2221, L2227 to L2229, then L2340 to L2352, and on the retest path, L2520 to L2522, L2525 to L2527, L2532 to L2534,
  then L2616 to L2625 (gate and retest lines added in the check). (c) L1501 to L1532 (`ac.requeued = True` L1503; `put`
  L1520; release L1522 to L1524 inside a `try` that catches only `queue.Full`); L1188 to L1191; L1233 to L1243; L1267
  (no claim release when re-queued; `admit_prepared`'s L1699 likewise); L897 (the retry's first write). (d) (added in
  the check) `core/exceptions.py` L243 (`class BrokerRateLimitError(BrokerError)`, A01's file); `broker/zerodha_adapter.py`
  L621 to L622 (the `order` wait before the request, A04's file); `orders/order_placer.py` L1344 to L1475 (the
  placer's handlers, the last `except BrokerError` at L1466); processor L1486 to L1525 (the FIX-069 re-queue and its
  release at L1523).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** (a) FACT (P05-S052): for a `BrokerError` from `place()`, the placer's
  failure path calls a release and re-raises; the processor's handler, with the handle still set (L1461 not reached),
  then releases the same reservation after its `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write; this includes a broker 429 that the placer has
  retried and then failed (L1403 to L1419) and a client-side limit that FIX-069 no longer re-queues (`retry_count >= 3`
  or a full queue). (b) FACT (P05-S017 for the FCFS lines; re-read in the check for the gate and retest lines): if a
  late-abort release raises, the handle is not cleared and the reject is not raised; the generic handler writes
  `PLACEMENT_FAILED` instead of `REJECTED_KILL_SWITCH_LATE`, `REJECTED_SHUTDOWN` or `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` and calls
  a release again; the gate and retest paths have the same three unguarded releases before their own generic handlers.
  (c) FACT (re-read for this part; the STUDY records only that L1523's `try` catches `queue.Full`, P05-S036 row 14): if the
  re-queue `put` succeeds and the release at L1523 raises, the exception leaves the branch with `ac.requeued` True and
  the handle set; the generic handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` and calls a release again; the `finally` keeps the
  receiver's claim; the dict is already in the queue. INTERPRETATION: a worker later takes the dict, writes
  `PROCESSING` over `PLACEMENT_FAILED` (EV-01 route (iii)) and runs intake, screening and admission again, so one
  signal is recorded as failed and then processed again. (d) FACT (added in the check; `core/exceptions.py` L243; A04
  RL-02, approved, records as FACT that a client-side wait timeout of `place_order` reaches the signal pipeline
  "re-raised by the placer's `except BrokerError`, L1466 to L1475"): the client-side `BrokerRateLimitError` is a
  `BrokerError` and is none of the classes of the placer's three earlier handlers (`OrderRejectedError`,
  `BrokerRateLimit429Error`, `BrokerTimeoutError`), so the placer's L1466 handler calls `_handle_placement_failure`
  before re-raising it; the processor's FIX-069 branch then re-queues the signal and, at L1523, calls a release for the
  same reservation (when the re-queue is not possible, route (a) applies). Current external context (Python Language
  Reference, "The try statement", 3.15.2, read 18-Nov-2026): "The raised exception matches an except clause whose
  expression evaluates to the class or a non-virtual base class of the exception object". INTERPRETATION: on every
  FIX-069 re-queue the placer has already, by its docstring, marked its trade FAILED and released the reservation that
  the processor then releases again, so FIX-069's "Release reservation but keep lock" is a second release call; what
  the placer's failure path does to its trade row is A10's. In each case one reservation receives two release calls;
  what a second release does is A09's, and which placer paths run is A10's (A04 CS-05).
- **Evidence:** P05-S017, P05-S036 (row 14), P05-S052, P05-S053; P04-S052; P09-S060; A04 RL-02 (the re-raise through
  L1466 to L1475); code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Basis: the code's own single-owner principle
  for reservations (source (1)) and its reject comments (source (2)), against routes that release one reservation
  twice and, in (c), record a signal as failed and then process it again (Document 04, section 5.3: a status updated
  twice or inconsistently; the same item processed again). Part (c) is added by this part; part (d) and the gate,
  retest and `admit_prepared` lines of (a) to (c) are added in the check. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a)
  holds; (b) holds by Python semantics for routes (b) and (c) whenever the release raises, and for routes (a) and (d)
  whenever the placer's failure path runs; (c) fails for routes (b) and (c): no source says the code handles a failing
  release; for routes (a) and (d) the code says it handles the exception (the handlers and FIX-069), but no source
  says which module releases; (d) not established (what a double release does is A09's).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: routes (a) and (d) follow every `BrokerError` placement
  failure, and every FIX-069 re-queue, on which the placer's failure path runs, and their effect depends on A09's
  release; routes (b) and (c) need a failing release. Whether any occurred is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A06 (the processor's handlers and the re-queue branch); A10 (the placer's release); A09 (the effect of a
  second release); A04 CS-05.
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 61 (fourth part) and the added routes (c) and (d); A04 CS-05, RL-02; IS-82, IS-105,
  IS-173; EV-01, EV-03, EV-06, EV-12; 18.1 point 46 (part ET's allocation, disposed there as covered by route (b): the
  unguarded release at the throttle site; not counted in ET).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### EV-06 — The FIX-069 re-queue leaves the row `RESERVED` with no reservation until the retry starts; a re-queue that fails is recorded `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with no reason

- **Area and flow:** the processor's `BrokerRateLimitError` branch (FIX-069) and the row's status while the item
  waits.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (a status not updated); Failure handling (the cause of an abandonment only logged).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) M20 SP9 L141 (2026-04-17): "RESERVED -> set after step 7 success" (IS-99): the
  status names a held reservation. (2) FIX-069 comments L1260 to L1262 and L1487 to L1489 (lines 2026-05-16 and
  2026-07-12) and L1521 ("Release reservation but keep lock - reconciler will retry"): the claim travels with the
  signal, the reservation is released (IS-82, IS-104). (3) P16 (IS-98): a final status "with timestamp and reason".
  No source states the row's status while the item waits (intent-sources file, section 6.1, point 62). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** L1486 to L1532 (no status write; `put` L1520; release L1522 to L1524; on `queue.Full`
  `ac.requeued = False` and a bare `raise`, L1531 to L1532); L897 (the retry's `PROCESSING`); L1233 to L1243 (the
  handler writes `str(exc)` as reason); `core/state_store.py` L1425 (`reason if reason else None`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S009): the re-queue writes no status; the processor calls a
  release of the reservation (L1523; the placer's failure path has already called one, EV-05 route (d), added in the
  check); the row keeps `RESERVED` until the retry's worker writes `PROCESSING`. INTERPRETATION: meanwhile the row
  says `RESERVED` for a signal that holds no reservation; if the process stops then, the row stays `RESERVED` for good
  with no reservation and no order (EV-08). FACT: when the re-queue `put` raises `queue.Full`, the branch re-raises it
  and the handler writes `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with `str(exc)`. Current external context (CPython 3.12 `Lib/queue.py`:
  `put` raises a bare `raise Full`; Python "Built-in Exceptions", 3.15.2: `str()` of an exception with no arguments is
  "the empty string"; section 1); INTERPRETATION: the reason is empty, so the store writes NULL, and a signal abandoned
  because its retry could not be queued is recorded `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with no reason; only the ERROR line L1528 to
  L1530 names the cause. The re-queue writes no evidence record (P05-S027). The retry's lack of delay is part PQ's
  (CD-17).
- **Evidence:** P05-S009, P05-S027; P04-S052; code lines above; the two external pages of section 1.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Basis: SP9's meaning of
  `RESERVED` (source (1)) and P16's reason with each final status (source (3)). Minor: the first part lasts until the
  retry starts (or, after a stop, is EV-08's case); the second concerns the reason text only. The installed Python
  version is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the empty reason holds for the published 3.12 source and the 3.15.2
  documentation. KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no mechanism for the row's status during the wait).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06; A09 (the release).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 62; IS-82, IS-98, IS-99, IS-104; EV-05 part (c), EV-08; part PQ (the retry, CD-17,
  18.1 point 53).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### EV-07 — Status vocabulary: no code writes `TRADED`, which P16, the schema and readers expect (IC-28 resolved for `PROCESSED`); a latent double prefix; the schema's status comment omits statuses A06 writes

- **Area and flow:** the status values A06 code writes to `signals` against the documented vocabularies (writer side;
  the reader-side lists are part XA's, STUDY section 14).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two sources disagreeing on a status); Intended against actual
  (documentation against code).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** IC-28 (all 2026-04-17; readers 2026-07-18): P16 lists `TRADED` (IS-98); the schema
  admits `TRADED` and keeps `trade_id` "set if signal became a trade" (L63, L72; IS-102); A15's readers count `TRADED`
  (IS-140); against M20 SP9 and M30 SPW3 step 9: "If full success: status = PROCESSED" (IS-99, IS-101). Neither side
  names the other. The `_PipelineReject` comment on L99 (2026-04-17): the check is "used as REJECTED_<check> suffix".
  The schema's status comment L50 "QUEUED/PASSED/PROCESSED/REJECTED*/GATE_*/DROPPED_*/SKIPPED_*" and the O2 comment L51
  to L59, both from `4f6c3c5` (2026-06-14, FIX-172; L50's date corrected in the check, IS-102), the commit that also
  wrote the CHECK L60 to L70: an open set whose "stable non-prefixed values are enumerated", "Verified complete against
  the full test suite". All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** L1543, L2290, L2583 (`PROCESSED`); L1201, L2316, L2598 (`REJECTED_` plus the check); L1803 to
  L1806 and L1913 to L1918 (`_PipelineReject("REJECTED_NO_ATR_DATA", ...)` under `atr_fallback_mode == "HALT"`); L177
  (default `"WARN"`); `core/schema.sql` L50 to L70; `orders/order_manager.py` `link_signal_trade` (A10's; interface,
  P05-S012).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S014, P05-S015): no code writes `TRADED`; success is written
  `PROCESSED` and the trade link is the `trade_id` column, set by A10's order manager. INTERPRETATION: a reader that
  counts `TRADED` finds no row written by the code at the baseline (A15's readers, interface only). FACT (P05-S013):
  the label `REJECTED_NO_ATR_DATA` is stored as `REJECTED_REJECTED_NO_ATR_DATA`; it is raised only under `HALT`, whose
  code default is `"WARN"` and which the committed file does not set. FACT: the comment L50 omits `PROCESSING`,
  `RESERVED`, `TIMEOUT`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`, `PLACEMENT_FAILED` and `QUEUE_FULL`, which A06 writes and the CHECK
  admits; the same commit wrote the comment and the CHECK. MATCH part: every value A06 writes passes the CHECK (a listed value or `GLOB 'REJECTED*'`), as the O2 comment
  intends.
- **Evidence:** P05-S012 to P05-S015; P10-S173; P11-S016, P11-S057; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. (a) A conflict of same-date sources (IC-28)
  that the code resolves for SP9 and SPW3, leaving `TRADED`, which P16, the schema and the readers rely on, never
  written; (b) the double prefix contradicts the suffix rule of L99 (minor, latent: not reached at the committed
  values); (c) the schema comment omits written statuses (minor; A03's file, a comment). KNOWN DEFECT considered for
  (b) and not given: (a) holds (the suffix rule); (b) holds (string formatting doubles the prefix whenever `HALT` meets
  an ATR method); (c) not established: the committed set-up does not reach it; (d) not established: no source names the
  stored value. For (a) the sources conflict, so condition (d) cannot hold.
- **Severity or impact where established:** (a) not established (how A15's reports present a zero `TRADED` count is
  A15's); (b) latent and (c) minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06 (the writer side); A15 (the readers); A03 (the schema comment); A10 (the `trade_id` link).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 63; STUDY question 30; IC-28; IS-98, IS-99, IS-101, IS-102, IS-140; EV-14 (18.2 item
  17); part XA (the vocabulary lists, STUDY section 14).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 30 asks which status was meant (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### EV-08 — A stop leaves rows `PROCESSING`, `PASSED`, `RESERVED` or parked; only earlier-day `PROCESSING` rows are swept, nothing re-reads rows at start-up and such rows are never deleted

- **Area and flow:** the statuses a stop leaves behind and what later closes them (the A06 side; the cleanup is A12's
  file, read at the interface; `QUEUED` rows with no queue item are WD-05's and are not counted again).
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (no safe recovery on restart); Flow and state (a status not updated).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) P16 (IS-98): every id "gets a final status before the system 'forgets' it";
  SCHEMA L38: "every signal has a final status" (IS-102). (2) The cleanup's docstring, `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` L6
  (A12's file; the docstring 2026-05-31, `25c4a77`, but L6 itself 2026-07-14, `501d14f`, the M-SC3 commit; date
  corrected in the check, IS-141): "Mark stale PROCESSING signals as EXPIRED" (IS-141); its M-SC3 comment L110 to L112
  (`501d14f`, 2026-07-14; IS-218, added in the cross-part check): the old filter used a status "the pipeline NEVER persists
  ... → dead code, stuck signals never cleaned. Filter the real in-flight status." INTERPRETATION: the stated aim was to
  clean stuck in-flight signals, and `PROCESSING` was taken as "the real in-flight status". (3) IC-30: the docstring's
  "(>7 days)" against the same file's later "default 90" (A12's file). No source names a mechanism for `QUEUED`,
  `QUEUE_FULL`, `PASSED` or `RESERVED` (intent-sources file, section 6.2, question 37). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** the processor's writes, each its own transaction (P05-S009, P05-S022); `scripts/eod_cleanup.py`
  L105 to L127 (`UPDATE signals SET status = 'EXPIRED' WHERE status = 'PROCESSING' AND SUBSTR(triggered_at, 1, 10) <
  ?`); no start-up re-read (P04-S020); the prune (P11-S034).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (P05-S022, P05-S023): a hard stop leaves the row at its last
  committed status: `PROCESSING`, the screener's `PASSED`, `RESERVED` or a parked `GATE_*`/`RETEST_*` (and `QUEUED`,
  WD-05). FACT: the cleanup turns only rows still `PROCESSING` with an earlier trigger date into `EXPIRED`; same-day
  `PROCESSING`, `PASSED` and `RESERVED` rows are not touched; no start-up code re-reads `signals`; the prune deletes
  only `EXPIRED`, `DUPLICATE` and `REJECTED*` rows with no trade, so these rows are never deleted (P11-S033, P11-S034).
  INTERPRETATION: `PASSED` and `RESERVED` are in-flight statuses of the same pipeline (P05-S009), so the reaper meant
  for "stuck signals" covers one of the three; and a `RESERVED` row can stand for a signal whose order was sent before
  the stop (P05-S023) or, after a FIX-069 wait, for one with no reservation and no order (EV-06): the row cannot tell
  which.
- **Evidence:** P05-S009, P05-S022, P05-S023; P04-S020; P11-S033, P11-S034; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Against source (1) and the aim source (2)
  states. IC-30 is recorded at the interface (A12's file; owner A12). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: condition
  (a) fails for `PASSED` and `RESERVED` (no mechanism targets them).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: it needs a stop during processing; how often the process
  stopped is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (18.2 item 13).
- **Owner:** A06 (the statuses the pipeline leaves); A12 (the cleanup and its docstring); A03 (retention); A09, A10 and
  A12 (reservations and orders across a restart).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 64; STUDY question 37; IS-98, IS-102, IS-141; IC-30; WD-05; EV-06; part XA
  (retention; the three date derivations, P11-S032).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 37 bears on treatment (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### EV-09 — The evidence recorder adds a synchronous `fsync` under one process-wide lock on the worker before every placement and in each reject handler; the processor still says "never blocks admission", which the recorder's later docstring withdraws; whether it ran in production is unknown

- **Area and flow:** the forward evidence capture at A06's call sites (`P1_ACCEPT`, `P2_REJECT`); the recorder is
  A15's.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documentation against code); Consistency across components (two
  docstrings disagreeing).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The processor's banner L557 to L558 and `_evidence_capture` docstring L583
  (`8d27fdb`, 2026-09-10 19:07 IST): "OBSERVER — never affects a decision"; "⛔ NEVER raises, ⛔ never blocks
  admission" (IS-106). (2) The recorder's docstring, `core/evidence_contract.py` L495 to L501 (`d646347`, 2026-09-10
  20:48 IST; IS-197, added in the cross-part check): "It is NOT free, and the first build's "never blocks trading" overstated
  it: the append runs SYNCHRONOUSLY on the calling (trading) thread -- open + write + fsync under one process-wide lock";
  "The VMs' volumes are UNMEASURED"; whether the cost "belongs on the decision path ... is an OPEN item for review".
  Source (2) is later and names the earlier claim; source (1) was not updated. (3) The module docstring L3 to L11
  (`d2aa35d`, 2026-09-10): "Nothing here may change a single trading decision"; the purpose is "the decision context at
  the moment of the decision". (4) The `853a3a1` message: "Batch 1: NOT deployed to either VM" (a runtime statement;
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED as a fact). All HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** L1420 to L1445 (`P1_ACCEPT` inside the `try` that calls `place()`, just before it); L1202 to
  L1211, L1663 to L1672, L1716 to L1727, L2319 to L2328, L2599 to L2606, L443 to L455 (`P2_REJECT`); L582 to L612;
  `core/evidence_contract.py` L445 (`_write_lock`), L488 to L600; `main.py` L3248 to L3279, L3622 (one recorder,
  `enabled` default True, L439).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S026 to P05-S029): each capture is appended with `write`, `flush`
  and `os.fsync` under the recorder's one lock, on the calling worker thread; every failure is caught and cannot change
  a status, a reservation or a placement. INTERPRETATION: the recorder cannot alter a decision (a MATCH part with
  sources (1) and (3)), but it puts a disk sync and a lock shared by every worker and the screener on the path to
  `place()`, so "never blocks admission" does not hold; the delay on the legacy hosts is UNKNOWN (source (2)'s own
  note). FACT (P05-S027): no record is written for `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, `TIMEOUT`, `PROCESSED`, `PROCESSED_NO_PLACER`,
  the re-queue or the divert; no source asks for them (source (3) aims at the decision, and these follow it): REJECTED
  as an issue. FACT (section 1): every processor change after `7d4970a` is evidence capture. INTERPRETATION: whether
  production ran the recorder is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (P05-S025; D-21).
- **Evidence:** P05-S024 to P05-S029; code lines above; `git log` of the evidence commits (section 1).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH and UNKNOWN parts. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Basis:
  the processor's docstring states a property that the later recorder docstring withdraws and the code does not have;
  the legacy left the cost open. Minor: the effect is a delay, not a changed decision, and its size is not established.
  KNOWN DEFECT not considered (no failing mechanism).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor (basis above).
- **Owner:** A06 (the call sites and the processor's docstring); A15 (the recorder); A17 (deployment).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 65; STUDY question 32; 18.2 item 8 (the evidence commits; the receiver's WR4b is
  WI-24); IS-106; EV-02 (an `ACCEPTED` record beside a `PLACEMENT_FAILED` row).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 32 bears on treatment.

##### EV-11 — The four other entry points into the processor run outside its executor: uncounted, not awaited by `stop()`, with no guard against a second resumption; their bypass of the age check, screening and claim release is as stated

- **Area and flow:** `continue_from_gate`, `continue_from_retest`, `admit_prepared` and `reject_prepared` (A06's code;
  the gate is A07's, the retest monitor A08's, the allocator A18's); the A06 sides of A03 Q-11 and A01 B-28.
- **Issue category:** Flow and state (the same item processed twice; a route that skips a guard another route
  applies); Failure handling (work not awaited at shutdown).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The gate docstring L2011 to L2026 (lines 2026-04-17 and 2026-05-14): Steps 1
  to 4 "are bypassed. Kill-switch and market-window are re-checked as a last-mile gate. in_flight tracking is NOT
  released here -- the EntryGate already manages it" (IS-83). (2) The retest docstring L2376 to L2384 and comment L2396
  (`9f56b07`, 2026-06-27): the gates are re-checked, "NOT the :607 60s expiry -- this is a park-and-resume" (IS-83).
  (3) L213 to L216 (`132e571`, 2026-08-01): "the two resume-path dispatch sites are production-unreachable and
  deliberately uncounted" (IS-91; the effect-telemetry counter). (4) L2236 to L2238 (`3fc1d2e`, 2026-09-10): "DORMANT
  today: nothing calls EntryGate.add(), so only gate_state rows rehydrated at boot can reach it". (5) The
  `admit_prepared` docstring L1647 to L1650 (`536172c`, 2026-07-12): it "mirrors _process_one's reject/exception
  handlers + finally cleanup (R5 migration scaffolding ...)". (6) M20 SP4 and the `stop` docstring L327 to L330
  (2026-04-17): `stop()` waits "for in-flight tasks to complete" (IS-73, IS-77). (7) A03 Q-11's question (P09-S055):
  whether the resume paths guard against a second resumption of the same signal. No source states such a guard, or
  that these paths are meant to be counted or awaited. All HISTORICAL except (7), a project record.
- **Actual code path:** L2010 to L2370, L2375 to L2640, L1646 to L1737; L463 to L480 (`_active_workers` changed only
  in the FCFS wrapper); L326 to L347 (`stop()` shuts only the processor's own executor); L2027 to L2034 and L2385 to
  L2393 (`PROCESSING` first, only local assignments before it); L2215, L2524 (`_stop_event` before placement);
  `main.py` L865 to L893 (the module-level two-worker gate-release pool; the worker logs an escaping exception, L872 to
  L877).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S043 to P05-S046): the four run on threads outside the
  processor's executor, are not counted in `_active_workers` and have no wrapper like `_process_one_safe`; the gate and
  retest paths have no age check and no screening and release no receiver claim. FACT: `stop()` sets `_stop_event`,
  joins the dispatcher and shuts its own executor; it does not wait for the gate-release pool, the retest monitor's
  thread or the allocator's worker. INTERPRETATION (A01 B-28, `A01_AUDIT_2026-11-05.md` L1244 to L1252: the pool is
  never stopped, its tasks are joined at interpreter exit after the instance lock is released): a `continue_from_gate`
  task can still run after `signal_proc.stop()` has returned and while the lock is free; FACT (L2215): a task that
  reaches the pre-placement check after `stop()` set `_stop_event` is refused `SHUTDOWN`; one already past it completes
  its placement and status writes. FACT (P09-S055): both resumptions write `PROCESSING` first with no check of the
  current status or of an earlier resumption. INTERPRETATION: a second resumption of one signal (A03 Q-11's case: a
  parking row whose release failed, rehydrated at a restart) would overwrite the row's status (EV-01) and run
  reservation and placement again; only the later admission checks (A09's) could stop a second order. FACT and
  INTERPRETATION (P05-S038, P05-S041, P05-S043, P05-S045): at the committed values the retest path is not reached
  (`wait_for_retest_enabled: false`), the gate path is reached only from rehydrated `gate_state` rows, and the enforce
  functions only under `allocator_mode: "enforce"` (committed `"shadow"`); the effective values are UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** P05-S011, P05-S038, P05-S041, P05-S043 to P05-S046; P04-S011, P04-S012, P04-S042; P09-S024,
  P09-S055; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (latent), with MATCH parts. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. MATCH parts: the
  bypass of the age check and screening and the claim handling, as sources (1) and (2) state; the uncounted telemetry,
  as source (3) states. ISSUE parts: (a) no guard against a second resumption (A03 Q-11's A06 side, confirmed; Q-11 not
  counted again); (b) work that `stop()` does not await, so the processor reports itself stopped while a resumption
  can still place, against source (6) (A06's side of A01 B-28, completing the STUDY's partly covered point (b); B-28 not
  counted again). Latent: each route is unreachable or dormant at the committed values. KNOWN DEFECT not considered:
  no guard mechanism exists for (a), and (b) has none (condition (a) fails).
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent (basis above); runtime UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (EV-14 (1)).
- **Owner:** A06 (the entry points and `stop()`); A07 (the gate and its rehydration); A08 (the retest monitor); A18
  (the allocator's worker); A01 (the never-stopped pool; B-28); A03 (Q-11, the release methods).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 67; 18.2 item 12; STUDY question 21 (part PQ's); A03 Q-11; A01 B-28; IS-73, IS-77,
  IS-83, IS-91; EV-01, EV-14.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### EV-12 — MATCH (result changed in the check from UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; block kept in place, not renumbered): a broker 429 and a client-side limit from `place()` take different processor branches, as the sources state: the client-side limit, raised before any request, is re-queued; a broker 429 reaches the processor only after the placer's own retries and is marked `PLACEMENT_FAILED` as terminal

- **Area and flow:** the processor's mapping of rate-limit exceptions from `place()` (the A06 side of A04 CS-03).
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (retry policy); Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) FIX-069 comment L1487 to L1489 (lines 2026-07-12; FIX-069 2026-05-16): "a
  client-side rate-limit is raised PRE-submission (nothing was sent to the broker) -> re-queuing for retry is
  idempotent" (IS-82, IS-173). (2) A-2 (IS-105, IS-173; the later side of IC-25): a timeout is ambiguous and is not
  retried. (3) (added in the check; IS-200, added to the intent-sources file in the cross-part check) BL-6 and BL-19, all 2026-04-19 (`dfc477959f`): the
  commit's title, "OrderPlacer treats rate-limit-exhausted as terminal", and body, "OrderPlacer runs a narrow retry loop
  scoped only to BrokerRateLimit429Error (max 3 retries)", "exhaustion triggers _handle_placement_failure (trade
  FAILED, reservation released)"; the placer's module docstring OP-BL19a, `orders/order_placer.py` L88 to L93 (the
  retry is "scoped ONLY to BrokerRateLimit429Error"), and its comment L1467 ("non-429 BrokerError = single attempt");
  the adapter's BL-6 docstring, `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L70 to L80: "The caller (OrderPlacer, BL-19) owns retry";
  the 429 class docstring, `core/exceptions.py` L261 to L273: raised "when we DID reach the broker", unlike the
  client-side class, "pre-emptive; never reached the broker" (A10's, A04's and A01's files; interface only). (4) The
  `place()` docstring, `orders/order_placer.py` L893 to L894 (2026-04-17; added in the check): "Raises BrokerError on
  hard broker failure (after cleanup). signal_processor catches this and marks signal PLACEMENT_FAILED". (5) A04 IC-12
  and A04 RL-01 (approved project records): G7's 1 s / 5 s / 30 s / soft-kill ladder against BL-6 and BL-19, with the
  code following BL-6 and BL-19, "only the placer repeats an entry, at most three times, then fails it"; G7
  (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L135 to L145) names only `broker/rate_limiter.py` and `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` as
  affected, so neither side of A04 IC-12 asks the signal processor to retry a broker 429. Sources (1) to (4)
  HISTORICAL; (5) project records. The intent-sources file's note "No source on a broker 429" (section 6.1, point 68)
  does not hold against sources (3) and (4); that file is not changed here. The note was corrected in
  the cross-part check (IS-200, IS-201, IS-221).
- **Actual code path:** L1486 to L1532 (`BrokerRateLimitError`); L1533 to L1536 (`BrokerError`, re-raised); L1537 to
  L1538 (any other exception); L1233 to L1246; `core/exceptions.py` L158, L215, L243, L261, L284 (A01's file);
  `orders/order_placer.py` L1403 to L1419 (the placer's 429 loop and its exhaustion; A10's file, interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S049): a `BrokerRateLimitError` is re-queued up to three times
  (without delay, part PQ's CD-17); a `BrokerRateLimit429Error` (a `BrokerError`, not a `BrokerRateLimitError`) goes
  to `PLACEMENT_FAILED` and the reservation release (with the placer's own release, EV-05 route (a)); a
  `RateLimitAbortedError` (a `TradingSystemError`, the limiter's shutdown abort) to the same handler. FACT (interface,
  added in the check): the placer re-raises a broker 429 only when its own loop is exhausted ("exhausted -- same
  cleanup as any BrokerError", L1405, then `_handle_placement_failure` and `raise`, L1416 to L1419). INTERPRETATION:
  the two processor branches separate an exception raised before any request (re-queued, source (1)) from one raised
  after the broker answered 429 and the placer's retries ran out (terminal, sources (3) and (4)); whether a broker 429
  leaves no order, and so whether the placer's retries are safe, is A04's and A10's (the placer's comment L1307 to
  L1309 states its own reasoning) and is not needed to classify the A06 mapping. The placer also runs its failure path
  before it re-raises a client-side limit, so the FIX-069 re-queue follows the placer's release: that is EV-05 route
  (d), counted there. No source states the signal status after a shutdown abort; it is written `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, a
  step-8 exception in SP9's terms (not judged further; the abort is A01 C14's and A04 CS-05's).
- **Evidence:** P05-S049, P05-S052; P04-S052; P09-S059; A04 IC-12, RL-01, RL-02; code lines above; `git log -1 dfc477959f`.
- **Result and classification:** MATCH (changed in the check from UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, on sources (3) and (4),
  which the draft did not have). FACT (the mapping); INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. The processor re-queues the
  pre-submission client-side limit (FIX-069), does not retry a timeout (A-2; EV-21) and marks a broker 429 that the
  placer has already retried and failed `PLACEMENT_FAILED`, as BL-19 and the `place()` docstring state. A04 CS-03 keeps
  its classification; A04 IC-12 and RL-01 are A04's and unchanged. The double release on these routes is EV-05's.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none (MATCH).
- **Owner:** A06 (the mapping); A10 (the placer's 429 retry, BL-19); A04 (the exception classes, the adapter's

##### EV-13 — UNKNOWN: whether the processor was meant to call A04's limiter; the FIX-007 pre-check has never been wired by `main.py`, and if wired it would spend a second `order` token per entry (A04 RL-18's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** the A04 rate-limiter boundary at the processor (direction question 24); the A06 sides of A04
  RL-14, RL-16 and RL-18, which keep A04's classifications.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a documented control not in effect); Weakness (code that does not run).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) The FIX-007 comment L415 to L417 (`31075d0`, 2026-05-12): "non-blocking
  rate-limiter pre-check. If the order token bucket is exhausted, re-enqueue the signal and return immediately rather
  than blocking the worker thread on adapter.place_order()'s acquire() call"; the constructor comments L189 and L233:
  "optional RateLimiter for order pre-check", "optional pre-check" (IS-81). (2) FIX-048, commit `d604c95`
  (2026-05-15): on a full queue the signal is abandoned with a `REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL` warning (IS-81); Record 02 names
  that status (IS-150). (3) A04 RL-18 (P09-S064): "its only non-test caller, the signal processor's pre-check, is never
  reached because `main.py` passes no limiter ... if wired, a successful pre-check would itself spend an `order` token
  before `place_order` took another (A06's, section 5)". No source says that the pre-check was meant to be wired
  (intent-sources file, section 6.2, question 24: OPEN QUESTION narrowed). (1) and (2) HISTORICAL; (3) a project
  record.
- **Actual code path:** L189, L233, L415 to L461; `main.py` L3575 to L3623 (no `rate_limiter`); the
  `SignalProcessor(...)` call at `31075d0`, `686df1c` and `970aabf` (P05-S048).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (P05-S048): `main.py` builds one `RateLimiter` and passes it to the
  adapter, the smart-target manager and the S&R fetch closure, never to the processor, at any of the three commits.
  INTERPRETATION (P05-S049, P05-S050): under `main.py` A06 code never calls A04's limiter; an entry's `order` token is
  taken below `place()` in the adapter, on the worker thread, after `portfolio_lock` is released, so the aim of source
  (1), not to block a worker on `acquire()`, is not met: the worker can wait in `place()` up to the limiter's 30 s
  bound (A04 RL-16's A06 side; part PQ's). If a limiter were passed, the pre-check would run before `_process_one`,
  outside every `try`, and each entry would spend two `order` tokens (RL-18's note). The dead branch's own defects (the
  `KeyError` for a retry dict; a full queue written three ways) are part PQ's (18.1 point 54). RL-14's A06 side (the
  throttle limits entries, not broker requests) is part ET's.
- **Evidence:** P05-S047 to P05-S050; P04-S053, P04-S054; P09-S062 to P09-S064, P09-S090, P09-S091; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT (the wiring); INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. Whether the
  pre-check was meant to be active is not established; as wired, it never runs. Were it meant to be wired, its double
  spending of tokens would be a defect of the pre-check (INTERPRETATION only). A04 RL-14, RL-16 and RL-18 keep their
  classifications.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established.
- **Owner:** A06 (the pre-check and the processor's wiring contract); A01 (`main.py`'s wiring); A04 (the limiter;
  RL-14, RL-16, RL-18).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 54 (part PQ's); STUDY question 24; A04 RL-14, RL-16, RL-18; IS-81, IS-150; EV-12;
  parts PQ and ET.

## 10. Part ET — Entry throttle


**Baseline:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch main, commit 970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb (written `970aabf` below)

**Scope:** STUDY section 9 (evidence part P06, `evidence/A06_ENTRY_THROTTLE_2026-11-17.md`): `signals/entry_throttle.py` as a whole and its three call sites in `signals/signal_processor.py` (Rama's decision B of 17-Nov-2026); the admit algorithm, its windows (20 s, 3 per 60 s, 300 s per symbol), when an entry is counted (before `place()`), locking, restart, configuration keys and defaults, and the intent sources for it. Direction question 11, with the throttle side of questions 12 and 14, and questions 26 to 28. The admission decisions at the call sites stay with A07 and A09; the A06-to-A07 handoff line stays OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY section 17) and is not used here.
