### 10.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### ET-01 — An entry is counted at the admit, before `place()`, whatever happens next; the sources' "PLACED entries" against the docstring's own "records the placement before returning" (IC-14); an attempt that sends nothing, or that the broker refuses, still consumes the 20 s gap and the 300 s cooldown

- **Area and flow:** the entry throttle's record of an admitted entry at its three call sites, and what follows the record.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (one set of words read two ways, IC-14); Documentation against code (the gate descriptions against the counted event, as the legacy's own 07-Aug record reads them); Consistency (the record point against the outcome it is meant to count).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) "Placed" entries: THR L7 to L8 (lines 2026-06-19, `c0554c6`): "minimum seconds between any two PLACED entries"; "at most `burst_max` placed entries per rolling `burst_window`" (IS-109); SYSCFG L350 and L352 (lines 2026-06-19, `575a1dc`, `c0554c6`): ">=20s between any two PLACED entries (global)"; "max 3 placed entries per 60s (0 = off)" (IS-113); CFGLOADER L576 to L580 (same commits): "spaces out placed entries"; "reject a new entry placed < this since the last" (IS-132); CONFIG-GUIDE L343 to L344 (2026-06-19, `3fa0b4d`): "≥20 s between any two *placed* entries" (IS-113). (2) The record at the admit: THR L13 to L17: `admit(symbol)` "records the placement before returning allowed=True", deliberately atomic, because a split check and record "would leave a TOCTOU window" (IS-109); commit `575a1dc` (2026-06-19): the check is "called right before placement so only PLACED entries count" (IS-111; P06-S019); SMAP L76 and `docs/audit/rulings_1_2_verification_07aug2026.md` L618 to L627 (2026-08-07; IS-116): `admit()` "records the placement BEFORE `place()` is called", so "a placement the broker REJECTS still consumes the symbol's full 300 s cooldown"; "Not a defect and not proposed for change". The same document says "the throttle counts its own **dispatch** where a reader would assume it counts an **entry**" (L626) and heads the passage "A CONSEQUENCE THIS SURFACED THAT WAS IN NO DOCUMENT" (L614). The intent-sources file records the pair as IC-14 (a conflict only if "PLACED" means accepted by the broker).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/entry_throttle.py` L74 to L115 (the record at L109 to L114); `signals/signal_processor.py` L1410 then L1420 to L1538 (`place()` L1446; `BrokerTimeoutError` L1464 to L1484; `BrokerRateLimitError` L1486 to L1532; other errors L1533 to L1538 and the generic handler L1233 to L1246); gate path L2225 then L2235 onward (handler L2340 to L2355); retest path L2530 then L2538 onward.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: when `admit()` returns allowed, the throttle has already set `_last_entry`, appended to `_recent` and set `_per_symbol_last[symbol]` (L109 to L114); `place()` is called afterwards (L1446, L2251, L2553) and the throttle is never told its outcome (P06-S034). The outcomes after an admit are: `place()` returns (`PROCESSED`, `entries_placed`); it raises `BrokerTimeoutError` (`TIMEOUT`); on the fused and enforce path it raises `BrokerRateLimitError`, which FIX-069's own comment says is "raised PRE-submission (nothing was sent to the broker)" (L1487 to L1488), and the signal is re-queued or marked `PLACEMENT_FAILED`; or it raises another exception, a broker refusal included (`PLACEMENT_FAILED`; the placer re-raises `OrderRejectedError` after its own handling, `orders/order_placer.py` L1358 to L1402, A10's file, read at the interface only). INTERPRETATION (P06-S008, P06-S034; at the file values): in every one of these outcomes the 20 s global gap and the 300 s cooldown for that symbol text run from the admit; an attempt that sent nothing to the broker, or that the broker refused, keeps every other symbol out for 20 s and the same symbol out for 300 s. `entries_admitted` counts admits and can exceed `entries_placed` (P06-S017). MATCH parts: the record is taken atomically at the last step before placement, as (2) states and as `c0554c6` explains; the counter's name says what it counts.
- **Evidence:** P06-S008, P06-S009, P06-S010, P06-S017, P06-S019, P06-S020, P06-S021, P06-S034; IS-109, IS-111, IS-113, IS-116, IS-132; IC-14; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with MATCH parts. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The sources can be read two ways (IC-14): if "PLACED entries" means entries that reached the placement step, the code matches them; if it means orders the broker accepted, it does not. The code applies the first reading, which the later 07-Aug record calls design; the gate descriptions in the configuration, the schema, the guide and the docstring were not changed to say so, and the 07-Aug record itself names the gap between the words and the behaviour. This part chooses neither reading. Basis of the ISSUE, which holds under either reading: under the second, IC-14 is a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way; under the first, a legacy source of 07-Aug itself records that the throttle "counts its own **dispatch** where a reader would assume it counts an **entry**" (L626) and that this consequence "WAS IN NO DOCUMENT" (L614), while the descriptions it refers to stayed unchanged at the baseline (Document 04, section 5.3: documented or stated behaviour that the code does not implement, as that record reads it). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (d) fails (THR L13 to L14, `575a1dc` and the 07-Aug record intend the admit-time record).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether any admitted entry failed to reach the broker in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the 07-Aug document's measured instances are runtime claims (HISTORICAL).
- **Owner:** A06 (the throttle and its call sites); A10 (what `place()` does, and whether it can decline an entry without raising: 18.2 item 14, ET-07).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 38; STUDY question 40; IC-14; ET-02 (the retry case); ET-07 (5).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 40 bears on the intended rule (OPEN FOR RAMA).

##### ET-02 — The FIX-069 rate-limit retry meets the throttle measured from its own first admit: at the file values it is admitted only if it comes back at least 300 s after that admit and within the 600 s age, so the retry that FIX-069 and A-2 keep ends `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` unless it spends most of the cooldown before reaching the throttle (A04 RL-16's throttle side)

- **Area and flow:** the FIX-069 re-queue in `_admit_and_place` and the retry's second call to `admit(symbol)`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated retry that another mechanism defeats); Flow and state (two components disagree; the retried signal is dropped).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) FIX-069 comment PROC L1487 to L1489 (lines 2026-07-12, `536172c`; FIX-069 itself 2026-05-16, `397105d`): a client-side rate limit is "raised PRE-submission (nothing was sent to the broker) -> re-queuing for retry is idempotent"; "Max 3 retries" (IS-82; its "15s each" is CD-17, part PQ's). (2) Commit `fd09a38` (A-2, 2026-07-02): "BrokerRateLimitError (raised pre-submission, nothing sent) KEEPS its retry" (IS-173; IC-25, which the code resolves in A-2's favour, PQ-04); its test `tests/unit/test_signal_processor.py` L2325 to L2344 (lines 2026-07-02, `fd09a38`; HISTORICAL, never run): the rate-limit path "must STILL re-queue the signal (retry preserved)"; it checks the re-queue only, not what the retry meets at the throttle (P06-S036). (3) `docs/audit/system_security_audit_02jul2026.md` L52 (functional finding A-2 only; IS-202, added in the cross-part check): "only client-side `BrokerRateLimitError`/429 (raised pre-submission ...) is retry-safe". (4) The same 02-Jul documents (IS-202) record the throttle's effect on a near-immediate retry: `docs/design/a2_timeout_retry_design_02jul2026.md` L43 to L48 and the audit L50: "The near-immediate retry is rejected as `ENTRY_THROTTLED`. **This is a rate-limiter, not an idempotency guard.**" (written of the then-combined timeout and rate-limit retry); of the timeout retry, PROC L1466 to L1467 says "the entry throttle only masks it" and `fd09a38` "The entry throttle only MASKED this". No source addresses a rate-limit retry meeting the throttle (intent-sources file, STUDY question 41: no source found).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L1486 to L1532 (`retry_count` + 1, `put(..., timeout=1.0)`, release, return); L1510 to L1517 (the retry dict keeps `symbol` and the original `triggered_at`); L914 to L921 (the age check against `signal_expiry_sec`, rejecting only when the age is greater, L917); L1410 to L1418 (the second admit); `signals/entry_throttle.py` L77 to L107; SYSCFG L127 (`expiry_sec: 600`), passed as `signal_expiry_sec` by `main.py` L3614, and L350 and L353; `broker/rate_limiter.py` L148 to L155, L201 to L223 (A04's file, interface only: the 30 s deadline and the timeout error).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the first attempt's admit was recorded before `place()` raised (ET-01). The retry is put back on the queue at once, with no delay (PQ-04), runs the whole intake and A07 path again, including the age check against the original `triggered_at`, and, if it passes every step, calls `admit(symbol)` with the same symbol text (P04-S052, P06-S036, P06-S052). INTERPRETATION (P06-S008, P06-S036, P06-S052; at the file values): the retry is admitted only if it reaches the throttle at least 20 s after the last admit of any symbol and at least 300 s after its own first admit, while its age at intake is at most 600 s. A retry that comes back sooner is refused (by `min_gap` within 20 s of any admit, otherwise by `per_symbol`, or, in the exact-equality case of ET-10, by `burst`), ends `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` with its reservation released, and is not retried again: a refusal is final (P06-S040). FACT (A04's file, interface only; RL-16): in non-test code `BrokerRateLimitError` is raised only by the limiter, at once when the tokens asked exceed the bucket's capacity (not reachable from the committed callers, RL-16) or when its wait reaches the 30 s deadline (`max_wait_sec` 30.0, never passed); a broker HTTP 429 is a different class, `BrokerRateLimit429Error`, which FIX-069 does not catch (`core/exceptions.py` L243, L261). INTERPRETATION (arithmetic): the limiter's timeout comes at least 30 s after the admit, so the retry is put back at least 30 s after it and needs at least about 270 s more, in the queue or in its own steps, before a second admit can pass the cooldown. So "Max 3 retries" can lead to an order only in that case; otherwise the first retry ends the signal.
- **Evidence:** P04-S037, P04-S052; P06-S034, P06-S036, P06-S040, P06-S050, P06-S052; PQ-04; IS-82, IS-173; IC-25; code lines above; `git show -s fd09a38` (message only).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The retry that FIX-069 and A-2 keep, and that the 02-Jul audit calls retry-safe, is refused by the throttle unless it returns after most of the cooldown; the 02-Jul documents knew that the throttle refuses a near-immediate retry and kept the rate-limit retry without addressing that. Basis: Document 04, section 5.3 (documented behaviour that the code does not deliver; two components that disagree). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a retry mechanism exists); (b) fails: the refusal is not certain; it depends on the effective settings (UNKNOWN), on whether the retry passes intake and screening again, and on how long it takes to reach the throttle (one that arrives at least 300 s after its first admit and within the 600 s age passes); (c) holds (FIX-069 says it handles a client-side limit by a retry, and the case arises whenever A04's limiter raises after its wait, RL-16); (d) appears to hold (no source intends the rate-limit retry to be refused), not weighed further because (b) fails. This record is the throttle side only: the retry's timing and comments are PQ-04's; the double release on the re-queue is EV-05 route (d).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether a `BrokerRateLimitError` ever reached the processor in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (ET-07 (6)).
- **Owner:** A06 (the throttle and the re-queue); A04 (RL-16, the limiter that raises); A10 (placement).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 39; STUDY question 41; A04 RL-16 (throttle side; RL-16 unchanged); PQ-04; EV-05; IS-82, IS-173; IC-25; ET-01.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 41 bears on intent.

##### ET-03 — The `INTRADAY SIGNAL` alert, which reads as an entry ticket, is sent before the throttle on all three paths; a signal the throttle then refuses has been announced and is dropped with no alert of the refusal; reserving before the throttle and releasing on a refusal is the stated design

- **Area and flow:** the order of steps at the three throttle sites: reservation and `RESERVED`, the `INTRADAY SIGNAL` alert, the late kill-switch and shutdown checks, then the throttle.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an alert that fires wrongly); Flow and state (steps in an order that announces an entry the next step can refuse).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) The reservation before the throttle and its release on a refusal: commit `575a1dc` (2026-06-19): the check "rejects a too-soon/burst entry and releases its reservation so the slot frees for a later signal" (IS-111); PROC L1407 to L1409 (lines 2026-07-12, `536172c`; the same text at L2224 from `575a1dc`): "Released reservation on reject so the slot frees for a later signal" (IS-112); DEC10 L7 (2026-07-19): the throttle is "evaluated after risk approval" (IS-115, describing the present state). (2) The alert: PROC L1370 (line 2026-07-12, `536172c`): "Telegram alert: INTRADAY SIGNAL (fires before order placement)" (IS-161); the `_emit_signal_alert` docstring L498: "Send INTRADAY SIGNAL telegram alert (optional; never crash)". No source says whether the alert should go out for an entry that the throttle then refuses (intent-sources file, point 41 and STUDY question 44).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/signal_processor.py` L1302 to L1344 (in-flight increment, reservation, `RESERVED`), L1371 to L1380 (alert), L1389 to L1405 (late checks), L1410 to L1418 (throttle); gate path L2133 to L2171, L2191 to L2201, L2203 to L2222, L2225 to L2233; retest path L2469 to L2501, L2514 to L2517, L2519 to L2528, L2530 to L2536; `_emit_signal_alert` L486 to L555; refusal handlers L1193 to L1231, L2309 to L2338, L2594 to L2614.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: on all three paths the reservation and `RESERVED` come first, then the alert, then the late kill-switch and shutdown checks, then the throttle (P06-S027, P06-S030, P06-S031, P06-S035). When a notifier is wired (L499 to L500) the alert is titled "INTRADAY SIGNAL — <symbol>" and gives the direction, the entry price marked "(LIMIT)", the stop, the target, the quantity, the exposure, the risk and "Timeout: 10 min" (L533 to L549); on the retest path, whose entry is a MARKET order by its own docstring and comment (L2379 to L2381, L2541 to L2542), the same body still says "(LIMIT)" (dormant at the file value; noted for A14, not counted). The refusal handlers write the status, the evidence record and the counters; none sends an alert (L1193 to L1231, L2309 to L2338, L2594 to L2614). INTERPRETATION: a throttled signal has been reserved, counted in the FIX-018 in-flight figure while it held the reservation, and announced to the operator with its order details; it then ends `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` with no further alert, so the alerts show an entry that was never sent. At the file values every approval within 20 s of an admit is refused (P06-S041), so in an opening burst each refused signal is announced when a notifier is wired. MATCH part: reserve, then throttle, then release on a refusal "so the slot frees" is the stated design (1); the alert fires before order placement, as (2) states.
- **Evidence:** P06-S027, P06-S028, P06-S030, P06-S031, P06-S035, P06-S041, P06-S042; P06-S054 (HISTORICAL test: "throttle rejects AFTER reserve"); IS-111, IS-112, IS-115, IS-161; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The intended order of the alert and the throttle is not established; the basis is what the code demonstrates: an alert that describes an entry order is sent for signals that the next step refuses, and the refusal raises no alert (Document 04, section 5.3: alerts that fire wrongly). The late kill-switch and shutdown aborts at the same sites also come after the alert; that is context here (A13's and A14's matters) and is not counted. "Minor": the alert is an INFO notice and the refusal is recorded in the `signals` row; how the operator used the alerts is not established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether any throttled signal was announced in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (STUDY 18.2 item 16; ET-07).
- **Owner:** A06 (the order of steps at the throttle sites); A14 (the alert and its delivery); A09 (the reservation and what other signals see while it is held).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 41; STUDY question 44; EV-23 (the alert call cannot reject or alter a signal; MATCH); ET-13.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 44 bears on the intended alert policy.

##### ET-04 — Which gate refused is kept only in the free-text reason and in counters that last for the life of the process; the `category` the throttle returns is never read; the daily review groups the status with capital-check rejects

- **Area and flow:** the throttle's result record and counters; the status and reason written for a refusal; their readers at the interface.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (a value produced and never read); Consistency across components (a classification carried only in prose); Failure handling (an error swallowed silently; the latent part).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) THR `admit` docstring L66 to L68 (2026-06-19, `c0554c6`): on a refusal it returns "allowed=False with the blocking gate's category/reason (and increments that gate's counter)"; L17: "Each gate also counts its rejections for /metrics"; the `metrics` docstring L118: "Per-reason throttle counters for /metrics observability" (IS-109, IS-110). (2) PROC L706 ("per-reason throttle breakdown (which gate fired)"; line 2026-06-19, `c0554c6`) and the `get_runtime_metrics` docstring L684 to L692 (lines 2026-06-19, `262614e`; IS-114); commit `c0554c6`: "get_runtime_metrics merges per-reason breakdown" (IS-111). (3) DEC10 L23 (2026-07-19): "the throttle's gate category is recorded only in the free-text `rejection_reason`" and so "effectively invisible in every report"; the 19-Jul report L281 to L282: "free-text only ... Recorded, not fixed" (IS-115, IS-172; their counts are runtime claims).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/entry_throttle.py` L30 to L34, L81 to L107, L117 to L125; `signals/signal_processor.py` L1410 to L1418, L2225 to L2233, L2530 to L2536 (only `allowed` and `reason` read); L1415, L2230, L2535 (`entries_throttled`); L676 to L681 (`_bump_metric`, guarded); L706 to L710 (`metrics()` merged, guarded); L1201, L2316 to L2318, L2598 (status and reason); `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L734 to L744 (A15's file, interface only).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `ThrottleResult.category` is set for each gate and no caller reads it (P06-S033). The status is one value, `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED`, for all three gates; the gate is the first word of the stored reason ("Entry throttled: min_gap ...", "... burst ...", "... per_symbol ..."). The per-gate counters are cumulative for the life of the process and reach `/metrics` through `get_runtime_metrics` (P06-S017). FACT, interface only: the daily trade review puts any status containing `ENTRY_THROTTLED` in its `CAPITAL_CHECK` stage with the sizing, position-count, daily-trade, loss, strategy-cap and strategy circuit-breaker rejects (L741 to L744; P06-S053). INTERPRETATION: the sum of the three per-gate counters equals the processor's `entries_throttled` unless the site's release raises; then the gate's counter has risen, `entries_throttled` has not, and the signal is written `PLACEMENT_FAILED` (EV-05 route (b)), which the same report places in its `ORDERABLE` stage (L739 to L740). FACT: `get_runtime_metrics` merges `metrics()` inside `try: ... except Exception: pass` with no log line and no comment of its own (L707 to L710; lines 2026-06-19, `c0554c6`). The comments that call metrics best-effort belong to other guards: `_bump_metric`'s "metrics must never affect the pipeline" (L681, `3b2fcc0`), the screener-stats guard's "_stats absent in a bare/test instance" (L705) and the broker-quota block's "Best-effort and fully guarded" (L722, `70b5045`). The `fail_fast_no_silent` principle (IS-103; `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L434 to L437, 2026-04-17, scope "Every .py file") reads in full: "Every exception must either be logged with full traceback AND raised, OR explicitly handled with documented recovery. NO bare except, NO try/except/log/continue patterns that swallow errors." INTERPRETATION: no source states this guard's silence as intended (unlike `_bump_metric`'s, PQ-06 (iii)); whether the neighbouring comments were meant to cover it as "documented recovery" is not established. INTERPRETATION (P06-S018): `metrics()` reads four integers under the lock and cannot raise for ordinary input, so the guard hides nothing at the baseline; it is a part of this record, not a separate count. MATCH part: the per-gate counters exist and reach `/metrics`, as (1) and (2) state; the reason text names the gate.
- **Evidence:** P06-S002, P06-S017, P06-S018, P06-S028, P06-S033, P06-S053; EV-05; PQ-06; IS-103, IS-109, IS-110, IS-114, IS-115, IS-172; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The module computes a structured gate category for its caller (1) and no caller keeps it, so the only durable record of which gate refused is free text, and the per-gate counts last only as long as the process (ET-14); the legacy's own decision record names this as a visibility gap (3). Basis: Document 04, section 5.3 (a value produced and discarded; a classification carried only in prose). A further, latent part: the guard on the merge into `/metrics` swallows any exception with no log line and no comment of its own, against IS-103, which admits only explicit handling "with documented recovery" (Document 04, section 5.3: errors swallowed); it hides nothing at the baseline. "Minor": no decision depends on the category.
- **Severity or impact where established:** no effect on any decision; the effect is on records and reports (A15's).
- **Owner:** A06 (the throttle and its call sites); A15 (the review's stages, `/metrics`); A03 (the `signals` columns).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 42; STUDY question 45 (second half); EV-05 route (b); ET-14.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### ET-05 — The same-symbol cooldown runs from entry to entry and has no exit input, as every throttle source states; the later 07-Aug ruling, as the legacy documents attribute it, says a flat symbol "immediately becomes eligible again" (IC-24; the legacy's OPEN-2, unanswered at the baseline)

- **Area and flow:** the per-symbol cooldown gate; re-entry of a symbol after an exit.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) THR L9 to L11 (2026-06-19, `c0554c6`): "no re-entry of the SAME symbol within `per_symbol_cooldown` seconds (stops rapid same-symbol churn, e.g. enter X 10:00 → exit 10:02 → re-fire 10:03 → wait)"; SYSCFG L353: "no re-entry of same symbol within 5 min"; CFGLOADER L584 to L586; CONFIG-GUIDE L346; commit `c0554c6`: "no re-entry of the SAME symbol within 5 min" (IS-109, IS-111, IS-113, IS-132). The intent-sources file (IC-15 withdrawn): the docstring's example fits a cooldown measured from the entry (10:03 is 180 s after the 10:00 entry). (2) SMAP L76 and `docs/audit/rulings_1_2_verification_07aug2026.md` §8.1 L440 (2026-08-07; IS-116): "it measures PREVIOUS ENTRY → NEW ENTRY. There is no exit hook anywhere in the class", "CONFIRMED DESIGN". (3) The 07-Aug ruling as the legacy documents attribute it to Rama (IS-175; RS-12: an attribution by those documents, not a statement of Rama recorded by this project): a symbol with no open position "immediately becomes eligible again for every pipeline"; the same document's OPEN-2 (L395): "`per_symbol_cooldown_sec: 300` contradicts the ruling's wording ... that is a decision, not an inference"; §8.8 (L740 to L744): the two "can coexist without amendment", but "that is still a decision"; no answer is found at the baseline (IC-24).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/entry_throttle.py` L97 to L113; `signals/signal_processor.py` L1410, L2225, L2530.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `_per_symbol_last[symbol]` is written only at an admit (L112 to L113) and compared at the next attempt for that symbol text (L97 to L107); the class has no input from fills, exits or positions (P06-S003, P06-S015). INTERPRETATION (at the file value 300 s): a symbol whose position closes within 300 s of its entry cannot be entered again until 300 s after that entry, whatever the exit; the key is the symbol text alone, across directions and products (the 07-Aug document §8.5, L640 to L649, reads the same). MATCH part: the entry-to-entry base matches (1) and (2).
- **Evidence:** P06-S003, P06-S011, P06-S015, P06-S051; IS-109, IS-111, IS-113, IS-116, IS-132, IS-175; IC-24; RS-12; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, with MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. Sources (1) and (2) and the later ruling as attributed (3) conflict on whether a flat symbol may be entered again at once (IC-24), and the code applies the cooldown. This part chooses neither side: the legacy left OPEN-2 unanswered, and the ruling is A09's rule; whether "immediately" was meant to govern the throttle is not established. KNOWN DEFECT not given: (d) fails (the code does what (1) and (2) state).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether a re-entry within 300 s of an entry was ever refused after an exit is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the 07-Aug document's measured cases are runtime claims (HISTORICAL).
- **Owner:** A06 (the cooldown); A09 (the symbol-direction rule and the ruling it concerns).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 44; STUDY question 42; IC-24; RS-12; 18.1 point 99 (part EV, no record: A04-33's input (4) is a Phase 2 input, not legacy intent).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; the intended rule is OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY question 42; the legacy's OPEN-2).

##### ET-06 — UNKNOWN: which of several near-simultaneous approvals the throttle admits is decided by who takes its lock first, after risk approval and reservation, not by receipt order, `triggered_at` or score; the one legacy decision on this (DEC10) is "OPEN — Rama's call" at the baseline

- **Area and flow:** concurrency at the throttle: several approved signals reaching `admit()` together, from the processor's workers or the other callers.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED); Flow and state (the order in which signals are admitted).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) DEC10 L1 to L9 (2026-07-19, `5a83a46`): "Status: OPEN — Rama's call", between Option A, "leave arrival-order admission as-is: a global 20-second `min_gap` between placed entries, first-come-first-served, evaluated after risk approval", Option B (change the parameters) and Option C (the V3 ranked-admission batching window); L28: "The purpose the 20s `min_gap` was set to serve" is "not re-derived here" (IS-115; RS-06, the documents' attribution of a pending call); `docs/decisions/00_INDEX.md` L20 and L26 still list it "OPEN" at the baseline, and no later decision was found (IS-159). (2) SMAP L268 (line 2026-07-12; IS-93): the V3 allocator plan describes the present first-come-first-served admission as the design it is to replace. (3) The 19-Jul report behind DEC10 (IS-172; RS-14): the throttle's selection is by "Time/arrival-density", "quality-neutral", and the decisions it bears on "are Rama's". (4) SYSCFG L565 (committed `allocator_mode: "shadow"`; A18's, interface only; IS-203, added in the cross-part check): shadow leaves live admission "BYTE-IDENTICAL"; enforce is gated on shadow sessions, a regret review "+ Rama + an off-market cutover" (an attribution of a pending gate, not Rama's recorded intent).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/entry_throttle.py` L74 to L115 (one lock; the clock read inside it); `signals/signal_processor.py` L1302 to L1344 then L1410; L2133 to L2171 then L2225; L2469 to L2501 then L2530; the callers: the five workers, the two `gate-release` threads, the retest monitor's thread, the allocator's thread (P06-S037).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the read of state, the three tests and the record of an admit happen under one `threading.Lock` (L74 to L115), after each caller's own risk approval and reservation. Current external context (Python documentation, `threading`, Lock objects, read 18-Nov-2026): "When more than one thread is blocked in acquire() waiting for the state to turn to unlocked, only one thread proceeds when a release() call resets the state to unlocked; which one of the waiting threads proceeds is not defined, and may vary across implementations." INTERPRETATION (P06-S038, P06-S041): of near-simultaneous approvals the first to take the lock is admitted and, at the file values, every other one within 20 s is refused; which caller is first depends on how long each one's earlier steps took (screening, quotes, sizing, the wait for `portfolio_lock`), not on receipt order at the receiver, `triggered_at` or score; among callers already waiting on the lock the order is not defined. This is the admission that DEC10's Option A and SMAP L268 describe as the present design. In the allocator's enforce mode (not at the file value) the allocator holds `portfolio_lock` for a ranked batch and calls `admit_prepared` inside it (P06-S039), so the batch's order would decide who reaches the throttle first (A18's logic, interface only).
- **Evidence:** P06-S037, P06-S038, P06-S039, P06-S041, P06-S051; P10-S146; IS-93, IS-115, IS-159, IS-172; RS-06, RS-14; code lines above; the `threading` page.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL; OPEN QUESTION. The intent is not established: the one legacy record that poses the question (DEC10) leaves it "OPEN — Rama's call" at the baseline, and no answer of Rama is recorded (no Open Issues Register entry covers it). The code shows no defect against a stated intent: it implements the "first-come-first-served, evaluated after risk approval" admission that DEC10 and SMAP L268 describe; the undefined order among waiting threads is a property of the lock that no source addresses. A lack of intent evidence alone is not an ISSUE.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established; the runtime order is UNKNOWN (ET-07 (4)); DEC10's counts are runtime claims (HISTORICAL).
- **Owner:** A06 (the throttle's admission order); A07 and A09 (the admission decisions before it, decision B); A18 (the allocator's ranked admission, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 43; STUDY question 43; STUDY 18.2 item 13 (ET-07); IS-115; RS-06.
- **Rama:** needed: OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY question 43: which approval was meant to be admitted, and whether the legacy decision DEC10 was ever taken and where it is recorded).

##### ET-07 — UNKNOWN: the runtime and environment facts of the throttle (effective values, deployment, restarts and processes, lock order, a `place()` that declines without raising, refusals and retries that occurred, the dormant paths, the Python version)

- **Area and flow:** every runtime fact of the entry throttle and its three call sites.

- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: (1) the effective values of the four keys on the legacy machines; only the committed values (20, 60, 3, 300) are known (P06-S021; STUDY 18.2 item 2; A02). (2) Whether and when `575a1dc` and `c0554c6` (2026-06-19) and the later call-site commits `9f56b07` (2026-06-27) and `536172c` (2026-07-12) were deployed (P06-S019; STUDY 18.2 item 8). Narrowed: FACT, interface only, the placer re-raises `OrderRejectedError` after its own handling (`orders/order_placer.py` L1358 to L1402), so a broker refusal does reach the processor as an exception; any other path is A10's. (6) Whether any throttle refusal, `BrokerRateLimitError` or FIX-069 retry occurred, and the counters' values (STUDY 18.2 item 1; P06-S036, P06-S041). (7) Whether the gate-release path, the retest path or the allocator's enforce mode ever reached the throttle (STUDY 18.2 item 12; P06-S029 to P06-S031). ##### ET-08 — A unit test comment names the burst gate for a refusal that the code makes at the gap gate; the assertion does not check which gate refused

- **Area and flow:** the tests about the throttle (HISTORICAL; never run).
- **Issue category:** Documentation against code (a test comment that misnames the gate).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** the module's gate order, THR L4 to L5: "evaluated global-first (global takes precedence)", with the gap gate tested first (IS-109); the commit's test description: "a 19-Jun replay -> 1 admitted" (IS-111).
- **Actual code path:** `tests/unit/test_entry_throttle.py` L20 to L35 (fake clock starting at 1000.0; helper), L134 to L148 (lines 2026-06-19, `c0554c6`); `signals/entry_throttle.py` L77 to L95.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (arithmetic on the test's own fake clock): with gap 20, window 60, burst 3 and cooldown 300, the loop admits only the first of five attempts (at 1000.0) and leaves the clock at 1005.0; "X" is admitted at 1025.0 and "Y" at 1045.0 (a gap of exactly 20.0 passes the strict test, L80); "Z" at 1046.0 is refused by the gap gate (1.0 s < 20 s, L77 to L83), which returns before the burst gate (L85 to L95) is reached. The comment at L148 says "4th in 60s -> burst"; the assertion checks only `not ... allowed`, not the category (P06-S016).
- **Evidence:** P06-S008, P06-S016; IS-109, IS-111; code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL. The test's comment describes a refusal the code does not make; the test would also pass if the burst gate were removed, so it does not show the burst gate working at these values (ET-10). Basis: Document 04, section 5.3 (documentation against code). "Minor": a test comment; no runtime effect.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at runtime.
- **Owner:** A06 (the throttle's tests); A19 (test practice in general, if it takes the point).
- **Cross-reference:** 18.1 point 47; ET-10; STUDY section 19 note (the test comment is listed there as minor and answerable from the code).
- **Rama:** not needed.

A contended lock means a wait with no timeout (Python `threading`, read 18-Nov-2026: a timeout of -1 "specifies an unbounded wait"); the work under the lock has no I/O; the only lock order is `portfolio_lock` then the throttle's lock (enforce mode only), so the throttle cannot take part in a deadlock (P06-S018, P06-S034, P06-S039, P06-S043, P06-S045) | MATCH; FACT; INTERPRETATION | A06; A18 (the enforce path) |
| ET-16 | The throttle against broker limits (A04 RL-14 and Open Issues Register A04-31, A06 side) | the throttle's stated purpose: the 19-Jun burst ("5 entries in ~5s") and same-symbol churn (THR L4, L9 to L11; commits `575a1dc`, `c0554c6`; SYSCFG L349; CFGLOADER L576 to L578) (IS-109, IS-111, IS-113, IS-132); A04 RL-14's record that the throttle "limits entries, not broker requests" (A04 AUDIT L3566 to L3571; IS-154) | `signals/entry_throttle.py` L21 to L27 (standard-library imports only), L65 to L115 | the throttle uses no broker limiter, holds no broker figure and counts admitted entries (placement attempts), one throttle per process; at the file values at most one admit per 20 s and three per 60 s, a ceiling on placement attempts; how many broker requests one placement makes is A10's and A04's; none of its sources names a broker limit, so nothing here answers A04-31's history question (P06-S001, P06-S046, P06-S047, P06-S049) | MATCH (A06 side); FACT; INTERPRETATION. RL-14 keeps "UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED" (no treatment); A04-31 stays "History: OPEN QUESTION" | A04 (RL-14, A04-31); A06 (the throttle) |
