### 11.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### CK-01 — The scanner check uses GET in both fetchers; five sources of 17-Apr-2026 say HEAD and two say GET (A01 B-07's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** the outbound scanner-URL reachability check: the method each fetcher sends to every
  `chartink_url`, at boot (`_http_fetch`) and in `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency
  across components (sources of the same date disagree).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** HEAD: (1) LOCKED-YAML P17 L372 (lines 2026-04-17): "all 15
  Chartink scanner URLs reachable via HTTP HEAD" (IS-118); (2) S15 L2790: "Simple name:url map for P17 HTTP HEAD
  pre-flight check" (IS-119); (3) CKS L4 to L5 (lines 2026-04-17): the script "sends an HTTP HEAD request to each
  URL" (IS-122); (4) CFGLOADER L2382 (lines 2026-04-17): "URLs checked via HTTP HEAD at startup preflight"
  (IS-123); (5) SPEC C.1 L655 (2026-04-17): "HTTP HEAD all 15 Chartink scanner URLs" (IS-205 since the cross-part
  check; cited by A01 S-44). GET: (6) M35 PF3 L33 (2026-04-17): "Fetch Chartink URL via requests.get (timeout 10s)"
  (IS-121); (7) M33 SC7 L134 (2026-04-17): the fetcher is an "injected callable for HTTP GET" (IS-163). The conflict
  is IC-17 (method part); A01 recorded the boot side as its IC-31. A repository-wide search (`HTTP HEAD|HEAD
  request|via HEAD|requests\.head`, with the exclusions; check, 18-Nov-2026) finds no other HEAD source; the two
  further hits are the identical early copies of P17 and SPEC C.1 in `docs/web_claude/00_source_documents/`
  (`bcf03b5`), not counted as separate sources.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L101; `main.py` L246 to L252 (FIX-184, `c48d4fd`,
  2026-06-18) and, before it, `git show bcf03b5:main.py` L144 (`urlopen(url, ...)`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the script calls `requests.get(url, headers=_HEADERS, timeout=t)`
  (L101); the boot fetcher builds `urllib.request.Request(url, headers=...)` with no data and no method (L246 to
  L248); the initial commit's version called `urlopen(url, ...)` with no data (`git show bcf03b5:main.py` L144).
  Current external context (Python 3.11 `urllib.request`, as cited by P07-S044, read 17-Nov-2026): a `Request` with
  no data uses GET. FACT: neither fetcher sends HEAD. Current
  external context (Requests 2.34.2, as cited by P07-S032): "By default Requests will perform location redirection
  for all verbs except HEAD". INTERPRETATION: the choice changes more than the method name: a GET downloads the page
  (the whole body with `requests`, L102; at boot the fetcher reads at most 256 bytes, L251), and with `requests` a HEAD would not
  follow redirects by default, so a redirecting address would end in a 3xx and fail the 2xx rule. No source states
  which of the two outcomes was wanted for a redirect.
- **Evidence:** P07-S017, P07-S031, P07-S032, P07-S039, P07-S044; P09-S026; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. A
  conflict between sources of the same date that the code resolves one way: both fetchers follow PF3 and SC7 (GET);
  P17, S15, the URL file's header, the schema comment and SPEC C.1 still say HEAD and were never corrected. A01 B-07
  ("ISSUE; FACT"; Group F, AGREED, 06-Nov-2026) keeps its record for the boot fetcher; this record is the A06 side
  (the script's fetcher and the check's meaning) and is not a reclassification of B-07.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the method does not change which addresses are checked or the
  2xx rule; its effect on redirects and download size is INTERPRETATION only.
- **Owner:** A06 (the check's meaning; the script, whose owner is OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the
  A01 records); A01 (`_http_fetch`, B-07); A02 (the comments in `config/chartink_scanners.yaml` and
  `core/config_loader.py`).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 88; IC-17; A01 B-07, A01 IC-31; CK-02.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CK-02 — Severity of a failed scanner check: "CRITICAL" (P17, the URL file, SPEC C.1) against a warning at boot and an exit code with no alarm in the script; the sources conflict (A01 S-44's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** what follows an unreachable scanner address: at boot (A01's step 7, interface only) and in
  `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Failure handling (a failure reported only by an exit code and text).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** CRITICAL: (1) LOCKED-YAML P17 L372 (17-Apr-2026): "CRITICAL
  on any failure" (IS-118); (2) CKS L4 to L5 (17-Apr-2026): the script "fails CRITICAL if any is unreachable at
  startup" (IS-122); (3) SPEC C.1 L659 (17-Apr-2026): "if any failure: Telegram CRITICAL + exit(1)". Warning or
  operator decision: (4) M33 SC7 L158 "Log WARNING per unreachable scanner" and SC12 L247 to L250 "scanner
  unreachable (log, continue)" (17-Apr-2026; IS-163); (5) M35 PF1 L23 (17-Apr-2026): "Exit 0 if all good, 1 if any
  unreachable" (IS-121); (6) the A.4 comment of the script, L67 to L71 (`53e61e2`, 2026-04-26): "Operator runs this
  script before every trading day, so a single network blip causing a false-failure prompts cancelling trading --
  expensive", and its audit item (`deep_system_audit_2026-04-25.md` L40, file 2026-04-26): "Operator may cancel
  trading on a false positive"; (7) `PATHS.md` L185 item (3) (2026-07-13): the URL file serves "pre-flight
  connectivity, WARN-only"; (8) for the boot only, three legacy records of 20-Jul-2026
  (`boot_path_pair_design_20jul2026.md` L248 to L250 and L369 to L370; `decision_readiness_triage_19jul2026.md` L64;
  `monday_preboot_readiness_19jul2026.md` L46): "`scanner_unreachable` must remain a WARNING, never escalated to
  blocking", because "a transient chartink.com blip would halt a trading day"; (9) found in the check: the external
  pre-live audit report `docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/trading_system_v2_audit_report.md` (`9f58848`,
  2026-04-21) L3903: the script "runs pre-market, fails closed", while the same report lists the script as not read
  (L2750, L3376; L3813: "Not deep-read", with "only runs if scanners reachable check is enabled"; no such enabling
  setting exists at the baseline: a search of non-test, non-doc code for `scanner…enabled`, `enable…scanner` and
  `scanner_check…` finds only `scanner_check_delay_sec`), so it describes the script without reading it and gives no
  CRITICAL alarm; it is consistent with an exit code of 1. The conflict is IC-17 (severity part);
  A01 recorded the boot side as IC-31 and S-44; IC-19 (the 27-Jul "degrade, do not block" rule with an out-of-band
  alarm) concerns the webhook self-check, and whether it was meant generally is INTERPRETATION (A01 S-44).
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L176 to L181, L216 to L231; `utils/startup_checks.py`
  L705 to L722, L1596 to L1597 (A01's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (script): an unreachable address gives one WARNING line on standard
  error from the library (the first `None` status per scanner per process; a non-2xx status every time) and, on
  standard output, the table and "FAILED: <k> of <n> scanner(s) unreachable: <names>"; the script returns 1. It
  writes no CRITICAL line, sends no alert and writes no file or row (P07-S035). FACT (boot, A01 S-44): the step adds
  `scanner_unreachable` to `warnings`, never to `blocking_failures`, and a normal boot does not read the warnings
  (P07-S042).
- **Evidence:** P07-S034, P07-S035, P07-S039, P07-S040, P07-S042; P09-S021, P09-S072; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The sources conflict on the severity, all
  three CRITICAL sources and sources (4) and (5) being of 17-Apr-2026. The code resolves the conflict the
  warning way at boot (S-44) and as an exit code for the operator in the script (PF1). INTERPRETATION: the later
  sources fit the code: the A.4 comment (26-Apr) treats a script failure as an input to the operator's decision
  whether to trade, the 21-Apr report (source 9, written without reading the script) calls it "fails closed", and the
  20-Jul records keep the boot outcome a warning; no source after 17-Apr gives the script a CRITICAL alarm. P17, the URL file's header and SPEC C.1 were not updated. A01 S-44 ("ISSUE; FACT, OPEN
  QUESTION"; Group F and A01-22, AGREED) keeps its record and classification; A01-22's history stays OPEN (Rama does
  not remember). The 20-Jul records are a further legacy source on the boot side, recorded here for information
  only; they reopen nothing in A01's records.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established: whether any check failed, and what the operator did,
  is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (CK-11).
- **Owner:** A06 (the script's outcome; the script's owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01
  records); A01 (the boot step, S-44, A01-22); A14 (alerts, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 88; STUDY question 47; IC-17, IC-19; A01 S-44, A01 IC-31; Open Issues
  Register A01-22; CK-01, CK-03, CK-04.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (the conflict is the ISSUE). STUDY question 47's severity part (was a script
  failure meant to stop trading, to alarm, or only to be reported?) bears on treatment; the boot side is A01-22's
  and is not asked again.

##### CK-03 — The Appendix A script's scope: P17 and SPEC C.1 give it four checks and a CRITICAL exit; PF1 to PF8 of the same date make it a scanner-URL check only, which the code follows

- **Area and flow:** what `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` is meant to check.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components (sources of the same date disagree).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL, all 2026-04-17):** (1) LOCKED-YAML P17 L372 to L376 (IS-118):
  one "Pre-flight scanner check" with (a) the scanner URLs, (b) every map entry mapped to an existing strategy file,
  (c) the own endpoint by a local `/health` call, (d) no duplicate scanner names; it "affects"
  `utils/startup_checks.py` and `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py (new)`. (2) SPEC C.1 L653 to L659: under
  "scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py": HEAD of the URLs, "Verify scan_webhook_map has no duplicates", "Verify every
  map entry points to a valid strategy YAML", "Verify own webhook /health endpoint reachable", and "Telegram CRITICAL
  + exit(1)"; SPEC L516 lists the script's dependencies as `core/config_loader` and `broker/zerodha_adapter`. (3) M35
  PF1 to PF8 L20 to L60 (IS-121): the script verifies the Chartink scanners only (PF1, PF3), as "a thin wrapper"
  around `check_scanner_connectivity` (PF6), with the imports of PF7 (no broker adapter).
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L19 to L27, L173 to L231 (whole file).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the script checks scanner-URL reachability only; it checks neither
  the map against the strategy files, nor duplicate names, nor the local endpoint, and imports no broker adapter
  (P07-S036; whole file). FACT (elsewhere, interface only): no startup path performs (b) or (d) (A02 T-02, L-09);
  (c) is `main.py`'s post-start self-check (A01 S-45, C28; part WI, WI-08), not the script.
- **Evidence:** P07-S028, P07-S036, P07-S039; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL. A conflict between sources of the same date that
  the code resolves one way (PF1 to PF8). This record counts only the script's documented scope. The absence of (b)
  and (d) at start-up is A02 T-02 ("ISSUE / FACT", A02's; its A06 side is part XA's) and is not counted here; (c) is
  A01's; the severity part is CK-02.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: documentation only; the checks that P17 lists are each recorded
  where they exist or are missing.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script; owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01 records); A02 (T-02); A01
  (the self-check).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 88 (context); STUDY question 25 (S10; part XA); A02 T-02, L-09; A01 S-45,
  C28; WI-08; CK-02.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CK-04 — PB-01's "connectivity check is WARN-only" (13-Jul-2026): no code reads the scanner type in the check, so in the script an unreachable PB-01 address returns 1 like any other

- **Area and flow:** the PB-01 end-of-day entry in the reachability check.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** CKS L31 (`5a71fb6`, 2026-07-13): "DAILY/EOD scanner (connectivity
  check is WARN-only)" (IS-158; CD-35). `PATHS.md` L185 item (3) (same date): the URL file serves "pre-flight
  connectivity, WARN-only", said of the file as a whole (IS-129 cites the same line). Two readings: (a) PB-01 alone
  was meant to be a warning; (b) the line repeats that the check as a whole is a warning. This record adopts neither.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L687 to L745 (A01's; no `scanner_type` read);
  `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L222 to L231.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the check iterates every map entry, PB-01 included, with the same
  rule; no code in the check or the script reads `scanner_type` (P07-S021, P07-S034). In the script an unreachable
  PB-01 address makes `all_reachable` False and the script return 1 (L222 to L231). At boot every unreachable
  scanner, PB-01 included, is a warning (S-44).
- **Evidence:** P07-S021, P07-S034; IS-158; CD-35; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; HISTORICAL. Under reading (a) the script
  differs (PB-01 fails the run); under reading (b) the script differs for every scanner, which is CK-02's conflict.
  MATCH part: at boot PB-01's result is a warning, as either reading says. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given:
  condition (a) fails (no mechanism for a per-scanner severity exists).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the script's exit code only.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script; owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01 records); A02 (the comment);
  A18 (PB-01, context only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 88; STUDY question 49; CD-35; CK-02.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CK-05 — Which file supplies the URL the check fetches: S15, the URL file's header, M33 SC7 and two later legacy records say `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`; the code always uses the map's own `chartink_url`, so the URL file's values reach no check (IC-22; A02 F-03's A06 side)

- **Area and flow:** the URL source of the reachability check (boot and script) and the second scanner file.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components (two files carry the same data; the sources disagree on which
  one counts); Intended against actual; Weakness (unreachable branches; no consistency check between the files).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** the URL file: (1) S15 L2790 (17-Apr-2026): "Simple name:url map
  for P17 HTTP HEAD pre-flight check" (IS-119); (2) CKS L3 (17-Apr-2026): "Canonical Chartink scanner URLs used by
  the pre-flight health check (P17)" (IS-122), and the schema docstring CFGLOADER L2386; (3) M33 SC7 L153 to L154
  (17-Apr-2026): "For each scanner in scan_webhook_map: Look up chartink_url from chartink_scanners.yaml" (IS-163);
  (4) `PATHS.md` L185 item (3) (2026-07-13): the URL file is the "pre-flight connectivity" file of the three-file
  checklist for a new scanner (IS-129); (5) `boot_path_pair_design_20jul2026.md` L220 to L222 (2026-07-20):
  "Following `_resolve_url` → `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`, it calls external public Chartink screener pages".
  The map's own URL: (6) S14 L2777 and SCANMAP L12 (17-Apr-2026): `chartink_url` "for P17 pre-flight check"
  (IS-119, IS-123); (7) the library docstring `utils/startup_checks.py` L663 to L664 (17-Apr-2026): "uses each
  entry's chartink_url. Falls back to chartink_scanners.scanners[name] if chartink_url not found on the entry";
  (8) found in the check, the schema-fix instruction `docs/web_claude/05_deployment/fixes/fix2_scan_webhook_schema.txt`
  L76 to L83 (`37f6ba5`, 2026-04-18): "If any code wants the URL (unlikely in hot path but possible for logging or
  preflight): url = cfg.scanners[scan_name].chartink_url", naming `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` among the call
  sites to check (IS-210 since the cross-part check; the first-pass file read this file only at its A06 lines). So the map's URL has
  a later source (18-Apr) as well as the URL file (13-Jul, 20-Jul). The conflict is IC-22. No source states that the
  two files must agree or which one wins if they differ.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L680 to L699, L756 to L776 (A01's file; A06 for the meaning);
  `core/config_loader.py` L2347 to L2364, L2385 to L2388 (A02's); callers `main.py` L2458 to L2459 and
  `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L208 to L214.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: only the map is iterated (L687); `_resolve_url` returns
  `str(entry.chartink_url)` whenever the entry has that attribute (L767 to L768); both callers pass
  `app_config.scan_webhook_map`, whose entries are `ScannerEntry` objects with a required `chartink_url` that must
  start with `http://` or `https://` (L2351, L2359 to L2364). INTERPRETATION (with P07-S041): the fallback to the URL
  file (L776) and the "no_url_configured" branch (L689 to L699) are never reached with a loaded configuration; an
  entry present only in the URL file is not checked; a value changed only in the URL file changes nothing in either
  check. FACT: the URL file's schema has no `http` check (L2388) and no validator compares the two files (P07-S017).
  FACT (scripted check, section 1): at the baseline the two files hold the same 16 keys and identical values, so no
  difference arises today. The URL file's only effects are that it must exist and validate (a missing or invalid file
  stops the boot with exit 5 and the script with exit 1; P07-S018, P07-S038) and that its name is in three
  file-presence lists (`utils/startup_checks.py` L840; A20's `scripts/preflight/checks/config_integrity.py` L23; the
  deprecated `scripts/premarket_healthcheck.py` L61) (P07-S021).
- **Evidence:** P07-S016 to P07-S018, P07-S021, P07-S041; IC-22; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. A conflict between sources that
  the code resolves one way (the map's URL), with the build instruction M33 SC7 and two later legacy records (13-Jul
  and 20-Jul) describing the path the code never takes; the unreachable fallback and the absence of any check that
  the two copies agree are weaknesses the code itself shows. A02 F-03 ("ISSUE"; A02's; an "also naming A06" record)
  keeps its record and counts the file side (a required file whose values cannot reach the check); this record counts
  the check's side, which A06 owns under decision C, and adds M33 SC7, the July records and the 18-Apr fix
  instruction, which A02 did not use (A02's AUDIT cites M33 only for SC6).
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent: at the baseline the copies agree; an operator who replaced an
  address in the URL file only, as S15 and that file's own note direct (L12 to L13), would see no change in either
  check.
- **Owner:** A06 (scanner-URL reachability, decision C; the script's owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20
  in the A01 records); A01 (`_resolve_url` in `utils/startup_checks.py`); A02 (the files, the schemas, F-03).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 89 and 82; STUDY question 48; IC-22; A02 F-03; CK-06.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify. STUDY question 48 (was the URL file meant to stay a separate list, and as the URL
  source or a fallback?) bears on treatment only.

##### CK-06 — The 32 committed URL values: the repository's own note still calls them placeholders to be replaced "once scanners are active", while legacy records from late April 2026 onward describe Chartink alerts in use and later sources call the values real; which values production checked is not established (IC-18)

- **Area and flow:** the `chartink_url` values of `config/scan_webhook_map.yaml` (the ones fetched) and their copies in
  `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a stated instruction not carried out in the repository, or a note that
  no longer describes the values); Consistency across components (sources disagree).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** placeholders, to be replaced: (1) S14 L2778 and S15 L2787 to
  L2789 (17-Apr-2026): "All 15 scanners populated with placeholder Chartink URLs"; "URLs are placeholders; replace
  with actual Chartink screener URLs once scanners are active" (IS-119); (2) CKS L12 to L13 (17-Apr-2026): "URLs are
  placeholders. Replace with actual Chartink screener URLs once scanners are configured and active in Chartink"
  (IS-122); (3) INTEGRITY-AUDIT (01-Aug-2026): the committed URL set "has never been verified against the live
  Chartink account" (IS-127). Real or working: (4) FIX-184, `main.py` L241 to L242 (2026-06-18): "the URLs are fine
  (any non-urllib UA returns 200)" (IS-124); (5) the dashboard reader's docstring (2026-08-16): "THE URL IS REAL",
  "the production file carries the actual screener URLs (measured 16-Aug ...)" (IS-125). Runtime statements in later
  records, not runtime evidence: (6) `boot_path_pair_design_20jul2026.md` L237 to L239 (2026-07-20): "Monday's boot
  logged `run_all_startup_checks: OK warnings=[]`, so every Chartink URL answered 2xx"; (7)
  `ops_dashboard/frontend/templates/logs.html` L13 to L14 (lines 2026-08-15, `59faabb`): "production writes
  `warnings=['scanner_unreachable']`". Scanners in use (HISTORICAL statements, not runtime evidence): the paper-trial
  plan of 17-Apr-2026 tells the operator to "Verify Chartink webhooks point to your machine" (IS-176, L248 to L251);
  the 18-Apr deployment step says to open port 5000 "for Chartink webhooks" (IS-171; whether applied: UNKNOWN); the
  receiver comment and commits of 28-Apr-2026 describe what "Chartink sends" (`signals/webhook_receiver.py` L617 to
  L619, `114d466`; commit `31a03c7`; IS-41); the PB-01 scanner was registered on 12/13-Jul-2026 "so Rama can save his
  alert" (IS-129; RS-07). The conflict is IC-18.
- **Actual code path:** `config/scan_webhook_map.yaml` L19 to L83 (the `chartink_url` lines); `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`
  L16 to L32; history `git log` (P07-S012); readers `utils/startup_checks.py` L764 to L768 and the dashboard (A15).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (scripted check, section 1; P07-S011, P07-S012): all 32 values are
  `https://chartink.com/screener/` followed by the key with underscores replaced by hyphens; the 15 original values
  are unchanged since `bcf03b5` (2026-04-17), and `5a71fb6` (2026-07-13), made after scanners were in use, added the
  PB-01 value in the same form; the note CKS L12 to L13 is unchanged. Current external context (Chartink "Stock
  Screener FAQ", posted 27-Jun-2017, second date 07-Jan-2025 shown, read 18-Nov-2026 in part, re-read in the check):
  screener addresses quoted in the page's comments, given as bare addresses with no scan title beside them, are
  lower-case words joined by hyphens (for example `open-equals-low-15-minute-timeframe`, `100-sma-crossover`), several
  ending in a numeric suffix (`-1`, `-6`, `-9`, `-84`) and one a single word; the page does not state how an address
  is assigned, or that a number is added when a name is taken. Current external context (Chartink "Webhook support
  for Alerts", as cited by P07-S048, read 17-Nov-2026): the sample body pairs `scan_name` "Short term breakouts" with
  `scan_url` "short-term-breakouts", a lower-case, hyphenated form of the scan's name. INTERPRETATION: an address of
  the repository's form (a hyphenated name) is also a form that real Chartink screener addresses take, so the pattern
  alone does not show that the values are placeholders (this weakens the pattern-based basis of the STUDY's
  interpretation P07-S011, whose other basis, the files' own "placeholders" note, stands); whether a real scan named
  like the key would have exactly this address, or one with a numeric suffix, is not stated by Chartink. FACT: the code fetches whatever values are loaded
  (P07-S041) and uses them for nothing else (P07-S024).
- **Evidence:** P07-S011, P07-S012, P07-S014, P07-S024, P07-S048; IS-41, IS-119, IS-122, IS-124, IS-125, IS-127,
  IS-129, IS-171, IS-176; IC-18; the July record, the dashboard template and the receiver comment above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with an UNKNOWN part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current
  external context. If the scanners were active, as the legacy records from 28-Apr onward describe (HISTORICAL), the
  repository cannot satisfy both of its own statements: either the values are still the placeholders, and the
  replacement that S15 and the note call for (S14 calls the map's values placeholders) was never made in the
  repository (the July addition even repeated the pattern), or the values are real addresses, and the note and S14
  and S15 no longer describe them. The later sources do not settle which: FIX-184 and the dashboard docstring say the
  URLs work or are real; the integrity audit says they were never verified (L147 to L149, citing the note itself); the
  July (20-Jul) and August (15-Aug) records report opposite boot outcomes. A02 F-03 already records the
  placeholder-against-real conflict for the identical values of the URL file; this record adds the map's values,
  which are the ones fetched, and the later sources. UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether any value was a working screener address, whether the
  production file differed from the repository, and which values the boot step and the script fetched in production.
  A02 T-21 ("UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED"; A02's) and F-03 ("ISSUE"; A02's) keep their records for the configuration in
  force and the file; this record is the A06 side (what the reachability check was given to fetch).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: the values affect only the reachability check and the dashboard's
  display, never intake (P07-S024); the meaning of a pass or a failure depends on them (CK-10).
- **Owner:** A06 (scanner-URL reachability, decision C); A02 (the files, T-21, F-03); A15 (the dashboard docstring
  and template, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 87; STUDY 18.2 item 10; STUDY question 46; IC-18; A02 T-21, F-03; CK-05,
  CK-10, CK-11.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify. STUDY question 46's second half (was the replacement ever made on the production
  machine?) is a runtime fact Rama may know; it would settle the UNKNOWN part, not the ISSUE.

##### CK-07 — A network failure loses its cause: both wired fetchers return the exception text as the "body", so the library records no error; the script's table says `has-content`, its verbose view "(empty body)", and the boot log names only the scanner

- **Area and flow:** the result of a scanner fetch that fails at the network level (timeout, name resolution, TLS,
  refused connection), in the script and at boot.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling (an error swallowed: its text is dropped); Intended against actual (a stated
  expectation not met on the wired path); Consistency across components (the fetcher contract and the library's error
  path disagree).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) M33 SC7 L134 to L135 (17-Apr-2026): the fetcher returns
  "(url: str, timeout: float) -> tuple[status, body_snippet]", and `ScannerCheck` carries `error: str | None` (L149);
  M33's test list L327: "One scanner times out -> reachable=False with error" (IS-163). (2) The test
  `tests/unit/test_startup_checks.py` L585 to L594 (lines 2026-04-17; A19's, not run): "http_fetcher_fn raises ->
  reachable=False with error (SC7)", with a fetcher that raises `ConnectionError("timed out")` (L211 to L212). (3)
  The script's own verbose branch, L163 to L164 (2026-04-17): when there is no snippet, print "error: <r.error>". (4)
  FIX-184, `main.py` L242 to L244 (2026-06-18): report real HTTP codes "so a genuinely bad URL (404) is
  distinguishable from a true connection failure (None)" (IS-124). (5) M35 PF5 L45: "If --verbose: print response
  snippet per scanner" (IS-121).
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L260 to L261 (and, since the initial commit, `git show bcf03b5:main.py` L147 to
  L148); `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L111 to L117, L142 to L166; `utils/startup_checks.py` L701 to L742.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the boot fetcher returns `(None, str(exc))` for every exception
  other than `HTTPError` (L260 to L261), and has returned that form since `bcf03b5`; the script's fetcher returns
  `(None, str(exc))` after the last `requests.RequestException` and sets the kept snippet to "" (L112 to L117).
  Neither raises for a network failure, so the library takes the returned path: it logs "check_scanner: %s returned
  status None (unreachable)" with the scanner name only, at WARNING the first time per scanner in a process and at
  DEBUG after that (L706 to L717, FIX-D), and stores `error=None` and `response_has_content=bool(body and
  body.strip())` (L723 to L730), which is True whenever the exception text is not blank (INTERPRETATION: the usual
  case; P07-S035). Only an exception raised by the fetcher reaches L731 to L742, which
  logs the URL and the exception and stores `error=str(exc)`. INTERPRETATION (script, L142 to L166; P07-S035): a
  network failure prints `NO`, status `-` and `DETAIL` `has-content`; `--verbose` prints "(empty body)"; the
  `error:` line of L163 to L164 appears only for an exception that is not a `RequestException` and for the
  unreachable "no_url_configured" case. INTERPRETATION (boot): the cause appears in no log line; a repeated `None`
  for the same scanner in the same process is logged at DEBUG only.
- **Evidence:** P07-S031, P07-S035, P07-S040, P07-S044; the code and test lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION. The verdict is right (unreachable;
  exit 1 in the script; a warning at boot) and FIX-184's stated aim (a `None` distinguishable from an HTTP code) is
  met. But the error path that M33's test list and the test describe for a timeout is never taken with the two wired
  fetchers, so the cause of every network failure is dropped, and the script reports the failure as `has-content`.
  KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the `error` field and the script's verbose `error:` branch exist
  for the case); (b) holds for the wired fetchers (by the code itself they never raise for a network failure;
  INTERPRETATION); (c) holds (M33's test list and the test name the timeout case); (d) fails: the verdict matches
  every source, and only the diagnostic detail contradicts M33's test expectation.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: diagnostics only; the operator sees that a scanner failed but not
  why.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script's fetcher and output; the script's owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the
  A01 records); A01 (`_http_fetch` and the library in `utils/startup_checks.py`, boot side); A19 (the test, stated
  expectation only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 91 (the `has-content` part); IS-163, IS-124; CK-02.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CK-08 — Run as documented, the script depends on the repository root being importable, which no procedure arranges and the script, unlike most scripts in its folder, does not arrange itself; then it ends with "cannot import core.config_loader" before any check

- **Area and flow:** starting `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` by the documented command.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (a documented invocation that depends on an unstated environment setting); Failure
  handling (two imports outside any `try`).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** the usage `python scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py [--config
  PATH] [--verbose] [--timeout N]`: PFS L10 and M35 PF2 L26 (17-Apr-2026; IS-121); the operator procedures of
  17-Apr-2026 that run it so: `v2_foundation_wrapup.txt` L39 (`python scripts\preflight_scanner_check.py`, after
  activating the virtual environment, L31), `pending_items_snapshot.txt` L72 to L78 (the morning routine, step d),
  `task3_nse_reference_data.txt` L344, `module_37_integration_smoke.txt` L213, and the paper-trial plan (IS-176).
  None of them sets `PYTHONPATH` or another path. The A.4 comment L67 to L68 (2026-04-26): the operator runs the
  script "before every trading day". The docstring's exit code 1 includes "import error" (L14).
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L19 to L27, L190 to L194, L203.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the script never changes `sys.path` (whole file), whereas 53 of the
  65 top-level `scripts/*.py` files do (P07-S037); the repository has no packaging file at its root and `scripts/`
  holds no `core` or `utils` entry (`git ls-tree`). FACT: the two scripts run just before it in the same morning
  routines (`pending_items_snapshot.txt` L74 to L77; `task3_nse_reference_data.txt` L340 to L344) insert the
  repository root themselves (`scripts/zerodha_login.py` L255; `scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L49). FACT: the
  script's own imports needing the root are `core.config_loader` (L191, inside `try`) and `utils.startup_checks`
  (L203, outside `try`). Current external context (Python 3.11 `sys.path`, as cited by P07-S037, read 17-Nov-2026;
  re-opened in the check, Python 3.11.18 "The initialization of the sys.path module search path",
  https://docs.python.org/3.11/library/sys_path_init.html, read 18-Nov-2026): "The first entry in the module search
  path is the directory that contains the input script, if there is one"; the contents of `PYTHONPATH`, if set, are
  added; then the `site` module adds `site-packages` (where `.pth` files are processed). INTERPRETATION: for the
  documented command the first entry is `scripts/`, not the working directory; unless `PYTHONPATH`, a `.pth` file or
  another environment setting makes the repository root importable,
  L191 raises `ImportError` and the script prints "ERROR: cannot import core.config_loader: ..." and returns 1, before
  any scanner is checked. FACT: the imports at L27 (`requests`, at module level) and L203 (`utils.startup_checks`) are
  outside any `try` (P07-S037). HISTORICAL: the A19 tests load the script by file path
  (`tests/unit/test_preflight_scanner.py` L30 to L39), not by the documented command.
- **Evidence:** P07-S028, P07-S030, P07-S037; the code lines and procedures above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with an UNKNOWN part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current
  external context. Basis: the documented invocation, repeated in five procedures of 17-Apr-2026, works only in an
  environment that none of them describes, and the script does not make itself importable as most scripts in its
  folder do, including the two run before it in the same routines. MATCH part: an import failure at L191 to L194 gives the documented exit 1 with its own text (CK-15).
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the operator's environment, whether any run ended at the import, and whether the operator
  ran the script at all. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: condition (b) fails (the outcome depends on the
  environment, which is not established; it is not certain by language semantics alone).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established; if it occurred, the operator would see an ERROR line
  instead of the scanner table, not a false scanner failure.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script; owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01 records); A17 (the operator
  environment, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 91 (the import part); STUDY question 47 (how it was run); CK-11, CK-15.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify. How the script was run (STUDY question 47) would settle the UNKNOWN part.

##### CK-09 — The script's stated figures and outputs against the code: "3 attempts * 10s timeout = 30s" is no bound; PF3's "response size" is neither recorded nor printed; "returning expected shape" is not tested

- **Area and flow:** the script's timing comment, its table and its success statement.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) the A.4 comment L67 to L71 (`53e61e2`, 2026-04-26): two
  retries with 1 s backoff, "without materially slowing a real-failure case (3 attempts * 10s timeout = 30s)". (2)
  M35 PF3 L34 to L36 (17-Apr-2026): "Record status, response size"; the table "scanner_name, URL, reachable,
  status_code, response_size" (IS-121; CD-23). (3) M35 PF1 L21 to L22 and PFS L5 to L6 (17-Apr-2026): verify the
  scanners "are reachable and returning expected shape" (IS-121). (4) Against (3): PF3 step 4, L37: "Exit 0 if all
  reachable with 2xx"; PF8 L59 excludes "Detecting Chartink HTML changes"; M33 SC7 L156 to L157: "DO NOT parse
  Chartink HTML (just reachability)". PF1's and P17's "15" scanners is the count at 17-Apr-2026 (CD-24; PB-01 added on
  13-Jul) and is not counted here.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L74 to L75, L95 to L118, L127 to L166, L216 to L231;
  `utils/startup_checks.py` L703, L729, L744.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: up to three `requests.get` calls with `timeout=t` and two 1.0 s
  sleeps (L99 to L117); `resp.text` is read in full and 300 characters kept (L102). Current external context
  (Requests 2.34.2, as cited by P07-S032, read 17-Nov-2026): the timeout is "not a time limit on the entire response
  download" but the longest wait for bytes, applied to the connect and the read timeouts separately, and the body is
  downloaded at once by default. INTERPRETATION: 30 s is not an upper bound per scanner (each attempt can exceed 10 s;
  two sleeps add 2 s), and the 16 scanners are fetched one after another with no overall limit; actual durations are
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT: no response size is recorded or printed; `DETAIL` shows the error or
  `has-content`/`empty-body` (L145; P07-S035). FACT: no test of the page's shape exists; `response_has_content` (L729)
  is printed but decides neither `reachable` (L703) nor `all_reachable` (L744) nor the exit code (L222).
- **Evidence:** P07-S031 to P07-S035, P07-S039; CD-23, CD-24; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current external context. Three
  stated behaviours that the code does not implement: the 30 s figure, the response size and the "expected shape";
  for the last, the narrower PF3 step 4 rule (2xx) is what the code applies, and no source defines "expected shape".
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: none of the three changes the verdict; the timing figure can
  understate how long a failing run takes.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script; owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01 records).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 91 and 94; CD-23, CD-24; CK-13, CK-18; A01 C32 (the boot step's duration,
  A01's).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### CK-10 — What the checks can show: a pass means only that chartink.com answered the configured addresses with a final 2xx from the legacy host; nothing ties those addresses to the alerts, the webhook path or the receiver, so the result can pass while intake is broken and fail while intake works

- **Area and flow:** the outbound reachability check (boot and script), the local endpoint check and the inbound
  webhook, taken together: what an operator can learn from them about whether Chartink signals will arrive.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (a check weaker than its stated purpose; missing validation of the Chartink side);
  Intended against actual (a stated purpose served only in part).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL, except the external pages):** (1) LOCKED-YAML P17 rationale L373
  (17-Apr-2026): the check "Catches the most common failure modes (scanner deleted, URL changed, duplicate config,
  Flask not bound)" (IS-118). (2) The A.4 comment L67 to L71 and its audit item (26-Apr-2026): the operator runs the
  script before every trading day and a failure "prompts cancelling trading" (CK-02): the result was to inform a
  go/no-go decision. (3) P17 (c) and M33 SC8 L174 to L175 (17-Apr-2026): the own endpoint by a local `/health` call,
  "to confirm Flask is listening" (IS-118, IS-164). (4) The paper-trial plan (17-Apr-2026): "Verify Chartink webhooks
  point to your machine", a manual step (IS-176). (5) INTEGRITY-AUDIT (01-Aug-2026): "Chartink-side scanner/webhook
  arming is manual and has no in-system reconciliation" (IS-127; RS-08); it names two scanners whose alerts it says
  never arrived, with only an aggregate silence check to notice (L145 to L149, L389 to L391; runtime statements, not
  evidence). (6) A20's pre-flight
  `scripts/preflight/checks/signals.py` L7, L31 to L32 and L40 (A20's file; interface only): a loopback `/health`
  answer shows that "Chartink signals can physically arrive", and its failure text says they "cannot arrive". (7)
  Current external context (Chartink "Alerts Guide", as cited by P07-S050, read 17-Nov-2026): the receiving system
  "must be deployed on the internet and reachable by a domain or an IP address"; (CK-WEBHOOK, P07-S048) "only one
  webhook for one scan and alert". (8) Current external context (Chartink "Stock Screener FAQ" and "Scanner User
  Guide", read 18-Nov-2026, in part; re-read in the check): private scans are "hidden and inaccessible to others"
  (FAQ); a support reply speaks of "restoring a deleted scan" (FAQ); a reader comment on the Guide refers to scans
  "NOT marked as Public" (the Guide's own text does not explain visibility); neither page states what a screener
  address answers for a private, deleted or never-existing scan.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L687 to L745 (the 2xx rule, L703); `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py`
  L208 to L231; `main.py` L246 to L261; the receiver's route and checks (P01, P02); `scripts/preflight/checks/signals.py`
  L17 to L42 (A20's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: success is any final 2xx answer to a GET of each map entry's
  `chartink_url`, after redirects (CK-18; P07-S032, P07-S044); no code compares the screener address with the alert,
  the body's `scan_url` or `alert_name`, the webhook path or the secret (P07-S023, P07-S024); the local check calls
  the process's own `/health` on the loopback address (P07-S045, P07-S046); no check (the boot step, the post-start
  self-check, the script or A20's pre-flight) tests the receiver from outside the host or reads the Chartink alert
  configuration (P07-S036, P07-S046, P07-S047; check search of non-test code for public-address and external-URL
  terms: the only public-address lookup, `broker/auth_recovery.py` L67 to L71, serves the broker allow-list; A19's
  `scripts/load_test_signals.py` posts synthetic alerts to a target given to it, default the local host, L52, L97;
  decision E, a test tool, not a check). INTERPRETATION (P07-S024,
  P07-S047): a pass shows only that chartink.com answered the 16 addresses from the legacy host; it does not show
  that each address is the scan an alert uses, that the alerts exist and are active, that they post to
  `/webhook/<key>` with the token, or that Chartink can reach the receiver. A failure does not show that intake is
  affected: a stale or wrong address fails the check while alerts still arrive. P17's "scanner deleted" is caught
  only if chartink.com answers a deleted (or private) scan's address with a final non-2xx status, which no admitted
  source states; "URL changed" is caught for the screener address only, never for an alert's webhook address; "Flask
  not bound" is caught by the local check, as P17 (c) states.
- **Evidence:** P07-S023, P07-S024, P07-S032, P07-S036, P07-S044 to P07-S048, P07-S050, P07-S051; IS-118, IS-127,
  IS-164, IS-176; the code lines above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with MATCH parts and an UNKNOWN part. FACT; HISTORICAL;
  INTERPRETATION; current external context. Basis: the check's result and the health of signal intake are decoupled,
  so a check meant to catch "scanner deleted, URL changed" and to inform the operator's decision to trade can give
  false passes and false failures (a weak check, Document 04, section 5.3); the legacy's own integrity audit recorded
  the missing Chartink-side reconciliation (IS-127). MATCH parts: no HTML is parsed, as P17, PF8 and SC7 state; the
  local check confirms only that the process answers, as P17 (c) and SC8 state (A01 S-45; part WI, WI-08); Chartink's
  side was to be verified by hand (IS-176). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: what chartink.com answers for a deleted,
  private or never-existing screener address, and whether the alerts were verified by hand. A20's statement that a
  loopback answer shows that Chartink signals "can physically arrive" is A20's and is handed over (section 5).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established; depends on CK-06's URL values and on Chartink's answers
  (UNKNOWN).
- **Owner:** A06 (scanner-URL reachability, decision C; the script's owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20
  in the A01 records); A01 (the boot step and the self-check); A20 (the pre-flight checks and their texts).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 points 91 (the success-rule part) and 92; direction question 5; IS-127; CK-06,
  CK-18; WI-08, WI-09.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### CK-11 — UNKNOWN: the Chartink-side configuration, the checks' runtime results and the library versions on which part CK depends

- **Area and flow:** the facts that would settle the UNKNOWN parts of CK-02, CK-06, CK-08 and CK-10, and the points
  handed to CK by parts WI, WA and WD.
- **Issue category:** UNKNOWN (runtime and external facts without surviving evidence).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not applicable; the questions are what happened and how external systems
  behave.
- **Actual code path:** as in CK-01 to CK-10.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (no production log, database row, deployed
  configuration or Chartink account record of the legacy period is admitted evidence; Document 06, section 4.2):
  (1) which Chartink alerts existed, the webhook address each posted to, each alert's frequency and unique or
  duplicate setting, whether result columns were added to their payloads, and the scan names sent as `scan_name`;
  no configuration file records the webhook addresses (P07-S026, P07-S048 to P07-S051; RS-07, RS-08); (2) whether
  any committed `chartink_url` was a working screener address, and the scanner files in force on the machines
  (CK-06; A02 T-21); (3) whether the boot scanner step ran (it is skipped on COLD, A01's), what it found, and how long
  it took (A01 C32; the July and August records' statements are not evidence, CK-06); (4) whether, when and how
  the operator ran the script, in which environment, and what it printed (CK-08); (5) what chartink.com answers for a
  deleted, private or never-existing screener address, and whether its edge treats the two User-Agent texts

##### CK-19 — `--timeout` accepts zero and negative values; under the published Requests and urllib3 sources every fetch then fails before any request is sent, and the script reports every scanner "unreachable" (added in the check)

- **Area and flow:** the script's `--timeout` option through its fetcher and the library's error path.
- **Issue category:** Weakness (missing validation of an operator input); Failure handling (a failure reported
  as an outcome it is not).
- **Intended behaviour and source (HISTORICAL):** M35 PF2 and PF4 L25 to L28, L41 (17-Apr-2026): "Timeout: 10s
  default, --timeout override" (IS-121); no source states a valid range. The A.4 comment L67 to L71 (26-Apr-2026):
  a false failure "prompts cancelling trading -- expensive". Intent for an invalid value: not established.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` L50 to L56, L96, L101, L111, L206, L213;
  `utils/startup_checks.py` L731 to L742.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `--timeout` is `type=int` with no range check (L50 to L56;
  P07-S029); the value reaches `requests.get(..., timeout=t)` (L96, L101); the fetcher catches only
  `requests.RequestException` (L111). Current external context (published source on the projects' official
  repositories, read 18-Nov-2026; copies in the work area only, nothing installed or imported): Requests `src/requests/adapters.py`
  builds `TimeoutSauce(connect=timeout, read=timeout)`, urllib3's `Timeout` (import L29 at `v2.33.1`, L32 on
  `main`), outside any `try` for a non-tuple timeout (tag `v2.33.1`, the repository pin, L642; `main`, `__version__`
  2.34.2, L693, before `conn.urlopen` at L696); urllib3
  `src/urllib3/util/timeout.py` (`main`; latest changelog entry 2.8.0, 2026-09-15) `Timeout._validate_timeout` raises
  `ValueError` "the timeout cannot be set to a value less than or equal to 0" (L158 to L163); Requests
  `src/requests/exceptions.py` L20: `class RequestException(IOError)`. Current external context (Python 3.11.18
  built-in exceptions, https://docs.python.org/3.11/library/exceptions.html, read 18-Nov-2026): `IOError` is an alias
  of `OSError`, and `OSError` and `ValueError` are separate subclasses of `Exception`. INTERPRETATION (for those
  versions): with `--timeout 0` or a negative value each fetch raises `ValueError` on its first attempt, which is not
  a `RequestException`, so it leaves the fetcher without retry and is caught by the library's `except Exception`
  (L731 to L742): every scanner is `reachable=False` with the `ValueError` text as `error`, a WARNING line per scanner
  names it, its URL and the text (L732 to L734), `DETAIL` shows the text, and the script prints "FAILED: <n> of <n> scanner(s) unreachable" and
  returns 1, although no scanner address was contacted.
- **Evidence:** P07-S029, P07-S031, P07-S032, P07-S040; the code lines and external sources above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with an UNKNOWN part. FACT; HISTORICAL; INTERPRETATION; current
  external context. Basis: missing validation of an operator input (Document 04, section 5.3, "Weakness") whose
  outcome, under the published versions, is a false "unreachable" verdict for every scanner, the kind of false
  failure the A.4 comment says is expensive; the `error` text does show the cause. UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the
  installed Requests and urllib3 versions (CK-11), and whether the option was ever given such a value. KNOWN DEFECT
  considered and not given: condition (a) fails (no mechanism for an invalid timeout exists).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: an operator input error only; the printed error names the cause.
- **Owner:** A06 (the script; owner OPEN FOR RAMA: A06 under decision C, A20 in the A01 records); A17 (installed
  versions, interface only).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.2 item 4; P07-S029; CK-07, CK-11, CK-13.
- **Rama:** not needed.

  registers, the A01 to A05 AUDITs and RECONCILED TOPIC RECORDS) are cited by file and line.
- **External pages read by this part** (current external context; read 18-Nov-2026): SQLite "Date And Time
  Functions", https://www.sqlite.org/lang_datefunc.html ("last updated on 2026-07-11" as shown): `date()` "returns the
  date as text in this format: YYYY-MM-DD"; a "[+-]HH:MM" suffix "is subtracted from the indicated date and time in
  order to compute zulu time". Python 3.15.2 "Built-in Functions", https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html
  (first 100,000 of about 131,000 characters): `getattr`: "If the named attribute does not exist, default is returned
  if provided, otherwise AttributeError is raised." pydantic "Models", https://pydantic.dev/docs/validation/latest/concepts/models/
  (reached by the redirect from docs.pydantic.dev; "Latest (stable)", no version number shown): "`name` wasn't set
  when `user` was initialized, so the default value was used"; `extra='forbid'`: "Providing extra data is not
  permitted." The pydantic published source at tag v2.13.0 (the version `requirements.txt` pins) is used through
  P08-S006 (read 17-Nov-2026). The installed versions of Python, pydantic and SQLite on the legacy machines are
  UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (STUDY 18.2 item 3).
- **Re-read by the check helper** (18-Nov-2026): the SQLite "Date And Time Functions" page (the two quotes confirmed;
  "This page was last updated on 2026-07-11 15:07:16Z"); the pydantic "Models" page at
  https://pydantic.dev/docs/validation/latest/concepts/models/ (both quotes confirmed; the version selector shows
  "Latest (stable)" and releases to v2.12, with notes naming v2.13 and v2.14); the Python 3.15.2 "Built-in Functions"
  page (the `getattr` quote confirmed, in its first 100,000 characters); and sqlite.org "Quirks, Caveats, and Gotchas In
  SQLite", https://www.sqlite.org/quirks.html, section 8 (quoted in XA-03).
- **Rules applied.** Nothing in the clone was written, checked out, run, imported or tested; no broker, scanner,
  webhook or Chartink address was contacted. Labels, results and record fields as the A05 AUDIT, sections 1.5 to 1.7
  (the A01 AUDIT's four-condition KNOWN DEFECT rule unchanged). The A06-to-A07 handoff line stays OPEN FOR RAMA (STUDY
  section 17 point 1) and is adopted nowhere here; who confirms A02's effect readings for T-07, T-12 and A02-03 stays
  OPEN FOR RAMA (point 2); the owner of `scripts/preflight_scanner_check.py` stays recorded both ways (point 3);
  the network path stays OPEN FOR RAMA (point 4). Inherited items and Open Issues Register entries keep their owners,
  IDs, classifications, statuses and treatments (D-13); A06 records its own side only; a recorded treatment or Phase 2
  input is never used as legacy intent. Credential exposure is out of scope (D-07); the webhook secret is named only as
  `WEBHOOK_SECRET`. No account identifier, personal name other than Rama's, address, e-mail address, machine path or
  secret value is written.
- **Records observations** (true discrepancies in a project record or in the STUDY) are listed in section 3.2 and are
  not counted as MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN, as WA-02 and EV-15 are.

### 12.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

#### 12.3.1 Records

##### XA-01 — Which refusals leave a `signals` row, and the status names readers expect: the receiver's refusals other than layer 1 store no row (P16 and the schema comment say every signal gets a row); readers in A15 and A12 count, classify or prune `DUPLICATE`, the answer-only statuses and a `DROPPED_` family that no code writes, and two A15 readers test for `DUPLICATE` by substring, which also matches the `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` that the processor writes

- **Area and flow:** the receiver's per-symbol refusals and what they leave in `signals`; the status vocabulary held
  by the readers of `signals` (A15's classifier, dashboard and reports; A12's prune), the schema CHECK and P16
  (reader side; the writer side is EV-07, WR10's list WI-15, layer 1's refusal WD-07).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Consistency
  across components (modules disagreeing on a status); Flow and state (a refused signal leaves no per-signal record).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) LOCKED-YAML P16 L360 to L368 (2026-04-17; IS-98): "Every
  signal_id assigned at webhook gets a final status before the system 'forgets' it"; the list names `REJECTED_DUP`,
  `REJECTED_EXPIRED`, `REJECTED_KILL`, `REJECTED_MARKET_HOURS`, `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT`, `DROPPED_QUEUE_FULL` among
  others; "Every status writes a row to signals table with timestamp and reason"; rationale "many signals vanish
  silently". (2) SCHEMA L36 to L38 (2026-04-17; IS-102): `signals` is the "Immutable log of EVERY incoming webhook
  signal. One row per (symbol, scanner) pair". Against (1) and (2): M19 WR9 (IS-97): a row "For each accepted"
  signal; WR10 (IS-46): the other outcomes are per-symbol statuses in the 200 body (IC-29; neither side later).
  (3) SCHEMA O2 comment L51 to L59 (2026-06-14, `4f6c3c5`; IS-102): the status is "an OPEN set"; the CHECK is "a
  shape/typo guard ..., NOT a closed enum — by design". (4) A15's review contract, `reports/daily_trade_review.py`
  L709 to L718 (2026-07-01, `01e07b7`; IS-139): duplicates "have NO per-signal row — only the aggregate
  webhook_audit.signals_rejected count". (5) A15's classifier comment, `reports/signal_status.py` L43 to L45 (lines
  2026-07-18, `b3a960d`): a status outside its known sets is "a real schema-drift alarm, not a silent catch-all"; its
  `is_dedup_duplicate` docstring L118 to L120 (same date): "Deliberately NOT a substring test: `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL`
  also contains "DUPLICATE"". (6) A15's dashboard reader, `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/db_reader.py` L172 to L177
  (2026-07-03, `2f0d4b5`): stored `DUPLICATE` rows are "typically ~0 — the webhook TTL dedup drops dupes PRE-INSERT";
  L667 (2026-07-03, `c62bcfe`): "duplicated = DUPLICATE (post-insert race only; bulk dedup is pre-insert)". (4) to (6)
  are other topics' files, read at the interface as intent sources only ((4) is IS-139; the lines of (5) and (6) are
  IS-219, added in the cross-part check; IS-140 cites `reports/signal_status.py` L40 only).
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L904 to L926 (`INVALID_SYMBOL`, `INVALID_PRICE`, `EXPIRED`,
  returned before the claim), L934 to L945 (`IN_PROCESS`; the TTL layer's `DUPLICATE`), L957 (`new_signal_id()`),
  L963 to L981 and L1015 (the fingerprint layer's `DUPLICATE` after the INSERT is refused), L708 to L717
  (`REJECTED_EXCLUDED_SYMBOL`), L581 to L583 (the outside-window 403 for the whole request); `signals/signal_processor.py`
  L1201 (`f"REJECTED_{rej.check}"`; checks such as `KILL_SWITCH` L907, `OUTSIDE_ENTRY_WINDOW` L912, `EXPIRED` L918 to
  L919, `UNKNOWN_STRATEGY` L935 to L953); the receiver's L1016 to L1044 (`STORE_ERROR`) and L987 to L1013 (the
  `QUEUE_FULL` retry route); `capital/risk_engine.py` L788 to L794
  (A09's check 10, `DUPLICATE_SYMBOL`) and `signals/signal_processor.py` L1324 to L1325 (its label passed to
  `_PipelineReject`, stored by L1201);
  `core/schema.sql` L60 to L70 (A03's); readers (interface only): `reports/signal_status.py` L46 to L48, L76, L115 to
  L122; `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/db_reader.py` L182, L676, L677; `reports/daily_report.py` L708, L711;
  `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L826 to L828, L862, L877; `scripts/generate_screened_stocks_csv.py` L65 to L67, L98,
  L150; `scripts/eod_cleanup.py` L234 (A12's).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: none of the receiver's per-symbol refusals writes a row:
  `INVALID_SYMBOL`, `INVALID_PRICE`, `EXPIRED` and the TTL `DUPLICATE` return before any identifier is made;
  `REJECTED_EXCLUDED_SYMBOL` returns before `_process_signal`; the fingerprint `DUPLICATE` returns after an identifier
  has been made at L957 and the INSERT refused, so that identifier is discarded with no row (the case P16's "Every
  signal_id assigned at webhook" names). FACT (added in the check): the L957 identifier is also discarded with no row
  of its own when the INSERT fails for another reason (`STORE_ERROR`, L1016 to L1044: the transaction rolls back; the
  batch answers a retryable 503, WD-19) and on the `QUEUE_FULL` retry route (L987 to L1013: the earlier row's
  identifier is re-queued, or the answer is `QUEUE_FULL` with that row unchanged). A full queue is written into the
  row as `QUEUE_FULL`. The processor writes `REJECTED_<check>` names (for example `REJECTED_KILL_SWITCH`,
  `REJECTED_OUTSIDE_ENTRY_WINDOW`, `REJECTED_EXPIRED`, and `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` when A09's risk check 10
  refuses: `capital/risk_engine.py` L788 to L794, `signals/signal_processor.py` L1324 to L1325, L1201).
  FACT (search of non-test, non-document code): no code writes `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT`, `DROPPED_QUEUE_FULL` or any
  `DROPPED_` status, and no code at all (writer or reader) names P16's `REJECTED_DUP`, `REJECTED_KILL` or
  `REJECTED_MARKET_HOURS`; the readers `reports/signal_status.py` L76, `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/db_reader.py`
  L677, `ops_dashboard/backend/services/scanner_attribution.py` L187, `ops_dashboard/backend/services/trade_logs.py`
  L178 and L680 and `scripts/generate_screened_stocks_csv.py` L98 and L150 test for a `DROPPED_` prefix, and the last
  names `DROPPED_DEDUP`, `DROPPED_EXPIRED` and `DROPPED_KILL_SWITCH` (L65 to L67; 2026-05-14, `1b7225c`). FACT
  (readers, interface only): A15's classifier lists `ACCEPTED`, `IN_PROCESS`, `DUPLICATE`, `INVALID_SYMBOL`,
  `INVALID_PRICE` and `OUTSIDE_HOURS` among its known statuses (L46 to L48) and tests `DUPLICATE` by equality (L122);
  the dashboard counts rows with `status='DUPLICATE'` (L182) and maps them to "duplicated" (L676); A12's prune deletes
  `DUPLICATE` rows (L234). FACT (added in the check): the daily report (`"DUPLICATE" in status.upper()`, L708) and the
  daily trade review (`su == "DUPLICATE" or "DUPLICATE" in su`, L826; lines 2026-07-01, `01e07b7`) test by substring,
  the test the classifier's docstring (L118 to L120) says also matches `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL`; the review marks
  such a row "duplicate" and counts it in `dup_stored` (L826 to L828, L862, L877), and the report treats it as not
  queued (L708 to L710). The daily report's "selected" test also names `PLACED`, `FILLED`, `SIZED` and `APPROVED`
  (L711), which the CHECK does not admit (L60 to L70), so no row written under the current schema holds them.
  INTERPRETATION: at the baseline no A06 writer stores `DUPLICATE`, an answer-only status or a `DROPPED_` status, so
  the equality and prefix branches above can find only rows written by some other process (none found in code;
  whether any process wrote such rows is UNKNOWN, XA-05 (4)); the two substring tests also count the risk engine's
  refusals as duplicates. The dashboard docstring's "post-insert race" (source (6)) does not match the code: the
  race is answered `DUPLICATE` and writes no row (L981 to L1015). MATCH part (FACT): every status A06 writes passes
  the CHECK, which admits the answer-only values as a deliberate superset (source (3); EV-07 records the same for the
  writer side); the review contract's "no per-signal row" for duplicates agrees with the code (source (4)).
- **Evidence:** P02-S069; P03-S001, P03-S011, P03-S014; P05-S013; P11-S013, P11-S014, P11-S016, P11-S034, P11-S049,
  P11-S057; the code lines above; the searches of section 1 and, in the check, `DROPPED_`,
  `REJECTED_DUP\b|REJECTED_KILL\b|MARKET_HOURS`, `DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` and the literal writes of `PLACED`, `FILLED`, `SIZED`
  and `APPROVED` (non-test, non-document code, with the four exclusions).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. FACT; INTERPRETATION; HISTORICAL. (1) Row coverage:
  a conflict between sources of the same date (P16 and the schema comment against WR9 and WR10; IC-29) that the code
  resolves one way, leaving P16's per-signal rows unimplemented for the receiver's refusals other than layer 1 (layer
  1, `IN_PROCESS` and `DROPPED_IN_FLIGHT`, is WD-07's and is not counted here). (2) Vocabulary: readers in other
  topics hold statuses that no code writes (`DUPLICATE`, the answer-only statuses, the `DROPPED_` family that also
  covers P16's two `DROPPED_` names, and `PLACED`, `FILLED`, `SIZED` and `APPROVED` at L711), and two of them take the written
  `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` for a duplicate, a consistency difference between components that Document 04, section
  5.3 lists. P16's `TRADED` is EV-07's, and the visibility of the `QUEUE_FULL` row against P16's `DROPPED_QUEUE_FULL`
  is weighed in WD-05; neither is counted here. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: for (1) the sources conflict, so condition
  (d) cannot hold; for (2) the reader branches are other topics' code (A15's, A12's), outside A06's mechanism.
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: no effect on a trading decision is shown. A refused signal other
  than those the audit row counts for a 200 answer leaves no per-signal trace (the counts are WI-18's); the readers'
  equality and prefix tests for `DUPLICATE` and `DROPPED_` count zero for every row the code writes, while the two
  substring tests count any stored `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` row as a duplicate (how A15's reports present it is
  A15's).
- **Owner:** A06 (the receiver's refusal routes; the statuses A06 writes); A15 (classifier, dashboard, reports); A12
  (prune filter); A03 (the CHECK and its comments).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 78; IC-29; IS-46, IS-97, IS-98, IS-102, IS-139; WD-07 (layer 1); WD-03 (every
  `IntegrityError` answered `DUPLICATE`); WD-05 (`QUEUE_FULL` against P16); WD-19 (`STORE_ERROR` roll-back); EV-07
  (writer side, `TRADED`); WI-15 (WR10's list); WI-18 (the counts); A02 K-04 (excluded symbols not stored); handoff A15
  (the substring tests), A09 (check 10's label).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### XA-02 — The five existing technical records against the code: no A06 claim differs, but three record statements describe the receiver and the processor otherwise than the code (construction "Flask object only", a separate EOD route, the processor named as a user of `build_sr_detector`), one boot-table statement is A01's, and no record treats A06 in depth

- **Area and flow:** documents and records against the code: the A06 claims of `01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md`,
  `02_REPOSITORY_AND_FILE_MAP.md`, `03_STARTUP_RUNTIME_AND_SCHEDULER.md`, `03A_CRON_REGISTRY_TABLE.md` and
  `LEGACY_SYSTEM_MANUAL_INDEX.md` (project folder `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/00_EXISTING_LEGACY_TECHNICAL_RECORDS/`;
  Document 08, section 9).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement; implemented
  behaviour that nothing describes).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the records are descriptions, dated 18-Sep-2026, of a worktree at `e7bf477` with
  an uncommitted change set, described as "`970aabf` plus the S&R shadow files"; `e7bf477` is not in the clone
  (P10-S007, P10-S008; IS-150). (1) Record 03 R03-01, L112: Phase 0e "webhook receiver constructed — Flask object
  only". (2) Record 02 R02-26, L972: "a separate EOD route (:751)". (3) Record 02 R02-54, L1143 to L1145:
  `build_sr_detector()` is "the single wiring point used by main.py, scripts/sr_level_export.py and
  signals/signal_processor.py" (P10-S079). (4) Record 03 R03-07, L149: 15 checks run at boot, including "webhook endpoint".
  (5) The manual index RMI-01, L42 to L43: chapters 05 "Signals and strategies" and 06 "Filtering and trade decision"
  NOT STARTED. HISTORICAL.
- **Actual code path:** (1) `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L216 to L220 (the constructor starts the daemon thread
  `in_flight_sweeper`), L227 and L258 to L292 (it reads `config/symbol_aliases.yaml`, L265); `main.py` L3407 (the
  construction), L3987 to L4000 (the server, later); (2) L364 and L406 (the only two routes), L562 to L564 (an `eod`
  scanner branches to `_handle_eod`, L751, inside the one POST route); (3) `signals/signal_processor.py` L640 (`from
  sr_detector import Candidate`, a lazy import inside the observer method); (4) `utils/startup_checks.py` L1587 to
  L1604 and `main.py` L2461 (`webhook_url=None`) (A01's; interface).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part (FACT): of the records' 94 A06 claims, 78 agree with the code at
  `970aabf` and none differs (6 AGREES IN PART, 2 CANNOT CHECK, 8 BASELINE DIFFERENCE: 78 + 6 + 0 + 2 + 8 = 94); the
  receiver (1,207 lines), the throttle (125) and the package file (0) have the line counts the records give (P10-S009,
  P10-S152). ISSUE part (FACT): (1) the receiver's construction is not "Flask object only": it starts the sweeper and
  reads the alias file (P10-S094, P10-S163); (2) the EOD path is a branch of the single POST route chosen by the
  scanner's configured type, not a separate route (P10-S051, P10-S166); the same record's "one `webhook_audit` row per
  POST regardless of outcome" falls short for an over-limit body (P10-S153; that missing audit row is WI-02's and is
  not counted here); (3) the processor does not call `build_sr_detector`: `main.py` builds the detector (L3493 to
  L3494) and passes it in, and the processor imports only `Candidate`, lazily inside its observer method (L640)

  (`REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL`) in PQ-05. The eight BASELINE DIFFERENCE results come from the processor's S&R shadow lines

  behaviour. The two CANNOT CHECK results stay UNKNOWN (XA-05).

  to P10-S155, P10-S163, P10-S166, P10-S176; the code lines above (re-read).

##### XA-03 — Legacy operating and architecture documents that the code contradicts: the architecture document's processor pipeline, the incident runbook's `webhook_audit` query on a column that does not exist, and README's "signal processor/queue"

- **Area and flow:** documents against the code: what the operator and a reader of the legacy documents are told about
  the processor's pipeline, the audit table and the `signals/` package.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (documented behaviour that the code does not implement); Weakness (an
  operator procedure that fails as written).
- **Intended behaviour and source (all HISTORICAL):** (1) `docs/01_system_architecture.md` L66 to L68 (lines
  2026-06-03, `37e6698`; IS-146; CD-32): the SignalProcessor "runs screening pipeline (quality scorer → step executor
  → secondary screener → entry gate), sizes positions, places orders". (2) `docs/05_incident_response.md` L239 to L244
  ("P2: Missed Signals"; lines 2026-06-03, file 2026-08-06, `5bafe3e`; IS-147; CD-33): step 1 runs `SELECT
  scanner_name, response_code, created_at FROM webhook_audit ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 10`. (3) `README.md` L66
  (2026-07-16, `bc5a2a9`; IS-28; CD-34): `signals/` holds the "webhook receiver, signal processor/queue". Whether the
  runbooks were meant to be kept in step: the legacy team corrected wrong runbook instructions in three documents on
  02-Aug-2026 (IS-160, `docs/MASTER_PENDING_01-Aug-2026.md` L1661), which did not include these two procedures.
- **Actual code path:** (1) `signals/signal_processor.py` the screening call (P05-S055) and `continue_from_gate` as
  the only route to the entry gate (P05-S043); (2) `core/schema.sql` L581 to L593 (`webhook_audit` columns: `id`,
  `ts`, `scanner_name`, `source_ip`, `payload_size_bytes`, `response_code`, `signals_accepted`, `signals_rejected`,
  `duration_ms`, `date`); (3) `main.py` L3402 to L3404 (the queue is a `queue.Queue`); `signals/` holds the receiver,
  the processor, the throttle and an empty package file (P10-S009).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: (1) the processor calls the secondary screener; it calls no quality
  scorer or step executor of its own (the `quality_scorer` argument is stored and never used: EV-10, CD-21); the step
  executor and the quality scorer run inside the screener's `screen()`, to which `main.py` passes them (L3281 to
  L3283; P05-S055; the screener's header, `screening/secondary_screener.py` L3 to L4: "runs step_executor, scores via
  quality_scorer"; A07's file, interface only); and the entry gate is reached only through `continue_from_gate`, with
  no non-test caller of `EntryGate.add` (P05-S043). INTERPRETATION: the document is right that those stages run, but
  places them before the screener, not inside it, and puts the entry gate on every signal's path, which the code does
  not. (2) the table has no `created_at` column, and has had none at any of the 45 revisions of `core/schema.sql`
  reachable from the baseline (script over `git log` and `git show`, in the check; the table was created with `ts` in
  `bcf03b5`, 2026-04-17); `core/migrations.py` at the baseline names no `created_at` for it. Current external context
  (sqlite.org "Quirks, Caveats, and Gotchas In SQLite", section 8 "Double-quoted String Literals Are Accepted",
  re-read 18-Nov-2026): SQLite "will also interpret a double-quotes string as string literal if it does not match any
  valid identifier", so "a misspelled double-quoted identifier will be interpreted as a string literal, rather than
  generating an error". INTERPRETATION: by that contrast, an unquoted name that matches no column, as the query's
  `created_at` is, raises an error, so the query run as written would be expected to fail rather than list rows; the
  page does not state this case in its own words, and the exact error and the SQLite version are UNKNOWN / NOT
  ESTABLISHED. (3) there is no queue module in `signals/` and none ever existed (P10-S125); the queue is a
  `queue.Queue` built in `main.py` (L3402 to L3404) and handed to the receiver and the processor. INTERPRETATION:
  read as naming a module, README's "signal processor/queue" names one that never existed (the v2 design's planned
  `signals/signal_queue.py`, P10-S125); read as the processor's handling of the queue, it agrees in substance; the
  text does not settle which was meant. MATCH part (FACT): the same architecture lines' "ThreadPoolExecutor (5
  workers)" and "503 at 80% queue capacity" agree with the file's `worker_count: 5` and P15's 240 of 300 (P08-S012;
  WD-10).
- **Evidence:** P05-S043, P05-S055; P10-S009, P10-S125, P10-S142, P10-S144, P10-S187; the document and code lines
  above (re-read).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor), with a MATCH part. HISTORICAL against FACT; current external context
  (re-read in the check, 18-Nov-2026); INTERPRETATION for (2)'s effect and for the reading of (3). Not counted elsewhere: the same architecture lines' "minute
  precision" is WD-06's; the operations runbook's anonymous `/health` check is WI-08's; the incident runbook's "Check
  ngrok/tunnel status" (L248) is a network-path source of WI-09. KNOWN DEFECT not applicable (documents).
- **Severity or impact where established:** minor: (2) would leave an operator without the audit rows at the first step
  of a missed-signal incident; (1), and (3) on one reading, mislead a reader only.
- **Owner:** A06 (the documents' A06 content); A03 (the table the runbook queries); the documents' maintenance is
  outside Phase 1.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 95; STUDY question 53; CD-32, CD-33, CD-34; IS-28, IS-146, IS-147, IS-160;
  WD-06; WI-08; WI-09; EV-10; WI-21 (the never-built queue module itself; only README's text is counted here).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 53 bears on treatment only.

##### XA-04 — UNKNOWN: no stored link between a `signals` row and the HTTP request (`webhook_audit` row) that produced it; no admitted source states whether one was intended

- **Area and flow:** storage at the A03 boundary: the two durable records of one webhook request.
- **Issue category:** none established (intent not established; STUDY question 36).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** not established. No source found for a link (intent-sources file, section 6.2,
  question 36: "no source found"). LOCKED-YAML `identity_traceability` L444 to L447 (2026-04-17; IS-103) requires
  `signal_id`, `trade_id` and `order_id` on "Every log line and report row that involves a trade activity";
  INTERPRETATION: a request-level audit row is not a trade activity, so the principle does not state such a link.
  SCHEMA L576 to L577 (IS-137): the audit row is "Used for forensics + rate analysis".
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L529 to L534 (`_write_audit(scanner_name, source_ip,
  payload_size, response_code, accepted_count, rejected_count, duration_ms)`, no signal identifier), L677 (one
  `received_at` per request), L963 to L980 (the INSERT, no audit key); `core/schema.sql` L42 to L79, L581 to L593.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no column links a `signals` row to its request's audit row, and the
  audit row names no signal; `received_at` is taken once per request before the loop, the audit `ts` when the row is
  written after processing (WD-14). INTERPRETATION (P11-S030): a row can be matched to its request only approximately
  (scanner, time window, counts); the stored `webhook_payload` of each row is the request body (sanitised), which
  narrows the match but is not a key.
- **Evidence:** P11-S030, P11-S031; P03-S050; the code lines above (re-read).
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: no intent source and no defect the code itself
  demonstrates (a lack of intent evidence alone is not an ISSUE; this AUDIT, section 1.6).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established.
- **Owner:** A06 (both writes); A03 (schema); A15 (the readers that reconcile the two tables, WI-18).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 18.1 point 74 (WI-18), STUDY question 36; WD-14; WI-18.
- **Rama:** needed: STUDY question 36 (whether a link between a `signals` row and its request was intended) is the
  only way to settle the intent; OPEN FOR RAMA.
