## 13. Records observations

True discrepancies found in a project record by the parts; none is a result about the legacy system, none is counted as MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN, and nothing in any register, record or the STUDY is changed here (D-13; Claude keeps the registers, Document 03). Each is OPEN FOR RAMA where a record's wording would change. The STUDY statements that the AUDIT refines or corrects are listed in Appendix A.

1. **WA-02 — Open Issues Register entry A02-13 dates the setting of `require_hmac` to false on 02-Jul-2026; the repository history shows 28-Apr-2026.** What the record says: the entry's question reads "After `webhook.require_hmac` was set false on 02-Jul-2026, ..."; its Evidence field and the A02 AUDIT and RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (IC-38 rows, X-01) date the value's comment by its line date (`d922007`, 02-Jul-2026); the STUDY quotes the entry exactly (P02-S036, P09-S075) and dates the comment the same way (P02-S041). What the history shows (FACT, `git log -G require_hmac` on `config/system_config.yaml`, with `git show` limited to those lines): `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026) adds `require_hmac: true`; `2d1e580` (27-Apr-2026) adds the bind comment that names `require_hmac=true`; `a1704df` (28-Apr-2026) changes the value to `false` ("TEMP: disabled for Chartink (no HMAC support)"); `67f21c0` (01-Jun-2026) keeps `false` and replaces the comment ("clarify require_hmac=false is permanent"); `d922007` (02-Jul-2026) changes only the bracketed text; no commit after `a1704df` changes the value, and no code sets it. Effect: none on any classification (A02 X-01 stays "ISSUE; FACT"); only the entry's premise date is inexact. OPEN FOR RAMA: whether the entry's wording is to be corrected, and how. A02-13 keeps its ID, its history (OPEN QUESTION, Rama's answer of 09-Nov-2026), its owner field, its status and its treatment (AGREED, with X-01). Evidence: section 6.3 (WA-02); IS-36, IS-152, IS-168, IS-185, IC-02.
2. **EV-15 — A03 P-02 says that no caller passes `ts` to `update_signal_status`; the screener does.** What the record says: the A03 AUDIT, P-02 (repeated in the register row): "`ts` is accepted and unused, and no caller passes it". What the code shows (FACT, `screening/secondary_screener.py` L611 to L620, A07's file, interface only, lines of 17-Apr-2026): the screener passes `ts=ts`; the processor's 26 calls pass none (P05-S054). Effect: none on P-02's substance, since the method discards `ts`; only the wording is inexact. OPEN FOR RAMA: whether the wording is to be noted or corrected; P-02 keeps its ID, owners, classification, treatment and status. Evidence: section 9.3 (EV-15); IS-100; STUDY 18.1 point 69.
3. **XA-12 — the Audit Findings Register's "Raised with Rama" cell of eleven rows handed to A06** (A03 P-02, P-11, P-12, Q-11, R-08; A04 RD-14, CS-05, CS-08, RL-16; A05 IN-08, XC-04) reads "Not yet: after ChatGPT's review of the AUDIT (Document 04, section 4, steps 7 and 8)", while the same rows' treatment cells record treatment groups decided in the later rounds (AGREED; for P-12 "OPEN" with status UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). The register's section notes describe the cell as recording the intent batches, and its field definition includes the treatment round's part or group; each of the eleven rows was re-checked by script; the same cell text stands in 413 lines of the register, beyond the eleven not examined. Neither cell is quoted as a status, and nothing in the register is changed. Recorded without judgement, as STUDY section 16 recorded it (P09-S099); whether the register's cells are to be read or updated differently is for Rama. Evidence: section 12.3.2 (XA-12); STUDY 18.1 point 101.

EV-16 (the direction's wording of the A06 boundary against the index) was withdrawn in the check: the wording exists only in the direction, a working instruction that is not a retained record (D-17), and every project record and the STUDY quote the index exactly, so there is no records discrepancy (section 9.3; STUDY 18.1 point 70).

| 53 | the runbooks meant to be kept in step with the code? | XA-03; WI-08 | OPEN QUESTION narrowed | No; for treatment (XA-03) |

Not repeated (STUDY section 19, its closing paragraphs): the questions whose answer or treatment an earlier record already holds (the all-interfaces bind on the URL token alone, A02-13 with X-01; a scanner outage at boot as a warning or CRITICAL, A01-22 with S-44; the boot latency, A01-25 with C32; the ATR intent for the positional strategies, A02-03; the broker limits behind the bucket values, A04-31 with RL-14; the cache's symbol text and the alias values, A05-39 and A05-41 with IR-10; the alias tool after 2026-07-05, A05-49 with IR-13; one rule for a symbol missing from the instrument cache, A05-02 with IN-09; A04-33's input (4), an agreed Phase 2 input "not to be raised again"), and the points the STUDY did not propose for Rama as minor or answerable from the code and the documents (the parts' tables record where each is disposed of, for example WI-15, WI-19, WI-20, PQ-08, ET-08, EV-07 and XA-07).

## 16. Findings for discussion with Rama

Every finding is for the discussion with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); nothing is raised with Rama before ChatGPT has reviewed this AUDIT, and none is decided here.

(a) Established by evidence: the ISSUE items in part order (sections 5 to 12; their classifications are in each record; "(minor)", "(latent)" or "(minor, latent)" after an ID follows the record's own qualifier, and "(KNOWN DEFECT, provisional)" marks the one KNOWN DEFECT). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items are not findings established by evidence; they are listed after the ISSUEs, and those whose classification needs Rama are in (b).

- WI-01: The in-flight heartbeat is never connected, so the sweeper evicts every claim older than 60 s and logs it as a stalled worker, against the FIX-011 design.
- WI-02: WR13 says every POST writes one `webhook_audit` row regardless of outcome; some POSTs write none.
- WI-03: Shutdown: the receiver's `stop()` neither flushes nor closes the server, nothing waits for requests in progress, and the documented orders conflict.
- WI-04 (minor): End-of-day alerts skip the kill switch and the excluded-symbol list as well as the entry window; only the window skip has a stated reason.
- WI-05 (minor): The server runs a fixed pool of eight waitress threads with a connection limit of 100, both literals in `main.py`; WR11 still describes one thread per request.
- WI-06 (minor): The 500 handler's CRITICAL line carries Flask's generic text, not the failing exception; the handler also depends on Flask debug mode staying off.
- WI-07: After a partial PB-01 wiring failure the receiver keeps the capture worker, against the code's own "continuing without it" and "fail-safe miss" texts.
- WI-08: Detecting a failed webhook server: the boot self-check works as stated, but a bind failure can be masked and nothing watches the server after boot; the runbook's first check calls `/health` anonymously.
- WI-09: The documented network path (loopback bind behind a TLS proxy) conflicts with the configured all-interfaces bind and with the receiver's address handling; which path production had is unknown.
- WI-10 (minor): The alias file is read once from a path relative to the working directory, outside `--config`; an alias miss keeps the received letter case.
- WI-11 (minor, latent): The in-flight sweeper has no exception handling and nothing supervises or restarts it.
- WI-12 (minor): The scan-name check: a non-text `scan_name` is answered 500, not 400; the space-to-underscore rule is as the comment states; WR4b depends on AR12's cached first result.
- WI-13 (minor): The FIX-074 numeric cast converts six body keys that nothing downstream ever receives; its only effects are a 400 for two of them, WARNING lines for the others and a 500 on overflow.
- WI-14 (minor): The body is parsed as JSON whatever its declared type; repeated keys and non-finite literals pass without notice; extra keys are tolerated as the code's own comments expect.
- WI-15 (minor): The receiver's own docstrings list response codes, statuses and a start-up call that differ from the code.
- WI-16 (minor): The intraday and end-of-day routes parse the same Chartink body with separate code and different rules; the EOD docstring says the formats are the same.
- WI-17 (minor, latent): The receiver writes the scanner name into the `signals.strategy` column; nothing later writes the strategy the scanner map assigns.
- WI-18: The audit row's counts do not match the rows stored on several routes, while a report states an identity between them.
- WI-19 (minor): The expected-manager registry's receiver entry and the `main.py` census comment carry line references from an older commit.
- WI-20 (minor): Schema comments state defaults for the receiver's queue and server settings that the schema does not implement.
- WI-21 (minor): Planned signal modules and exception classes never came into use; the `SignalError` docstring still names the receiver and a module that never existed.
- WI-25 (minor): The receiver has exactly the two routes WR2 names; the operator procedures of 18-Apr-2026 send kill requests to routes on port 5000 that never existed.
- WA-01 (minor): Authentication at the repository values rests on one shared secret, as a URL token or as the HMAC key over the raw body, with nothing that binds a request to a time; the mechanism matches the later sources, but the C6 docstring's "not replayable" holds only for the signature value.
- WA-03 (minor): A token or signature text holding a non-ASCII character makes `hmac.compare_digest` raise `TypeError`, so the request is answered 500, with a CRITICAL line, instead of 401.
- WA-04: Raw request body text is written to the log on every 400 path without the storage sanitiser; for a UTF-16 or UTF-32 body the sanitiser's matches also miss in the stored copy; two sanitiser comments point to stale lines.
- WA-05 (minor): `/health` withholds whether `require_hmac` is on, as its comment intends, while `/webhook` tells any unauthenticated caller the same thing in its 401 reason.
- WA-06: `triggered_at` is read as IST, as the sources say; a time-only text is dated with the day of receipt even when it is later than the moment of receipt, and no check refuses a time ahead of the receiver's clock; the `%p` match depends on the process locale.
- WA-07: Trigger prices "nan" and "inf" pass the positive-price check, which has no finiteness, upper-bound or tick test; a symbol is refused only when empty, although WR10 names "empty/malformed"; no instrument check.
- WA-08: The oversize path: the 1 MiB Flask limit bounds what the application reads, not what the server receives and buffers, and the body is read before the shutting-down and per-IP checks, so an oversized request is never counted by the limiter.
- WA-10 (minor): Two expiry notions with different start times stand side by side: both age checks measure from `triggered_at`, while the stored `expires_at` counts from receipt, so the stored deadline is not the one the code applies.
- WD-01: Claims kept after an exception inside the `IntegrityError` handler: the store-failure release does not cover the handler's own read and put (the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT P03-S034 weighed: condition (a) not established; ISSUE).
- WD-02 (minor, latent): Claims kept after an exception between the claim and the INSERT's `try`; one source shown (a symbol carrying an unpaired surrogate).
- WD-03 (minor, latent): Every `IntegrityError` from the INSERT is read as a fingerprint conflict; when no row carries the fingerprint the answer is still `DUPLICATE`.
- WD-04: The re-queue route treats every request that meets a `QUEUE_FULL` row as a retry: a new alert in the same slot revives the earlier signal with the new price and trigger time (A03 R-08's A06 side extended).
- WD-05: `QUEUE_FULL` recovery rests on a sender's retry, as stated; a failed `QUEUE_FULL` mark and a restart leave `QUEUED` rows with no queue item, whose retries are refused and which nothing re-reads.
- WD-06: The duplicate window: FIX-131's five-minute setting replaced the minute rule, but the two layers start their windows differently, the minute texts remain in five places, and FIX-131's "59s apart ... share same epoch" holds only while the cache holds the key.
- WD-07: Layer 1, keyed on the symbol alone, refuses another scanner's signal with `IN_PROCESS`, no stored row and a 200 answer; the sources of 17-Apr-2026 conflict on this case.
- WD-08 (latent): The in-flight claim records no signal: any release removes whatever claim the symbol holds, so after an eviction a late release can free a newer signal's claim (WR17 tracks "(signal_id, symbol)").
- WD-09 (minor): The per-IP limiter works as C-2 and AB-910 state, but its map has no fixed cap, against "so the map can't grow unbounded".
- WD-10: The 503 threshold fires at 240 as P15 states, but it measures only the dispatcher's hand-off buffer: work waiting for the five workers sits in an unbounded executor queue that back-pressure never sees.
- WD-11 (minor, latent): A capacity of 0 or less, or a `backpressure_pct` outside 0 to 1, reaches the receiver unguarded: every intraday request refused, or the request-level 503 never firing (A02 L-12's A06 side).
- WD-12 (minor): Back-pressure refusals leave no log line; the request-level 503 is recorded only as an audit row (503, 0, 0) that other 503 answers also write; no alert, as intended.
- WD-13 (minor): The queue settings carry different meanings at different consumers, and no A06 code supplies the live depth to the port-8080 `/metrics`, so its queue fields stay 0 (A20's check passes whenever it reads `/metrics`; A15's row shows UNAVAILABLE).
- WD-14 (minor): `webhook_audit.ts` is the time the row is written, after processing, while the schema says "when request arrived"; every `/webhook` answer waits for the audit write.
- WD-15 (minor): The EOD heartbeat, meant to make the 17:00 capture visible, can itself fail with no record of the failure: the receiver ignores the helper's False (A03 D-08's A06 side; registry and retention handed over).
- PQ-01: No per-signal deadline: `pipeline_timeout_sec: 30`, stated as a "hard per-signal processing deadline", is validated but read by nothing; a worker blocked in a call holds one of the five slots without limit.
- PQ-02: `stop()`: the executor wait has no limit, against SP4's "5s timeout" and the docstring's "up to 5s"; every drained or backlogged signal runs intake, screening, sizing, risk and reservation before the pre-placement `SHUTDOWN` reject; a timed-out join could leave items behind or drop one (A01 C18's A06 side).
- PQ-03 (minor): The `EXPIRED` alert: one warning per 60 s for the whole processor, read and written without a lock; its text repeats "age"; the receiver's own `EXPIRED` refusals raise none.
- PQ-04: FIX-069 retries re-queue at once, although the comment says "15s each" and "reconciler will retry"; each retry re-runs the whole intake and A07 path against the original age; the timeout retry was removed as the later A-2 decision states (A04 RL-16's A06 side).
- PQ-05 (minor, latent): The FIX-007 rate-limiter pre-check never runs under `main.py`; inside it, a FIX-069 retry dict would raise `KeyError` before its clean-up, and its full-queue reject writes bare `REJECTED` while its own log line, FIX-048 and Record 02 name `REJECTED_QUEUE_FULL`; a full queue is written three ways.
- PQ-06 (latent): Exceptions raised outside the worker's `try` blocks end in discarded futures, unlogged, with no status and no claim release; a dispatcher that ended would not be noticed or restarted, and nothing reads `is_running()`; several intake handlers swallow errors silently.
- PQ-07 (KNOWN DEFECT, provisional): A05 IN-08's A06 side: `_sector_for` asks the instrument cache for `sector_for` and `get_sector`, which do not exist, so it returns "UNKNOWN" for every symbol; all four KNOWN DEFECT conditions hold on A06's own sources (provisional).
- PQ-08 (minor): Texts in the processor and its configuration that the code contradicts, not counted elsewhere: the "naive datetime" docstring, the plain-dict map comments, the 60-second default, the resume paths' ":607 60s expiry", the "60s signal expiry" allocator comments, and the 60 s and 90 s figures of P9a, the exception docstring and Record 02; the cache parameter's "lot_size" purpose (added in the check).
- EV-01: Status writes are unconditional: a later write replaces the stored status and reason, so a final status can be overwritten; no time and no trail of changes is kept, against P16 and SP9.
- EV-02: A failing status write leaves a status that misdescribes the signal: `PLACEMENT_FAILED` for a signal never checked or for an order handed to the placer; `PROCESSING` after a screener reject; `PASSED` after an allocator drop (case (d), added in the check).
- EV-03: The reservation release depends on a status write that precedes it: a failing reject or `PLACEMENT_FAILED` write leaves the reservation held, and the safe wrapper retries only the status.
- EV-04: Placement timeout: a failing kill-switch call or `TIMEOUT` write sends the reservation to the `PLACEMENT_FAILED` handler, which releases it, against A-2's "do NOT release".
- EV-05: Two release calls for one reservation: after a `BrokerError` from `place()` (including a client-side limit that FIX-069 re-queues), after a failing late-abort release, and after a failing release that follows a successful FIX-069 re-queue (which also leaves the signal queued while its row reads `PLACEMENT_FAILED`).
- EV-06 (minor): The FIX-069 re-queue leaves the row `RESERVED` with no reservation until the retry starts; a re-queue that fails is recorded `PLACEMENT_FAILED` with no reason.
- EV-07: Status vocabulary: no code writes `TRADED`, which P16, the schema and readers expect (IC-28 resolved for `PROCESSED`); a latent double prefix; the schema's status comment omits statuses A06 writes.
- EV-08: A stop leaves rows `PROCESSING`, `PASSED`, `RESERVED` or parked; only earlier-day `PROCESSING` rows are swept, nothing re-reads rows at start-up and such rows are never deleted.
- EV-09 (minor): The evidence recorder adds a synchronous `fsync` under one process-wide lock on the worker before every placement and in each reject handler; the processor still says "never blocks admission", which the recorder's later docstring withdraws; whether it ran in production is unknown.
- EV-10 (minor): Collaborators documented but absent or unused: no `set_shadow_engine()` exists although the shadow engine says it is injected through it; `bus` and `quality_scorer` are required and never used; private names of A09's modules are read.
- EV-11 (latent): The four other entry points into the processor run outside its executor: uncounted, not awaited by `stop()`, with no guard against a second resumption; their bypass of the age check, screening and claim release is as stated.
- ET-01: An entry is counted at the admit, before `place()`, whatever happens next; the sources' "PLACED entries" against the docstring's own "records the placement before returning" (IC-14); an attempt that sends nothing, or that the broker refuses, still consumes the 20 s gap and the 300 s cooldown.
- ET-02: The FIX-069 rate-limit retry meets the throttle measured from its own first admit: at the file values it is admitted only if it comes back at least 300 s after that admit and within the 600 s age, so the retry that FIX-069 and A-2 keep ends `REJECTED_ENTRY_THROTTLED` unless it spends most of the cooldown before reaching the throttle (A04 RL-16's throttle side).
- ET-03 (minor): The `INTRADAY SIGNAL` alert, which reads as an entry ticket, is sent before the throttle on all three paths; a signal the throttle then refuses has been announced and is dropped with no alert of the refusal; reserving before the throttle and releasing on a refusal is the stated design.
- ET-04 (minor): Which gate refused is kept only in the free-text reason and in counters that last for the life of the process; the `category` the throttle returns is never read; the daily review groups the status with capital-check rejects.
- ET-05: The same-symbol cooldown runs from entry to entry and has no exit input, as every throttle source states; the later 07-Aug ruling, as the legacy documents attribute it, says a flat symbol "immediately becomes eligible again" (IC-24; the legacy's OPEN-2, unanswered at the baseline).
- ET-08 (minor): A unit test comment names the burst gate for a refusal that the code makes at the gap gate; the assertion does not check which gate refused.
- CK-01 (minor): The scanner check uses GET in both fetchers; five sources of 17-Apr-2026 say HEAD and two say GET (A01 B-07's A06 side).
- CK-02: Severity of a failed scanner check: "CRITICAL" (P17, the URL file, SPEC C.1) against a warning at boot and an exit code with no alarm in the script; the sources conflict (A01 S-44's A06 side).
- CK-03 (minor): The Appendix A script's scope: P17 and SPEC C.1 give it four checks and a CRITICAL exit; PF1 to PF8 of the same date make it a scanner-URL check only, which the code follows.
- CK-04 (minor): PB-01's "connectivity check is WARN-only" (13-Jul-2026): no code reads the scanner type in the check, so in the script an unreachable PB-01 address returns 1 like any other.
- CK-05 (minor): Which file supplies the URL the check fetches: S15, the URL file's header, M33 SC7 and two later legacy records say `config/chartink_scanners.yaml`; the code always uses the map's own `chartink_url`, so the URL file's values reach no check (IC-22; A02 F-03's A06 side).
- CK-06 (minor): The 32 committed URL values: the repository's own note still calls them placeholders to be replaced "once scanners are active", while legacy records from late April 2026 onward describe Chartink alerts in use and later sources call the values real; which values production checked is not established (IC-18).
- CK-07 (minor): A network failure loses its cause: both wired fetchers return the exception text as the "body", so the library records no error; the script's table says `has-content`, its verbose view "(empty body)", and the boot log names only the scanner.
- CK-08 (minor): Run as documented, the script depends on the repository root being importable, which no procedure arranges and the script, unlike most scripts in its folder, does not arrange itself; then it ends with "cannot import core.config_loader" before any check.
- CK-09 (minor): The script's stated figures and outputs against the code: "3 attempts * 10s timeout = 30s" is no bound; PF3's "response size" is neither recorded nor printed; "returning expected shape" is not tested.
- CK-10 (minor): What the checks can show: a pass means only that chartink.com answered the configured addresses with a final 2xx from the legacy host; nothing ties those addresses to the alerts, the webhook path or the receiver, so the result can pass while intake is broken and fail while intake works.
- CK-19 (minor): `--timeout` accepts zero and negative values; under the published Requests and urllib3 sources every fetch then fails before any request is sent, and the script reports every scanner "unreachable" (added in the check).
- XA-01 (minor): Which refusals leave a `signals` row, and the status names readers expect: the receiver's refusals other than layer 1 store no row (P16 and the schema comment say every signal gets a row); readers in A15 and A12 count, classify or prune `DUPLICATE`, the answer-only statuses and a `DROPPED_` family that no code writes, and two A15 readers test for `DUPLICATE` by substring, which also matches the `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL` that the processor writes.
- XA-02 (minor): The five existing technical records against the code: no A06 claim differs, but three record statements describe the receiver and the processor otherwise than the code (construction "Flask object only", a separate EOD route, the processor named as a user of `build_sr_detector`), one boot-table statement is A01's, and no record treats A06 in depth.
- XA-03 (minor): Legacy operating and architecture documents that the code contradicts: the architecture document's processor pipeline, the incident runbook's `webhook_audit` query on a column that does not exist, and README's "signal processor/queue".

UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (14; listed for completeness, not findings established by evidence):

- WI-22: The effective receiver and server settings and the installed library versions are not established.
- WI-23: Library behaviour at the server boundary: two points now read in waitress's published source, the rest not established.
- WI-24: Whether production ran WR4b (`3b15bbf`), the only receiver change after the last recorded deployment, is not established.
- WA-09: The 503 answers rely on a sender retry that Chartink's own pages do not describe; whether Chartink retried, and its other behaviour at the legacy dates, are not established.
- WA-11: Library behaviour at the authentication and parsing boundary: four points now read in published source (CPython; for storage, also SQLite); the installed versions and the rest are not established.
- WD-16: the runtime facts of duplicates, limits, claims and back-pressure; Chartink's retries and source addresses; the dispatcher's lag.
- PQ-09: the runtime and environment facts of the processor's intake (alerts sent, clock, versions, effective settings, backlogs, call durations).
- EV-13: whether the processor was meant to call A04's limiter; the FIX-007 pre-check has never been wired by `main.py`, and if wired it would spend a second `order` token per entry (A04 RL-18's A06 side).
- EV-14: the runtime facts of event creation, persistence and the handoff.
- ET-06: which of several near-simultaneous approvals the throttle admits is decided by who takes its lock first, after risk approval and reservation, not by receipt order, `triggered_at` or score; the one legacy decision on this (DEC10) is "OPEN — Rama's call" at the baseline.
- ET-07: the runtime and environment facts of the throttle (effective values, deployment, restarts and processes, lock order, a `place()` that declines without raising, refusals and retries that occurred, the dormant paths, the Python version).
- CK-11: the Chartink-side configuration, the checks' runtime results and the library versions on which part CK depends.
- XA-04: no stored link between a `signals` row and the HTTP request (`webhook_audit` row) that produced it; no admitted source states whether one was intended.
- XA-05: the runtime and other-owner facts on which part XA's items depend.

(b) Needing Rama's intent to classify (every record whose "Rama" field says his answer is needed to classify; each is marked OPEN FOR RAMA in its record; the questions are put only after ChatGPT's review):

## 18. Coverage gaps

- Runtime: no HTTP request, `signals`, `webhook_audit` or `cron_heartbeat` row, log line, metric, alert, crontab, unit state or deployed configuration of the legacy machines survives, and no file under `data_store/` is tracked (STUDY section 1.6). Whether any request reached the receiver, which routes and statuses occurred, whether any signal was accepted, queued, processed, expired, retried, throttled or rejected, whether any row was written, updated or deleted, how long claims were held, whether the sweeper evicted any claim, whether any exception route (WD-01's included) or release interleaving occurred, whether any alert was sent, whether the scanner checks or the script ran and what they found, and what Chartink sent, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 2.4; for example WI-22 to WI-24, WA-09, WA-11, WD-16, PQ-09, EV-14, ET-07, CK-11, XA-05). A schedule, unit or pre-flight declaration is never proof that a job ran (A16, A17, A20). The legacy documents' runtime statements ("deployed", "measured", "NOT deployed to either VM", request counts) are HISTORICAL and weighed for intent or impact only.
- Library versions and external behaviour: the repository pins `flask==3.1.3`, `waitress==3.0.2`, `cachetools==7.1.1`, `requests==2.33.1`, `pydantic==2.13.0` and `pyyaml==6.0.3`; Python, Werkzeug, urllib3 and SQLite are not pinned; the installed versions on the legacy machines are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Statements read from published sources (waitress 3.0.2, CPython, Requests, urllib3, SQLite, cachetools 7.1.1) are facts about the published text, not about the installed packages (WI-23, WA-11, CK-19). Not reachable: the Linux `socket(7)` manual page (WI-23: the effect of `SO_REUSEADDR` on a port held by another listener); in part WA's check, sqlite.org itself (SQLite's source was read from its official Git mirror). No generic Python test was needed or run. Chartink's stated behaviour is that of its pages as read on 17 and 18-Nov-2026 (current external context), four of the five pages read in part; its behaviour in the legacy period is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (WA-09, CK-11).
- Sources not in the repository: the Chartink side (which alerts existed, their paths, unique or duplicate settings, frequency and bodies; whether Chartink retried; its source addresses); the production network path in front of port 5000 (WI-09); the production configuration overlays, the environment file and the working directory (not read, D-07; WI-10, WI-22); the records' worktree at `e7bf477` (XA-05); the production databases and logs (both legacy machines terminated, as reported by Rama and not independently verified).
- Consumers not traced: the internals of the owner topics' code that A06's contracts reach (strategy control, screening and the entry gate, A07; the retest path and the zone warmer, A08; the price rule, risk, reservation and the governor, A09; the placer, A10; the end-of-day cleanup and the in-flight recovery, A12; the kill switch, A13; the notifier, A14; the health server, reports, dashboard and evidence recorder, A15; the cron tools and the heartbeat helper, A16; the capture worker, the allocator and the V3 chain, A18; the pre-flight checks, A20) are handed to their owners (section 14) as boundary items; A06 audited only its own side of each.
- Each part's own list of what it could not establish, with the evidence that would settle each item: sections 5.7 (WI), 6.7 (WA), 7.7 (WD), 8.7 (PQ), 9.7 (EV), 10.7 (ET), 11.7 (CK) and 12.7 (XA); and the intended-behaviour sources file, section 7.
- Security restriction (D-07; Rama's standing instructions): no `.env` file, token file, key file, `*.local.yaml` overlay or `config/accounts.csv` was opened; every repository-wide search excluded these files by pathspec; the webhook secret is named only as `WEBHOOK_SECRET` and credential variables are recorded by name only, as configuration; passages that hold an account identifier (the WR4b docstring, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L112 to L128) or machine paths are cited by line only or written with `<ACCOUNT_ID>`; passages of legacy documents that concern credential exposure, history or validity were not used; no credential-validity, credential-history or exposure investigation was made and no security finding, issue, alert or register entry of any kind is recorded. A03 G-19 is cited by its ID only and was not examined. Effective configuration values (overlays, the environment) were not read; every configuration value cited is the committed one.
- Checks done: each of the eight parts and the intended-behaviour sources file was drafted by one helper and checked against the code and the sources by a second, with the legacy clone at the baseline (`970aabf`, empty `git status --short`) before and after every pass (each part's sections .1 and .7). The cross-part check then completed the sources file (IS-177 to IS-223, each re-read at `970aabf` with its date; IS-34, IS-36, IS-49, IS-90, IS-129, IS-152, IS-168, IC-02 and CD-13 amended), checked the eight parts against each other (all 185 allocated items with one owning disposition each; no behaviour counted twice; every cross-reference resolving; every hash and date against `git log`; every quotation against its lines; owners against the Master System Investigation Index, among them `config/expected_managers.yaml` and `config/symbol_aliases.yaml` as A02's files; the A06-to-A07 line adopted nowhere; the inherited items' quotations identical to the registers and records), and mapped the parts' citations to IS numbers; no result, count or classification was changed, and the clone was at `970aabf` with an empty `git status --short` before and after. Claude assembled this document from the checked parts with a helper (the parts' check logs are working material, not kept: D-17): the totals were re-counted by script (58 MATCH, 84 ISSUE, 14 UNKNOWN, 2 KNOWN DEFECT at that check and 1 after Revision 1 (WD-01, ChatGPT's finding M-01), 156 counted, 160 IDs, one withdrawn, three records observations), every record ID of the eight parts was checked to appear once as a record heading or match row (EV-12, whose block stays in section 9.3, also has a pointer row in section 9.4), 58 references to working files and helper labels and eight references to the parts' own check logs were replaced in the parts' text and one other topic's ID written without its topic was corrected (part EV, section 9.5.2), each logged in the part's check log, and the redaction scan was run on the assembled file with no hit. The assembled document was then checked independently against sections 5 to 12, the sources file, the STUDY and its evidence and the registers: every total was re-counted by script from the record headings and match rows (unchanged), and the coverage tables, the owner index of section 14, the IC, CD and RS citations and the question table of section 15 were re-collected from the parts and the sources file; the 52 inherited items and the 11 register entries were compared with the Audit Findings Register, the Open Issues Register, the prior AUDITs and P09. In this document's own sections the check corrected the waitress files named in section 1.5, the convention of section 1.7 on prior topics' IDs, one KNOWN DEFECT consideration missing from section 1.8 (EV-08) and one pair description there, the register entries of section 4 (their Status and Owner fields now quoted), one item missing from the owner index of section 14 (ET-11, under A01) and one quotation in Appendix A (P06-S006); in the parts it corrected five embedding defects (a table row whose code text split its cells, WD-20; three bare references to STUDY sections 11 and 12 that collided with this AUDIT's numbering, parts WI and XA; one reference to the parts' check logs, part XA), each logged in the part's check log. No result, count or classification was changed.

## Appendix A — STUDY statements that the AUDIT refines

The independent check of the STUDY corrected its statements before this AUDIT began (STUDY section 1.5), and ChatGPT's review passed the STUDY, Revision 1, on 18-Nov-2026. The AUDIT corrects, narrows or adds to these STUDY statements (found in the parts' section .2 tables, records and check logs, and in the cross-part and assembly work, whose working records are not kept: D-17); the STUDY is not edited:

- P02-S036, P02-S041 and P09-S075 (and STUDY 18.1 point 13): the STUDY quotes Open Issues Register A02-13 exactly ("set false on 02-Jul-2026") and dates the value's comment by its line date (`d922007`, 02-Jul-2026). The repository history shows `require_hmac` false since `a1704df` (28-Apr-2026, "TEMP"), the comment rewritten by `67f21c0` (01-Jun-2026, "permanent") and `d922007` changing only its bracketed text; the value was set false the day after the bind comment that rests on `true` was written (WA-02, a records observation, section 13; IS-152, IS-168, IS-185).
- P03-S034 (STUDY 18.1 point 31): the STUDY's provisional KNOWN DEFECT is not confirmed for the handler-internal route: WD-01 classifies it as ISSUE, KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given, condition (a) not established (ChatGPT's AUDIT review of 18-Nov-2026, finding M-01); P03-S034 is not changed; also narrowed: a database I/O error joins the STUDY's lock-wait example as a trigger; the legacy's own P3-s14 investigation of 17-Jul-2026 (IS-189), not cited by the STUDY, lists the `IntegrityError` path among the reject paths that "release correctly" and directs the catch-all that commit `a9a686e` added; the historical sources do not establish whether L1016 was intended to cover any store failure or INSERT failures only; this ambiguity was weighed in WD-01: for the handler-internal route, condition (a) is not established and the AUDIT classification remains ISSUE. The source ambiguity is recorded as historical context, not as a pending classification question for ChatGPT or Rama.
- P09-S083 to P09-S085 and P04-S045 (A05 IN-08's A06 side; STUDY section 16, partly covered item (d)): the STUDY left conditions (c) and (d) for the AUDIT on A06's own sources; PQ-07 establishes both and gives KNOWN DEFECT (provisional) as A06's classification of its own code; A05's record of IN-08 is unchanged.
- P06-S006 (STUDY section 9): "the stored times have meaning only inside one process", said of the throttle's `time.monotonic` clock, differs from the Python 3.15.2 `time` page, which says the clock is the same for all processes since 3.5; ET-09 follows the page; its result (MATCH) is unchanged.
- P07-S011 (STUDY section 10; 18.1 point 87): its pattern-based basis is weakened: Chartink's webhook sample (P07-S048) pairs the scan name "Short term breakouts" with the address segment "short-term-breakouts", and the Stock Screener FAQ quotes real screener addresses as hyphenated lower-case words, so the repository's form is also a form real addresses take; the STUDY's other basis, the files' own "placeholders" note, stands (CK-06).
- P11-S028 (STUDY section 14): "per-thread connections with WAL": the store requests WAL mode on each connection (`core/state_store.py` L108) and does not read the pragma's answer; whether WAL was in effect on the production file is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (WD-01).
- P11-S016 (STUDY section 14; 18.1 point 78): to "no A06 writer produces such a row" for `DUPLICATE`, the AUDIT adds that two A15 readers test for `DUPLICATE` by substring, which also matches `REJECTED_DUPLICATE_SYMBOL`, a status the processor writes after the risk engine's check 10 (XA-01).
- P02-S069 (the answer table): waitress 3.0.2 also answers 501 for a `Transfer-Encoding` other than `chunked` (published source, read 18-Nov-2026); not a receiver answer and not an issue (section 6.2.4; WA-08).
- STUDY 18.1 point 2: narrowed to POSTs, since non-POST requests are outside WR13's words (WI-02).
- STUDY 18.1 point 72 and STUDY question 35 (P03-S053): the census time is stated by `main.py` L1658 to L1661 ("BEFORE any manager teardown") and the effect contract (IS-216), so the point is rejected as an issue (WD-22, MATCH).
- STUDY 18.1 point 68 (P05-S049): BL-6 and BL-19 (commit `dfc477959f`, 19-Apr-2026) and the `place()` docstring make the placer the owner of the broker 429 retry, so the processor's different branches MATCH (EV-12, changed in the check from UNKNOWN).
- STUDY 18.1 point 56: rejected as an issue: the intent (reconcile before the pipeline) is met by statement order (PQ-17, MATCH).
- STUDY 18.1 point 46: covered by EV-05 route (b), the unguarded late-abort releases on all three paths, the throttle site included; not counted in part ET.
- STUDY 18.2 item 4: narrowed for the published sources: waitress 3.0.2's worker threads are daemon threads and a bind failure propagates out of `serve()` (WI-23; A01 W-24's UNKNOWN stands for the installed version); CPython 3.11's `hexdigest()` is lower-case and `int()` of an infinite float raises (WA-11); Requests and urllib3 fail a zero or negative timeout before any request is sent, which the AUDIT records as CK-19 on the script's `--timeout` (added in the check).
- STUDY 18.2 item 15 (P02-S062): on the published CPython 3.11 and SQLite sources a NaN trigger price is stored as NULL and an infinity as a REAL infinity; the installed versions are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; the storage behaviour is A03's (WA-07, WA-11).
- STUDY section 16, partly covered items (a) to (d) (P09-S097): completed by the parts (WI-28 and PQ-17; EV-11; WI-25; PQ-07), as section 4 sets out.
